Namo tassa bhagavato arahato sammāsambuddhassa

Homage to the Blessed One, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One.

Vinayapiṭake

The Vinaya Piṭaka

Parivāra-aṭṭhakathā

Commentary on the Parivāra

Soḷasamahāvāro

The Sixteen Great Chapters

Paññattivāravaṇṇanā

Exposition on the Chapter of Enactments

Visuddhaparivārassa[P 7.1301][M 0.0137][V 0.0137], parivāroti sāsane;

Dhammakkhandhasarīrassa, khandhakānaṃ anantarā.

In the Dispensation of the Blessed One—who possesses a retinue of utmost purity and a body comprised of the aggregates of Dhamma—immediately after the Khandhakas,

Saṅgahaṃ yo samāruḷho, tassa pubbāgataṃ nayaṃ;

Hitvā dāni karissāmi, anuttānatthavaṇṇanaṃ.

Regarding that Vinaya known as the Parivāra, which was included in the canonical collection, having set aside the method that has come before, I shall now compose an exposition of its unobvious meaning.

1. Tattha [V 0.0138] yaṃ tena bhagavatā…pe… paññattanti ādinayappavattāya tāva pucchāya ayaṃ saṅkhepattho – yo so bhagavā sāsanassa ciraṭṭhitikatthaṃ dhammasenāpatinā saddhammagāravabahumānavegasamussitaṃ añjaliṃ sirasmiṃ patiṭṭhāpetvā yācito dasa atthavase paṭicca vinayapaññattiṃ paññapesi, tena bhagavatā tassa tassa sikkhāpadassa paññattikālaṃ jānatā, tassā tassā sikkhāpadapaññattiyā dasa atthavase passatā; apica pubbenivāsādīhi jānatā, dibbena cakkhunā passatā, tīhi vijjāhi chahi vā pana abhiññāhi jānatā, sabbattha appaṭihatena samantacakkhunā passatā, sabbadhammajānanasamatthāya paññāya jānatā, sabbasattānaṃ cakkhuvisayātītāni tirokuṭṭādigatāni cāpi rūpāni ativisuddhena maṃsacakkhunā ca passatā, attahitasādhikāya samādhipadaṭṭhānāya paṭivedhapaññāya jānatā, parahitasādhikāya karuṇāpadaṭṭhānāya [P 7.1302] desanāpaññāya passatā, arahatā sammāsambuddhena “yaṃ paṭhamaṃ pārājikaṃ paññattaṃ, taṃ kattha paññattaṃ, kaṃ ārabbha paññattaṃ, kismiṃ vatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ, atthi tattha paññatti…pe… kenābhata”nti.

1. Therein, for the question that proceeds by the method beginning, 'What was enacted by that Blessed One…', this, firstly, is the concise meaning: That Blessed One—who, for the sake of the long-lasting stability of the Dispensation, having been entreated by the General of the Dhamma, who placed his folded hands upon his head, raised by the force of his reverence and great esteem for the good Dhamma, laid down the Vinaya regulations in consideration of ten benefits; by that Blessed One—knowing the time for laying down each and every training rule, and seeing the ten benefits in the laying down of each and every training rule; and moreover, knowing by means of the knowledge of past lives and so forth, and seeing with the divine eye; knowing with the three true knowledges or, alternatively, with the six supernormal knowledges; seeing with the unobstructed, all-around eye; knowing with the wisdom capable of knowing all dhammas; seeing even forms beyond the range of the eye, such as those behind walls, with the exceedingly pure physical eye; knowing with the wisdom of penetration which has concentration as its proximate cause and accomplishes one's own welfare; seeing with the wisdom of teaching which has compassion as its proximate cause and accomplishes the welfare of others; the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One—'Which first Pārājika was laid down? Where was it laid down? Concerning whom was it laid down? In reference to what matter was it laid down? Is there an original enactment therein… by whom was it handed down?' This is the question.

2. Pucchāvissajjane [M 0.0138] pana “yaṃ tena bhagavatā jānatā passatā arahatā sammāsambuddhena paṭhamaṃ pārājika”nti idaṃ kevalaṃ pucchāya āgatassa ādipadassa paccuddharaṇamattameva, “kattha paññattanti vesāliyā paññattaṃ; kaṃ ārabbhāti sudinnaṃ kalandaputtaṃ ārabbhā”ti evamādinā pana nayena punapi ettha ekekaṃ padaṃ pucchitvāva vissajjitaṃ. Ekā paññattīti “yo pana bhikkhu methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭiseveyya pārājiko hoti asaṃvāso”ti ayaṃ ekā paññatti. Dve anupaññattiyoti “antamaso tiracchānagatāyapī”ti ca, “sikkhaṃ apaccakkhāyā”ti ca makkaṭivajjiputtakavatthūnaṃ vasena vuttā – imā dve anupaññattiyo. Ettāvatā “atthi tattha paññatti anupaññatti anuppannapaññattī”ti imissā pucchāya dve koṭṭhāsā vissajjitā honti. Tatiyaṃ vissajjetuṃ pana “anuppannapaññatti tasmiṃ natthī”ti vuttaṃ. Ayañhi anuppannapaññatti nāma anuppanne dose paññattā; sā aṭṭhagarudhammavasena bhikkhunīnaṃyeva āgatā, aññatra natthi. Tasmā vuttaṃ “anuppannapaññatti tasmiṃ natthī”ti. Sabbatthapaññattīti majjhimadese ceva paccantimajanapadesu ca sabbatthapaññatti. Vinayadharapañcamena gaṇena “upasampadā, guṇaṅguṇūpāhanā, dhuvanahānaṃ, cammattharaṇa”nti imāni hi cattāri sikkhāpadāni majjhimadeseyeva paññatti. Ettheva etehi āpatti hoti, na paccantimajanapadesu. Sesāni sabbāneva sabbatthapaññatti nāma.

2. In the question and answer section, however, the phrase 'the first pārājika by that Blessed One, knowing, seeing, the Arahant, the Perfectly Enlightened One' is merely a restatement of the initial words that appeared in the question. By the method beginning, 'Where was it laid down? It was laid down at Vesālī. Concerning whom? Concerning Sudinna, the son of Kalanda,' each term here is questioned and then answered. 'One original enactment': 'Whatever bhikkhu should engage in the act of sexual intercourse is defeated and no longer in communion'—this is the one original enactment. 'Two supplementary enactments': 'Even with a female animal' and 'Without having renounced the training'—these two supplementary enactments were stated in connection with the cases of the monkey and the Vajjian-prince monk. By this much, two parts of the question, 'Is there an original enactment, a supplementary enactment, or an unarisen enactment therein?' have been answered. To answer the third part, however, it is said, 'There is no unarisen enactment in this case.' For this, the so-called unarisen enactment, is one laid down before an offense has arisen; it applies only to bhikkhunīs by way of the eight grave rules, and is not found elsewhere. Therefore, it is said, 'There is no unarisen enactment in this case.' 'A universally applicable enactment' is an enactment for all places, both in the Middle Country and in the border regions. For these four training rules—ordination by a chapter of five monks including a Vinaya expert, multi-layered footwear, constant bathing, and leather ground-coverings—are enacted only in the Middle Country. An offense in relation to these occurs only there, not in the border regions. All the remaining rules are called universally applicable enactments.

Sādhāraṇapaññattīti [V 0.0139] bhikkhūnañceva bhikkhunīnañca sādhāraṇapaññatti; suddhabhikkhūnameva hi suddhabhikkhunīnaṃ vā paññattaṃ sikkhāpadaṃ asādhāraṇapaññatti nāma hoti. Idaṃ pana bhikkhuṃ ārabbha uppanne vatthusmiṃ “yā pana bhikkhunī chandaso methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭiseveyya, antamaso tiracchānagatenapi pārājikā hoti asaṃvāsā”ti bhikkhunīnampi [P 7.1303] paññattaṃ, vinītakathāmattameva hi tāsaṃ natthi, sikkhāpadaṃ pana atthi, tena vuttaṃ “sādhāraṇapaññattī”ti. Ubhatopaññattiyampi eseva nayo. Byañjanamattameva hi ettha nānaṃ, bhikkhūnaṃ bhikkhunīnampi sādhāraṇattā sādhāraṇapaññatti, ubhinnampi paññattattā ubhatopaññattīti. Atthe pana bhedo natthi.

A 'common enactment' is an enactment common to both bhikkhus and bhikkhunīs. For a training rule enacted purely for bhikkhus alone or purely for bhikkhunīs alone is called an 'uncommon enactment.' This rule, however, having arisen from a matter concerning a bhikkhu, was also enacted for bhikkhunīs thus: 'Whatever bhikkhunī should willingly engage in the act of sexual intercourse, even with an animal, is defeated and no longer in communion.' Indeed, for them, only the account of the resolved case is absent, but the training rule itself exists. Therefore, it is called a 'common enactment.' For an 'enactment for both,' this same principle applies. Herein, the difference is merely in the wording: because it is common to both bhikkhus and bhikkhunīs, it is a 'common enactment'; because it is enacted for both, it is an 'enactment for both.' In the meaning, however, there is no difference.

Nidānogadhanti “yassa siyā āpatti so āvikareyyā”ti ettha sabbāpattīnaṃ anupaviṭṭhattā nidānogadhaṃ; nidāne anupaviṭṭhanti attho. Dutiyena uddesenāti nidānogadhaṃ nidānapariyāpannampi samānaṃ “tatrime cattāro [M 0.0139] pārājikā dhammā”tiādinā dutiyeneva uddesena uddesaṃ āgacchati. Catunnaṃ vipattīnanti sīlavipattiādīnaṃ. Paṭhamā hi dve āpattikkhandhā sīlavipatti nāma, avasesā pañca ācāravipatti nāma. Micchādiṭṭhi ca antaggāhikadiṭṭhi ca diṭṭhivipatti nāma, ājīvahetu paññattāni cha sikkhāpadāni ājīvavipatti nāma. Iti imāsaṃ catunnaṃ vipattīnaṃ idaṃ pārājikaṃ sīlavipatti nāma hoti.

[The term] 'Included in the Preamble' [is used] because all offenses are included in the passage, 'Should there be an offense for anyone, one should declare it'; the meaning is that it is included in the Preamble. 'By the second recitation' means: although it is included in the Preamble, it comes up for recitation with the second recitation, which begins, 'These are the four pārājika dhammas...' 'Of the four downfalls' refers to the downfall in virtue and so on. For the first two aggregates of offenses are called the 'downfall in virtue'; the remaining five aggregates of offenses are called the 'downfall in conduct.' Wrong view and extremist views are called the 'downfall in view'; the six training rules laid down on account of livelihood are called the 'downfall in livelihood.' Thus, among these four downfalls, this pārājika offense is called the 'downfall in virtue.'

Ekena samuṭṭhānenāti dvaṅgikena ekena samuṭṭhānena. Ettha hi cittaṃ aṅgaṃ hoti, kāyena pana āpattiṃ āpajjati. Tena vuttaṃ “kāyato ca cittato ca samuṭṭhātī”ti. Dvīhi samathehi sammatīti “āpannosī”ti sammukhā pucchiyamāno “āma āpannomhī”ti paṭijānāti, tāvadeva bhaṇḍanakalahaviggahā vūpasantā honti, sakkā ca hoti taṃ puggalaṃ apanetvā uposatho vā pavāraṇā vā kātuṃ. Iti sammukhāvinayena ca paṭiññātakaraṇena cāti dvīhi samathehi sammati, na ca tappaccayā koci upaddavo hoti. Yaṃ pana upari paññattivagge “na katamena samathena sammatī”ti vuttaṃ, taṃ samathaṃ otāretvā anāpatti kātuṃ na sakkāti imamatthaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ.

From a single origination means: from one origination with two factors. Herein, the mind is a factor, but one commits an offense by means of the body. Therefore, it was said, 'It arises from the body and from the mind.' It is settled by two settlement procedures means: when asked face-to-face, 'Have you committed an offense?' and one acknowledges, 'Yes, I have committed an offense,' at that very moment quarrels, disputes, and contentions are allayed, and it is possible to remove that person and perform the Uposatha or the Pavāraṇā. Thus, it is settled by two settlement procedures: by the in-the-presence procedure and by the procedure of acting on an admission. And on account of that, no trouble arises. As for what was said above in the Chapter on Enactments, 'By which settlement procedure is it not settled?', that was said with reference to this meaning: it is not possible to make it a non-offense by applying a settlement procedure.

Paññatti vinayoti “yo pana bhikkhū”tiādinā nayena vuttamātikā paññatti vinayoti attho. Vibhattīti padabhājanaṃ vuccati; vibhattīti hi vibhaṅgassevetaṃ nāmaṃ. Asaṃvaroti [V 0.0140] vītikkamo. Saṃvaroti avītikkamo. Yesaṃ vattatīti yesaṃ vinayapiṭakañca aṭṭhakathā ca sabbā paguṇāti attho. Te dhārentīti te etaṃ paṭhamapārājikaṃ [P 7.1304] pāḷito ca atthato ca dhārenti; na hi sakkā sabbaṃ vinayapiṭakaṃ ajānantena etassa attho jānitunti. Kenābhatanti idaṃ paṭhamapārājikaṃ pāḷivasena ca atthavasena ca yāva ajjatanakālaṃ kena ānītanti. Paramparābhatanti paramparāya ānītaṃ.

The enactment is the Vinaya means: the mātikā, which is the enactment spoken by the method beginning with 'Whatever bhikkhu...', is the Vinaya; this is the meaning. Analysis means: it is called the explication of terms; indeed, 'Analysis' is a name for the Vibhaṅga itself. Non-restraint means transgression. Restraint means non-transgression. For whom it is current means: for whom the entire Vinaya Piṭaka and the commentary are fluent; this is the meaning. They bear it in mind means: they bear this first pārājika in mind according to both the Pāli text and the meaning; for it is not possible for one who does not know the entire Vinaya Piṭaka to know its meaning. By whom was it brought? means: by whom was this first pārājika brought down to the present day, both in terms of the Pāli text and its meaning? Brought by tradition means: it was brought by succession.

3. Idāni yāya paramparāya ānītaṃ, taṃ dassetuṃ “upāli dāsako cevā”tiādinā nayena porāṇakehi mahātherehi gāthāyo ṭhapitā[M 0.0140]. Tattha yaṃ vattabbaṃ, taṃ nidānavaṇṇanāyameva vuttaṃ. Iminā nayena dutiyapārājikādipucchāvissajjanesupi vinicchayo veditabboti.

3. Now, to show the lineage by which it was brought, verses were established by the ancient great elders in the manner beginning, 'Upāli, Dāsaka, and so on.' Therein, whatever should be said has been said in the commentary on the introduction. By this method, the decision should be understood also in the questions and answers concerning the second pārājika and so on.

Mahāvibhaṅge paññattivāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

In the Mahāvibhaṅga, the commentary on the Chapter on Enactments is concluded.

Katāpattivārādivaṇṇanā

The Commentary on the Chapter on Offenses Incurred and so on.

157. Ito paraṃ “methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevanto kati āpattiyo āpajjatī”ti ādippabhedo katāpattivāro, “methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevantassa āpattiyo catunnaṃ vipattīnaṃ kati vipattiyo bhajantī”ti ādippabhedo vipattivāro, “methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevantassa āpattiyo sattannaṃ āpattikkhandhānaṃ katihi āpattikkhandhehi saṅgahitā”ti ādippabhedo saṅgahavāro, “methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevantassa āpattiyo channaṃ āpattisamuṭṭhānānaṃ katihi samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhahantī”ti ādippabhedo samuṭṭhānavāro, “methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevantassa āpattiyo catunnaṃ adhikaraṇānaṃ katamaṃ adhikaraṇa”nti ādippabhedo adhikaraṇavāro, “methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevantassa āpattiyo sattannaṃ samathānaṃ katihi samathehi sammantī”ti ādippabhedo samathavāro, tadanantaro samuccayavāro cāti ime satta vārā uttānatthā eva.

157. Hereafter, the Chapter on Offenses Incurred, which is distinguished by 'When engaging in sexual intercourse, how many offenses does one incur?', and so on; the Chapter on Failures, which is distinguished by 'The offenses of one engaging in sexual intercourse, to how many of the four failures do they belong?', and so on; the Chapter on Inclusion, which is distinguished by 'The offenses of one engaging in sexual intercourse, by how many of the seven classes of offenses are they included?', and so on; the Chapter on Origination, which is distinguished by 'The offenses of one engaging in sexual intercourse, from how many of the six origins of offenses do they arise?', and so on; the Chapter on Legal Cases, which is distinguished by 'The offenses of one engaging in sexual intercourse, which of the four legal cases is it?', and so on; the Chapter on Settlement, which is distinguished by 'The offenses of one engaging in sexual intercourse, by how many of the seven settlement procedures are they settled?', and so on; and the Chapter on Summaries immediately following it—these seven chapters are of self-evident meaning.

188. Tato paraṃ “methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevanapaccayā pārājikaṃ kattha paññatta”ntiādinā nayena puna paccayavasena eko paññattivāro, tassa vasena purimasadisā eva katāpattivārādayo satta vārāti evaṃ aparepi aṭṭha vārā vuttā, tepi uttānatthā eva. Iti ime aṭṭha, purimā aṭṭhāti mahāvibhaṅge soḷasa vārā dassitā. Tato paraṃ teneva nayena bhikkhunivibhaṅgepi [V 0.0141] soḷasa vārā āgatāti evamime ubhatovibhaṅge dvattiṃsa vārā pāḷinayeneva [P 7.1305] veditabbā. Na hettha kiñci pubbe avinicchitaṃ nāma atthi.

188. After that, again, by way of conditionality, there is one Chapter on Enactments, distinguished by 'On account of engaging in sexual intercourse, where was the pārājika enacted?', and so on. By means of that, there are seven chapters beginning with the Chapter on Offenses Incurred, just like the previous ones. In this way, another eight chapters were stated, and they too are of self-evident meaning. Thus, these eight and the former eight—sixteen chapters are shown in the Mahāvibhaṅga. After that, by the same method, sixteen chapters also come in the Bhikkhunīvibhaṅga. Thus, these thirty-two chapters in both Vibhaṅgas are to be understood according to the method of the Pāḷi text itself. For there is nothing here whatsoever that has not been previously decided.

Mahāvibhaṅge ca bhikkhunivibhaṅge ca

In the Mahāvibhaṅga and in the Bhikkhunīvibhaṅga

Soḷasamahāvāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Sixteen Great Chapters is concluded.

Samuṭṭhānasīsavaṇṇanā

The Exposition of the Headings on Origins

257. Tadanantarāya [M 0.0141][V 0.0142] pana samuṭṭhānakathāya anattā iti nicchayāti anattā iti nicchitā. Sabhāgadhammānanti aniccākārādīhi sabhāgānaṃ saṅkhatadhammānaṃ. Nāmamattaṃ na nāyatīti nāmamattampi na paññāyati. Dukkhahāninti dukkhaghātanaṃ. Khandhakā yā ca mātikāti khandhakā yā ca mātikāti attho. Ayameva vā pāṭho. Samuṭṭhānaṃ niyato katanti samuṭṭhānaṃ niyatokataṃ niyatakataṃ; niyatasamuṭṭhānanti attho. Etena bhūtārocanacorivuṭṭhāpanaananuññātasikkhāpadattayassa saṅgaho paccetabbo. Etāneva hi tīṇi sikkhāpadāni niyatasamuṭṭhānāni, aññehi saddhiṃ asambhinnasamuṭṭhānāni.

257. Furthermore, in the discourse on origins that immediately follows, anattā iti nicchayāti means: they are decided as 'non-self'. Sabhāgadhammānanti means: 'of conditioned phenomena that have a similar nature' due to the characteristic of impermanence and so on. Nāmamattaṃ na nāyatīti means: 'not even a mere name is apparent'. Dukkhahāninti means: 'the destruction of suffering'. Khandhakā yā ca mātikāti means: 'the Khandhakas and the Mātikā'; this is the meaning. Alternatively, this is the reading itself. Samuṭṭhānaṃ niyato katanti means: a fixed origin. By this, the inclusion of the three training rules—informing on an actual offense, reinstating a thieving nun, and the rule concerning an unauthorized nun—should be understood. For indeed, these three training rules have fixed origins, having origins not mixed with others.

Sambhedaṃ nidānañcaññanti aññampi sambhedañca nidānañca. Tattha sambhedavacanena samuṭṭhānasambhedassa gahaṇaṃ paccetabbaṃ, tāni hi tīṇi sikkhāpadāni ṭhapetvā sesāni sambhinnasamuṭṭhānāni. Nidānavacanena sikkhāpadānaṃ paññattidesasaṅkhātaṃ nidānaṃ paccetabbaṃ. Sutte dissanti uparīti sikkhāpadānaṃ samuṭṭhānaniyamo sambhedo nidānanti imāni tīṇi suttamhi eva dissanti; paññāyantīti attho. Tattha “ekena samuṭṭhānena samuṭṭhāti, kāyato ca cittato cā”tiādimhi tāva purimanaye samuṭṭhānaniyamo ca sambhedo ca dissanti. Itaraṃ pana nidānaṃ nāma –

Sambhedaṃ nidānañcaññanti means: the mixed origin and the cause, which are other. Therein, by the word 'sambheda', the taking of 'mixed origin' should be understood; for indeed, setting aside those three training rules, the remaining ones have mixed origins. By the word 'nidāna', the nidāna of the training rules, known as the 'place of promulgation', should be understood. Sutte dissanti uparīti means: these three—the fixed origin, the mixed origin, and the cause of the training rules—are seen in the Sutta itself; they are apparent, this is the meaning. Therein, first of all, in the previous method, in the passage beginning 'it arises from one origin, from body and from mind', both the fixed origin and the mixed origin are seen. However, the other, called the nidāna, is as follows:

“Vesāliyā rājagahe, sāvatthiyā ca āḷavī;

Kosambiyā ca sakkesu, bhaggesu ceva paññattā”ti.

“At Vesālī, Rājagaha, Sāvatthī, and Āḷavī; at Kosambī, among the Sakyans, and also among the Bhaggas, they were promulgated.”

Evaṃ upari dissati, parato āgate sutte dissatīti veditabbaṃ.

Thus it is seen above; it should be understood that it is seen in the Sutta that comes hereafter, the Parivāra Pāḷi.

“Vibhaṅge [V 0.0143] dvīsū”ti gāthāya ayamattho – yaṃ sikkhāpadaṃ dvīsu vibhaṅgesu paññattaṃ uposathadivase bhikkhū ca bhikkhuniyo ca uddisanti, tassa yathāñāyaṃ samuṭṭhānaṃ pavakkhāmi, taṃ me suṇāthāti.

This is the meaning of the verse beginning ‘Vibhaṅge dvīsu’: I will explain, according to the method, the origin of that training rule which is promulgated in the two Vibhaṅgas and which both monks and nuns recite on the Uposatha day. Listen to it from me.

Sañcarittānubhāsanañcāti sañcarittañca samanubhāsanañca. Atirekañca cīvaranti atirekacīvaraṃ[P 7.1306]; kathinanti attho. Lomāni padasodhammoti eḷakalomāni [M 0.0142] ca padasodhammo ca. Bhūtaṃ saṃvidhānena cāti bhūtārocanañca saṃvidahitvā addhānappaṭipajjanañca. Theyyadesanā coriṃ cāti theyyasattho ca chattapāṇissa agilānassa dhammadesanā ca corivuṭṭhāpanañca. Ananuññātāya terasāti mātāpitusāmikehi ananuññātāya saddhiṃ imāni terasa samuṭṭhānāni honti. Sadisā idha dissareti idha ubhatovibhaṅge etesu terasasu samuṭṭhānasīsesu ekekasmiṃ aññānipi sadisāni samuṭṭhānāni dissanti.

As for ‘Sañcarittānubhāsanañca,’ it means the origin of acting as a go-between and the origin of repeated admonition. As for ‘Atirekañca cīvaraṃ,’ it means the origin of an extra robe; the meaning is the Kathina origin. As for ‘Lomāni padasodhammo,’ it means the origin of sheep's wool and the origin of teaching the Dhamma verse by verse. As for ‘Bhūtaṃ saṃvidhānena ca,’ it means the origin of informing on an actual offense and the origin of undertaking a long journey after arranging it. As for ‘Theyyadesanā coriṃ ca,’ it means the origin of journeying with a caravan of thieves, the origin of teaching Dhamma to one who is not sick and holds an umbrella, and the origin of reinstating a thieving nun. As for ‘Ananuññātāya terasa,’ with the ‘ananuññāta’ origin—that is, concerning a nun not authorized by her mother, father, or husband—these are the thirteen origins. As for ‘Sadisā idha dissare,’ here in the Ubhatovibhaṅga, in each one of these thirteen headings of origins, other similar origins are also seen.

Paṭhamapārājikasamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

The Explanation of the Origin of the First Pārājika

258. Idāni tāni dassetuṃ “methunaṃ sukkasaṃsaggo”tiādi vuttaṃ. Tattha methunanti idaṃ tāva paṭhamapārājikaṃ nāma ekaṃ samuṭṭhānasīsaṃ, sesāni tena sadisāni. Tattha sukkasaṃsaggoti sukkavissaṭṭhi ceva kāyasaṃsaggo ca. Aniyatā paṭhamikāti paṭhamaṃ aniyatasikkhāpadaṃ. Pubbūpaparipācitāti “jānaṃ pubbūpagataṃ bhikkhu”nti sikkhāpadañca bhikkhuniparipācitapiṇḍapātasikkhāpadañca. Raho bhikkhuniyā sahāti bhikkhuniyā saddhiṃ raho nisajjasikkhāpadañca.

258. Now, to show these, the words beginning ‘methunaṃ sukkasaṃsaggo’ were stated. Therein, ‘methunaṃ’ is, first of all, the one head-of-origin, namely the first pārājika; the remaining rules are similar to it. Therein, by ‘sukkasaṃsaggo’ is meant both the training rule on emission of semen and the one on bodily contact. By ‘Aniyatā paṭhamikā’ is meant the first undetermined training rule. By ‘Pubbūpaparipācitā’ is meant the training rule ‘jānaṃ pubbūpagataṃ bhikkhuṃ’ and the training rule on almsfood prepared by a bhikkhunī. By ‘Raho bhikkhuniyā sahā’ is meant the training rule on sitting in seclusion with a bhikkhunī.

Sabhojane raho dve cāti sabhojane kule anupakhajjanisajjasikkhāpadañca dve rahonisajjasikkhāpadāni ca. Aṅguli udake hasanti aṅgulipatodakañca udake hasadhammasikkhāpadañca. Pahāre uggire cevāti pahāradānasikkhāpadañca talasattikauggiraṇasikkhāpadañca. Tepaññāsā ca sekhiyāti parimaṇḍalanivāsanādīni khuddakavaṇṇanāvasāne vuttāni tepaññāsa sekhiyasikkhāpadāni ca.

By ‘Sabhojane raho dve ca’ is meant the training rule on not sitting down encroaching on a family at a meal, and the two training rules on sitting in seclusion. By ‘Aṅguli udake hasanti’ is meant the training rule on poking with a finger and the training rule on the practice of playing in the water. By ‘Pahāre uggire ceva’ is meant the training rule on giving a blow and the training rule on raising a hand-weapon. By ‘Tepaññāsā ca sekhiyā’ is meant the fifty-three Sekhiya training rules stated at the end of the commentary on the minor rules, beginning with ‘wearing the lower robe all around’.

Adhakkhagāmāvassutāti bhikkhunīnaṃ adhakkhakasikkhāpadañca gāmantaragamanaṃ avassutā avassutassa [V 0.0144] hatthato khādanīyabhojanīyaggahaṇasikkhāpadañca. Talamaṭṭhañca suddhikāti talaghātakaṃ jatumaṭṭhaṃ udakasuddhikādiyanañca. Vassaṃvuṭṭhā ca ovādanti vassaṃvuṭṭhā chappañcayojanāni sikkhāpadañca ovādāya agamanasikkhāpadañca. Nānubandhe pavattininti yā pana bhikkhunī vuṭṭhāpitaṃ pavattiniṃ dve vassāni nānubandheyyāti vuttasikkhāpadaṃ.

By ‘Adhakkhagāmāvassutā’ is meant the bhikkhunīs' training rule concerning the lower part of the body, the one on going to another village, and the training rule on a lustful bhikkhunī accepting hard or soft food from the hand of a lustful man. By ‘Talamaṭṭhañca suddhikā’ is meant the rule on striking with the palm, the rule on lacquered items, and the rule on accepting a water-strainer. By ‘Vassaṃvuṭṭhā ca ovādanti’ is meant the training rule on one who has completed the rains-residence going five or six yojanas, and the training rule on not going for an exhortation. By ‘Nānubandhe pavattiniṃ’ is meant the training rule stated as: ‘Whatever bhikkhunī should not attend for two years upon a probationer whom she has raised...’

Ime [M 0.0143] sikkhāti imā sikkhāyo; liṅgavipariyāyo kato. Kāyamānasikā katāti kāyacittasamuṭṭhānā katā.

By ‘ime sikkhā’ is meant ‘imā sikkhāyo’; a change of gender has been made. By ‘Kāyamānasikā katā’ is meant ‘made to have body and mind as their origin’ (kāyacittasamuṭṭhānā katā).

Dutiyapārājikasamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

The Explanation of the Origin of the Second Pārājika and Others

259. Adinnanti [P 7.1307] idaṃ tāva adinnādānanti vā dutiyapārājikanti vā ekaṃ samuṭṭhānasīsaṃ, sesāni tena sadisāni. Tattha viggahuttarīti manussaviggahauttarimanussadhammasikkhāpadāni. Duṭṭhullā attakāminanti duṭṭhullavācāattakāmapāricariyasikkhāpadāni. Amūlā aññabhāgiyāti dve duṭṭhadosasikkhāpadāni. Aniyatā dutiyikāti dutiyaṃ aniyatasikkhāpadaṃ.

259. First of all, this ‘Adinnaṃ’ is the one head-of-origin, which is ‘adinnādāna’ (taking what is not given) or the second pārājika; the remaining rules are similar to it. Therein, by ‘viggahuttarī’ is meant the training rules on human corpses and superhuman states. By ‘Duṭṭhullā attakāmin’ is meant the training rules on coarse speech and on service for one's own desire. By ‘Amūlā aññabhāgiyā’ is meant the two training rules on malicious accusation. By ‘Aniyatā dutiyikā’ is meant the second undetermined training rule.

Acchinde pariṇāmaneti sāmaṃ cīvaraṃ datvā acchindanañca saṅghikalābhassa attano pariṇāmanañca. Musā omasapesuṇāti musāvādo ca omasavādo ca bhikkhupesuññañca. Duṭṭhullā pathavīkhaṇeti duṭṭhullāpattiārocanañca pathavīkhaṇañca. Bhūtaṃ aññāya ujjhāpeti bhūtagāmaaññavādakaujjhāpanakasikkhāpadāni.

By ‘Acchinde pariṇāmane’ is meant the rule on snatching back a robe after having given it oneself, and the rule on diverting a gain belonging to the Saṅgha to oneself. By ‘Musā omasapesuṇā’ is meant the rules on false speech, contemptuous speech, and slander of a bhikkhu. By ‘Duṭṭhullā pathavīkhaṇe’ is meant the rule on informing on a coarse offense and the rule on digging the earth. By ‘Bhūtaṃ aññāya ujjhāpeti’ is meant the training rules on destroying plant life, on evasive speech, and on causing annoyance.

Nikkaḍḍhanaṃ siñcanañcāti vihārato nikkaḍḍhanañca udakena tiṇādisiñcanañca. Āmisahetu bhuttāvīti “āmisahetu bhikkhuniyo ovadantī”ti sikkhāpadañca, bhuttāviṃ anatirittena khādanīyādinā pavāraṇāsikkhāpadañca. Ehi anādari bhiṃsāti “ehāvuso gāmaṃ vā”ti sikkhāpadañca, anādariyañca bhikkhubhiṃsāpanakañca. Apanidhe ca jīvitanti pattādīnaṃ apanidhānasikkhāpadañca, sañcicca pāṇaṃ jīvitāvoropanañca.

By ‘Nikkaḍḍhanaṃ siñcanañca’ is meant the rule on dragging a bhikkhu out of a monastery and the rule on sprinkling grass, etc., with water. By ‘Āmisahetu bhuttāvī’ is meant the training rule ‘admonishing bhikkhunīs for the sake of material gain,’ and the training rule on inviting one who has finished eating with hard food, etc., that is not a leftover. By ‘Ehi anādari bhiṃsā’ is meant the training rule ‘Come, friend, to the village...,’ the rule on disrespect, and the rule on frightening a bhikkhu. By ‘Apanidhe ca jīvitan’ is meant the training rule on hiding a bowl, etc., and the rule on intentionally depriving a living being of life.

Jānaṃ sappāṇakaṃ kammanti jānaṃ sappāṇakaudakasikkhāpadañca punakammāya ukkoṭanañca. Ūnasaṃvāsanāsanāti [V 0.0145] ūnavīsativassasikkhāpadañca ukkhittakena saddhiṃ saṃvāsasikkhāpadañca nāsitakasāmaṇerasambhogasikkhāpadañca. Sahadhammikaṃ vilekhāti sahadhammikaṃ vuccamānasikkhāpadañca, vilekhāya saṃvattantīti āgatasikkhāpadañca. Moho amūlakena cāti mohanake pācittiyasikkhāpadañca, amūlakena saṅghādisesena anuddhaṃsanasikkhāpadañca.

By ‘Jānaṃ sappāṇakaṃ kammaṃ’ is meant the training rule on knowingly using water with living beings, and the rule on reopening a matter for a new formal act. By ‘Ūnasaṃvāsanāsanā’ is meant the training rule on ordaining one under twenty years of age, the training rule on communion with one who is suspended, and the training rule on consorting with an expelled novice. By ‘Sahadhammikaṃ vilekhā’ is meant the training rule on being spoken to in accordance with the Dhamma, and the training rule that is given as ‘it leads to scraping.’ By ‘Moho amūlakena ca’ is meant the pācittiya training rule on causing confusion, and the training rule on harassing a bhikkhu with a groundless saṅghādisesa offense.

Kukkuccaṃ dhammikaṃ cīvaraṃ datvāti kukkuccaupadahanañca, dhammikānaṃ kammānaṃ chandaṃ datvā khīyanañca, cīvaraṃ datvā khīyanañca. Pariṇāmeyya puggaleti saṅghikaṃ lābhaṃ [M 0.0144] puggalassa pariṇāmanasikkhāpadaṃ. Kiṃ te akālaṃ acchindeti “kiṃ te ayye eso purisapuggalo karissatī”ti āgatasikkhāpadañca, “akālacīvaraṃ kālacīvara”nti adhiṭṭhahitvā bhājanasikkhāpadañca, bhikkhuniyā saddhiṃ cīvaraṃ parivattetvā acchindanasikkhāpadañca. Duggahī nirayena [P 7.1308] cāti duggahitena duppadhāritena paraṃ ujjhāpanasikkhāpadañca, nirayena vā brahmacariyena vā abhisapanasikkhāpadañca.

By 'Kukkuccaṃ dhammikaṃ cīvaraṃ datvā' is meant the rule on stirring up remorse, the rule on complaining after having given consent for lawful formal acts, and the rule on complaining after having given a robe. By 'Pariṇāmeyya puggale' is meant the training rule on diverting a gain belonging to the Saṅgha to an individual. By 'Kiṃ te akālaṃ acchinde' is meant the training rule that is given as 'Venerable lady, what will this man do for you?', the training rule on dividing an out-of-season robe after having determined it to be an in-season robe, and the training rule on snatching back a robe after having exchanged it with a bhikkhunī. By 'Duggahī nirayena ca' is meant the training rule on annoying another with something poorly grasped and poorly considered, and the training rule on cursing with hell or with the holy life.

Gaṇaṃ vibhaṅga dubbalanti “gaṇassa cīvaralābhaṃ antarāyaṃ kareyyā”ti ca “dhammikaṃ cīvaravibhaṅgaṃ paṭibāheyyā”ti ca “dubbalacīvarapaccāsāya cīvarakālasamayaṃ atikkāmeyyā”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadāni. Kathinā phāsu passayanti “dhammikaṃ kathinuddhāraṃ paṭibāheyya, bhikkhuniyā sañcicca aphāsuṃ kareyya, bhikkhuniyā upassayaṃ datvā kupitā anattamanā nikkaḍḍheyya vā”ti vuttasikkhāpadāni. Akkosacaṇḍī maccharīti “bhikkhuṃ akkoseyya vā paribhāseyya vā, caṇḍikatā gaṇaṃ paribhāseyya, kule maccharinī assā”ti vuttasikkhāpadāni. Gabbhiniñca pāyantiyāti “gabbhiniṃ vuṭṭhāpeyya, pāyantiṃ vuṭṭhāpeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadāni.

“Group, division, weak” refers to the training rules stated thus: “One might create an obstacle to the group's robe-gain,” “One might obstruct a rightful distribution of robes,” and “One might let the robe season pass out of concern for a poor-quality robe.” “Kathina, ease, seeing” refers to the training rules stated thus: “One might obstruct a rightful Kathina withdrawal,” “One might intentionally cause discomfort to a bhikkhunī,” and “Having given lodging to a bhikkhunī, one might angrily and displeasedly turn her out.” “Abusive, fierce, miserly” refers to the training rules stated thus: “One might abuse or revile a bhikkhu,” “One might revile the group out of fierceness,” and “One might be miserly towards families.” “Pregnant and nursing” refers to the training rules stated thus: “One might ordain a pregnant woman,” and “One might ordain a nursing woman.”

Dvevassaṃ sikkhā saṅghenāti “dve vassāni chasu dhammesu asikkhitasikkhaṃ sikkhamānaṃ vuṭṭhāpeyya, sikkhitasikkhaṃ sikkhamānaṃ saṅghena asammataṃ vuṭṭhāpeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadāni. Tayo ceva gihīgatāti ūnadvādasavassaṃ gihigataṃ, paripuṇṇadvādasavassaṃ gihigataṃ “dve vassāni chasu dhammesu asikkhitasikkhaṃ dve vassāni sikkhitasikkhaṃ saṅghena asammata”nti vuttasikkhāpadāni. Kumāribhūtā tissoti “ūnavīsativassaṃ kumāribhūta”ntiādinā nayena vuttā [V 0.0146] tisso. Ūnadvādasasammatāti “ūnadvādasavassā vuṭṭhāpeyya, paripuṇṇadvādasavassā saṅghena asammatā vuṭṭhāpeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ.

“Two years, training, by the Saṅgha” refers to the training rules stated thus: “One might ordain a probationer who has not trained in the six rules for two years,” and “One might ordain a probationer who has trained but is not approved by the Saṅgha.” “And three who have gone to the lay life” refers to the training rules stated thus: “one who has gone to the lay life under twelve years,” “one who has gone to the lay life at a full twelve years and has not trained in the six rules for two years,” and “one who, having gone to the lay life, has trained for two years but is not approved by the Saṅgha.” “Three maidens” refers to the three training rules stated in the manner beginning with “a maiden under twenty years old.” “Approved under twelve years” refers to the pair of training rules stated thus: “One might ordain one under twelve years old,” and “One might ordain one who is a full twelve years old but not approved by the Saṅgha.”

Alaṃ tāva sokāvāsanti “alaṃ tāva te ayye vuṭṭhāpitenā”ti ca, “caṇḍiṃ sokāvāsaṃ sikkhamānaṃ vuṭṭhāpeyyā”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ. Chandā anuvassā ca dveti “pārivāsikachandadānena sikkhamānaṃ vuṭṭhāpeyya, anuvassaṃ vuṭṭhāpeyya, ekaṃ vassaṃ dve vuṭṭhāpeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Samuṭṭhānā tikā katāti tikasamuṭṭhānā katā.

“Enough, dwelling in sorrow” refers to the pair of training rules stated thus: “Enough, venerable, with being ordained by you,” and “One might ordain a fierce probationer dwelling in sorrow.” “Consent, annually, and two” refers to the trio of training rules stated thus: “One might ordain a probationer by giving consent for probation,” “One might ordain annually,” and “One might ordain two in one year.” “Origins, threefold, made” means they are made to have threefold origins.

Sañcarittasamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

The Explanation of the Origins of Acting as a Go-between

260. Sañcarī [M 0.0145] kuṭi vihāroti sañcarittaṃ saññācikāya kuṭikaraṇaṃ mahallakavihārakaraṇañca. Dhovanañca paṭiggahoti aññātikāya [P 7.1309] bhikkhuniyā purāṇacīvaradhovāpanañca cīvarapaṭiggahaṇañca. Viññattuttari abhihaṭṭhunti aññātakaṃ gahapatiṃ cīvaraviññāpanaṃ tatuttarisādiyanasikkhāpadañca. Ubhinnaṃ dūtakena cāti “cīvaracetāpannaṃ upakkhaṭaṃ hotī”ti āgatasikkhāpadadvayañca dūtena cīvaracetāpannapahitasikkhāpadañca.

260. “Sañcarī kuṭi vihāro” refers to acting as a go-between, the making of a hut by requesting, and the making of a large residence. “Dhovanañca paṭiggaho” refers to having an old robe washed by an unrelated bhikkhunī and the receiving of a robe. “Viññattuttari abhihaṭṭhu” refers to requesting a robe from an unrelated householder and the training rule on accepting more than that. “Ubhinnaṃ dūtakena ca” refers to the two training rules that are stated as “When a robe-fund has been prepared,” and the training rule on a robe-fund sent through a messenger.

Kosiyā suddhadvebhāgā, chabbassāni nisīdananti “kosiyamissakaṃ santhata”ntiādīni pañca sikkhāpadāni. Riñcanti rūpikā cevāti vibhaṅge “riñcanti uddesa”nti āgataṃ eḷakalomadhovāpanaṃ rūpiyappaṭiggahaṇasikkhāpadañca. Ubho nānappakārakāti rūpiyasaṃvohārakayavikkayasikkhāpadadvayaṃ.

“Kosiyā suddhadvebhāgā, chabbassāni nisīdananti” refers to the five training rules beginning with “a rug mixed with silk.” “Riñcanti rūpikā cevāti” refers to the training rule on having sheep's wool washed, which is found in the Vibhaṅga as “they abandon the recitation,” and the training rule on accepting money. “Ubho nānappakārakāti” refers to the pair of training rules on transacting with money and on buying and selling.

Ūnabandhanavassikāti ūnapañcabandhanapattasikkhāpadañca vassikasāṭikasikkhāpadañca. Suttaṃ vikappanena cāti suttaṃ viññāpetvā cīvaravāyāpanañca tantavāye upasaṅkamitvā cīvare vikappāpajjanañca. Dvāradānasibbāni cāti yāva dvārakosā aggaḷaṭṭhapanāya, “aññātikāya bhikkhuniyā cīvaraṃ dadeyya, cīvaraṃ sibbeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Pūvapaccayajoti cāti pūvehi vā manthehi vā abhihaṭṭhuṃ pavāraṇāsikkhāpadaṃ cātumāsapaccayappavāraṇājotisamādahanasikkhāpadāni ca.

“Ūnabandhanavassikāti” refers to the training rule on a bowl deficient in five bindings and the training rule on the rainy-season cloth. “Suttaṃ vikappanena cāti” refers to requesting thread and having a robe woven, and approaching a weaver and making an arrangement concerning a robe. “Dvāradānasibbāni cāti” refers to the trio of training rules stated thus: “up to the door-post for placing a bolt,” “one might give a robe to an unrelated bhikkhunī,” and “one might sew a robe.” “Pūvapaccayajoti cāti” refers to the training rule concerning an invitation to take one's fill of cakes or meal-cakes, and the training rules on the four-month requisite-invitation and on lighting a fire.

Ratanaṃ [V 0.0147] sūci mañco ca, tūlaṃ nisīdanakaṇḍu ca, vassikā ca sugatenāti ratanasikkhāpadañceva sūcigharasikkhāpadādīni ca satta sikkhāpadāni. Viññatti aññaṃ cetāpanā, dve saṅghikā mahājanikā, dve puggalalahukā garūti “yā pana bhikkhunī aññaṃ viññāpetvā aññaṃ viññāpeyyā”tiādīni nava sikkhāpadāni. Dve vighāsā sāṭikā cāti “uccāraṃ vā passāvaṃ vā saṅkāraṃ vā vighāsaṃ vā tirokuṭṭe vā tiropākāre vā chaḍḍeyya vā chaḍḍāpeyya vā, harite chaḍḍeyya vā chaḍḍāpeyya vā”ti evaṃ vuttāni dve vighāsasikkhāpadāni ca udakasāṭikāsikkhāpadañca. Samaṇacīvarena cāti “samaṇacīvaraṃ dadeyyā”ti idametaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ.

“Ratanaṃ sūci mañco ca, tūlaṃ nisīdanakaṇḍu ca, vassikā ca sugatenāti” refers to the training rule on jewels and the seven training rules beginning with the rule on the needle case. “Viññatti aññaṃ cetāpanā, dve saṅghikā mahājanikā, dve puggalalahukā garūti” refers to the nine training rules beginning with “Whatever bhikkhunī, having requested one thing, should request another.” “Dve vighāsā sāṭikā cāti” refers to the two training rules on leftovers, thus stated: “one might throw or have thrown excrement, urine, rubbish, or leftovers over a wall or over a fence,” and “one might throw or have thrown them on green plants”; and the training rule on the bathing cloth. “Samaṇacīvarena cāti” refers to this which is said with reference to “one might give an ascetic’s robe.”

Samanubhāsanāsamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

The Explanation of the Origins of Admonition

261. Bhedānuvattadubbacadūsaduṭṭhulladiṭṭhi [M 0.0146] cāti saṅghabhedānuvattakadubbacakuladūsakaduṭṭhullappaṭicchādanadiṭṭhiappaṭinissajjanasikkhāpadāni. Chandaṃ ujjagghikā dve cāti [P 7.1310] chandaṃ adatvā gamanasikkhāpadaṃ ujjagghikāya antaraghare gamananisīdanasikkhāpadadvayañca. Dve ca saddāti “appasaddo antaraghare gamissāmi, nisīdissāmī”ti sikkhāpadadvayañca. Na byāhareti “na sakabaḷena mukhena byāharissāmī”ti sikkhāpadaṃ.

261. The training rules on following a schism, being difficult to correct, corrupting families, concealing a serious offense, and not relinquishing wrong views. The training rule on going without giving consent, and the two training rules on going and sitting inside a house with giggling. And the two training rules on sound: “I will go into the house quietly, I will sit quietly.” The training rule on not speaking: “I will not speak with my mouth full of a morsel.”

Chamā nīcāsane ṭhānaṃ, pacchato uppathena cāti chamāyaṃ nisīditvā, nīce āsane nisīditvā; ṭhitena nisinnassa, pacchato gacchantena purato gacchantassa, uppathena gacchantena pathena gacchantassa dhammadesanāsikkhāpadāni. Vajjānuvattigahaṇāti vajjappaṭicchādana, ukkhittānuvattaka, hatthaggahaṇādisaṅkhātāni tīṇi pārājikāni. Osāre paccācikkhanāti “anapaloketvā kārakasaṅghaṃ anaññāya gaṇassa chandaṃ osāreyyā”ti ca “buddhaṃ paccakkhāmī”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ.

The training rules for teaching the Dhamma: for one sitting on the ground, for one sitting on a low seat; for one standing teaching one who is sitting, for one going behind teaching one who is going in front, for one going off the path teaching one who is going on the path. The three Pārājika rules concerning concealing an offense, following one who has been suspended, and seizing by the hand. Deposing and rejecting refers to the two stated training rules: “Without informing the officiating Sangha and without knowing the group’s consent, one might depose,” and “I reject the Buddha.”

Kismiṃ saṃsaṭṭhā dve vadhīti “kismiñcideva adhikaraṇe paccākatā”ti ca “bhikkhuniyo paneva saṃsaṭṭhā viharantī”ti ca “yā pana bhikkhunī evaṃ vadeyya saṃsaṭṭhāva ayye tumhe viharathā”ti ca “attānaṃ vadhitvā vadhitvā rodeyyā”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadāni. Visibbe dukkhitāya cāti “bhikkhuniyā cīvaraṃ visibbetvā vā visibbāpetvā vā”ti ca “dukkhitaṃ [V 0.0148] sahajīvini”nti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ. Puna saṃsaṭṭhā na vūpasameti “saṃsaṭṭhā vihareyya gahapatinā vā gahapatiputtena vā”ti evaṃ puna vuttasaṃsaṭṭhasikkhāpadañca “ehayye, imaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ vūpasamehī”ti vuccamānā, “sādhū”ti paṭissuṇitvā “sā pacchā anantarāyikinī neva vūpasammeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadañca. Ārāmañca pavāraṇāti “jānaṃ sabhikkhukaṃ ārāmaṃ anāpucchā paviseyyā”ti ca “ubhatosaṅghe tīhi ṭhānehi na pavāreyyā”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ.

Regarding association and striking: the stated training rules are “being unyielding in some dispute,” “nuns, however, live in association,” “whatever nun should say thus: ‘Sisters, you live in association,’” and “having struck herself again and again, she might weep.” Regarding unpicking and one who is afflicted: the two stated training rules are “having unstitched or caused to be unstitched a nun’s robe,” and “an afflicted co-resident.” Regarding renewed association and not settling: the stated training rule on association, again, is “she might live in association with a householder or a householder’s son;” and the stated training rule is “when being told, ‘Come, sister, settle this dispute,’ having agreed by saying, ‘Very well,’ she, being later without obstacle, does not settle it.” Regarding the monastery and the invitation: the two stated training rules are “knowingly, she might enter a residence with monks without asking,” and “she might not make the invitation in both Sanghas on three grounds.”

Anvaddhaṃ sahajīviniṃ dveti “anvaddhamāsaṃ bhikkhuniyā bhikkhusaṅghato dve dhammā paccāsīsitabbā”ti vuttasikkhāpadañca, “sahajīviniṃ vuṭṭhāpetvā dve vassāni neva anuggaṇheyya, sahajīviniṃ vuṭṭhāpetvā neva vūpakāseyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadadvayañca. Cīvaraṃ anubandhanāti “sace me tvaṃ, ayye, cīvaraṃ dassasi, evāhaṃ [M 0.0147] taṃ vuṭṭhāpessāmī”ti ca “sace maṃ tvaṃ, ayye, dve vassāni anubandhissasi, evāhaṃ taṃ vuṭṭhāpessāmī”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ.

Regarding the half-month and the two for a co-resident: the stated training rule is “every half-month, two things are to be expected by a nun from the Sangha of monks;” and the two stated training rules are “having ordained a co-resident, she might not support her for two years,” and “having ordained a co-resident, she might not take her away.” Regarding the robe and attending: the two stated training rules are “If you, venerable lady, will give me a robe, then I will ordain you,” and “If you, venerable lady, will attend on me for two years, then I will ordain you.”

Kathinasamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

The Explanation of the Arising of the Kathina.

262. Ubbhataṃ [P 7.1311] kathinaṃ tīṇīti “niṭṭhitacīvarasmiṃ bhikkhunā ubbhatasmiṃ kathine”ti vuttāni āditova tīṇi sikkhāpadāni. Paṭhamaṃ pattabhesajjanti “dasāhaparamaṃ atirekapatto”ti vuttaṃ paṭhamapattasikkhāpadañca “paṭisāyanīyāni bhesajjānī”ti vuttasikkhāpadañca. Accekañcāpi sāsaṅkanti accekacīvarasikkhāpadañca tadanantarameva sāsaṅkasikkhāpadañca. Pakkamantena vā duveti “taṃ pakkamanto neva uddhareyyā”ti bhūtagāmavagge vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ.

262. Regarding 'the three [rules] concerning the withdrawn Kathina': these are the three training rules stated from the very beginning as, 'when the Kathina has been withdrawn by a monk whose robe-work is finished.' Regarding 'the first [rule] concerning a bowl and medicines': this is the first training rule concerning a bowl, stated as, 'an extra bowl for at most ten days,' and the training rule stated as, 'medicines to be stored.' Regarding 'and also an urgent [robe] and a place of suspicion': this is the training rule concerning an urgent robe and, immediately after it, the training rule concerning a place of suspicion. Regarding 'or the two [rules] concerning one who is departing': this is the pair of training rules stated in the Bhūtagāmavagga as, 'departing, one should not withdraw it.'

Upassayaṃ paramparāti “bhikkhunupassayaṃ gantvā bhikkhuniyo ovadeyyā”ti ca “paramparabhojane pācittiya”nti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ. Anatirittaṃ nimantanāti “anatirittaṃ khādanīyaṃ vā bhojanīyaṃ vā”ti ca “nimantito sabhatto samāno”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ. Vikappaṃ rañño vikāleti “sāmaṃ cīvaraṃ vikappetvā”ti ca “rañño khattiyassā”ti ca “vikāle gāmaṃ paviseyyā”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Vosāsāraññakena cāti [V 0.0149] “vosāsamānarūpā ṭhitā”ti ca “tathārūpesu āraññakesu senāsanesu pubbe appaṭisaṃvidita”nti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ.

Regarding 'a nunnery and consecutive [meals]': this is the pair of training rules stated as, 'having gone to a nunnery, one should admonish the nuns,' and 'in consecutive eating, there is a pācittiya.' Regarding 'non-leftover [food] and an invitation': this is the pair of training rules stated as, 'non-leftover hard food or soft food,' and 'being invited and having a meal.' Regarding 'determination, a king, and the wrong time': this is the three training rules stated as, 'having determined a robe oneself,' 'of a khattiya king,' and 'one might enter a village at the wrong time.' And regarding 'dismissing and a forest [dwelling]': this is the pair of training rules stated as, 'standing in a manner of dismissing,' and 'in such forest lodgings, not having been informed beforehand.'

Ussayā sannicayañcāti “ussayavādikā”ti ca “pattasannicayaṃ kareyyā”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ. Pure pacchā vikāle cāti “yā pana bhikkhunī purebhattaṃ kulāni upasaṅkamitvā”ti ca, “pacchābhattaṃ kulāni upasaṅkamitvā”ti ca, “vikāle kulāni upasaṅkamitvā”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Pañcāhikā saṅkamanīti “pañcāhikā saṅghāṭicāraṃ atikkameyyā”ti ca “cīvarasaṅkamanīyaṃ dhāreyyā”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ. Dvepi āvasathena cāti “āvasathacīvaraṃ anissajjitvā paribhuñjeyya, āvasathaṃ anissajjitvā cārikaṃ pakkameyyā”ti ca evaṃ āvasathena saddhiṃ vuttasikkhāpadāni ca dve.

Regarding 'jealousy and accumulation': this is the pair of training rules stated as, 'one who speaks out of jealousy,' and 'one might make an accumulation of bowls.' And regarding 'before, after, and the wrong time': this is the three training rules stated as, 'whatever bhikkhunī, having approached families before the meal,' 'having approached families after the meal,' and 'having approached families at the wrong time.' Regarding 'the five-day [limit] and what is transferable': this is the pair of training rules stated as, 'one might exceed the five-day period for being separated from the outer robe,' and 'one might wear a transferable robe.' And regarding 'the two [rules] with "lodging"': these are the two training rules stated together with the word 'lodging' thus: 'one might use a lodging-robe without having relinquished it,' and 'one might set out on a journey without having relinquished the lodging.'

Pasākhe āsane cevāti “pasākhe jātaṃ gaṇḍaṃ vā”ti ca “bhikkhussa purato anāpucchā āsane nisīdeyyā”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ.

And regarding 'a limb and a seat': this is the pair of training rules stated as, 'or a boil that has arisen on a limb,' and 'one might sit on a seat in front of a monk without asking.'

Eḷakalomasamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

The Explanation of the Origin of the Sheep's Wool Group

263. Eḷakalomā [M 0.0148] dve seyyāti eḷakalomasikkhāpadañceva dve ca sahaseyyasikkhāpadāni. Āhacca piṇḍabhojananti āhaccapādakasikkhāpadañca āvasathapiṇḍabhojanasikkhāpadañca. Gaṇavikālasannidhīti gaṇabhojanavikālabhojanasannidhikārakasikkhāpadattayaṃ[P 7.1312]. Dantaponena celakāti dantaponasikkhāpadañca acelakasikkhāpadañca. Uyyuttaṃ senaṃ uyyodhīti “uyyuttaṃ senaṃ dassanāya gaccheyya, senāya vaseyya, uyyodhikaṃ vā…pe… anīkadassanaṃ vā gaccheyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Surā orena nhāyanāti surāpānasikkhāpadañca orenaddhamāsanahānasikkhāpadañca. Dubbaṇṇe dve desanikāti “tiṇṇaṃ dubbaṇṇakaraṇāna”nti vuttasikkhāpadañca vuttāvasesapāṭidesanīyadvayañca. Lasuṇupatiṭṭhe naccanāti lasuṇasikkhāpadaṃ, “bhikkhussa bhuñjantassa pānīyena vā vidhūpanena vā upatiṭṭheyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadaṃ, “naccaṃ vā gītaṃ vā vāditaṃ vā dassanāya gaccheyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadañca. Ito paraṃ pāḷiṃ virajjhitvā likhanti. Yathā pana atthaṃ vaṇṇayissāma; evamettha anukkamo veditabbo.

263. By 'Eḷakalomā dve seyyā' is meant: the training rule on sheep's wool and the two training rules on shared bedding. By 'Āhacca piṇḍabhojana' is meant: the training rule on food brought on invitation and the training rule on almsfood in a lodging. By 'Gaṇavikālasannidhi' is meant: the triad of training rules on group meals, untimely meals, and storing food. By 'Dantaponenācelaka' is meant: the training rule on a tooth-cleaner and the training rule on a naked ascetic. By 'Uyyuttaṃ senaṃ uyyodhi' is meant: the triad of training rules stated as, 'One should go to see an army on the march,' 'one should stay with an army,' and 'one should go to a battle line... or to see a troop review.' By 'Surā orena nhāyana' is meant: the training rule on drinking liquor and the training rule on bathing within half a month. By 'Dubbaṇṇe dve desanikā' is meant: the training rule stated as 'of the three things that cause disrepute' and the remaining two Pāṭidesanīya rules. By 'Lasuṇupatiṭṭhe naccana' is meant: the training rule on garlic, the training rule stated as 'one should attend upon a bhikkhu who is eating with water or a fan,' and the training rule stated as 'one should go to see dancing, or singing, or instrumental music.' Hereafter, they write, having omitted the Pāḷi. However, as we shall explain the meaning, so should the sequence here be understood.

Nhānamattharaṇaṃ [V 0.0150] seyyāti “naggā nahāyeyya, ekattharaṇapāvuraṇā tuvaṭṭeyyuṃ, ekamañce tuvaṭṭeyyu”nti vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Antoraṭṭhe tathā bahīti “antoraṭṭhe sāsaṅkasammate, tiroraṭṭhe sāsaṅkasammate”ti vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ. Antovassaṃ cittāgāranti “antovassaṃ cārikaṃ pakkameyya, rājāgāraṃ vā cittāgāraṃ vā…pe… pokkharaṇiṃ vā dassanāya gaccheyyā”ti ca vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ. Āsandiṃ suttakantanāti “āsandiṃ vā pallaṅkaṃ vā paribhuñjeyya, suttaṃ kanteyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ.

By 'Nhānamattharaṇaṃ seyyā' is meant: the triad of training rules stated as, 'One should bathe naked,' 'one should lie down with a single covering,' and 'one should lie down on a single bed.' By 'Antoraṭṭhe tathā bahī' is meant: the pair of training rules stated as, 'within a territory deemed dangerous,' and 'outside a territory deemed dangerous.' By 'Antovassaṃ cittāgāraṃ' is meant: the pair of training rules stated as, 'One should set out on a journey during the rains-residence,' and 'one should go to see a royal residence or a picture gallery... or a lotus pond.' By 'Āsandiṃ suttakantana' is meant: the pair of training rules stated as, 'One should use a high seat or a couch,' and 'one should spin thread.'

Veyyāvaccaṃ sahatthā cāti “gihiveyyāvaccaṃ kareyya, agārikassa vā paribbājakassa vā paribbājikāya vā sahatthā khādanīyaṃ vā bhojanīyaṃ vā dadeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ. Abhikkhukāvāsena cāti “abhikkhuke āvāse vassaṃ vaseyyā”ti idametaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ. Chattaṃ yānañca saṅghāṇinti “chattupāhanaṃ dhāreyya, yānena yāyeyya, saṅghāṇiṃ dhāreyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Alaṅkāragandhavāsitanti “itthālaṅkāraṃ dhāreyya, gandhacuṇṇakena nahāyeyya, vāsitakena piññākena nahāyeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Bhikkhunītiādinā “bhikkhuniyā ummaddāpeyyā”tiādīni cattāri sikkhāpadāni vuttāni. Asaṅkaccikā āpattīti “asaṅkaccikā [M 0.0149] gāmaṃ paviseyya pācittiya”nti evaṃ vuttaāpatti ca. Cattārīsā catuttarīti [P 7.1313] etāni sabbānipi catucattālīsa sikkhāpadāni vuttāni.

By 'Veyyāvaccaṃ sahatthā ca' is meant: the pair of training rules stated as, 'One should perform service for a householder,' and 'one should give hard food or soft food with one's own hand to a householder, a male wanderer, or a female wanderer.' And by 'abhikkhukāvāsa' is meant this, which is said in reference to: 'One should spend the rains-residence in a dwelling without bhikkhus.' By 'Chattaṃ yānañca saṅghāṇi' is meant: the triad of training rules stated as, 'One should use an umbrella and footwear,' 'one should travel by vehicle,' and 'one should wear a waist-cloth.' By 'Alaṅkāragandhavāsita' is meant: the triad of training rules stated as, 'One should wear women's adornments,' 'one should bathe with scented powder,' and 'one should bathe with perfumed oil-cake.' By the words beginning with 'bhikkhunī', the four training rules beginning 'One should have a bhikkhunī massage...' are stated. By 'asaṅkaccikā āpatti' is meant: the offense stated thus, 'Should one enter a village without a breast-cloth, it is a pācittiya offense.' By 'Cattārīsā catuttarī' is meant: all these forty-four training rules are stated.

Kāyena na vācācittena, kāyacittena na vācatoti kāyena ceva kāyacittena ca samuṭṭhahanti; na vācācittena na vācatoti attho. Dvisamuṭṭhānikā sabbe, samā eḷakalomikāti idaṃ uttānatthameva.

'By body, not by speech-mind; by body-mind, not by speech' means that they arise by body and by body-mind; the meaning is that they do not arise by speech-mind, nor by speech alone. The phrase 'All are of dual origin, the same as the sheep's wool group' has a plain meaning.

Padasodhammasamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

Explanation of the Arising from [the Offense of Teaching] Dhamma Word by Word

264. Padaññatra asammatāti “padaso dhammaṃ, mātugāmassa uttarichappañcavācāhi dhammaṃ deseyya, aññatra viññunā purisaviggahena, asammato bhikkhuniyo ovadeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Tathā atthaṅgatena cāti “atthaṅgate sūriye ovadeyyā”ti idametaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ. Tiracchānavijjā dveti “tiracchānavijjaṃ pariyāpuṇeyya, vāceyyā”ti evaṃ vuttasikkhāpadadvayaṃ[V 0.0151]. Anokāso ca pucchanāti “anokāsakataṃ bhikkhuṃ pañhaṃ puccheyyā”ti idametaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ.

264. The phrase 'Padaññatra asammata' refers to these three stated training rules: 'teaching the Dhamma word by word'; 'one should teach the Dhamma to a woman with more than five or six words, unless an intelligent man is present'; and 'an unappointed [bhikkhu] should advise bhikkhunīs'. This phrase 'Tathā atthaṅgatena ca' is said in reference to this training rule: 'one should advise when the sun has set'. 'Tiracchānavijjā dve' refers to these two stated training rules: 'one should learn animal arts' and 'one should teach animal arts'. This phrase 'Anokāso ca pucchana' is said in reference to this training rule: 'one should ask a question of a bhikkhu who has not granted permission'.

Addhānasamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

Explanation of the Arising from Journeys

265. Addhānanāvaṃ paṇītanti “bhikkhuniyā saddhiṃ saṃvidhāya ekaddhānamaggaṃ paṭipajjeyya, ekaṃ nāvaṃ abhiruheyya, paṇītabhojanāni agilāno attano atthāya viññāpetvā bhuñjeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Mātugāmena saṃhareti mātugāmena saddhiṃ saṃvidhāya gamanañca “sambādhe lomaṃ saṃharāpeyyā”ti vuttasikkhāpadañca. Dhaññaṃ nimantitā cevāti “dhaññaṃ viññāpetvā vā”ti ca “nimantitā vā pavāritā vā khādanīyaṃ vā bhojanīyaṃ vā khādeyya vā bhuñjeyya vā”ti vuttasikkhāpadañca. Aṭṭha cāti bhikkhunīnaṃ vuttā aṭṭha pāṭidesanīyā vā.

265. The phrase 'Addhānanāvaṃ paṇītaṃ' refers to these three stated training rules: 'one should undertake a journey on the same road, having made an arrangement with a bhikkhunī'; 'one should board the same boat'; and 'not being ill, one should eat fine foods, having requested them for one's own sake'. The phrase 'Mātugāmena saṃhara' refers to the training rule on going, having made an arrangement with a woman, and the stated training rule: 'one should have hair removed in a private part'. The phrase 'Dhaññaṃ nimantitā ca' refers to 'having requested grain' and the stated training rule: 'being invited or offered, one should eat or consume hard food or soft food'. And 'Aṭṭha ca' refers to the eight Pāṭidesanīya rules stated for bhikkhunīs.

Theyyasatthasamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

Explanation of the Arising from [Traveling with] a Company of Thieves

266. Theyyasatthaṃ upassutīti theyyasatthena saddhiṃ saṃvidhāya ekaddhānamaggagamanañca upassutitiṭṭhanañca. Sūpaviññāpanena cāti idaṃ sūpodanaviññattiṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ. Rattichannañca okāsanti “rattandhakāre appadīpe, paṭicchanne [M 0.0150] okāse, ajjhokāse purisena saddhi”nti evaṃ vuttasikkhāpadattayaṃ. Byūhena sattamāti idaṃ tadanantarameva “rathikāya vā byūhe vā siṅghāṭake vā purisena saddhi”nti āgatasikkhāpadaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ.

266. The phrase 'Theyyasatthaṃ upassuti' refers to the [training rule on] going on the same road, having made an arrangement with a company of thieves, and the [training rule on] standing to eavesdrop. This phrase 'Sūpaviññāpanena ca' is said in reference to the training rule on requesting soup and rice. The phrase 'Rattichannañca okāsa' refers to these three stated training rules: 'in the darkness of night without a lamp'; 'in a concealed place'; and 'in an open place with a man'. This phrase 'Byūhena sattama' is said in reference to the training rule that comes immediately after: 'on a street, or in a troop-formation, or at a crossroads, with a man'.

Dhammadesanāsamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

Explanation of the Arising from Teaching the Dhamma

267. Dhammadesanāsamuṭṭhānāni ekādasa [P 7.1314] uttānāneva. Evaṃ tāva sambhinnasamuṭṭhānaṃ veditabbaṃ. Niyatasamuṭṭhānaṃ pana tividhaṃ, taṃ ekekasseva sikkhāpadassa hoti, taṃ visuṃyeva dassetuṃ “bhūtaṃ kāyena jāyatī”tiādi vuttaṃ, taṃ uttānameva. Nettidhammānulomikanti vinayapāḷidhammassa anulomanti.

267. The eleven training rules that have their origin in the Dhamma teaching are indeed clear. Thus, first, the mixed origin should be understood. The fixed origin, however, is of three kinds; it pertains to each single training rule. In order to show it separately, the passage beginning, ‘bhūtaṃ kāyena jāyati’ (a being is born through the body), has been spoken; that is indeed clear. As for ‘nettidhammānulomika,’ it means ‘in conformity with the Dhamma of the Vinaya Pāḷi.’

Samuṭṭhānasīsavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Explanation of the Headings on Origination is concluded.

Antarapeyyālaṃ

Intermediate Ellipsis

Katipucchāvāravaṇṇanā

The Explanation of the Section of ‘How Many?’ Questions

271. Idāni [V 0.0152][M 0.0151] āpattiādikoṭṭhāsesu kosallajananatthaṃ “kati āpattiyo”tiādinā nayena mātikaṃ ṭhapetvā niddesappaṭiniddesavasena vibhaṅgo vutto.

271. Now, for the purpose of generating skill in the sections beginning with offenses, having established the matrix by the method beginning with, ‘How many offenses?,’ the analysis has been spoken by way of exposition and counter-exposition.

Tattha kati āpattiyoti mātikāya ca vibhaṅge ca āgatāpattipucchā. Esa nayo dutiyapadepi. Kevalañhettha āpattiyo eva rāsivasena khandhāti vuttā. Vinītavatthūnīti tāsaṃ āpattīnaṃ vinayapucchā; “vinītaṃ vinayo vūpasamo”ti idañhi atthato ekaṃ, vinītāniyeva vinītavatthūnīti ayamettha padattho. Idāni yesu sati āpattiyo honti, asati na honti, te dassetuṃ “kati agāravā”ti pucchādvayaṃ. Vinītavatthūnīti ayaṃ pana tesaṃ agāravānaṃ vinayapucchā. Yasmā pana tā āpattiyo vipattiṃ āpattā nāma natthi, tasmā “kati vipattiyo”ti ayaṃ āpattīnaṃ vipattibhāvapucchā. Kati āpattisamuṭṭhānānīti tāsaṃyeva āpattīnaṃ samuṭṭhānapucchā. Vivādamūlāni anuvādamūlānīti imā “vivādādhikaraṇaṃ anuvādādhikaraṇa”nti āgatānaṃ vivādānuvādānaṃ mūlapucchā. Sāraṇīyā dhammāti vivādānuvādamūlānaṃ abhāvakaradhammapucchā. Bhedakaravatthūnīti ayaṃ “bhedanasaṃvattanikaṃ vā adhikaraṇa”ntiādīsu vuttabhedakaraṇapucchā. Adhikaraṇānīti bhedakaravatthūsu sati uppajjanadhammapucchā. Samathāti tesaṃyeva vūpasamanadhammapucchā. Pañca āpattiyoti mātikāya āgatavasena vuttā. Sattāti vibhaṅge āgatavasena.

Therein, ‘How many offenses?’ is the question about the offenses that have come in the matrix and in the analysis. This is the method in the second term as well. For here, it is just the offenses themselves that, by way of a collection, are called ‘aggregates.’ ‘Vinītavatthūni’ (grounds for removal) is the question about the removal of those offenses; for ‘vinītaṃ’ (removed), ‘vinayo’ (removal), and ‘vūpasamo’ (pacification) are one in meaning. The very things that are the means of removal are ‘vinītavatthūni’; this is the meaning of the term here. Now, to show those things in the presence of which offenses occur and in the absence of which they do not occur, the pair of questions beginning, ‘How many disrespects?,’ was spoken. But this ‘Vinītavatthūni’ is the question about the removal of those disrespects. And since there are no such offenses that have not reached a state of failure, therefore, ‘How many failures?’ is the question about the state of failure of the offenses. ‘How many origins of offenses?’ is the question about the origin of those same offenses. ‘The roots of dispute’ and ‘the roots of accusation’—these are the questions about the roots of the disputes and accusations that have come as ‘legal issues of dispute’ and ‘legal issues of accusation.’ ‘States conducive to harmony’ is the question about the states that cause the non-existence of the roots of dispute and accusation. ‘Grounds for creating a schism’—this is the question about the causes of schism spoken in passages such as, ‘or a legal issue leading to a schism.’ ‘Legal issues’ is the question about the states that arise when there are grounds for creating a schism. ‘Settlements’ is the question about the states that pacify those same things. ‘Five offenses’ are spoken of by way of what has come in the matrix. ‘Seven’ are spoken of by way of what has come in the analysis.

Ārakā etehi ramatīti ārati; bhusā vā rati ārati. Vinā etehi ramatīti virati[V 0.0153]. Paccekaṃ paccekaṃ viramatīti paṭivirati. Veraṃ maṇati vināsetīti veramaṇī. Na etāya ete āpattikkhandhā [P 7.1315] karīyantīti akiriyā. Yaṃ etāya asati āpattikkhandhakaraṇaṃ uppajjeyya, tassa paṭipakkhato akaraṇaṃ. Āpattikkhandhaajjhāpattiyā paṭipakkhato anajjhāpatti. Velanato velā; calayanato vināsanatoti attho[M 0.0152]. Niyyānaṃ sinoti bandhati nivāretīti setu. Āpattikkhandhānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. So setu etāya paññattiyā haññatīti setughāto. Sesavinītavatthuniddesesupi eseva nayo.

One delights far from these (aggregates of offenses), thus it is ‘ārati’; or, intense delight is ‘ārati.’ One delights without these, thus it is ‘virati.’ One refrains from each one individually, thus it is ‘paṭivirati.’ It destroys enmity, thus it is ‘veramaṇī.’ By this, these aggregates of offenses are not done, thus it is ‘akiriyā.’ That doing of an aggregate of offenses which would arise in the absence of this (restraint)—because of being the opposite of that, it is ‘akaraṇaṃ.’ Because of being the opposite of falling into an aggregate of offenses, it is ‘anajjhāpatti.’ From shaking, it is ‘velā’; the meaning is from causing to tremble or from destroying (the falling into an offense). It binds or obstructs the way of release, thus it is ‘setu’ (dam/barrier). This is a designation for the aggregates of offenses. That ‘setu’ is destroyed by this formulation (of a rule), thus it is ‘setughāta’ (destroyer of the dam). In the expositions of the remaining grounds for removal as well, this is the method.

Buddhe agāravādīsu yo buddhe dharamāne upaṭṭhānaṃ na gacchati, parinibbute cetiyaṭṭhānaṃ bodhiṭṭhānaṃ na gacchati, cetiyaṃ vā bodhiṃ vā na vandati, cetiyaṅgaṇe sachatto saupāhano carati, natthetassa buddhe gāravoti veditabbo. Yo pana sakkontoyeva dhammassavanaṃ na gacchati, sarabhaññaṃ na bhaṇati, dhammakathaṃ na katheti, dhammassavanaggaṃ bhinditvā gacchati, vikkhitto vā anādaro vā nisīdati, natthetassa dhamme gāravo. Yo theranavamajjhimesu cittīkāraṃ na paccupaṭṭhāpeti, uposathāgāravitakkamāḷakādīsu kāyappāgabbhiyaṃ dasseti, yathāvuḍḍhaṃ na vandati, natthetassa saṅghe gāravo. Tisso sikkhā samādāya asikkhamānoyeva pana sikkhāya agāravoti veditabbo. Pamāde ca sativippavāse tiṭṭhamānoyeva appamādalakkhaṇaṃ abrūhayamāno appamāde agāravoti veditabbo. Tathā āmisappaṭisanthāraṃ dhammappaṭisanthāranti imaṃ duvidhaṃ paṭisanthāraṃ akarontoyeva paṭisanthāre agāravoti veditabbo. Gāravaniddese vuttavipariyāyena attho veditabbo.

Regarding disrespect towards the Buddha and so on: whoever, when the Buddha is alive, does not go to attend upon him; when he has attained Parinibbāna, does not go to a cetiya-site or a bodhi-site; does not pay homage to a cetiya or a bodhi tree; or walks in a cetiya-courtyard with a parasol or wearing shoes—of this person it should be understood: ‘There is no reverence for the Buddha.’ Furthermore, whoever, though being able, does not go to listen to the Dhamma; does not recite the chants; does not give a Dhamma talk; leaves after breaking up a Dhamma-listening assembly; or sits with a distracted mind or without respect—of this person it should be understood: ‘There is no reverence for the Dhamma.’ Whoever does not establish a respectful attitude towards elders, newly-ordained, and middle-standing monastics; shows bodily insolence in places such as the Uposatha hall or the cloister for contemplation; or does not pay homage according to seniority—of this person it should be understood: ‘There is no reverence for the Saṅgha.’ Furthermore, one who, having undertaken the three trainings, simply does not train, should be understood as having disrespect for the training. And one who, standing in negligence and in the absence of mindfulness, does not develop the characteristic of diligence, should be understood as having disrespect for diligence. Likewise, one who simply does not perform the two kinds of hospitality—material hospitality and Dhamma hospitality—should be understood as having disrespect for hospitality. In the exposition on reverence, the meaning should be understood by way of the opposite of what has been said.

272. Vivādamūlaniddese “sattharipi agāravo”tiādīnaṃ buddhe agāravādīsu vuttanayeneva attho veditabbo. Appatissoti anīcavutti; na satthāraṃ jeṭṭhakaṃ katvā viharati. Ajjhattaṃ vāti attano santāne vā attano pakkhe vā; sakāya parisāyāti attho. Bahiddhā vāti parasantāne vā parapakkhe vā. Tatra tumheti tasmiṃ ajjhattabahiddhābhede saparasantāne vā saparaparisāya vā. Pahānāya vāyameyyāthāti mettābhāvanādīhi nayehi pahānatthaṃ vāyameyyātha; mettābhāvanādinayena hi taṃ ajjhattampi bahiddhāpi pahīyati. Anavassavāyāti appavattibhāvāya.

272. In the exposition on the roots of dispute, the meaning of the passage beginning, ‘disrespect also for the Teacher,’ should be understood by the very method spoken of regarding disrespect for the Buddha and so on. ‘Without deference’ means having non-lowly conduct; one does not live having made the Teacher the most senior. ‘Internally’ means either in one’s own mental continuum or in one’s own faction; the meaning is ‘in one’s own assembly.’ ‘Externally’ means in another’s mental continuum or in another’s faction. ‘Therein you’ refers to that division of internal and external, whether in one’s own and another’s mental continuum, or in one’s own and another’s assembly. ‘You should strive for abandoning’ means you should strive for the purpose of abandoning by methods such as the development of loving-kindness; for by the method of developing loving-kindness, it is abandoned both internally and externally. ‘For non-inflow’ means for the state of non-occurrence.

Sandiṭṭhiparāmāsīti [V 0.0154] sakameva diṭṭhiṃ parāmasati; yaṃ attanā diṭṭhigataṃ gahitaṃ, idameva saccanti gaṇhāti[P 7.1316]. Ādhānaggāhīti daḷhaggāhī.

‘One who clings to their own view’ means one misapprehends only their own view; whatever view has been grasped by oneself, one grasps it thus: ‘This alone is the truth.’ ‘One who grasps tenaciously’ means one who grasps firmly.

273. Anuvādamūlaniddeso [M 0.0153] kiñcāpi vivādamūlaniddeseneva samāno, atha kho aṭṭhārasa bhedakaravatthūni nissāya vivadantānaṃ kodhūpanāhādayo vivādamūlāni. Tathā vivadantā pana sīlavipattiādīsu aññataravipattiṃ āpajjitvā “asuko bhikkhu asukaṃ nāma vipattiṃ āpanno”ti vā, “pārājikaṃ āpannosi, saṅghādisesaṃ āpannosī”ti vā anuvadanti. Evaṃ anuvadantānaṃ kodhūpanāhādayo anuvādamūlānīti ayamettha viseso.

273. Although the exposition on the roots of accusation is the same as the exposition on the roots of dispute, nevertheless, for those who dispute based on the eighteen grounds for causing division, states such as anger and hostility are the roots of dispute. However, those who dispute in that way, having fallen into some failure among failures such as a failure in virtue, then accuse, saying, 'Such and such a monk has committed such and such a failure,' or, 'You have committed a Pārājika offense; you have committed a Saṅghādisesa offense.' For those who accuse in this way, states such as anger and hostility are the roots of accusation. This is the distinction here.

274. Sāraṇīyadhammaniddese mettacittena kataṃ kāyakammaṃ mettaṃ kāyakammaṃ nāma. Āvi ceva raho cāti sammukhā ca parammukhā ca. Tattha navakānaṃ cīvarakammādīsu sahāyabhāvagamanaṃ sammukhā mettaṃ kāyakammaṃ nāma. Therānaṃ pana pādadhovanabījanavātadānādibhedampi sabbaṃ sāmīcikammaṃ sammukhā mettaṃ kāyakammaṃ nāma. Ubhayehipi dunnikkhittānaṃ dārubhaṇḍādīnaṃ tesu avamaññaṃ akatvā attanā dunnikkhittānaṃ viya paṭisāmanaṃ parammukhā mettaṃ kāyakammaṃ nāma. Ayampi dhammo sāraṇīyoti ayaṃ mettākāyakammasaṅkhāto dhammo saritabbo satijanako; yo naṃ karoti, taṃ puggalaṃ; yesaṃ kato hoti, te pasannacittā “aho sappuriso”ti anussarantīti adhippāyo. Piyakaraṇoti taṃ puggalaṃ sabrahmacārīnaṃ piyaṃ karoti. Garukaraṇoti taṃ puggalaṃ sabrahmacārīnaṃ garuṃ karoti. Saṅgahāyātiādīsu sabrahmacārīhi saṅgahetabbabhāvāya. Tehi saddhiṃ avivādāya samaggabhāvāya ekībhāvāya ca saṃvattati.

274. In the exposition of the Sāraṇīya Dhammas, bodily action done with a mind of loving-kindness is called 'loving bodily action.' 'Publicly and privately' means in one's presence and in one's absence. Therein, becoming a companion to junior monks in tasks such as making robes is called 'loving bodily action in their presence.' Furthermore, for the elders, all respectful services, such as washing their feet and offering the breeze of a fan, are also called 'loving bodily action in their presence.' For both, when badly placed wooden articles and so on are stored away without contempt for them, as if they were one's own badly placed things, this is called 'loving bodily action in their absence.' 'This dhamma is also a sāraṇīya' means this dhamma, designated as loving bodily action, is to be remembered, as it generates mindfulness. The meaning is: whoever performs it, those for whom it is performed recollect that person with pleased minds, thinking, 'Oh, what a virtuous person!' 'Maker of endearment' means it makes that person dear to their fellow practitioners. 'Maker of respect' means it makes that person respected by their fellow practitioners. In 'for the sake of inclusion' and so on, it conduces to the state of being worthy of inclusion by fellow practitioners, to non-dispute with them, to the state of harmony, and to the state of unity.

Mettaṃ vacīkammantiādīsu devatthero tissattheroti evaṃ paggayha vacanaṃ sammukhā mettaṃ vacīkammaṃ nāma. Vihāre asante pana taṃ paṭipucchantassa “kuhiṃ amhākaṃ devatthero, kuhiṃ amhākaṃ tissatthero, kadā nu kho āgamissatī”ti evaṃ mamāyanavacanaṃ parammukhā mettaṃ vacīkammaṃ nāma. Mettāsinehasiniddhāni pana nayanāni ummīletvā pasannena mukhena olokanaṃ sammukhā mettaṃ manokammaṃ nāma. “Devatthero tissatthero arogo hotu, appābādho”ti samannāharaṇaṃ parammukhā mettaṃ manokammaṃ nāma.

In 'loving verbal action' and so on, extolling speech, such as, 'Elder Devata, Elder Tissa,' is called 'loving verbal action in their presence.' Furthermore, when they are not in the monastery, for one who asks about them, speech of endearment such as, 'Where is our Elder Devata? Where is our Elder Tissa? When, indeed, will he return?' is called 'loving verbal action in their absence.' Furthermore, opening eyes moist with the affection of loving-kindness and looking with a serene face is called 'loving mental action in their presence.' The mental advertence, 'May Elder Devata and Elder Tissa be free from disease, free from affliction,' is called 'loving mental action in their absence.'

Appaṭivibhattabhogīti neva āmisaṃ paṭivibhajitvā bhuñjati, na puggalaṃ. Yo hi “ettakaṃ [V 0.0155] paresaṃ dassāmi, ettakaṃ attanā [P 7.1317] bhuñjissāmi, ettakaṃ vā asukassa [M 0.0154] ca asukassa ca dassāmi, ettakaṃ attanā bhuñjissāmī”ti vibhajitvā bhuñjati, ayaṃ paṭivibhattabhogī nāma. Ayaṃ pana evaṃ akatvā ābhataṃ piṇḍapātaṃ therāsanato paṭṭhāya datvā gahitāvasesaṃ bhuñjati. “Sīlavantehī”ti vacanato dussīlassa adātumpi vaṭṭati, sāraṇīyadhammapūrakena pana sabbesaṃ dātabbamevāti vuttaṃ. Gilāna-gilānupaṭṭhāka-āgantuka-gamikacīvarakammādipasutānaṃ viceyya dātumpi vaṭṭati. Na hi ete vicinitvā dentena puggalavibhāgo kato hoti, īdisānañhi kicchalābhattā viseso kātabboyevāti ayaṃ karoti.

'One who partakes without dividing' means he partakes neither by dividing material things nor by dividing among persons. For whoever partakes after dividing, thinking, 'I will give this much to others and partake of this much myself,' or 'I will give this much to so-and-so and so-and-so, and partake of this much myself,' is called a 'paṭivibhattabhogī' (one who partakes after dividing). This one, however, without doing so, gives the alms-food that was brought, starting from the elder's seat, and partakes of what remains after it has been taken. Because of the Pali text 'with the virtuous,' it is also proper not to give to an unvirtuous person; however, it is said that one who is fulfilling the Sāraṇīya Dhammas should give to all. It is also proper to give after selecting among the sick, attendants of the sick, visitors, those about to travel, and those engaged in tasks like robe-making. For by giving after selecting these, a division among persons is not made, because for such persons, since their gains are obtained with difficulty, a distinction should indeed be made; thus he acts.

Akhaṇḍānītiādīsu yassa sattasu āpattikkhandhesu ādimhi vā ante vā sikkhāpadaṃ bhinnaṃ hoti, tassa sīlaṃ pariyante chinnasāṭako viya khaṇḍaṃ nāma. Yassa pana vemajjhe bhinnaṃ, tassa majjhe chiddasāṭako viya chiddaṃ nāma hoti. Yassa paṭipāṭiyā dve tīṇi bhinnāni, tassa piṭṭhiyaṃ vā kucchiyaṃ vā uṭṭhitena visabhāgavaṇṇena kāḷarattādīnaṃ aññatarasarīravaṇṇā gāvī viya sabalaṃ nāma hoti. Yassa antarantarā bhinnāni, tassa antarantarā visabhāgavaṇṇabinduvicitrā gāvī viya kammāsaṃ nāma hoti. Yassa pana sabbena sabbaṃ abhinnāni sīlāni, tassa tāni sīlāni akhaṇḍāni acchiddāni asabalāni akammāsāni nāma honti. Tāni panetāni bhujissabhāvakaraṇato bhujissāni. Viññūhi pasatthattā viññuppasatthāni. Taṇhādiṭṭhīhi aparāmaṭṭhattā aparāmaṭṭhāni. Upacārasamādhiṃ appanāsamādhiṃ vā saṃvattayantīti samādhisaṃvattanikānīti vuccanti. Sīlasāmaññagato viharatīti tesu tesu disābhāgesu viharantehi kalyāṇasīlehi bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ samānabhāvūpagatasīlo viharati.

In 'unbroken' and so on, the determination is thus: For one for whom a training rule among the seven classes of offenses is broken at the beginning or at the end, his virtue is called 'broken' (khaṇḍa), like a cloth torn at the edge. For one for whom it is broken in the very middle, his is called 'pierced' (chidda), like a cloth with a hole in the middle. For one for whom two or three are broken in sequence, his is called 'spotted' (sabala), like a cow with a dissimilar color—such as black or red—that has arisen on its back or belly. For one for whom they are broken here and there, his is called 'mottled' (kammāsa), like a cow variegated with spots of a dissimilar color here and there. But for one for whom the virtues are entirely unbroken in every way, his virtues are called 'unbroken,' 'unpierced,' 'unspotted,' and 'unmottled.' Furthermore, these are called 'liberating' (bhujissa) because they bring about a state of freedom. They are called 'praised by the wise' (viññuppasattha) because they are praised by the wise. They are called 'untouched' (aparāmaṭṭha) because they are not wrongly grasped by craving and views. They are called 'conducive to concentration' (samādhisaṃvattanika) because they lead to access concentration or absorption concentration. 'He dwells having attained commonality in virtue' means he dwells having virtue that has attained a state of sameness with the virtuous monks dwelling in those various regions.

Yāyaṃ diṭṭhīti maggasampayuttā sammādiṭṭhi. Ariyāti niddosā. Niyyātīti niyyānikā. Takkarassāti yo tathākārī hoti, tassa. Dukkhakkhayāyāti sabbadukkhassa khayatthaṃ. Sesaṃ yāva samathabhedapariyosānā uttānatthameva.

'Whatever view this is' means right view associated with the path. 'Noble' means faultless. It leads out, therefore it is 'leading out' (niyyānika). 'For one who does so' means for one who acts in accordance with that. 'For the destruction of suffering' means for the purpose of the destruction of all suffering. The remainder, up to the end of the analysis of serenity, has only a plain meaning.

Katipucchāvāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Section of Questions is concluded.

Khandhakapucchāvāro

The Section of Questions on the Khandhakas

Pucchāvissajjanāvaṇṇanā

The Exposition of Questions and Answers

320. Upasampadaṃ [M 0.0155][V 0.0156] pucchissanti upasampadakkhandhakaṃ pucchissaṃ. Sanidānaṃ saniddesanti nidānena ca niddesena ca [P 7.1318] saddhiṃ pucchissāmi. Samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ kati āpattiyoti yāni tattha samukkaṭṭhāni uttamāni padāni vuttāni, tesaṃ samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ uttamapadānaṃ saṅkhepato kati āpattiyo hontīti. Yena yena hi padena yā yā āpatti paññattā, sā sā tassa tassa padassa āpattīti vuccati. Tena vuttaṃ “samukkaṭṭhapadānaṃ kati āpattiyo”ti. Dve āpattiyoti ūnavīsativassaṃ upasampādentassa pācittiyaṃ, sesesu sabbapadesu dukkaṭaṃ.

320. They will ask about higher ordination; I will ask about the Khandhaka on higher ordination. With its source and explanation—I will ask together with the source and the explanation. Regarding the most excellent terms, how many offenses are there? That is, of those excellent, supreme terms stated there, how many offenses are there, in brief? For by whatever term whatever offense is laid down, that is called the offense of that term. Therefore, it was said, 'Regarding the most excellent terms, how many offenses are there?' There are two offenses: a pācittiya for one who confers the higher ordination on a person less than twenty years of age, and a dukkaṭa in all the remaining cases.

Tissoti “nassantete vinassantete, ko tehi attho”ti bhedapurekkhārānaṃ uposathakaraṇe thullaccayaṃ, ukkhittakena saddhiṃ uposathakaraṇe pācittiyaṃ, sesesu dukkaṭanti evaṃ uposathakkhandhake tisso āpattiyo. Ekāti vassūpanāyikakkhandhake ekā dukkaṭāpattiyeva.
