Three: For those with schism as their aim who say, 'Let them perish, let them be destroyed, what use are they?' there is a thullaccaya in performing the Uposatha. Performing the Uposatha together with a suspended monk entails a pācittiya, and in the remaining cases, a dukkaṭa. Thus, in the Uposathakkhandhaka, there are three offenses. One: In the Vassūpanāyikakkhandhaka, there is only one dukkaṭa offense.

Tissoti bhedapurekkhārassa pavārayato thullaccayaṃ, ukkhittakena saddhiṃ pācittiyaṃ, sesesu dukkaṭanti evaṃ pavāraṇākkhandhakepi tisso āpattiyo.

Three: For one who performs the Pavāraṇā with schism as their aim, there is a thullaccaya. Performing it together with a suspended monk entails a pācittiya, and in the remaining cases, a dukkaṭa. Thus, in the Pavāraṇākkhandhaka too, there are three offenses.

Tissoti vacchatariṃ uggahetvā mārentānaṃ pācittiyaṃ, rattena cittena aṅgajātachupane thullaccayaṃ, sesesu dukkaṭanti evaṃ cammasaṃyuttepi tisso āpattiyo. Bhesajjakkhandhakepi samantā dvaṅgule thullaccayaṃ, bhojjayāguyā pācittiyaṃ, sesesu dukkaṭanti evaṃ tisso āpattiyo.

Three: For those who take a young cow and kill it, there is a pācittiya. For touching the sexual organ with a lustful mind, there is a thullaccaya. In the remaining cases, there is a dukkaṭa. Thus, in the Cammakkhandhaka too, there are three offenses. In the Bhesajjakkhandhaka as well, for performing a surgical operation or applying a poultice within two finger-breadths around the sexual organ, there is a thullaccaya; for consuming bhojjayāgu, there is a pācittiya; and in the remaining cases, there is a dukkaṭa. Thus, there are three offenses.

Kathinaṃ [V 0.0157] kevalaṃ paññattimeva, natthi tattha āpatti. Cīvarasaṃyutte kusacīravākacīresu thullaccayaṃ, atirekacīvare nissaggiyaṃ, sesesu dukkaṭanti imā tisso āpattiyo.

The Kaṭhina is merely a regulation; there is no offense there. In the section connected with robes, for using robes of kusa-grass or bark-fibre, there is a thullaccaya. For an extra robe, there is a nissaggiya offense. In the remaining cases, there is a dukkaṭa. These are the three offenses.

Campeyyake ekā dukkaṭāpattiyeva. Kosambaka-kammakkhandhaka-pārivāsikasamuccayakkhandhakesupi ekā dukkaṭāpattiyeva.

In the Campeyyaka Khandhaka, there is only one dukkaṭa offense. In the Kosambaka, Kamma, Pārivāsika, and Samuccaya Khandhakas as well, there is only one dukkaṭa offense.

Samathakkhandhake [M 0.0156] chandadāyako khiyyati, khiyyanakaṃ pācittiyaṃ, sesesu dukkaṭanti imā dve āpattiyo. Khuddakavatthuke attano aṅgajātaṃ chindati, thullaccayaṃ, romaṭṭhe pācittiyaṃ, sesesu dukkaṭanti imā tisso āpattiyo. Senāsanakkhandhake garubhaṇḍavissajjane thullaccayaṃ, saṅghikā vihārā nikkaḍḍhane pācittiyaṃ, sesesu dukkaṭanti imā tisso āpattiyo.

In the Samathakkhandhaka, a giver of consent is reproached, and this reproachful act is a pācittiya; in the remaining cases, there is a dukkaṭa. These are two offenses. In the Khuddakavatthukkhandhaka, for cutting off one's own sexual organ, there is a thullaccaya; for ruminating, there is a pācittiya; and in the remaining cases, there is a dukkaṭa. These are three offenses. In the Senāsanakkhandhaka, for disposing of heavy goods, there is a thullaccaya; for expelling a monk from a monastery belonging to the Sangha, there is a pācittiya; and in the remaining cases, there is a dukkaṭa. These are three offenses.

Saṅghabhedebhedakānuvattakānaṃ thullaccayaṃ, gaṇabhojane pācittiyanti imā dve āpattiyo. Samācāraṃ pucchissanti vutte vattakkhandhake ekā dukkaṭāpattiyeva. Sā sabbavattesu anādariyena hoti. Tathā pātimokkhaṭṭhapane. Bhikkhunikkhandhake [P 7.1319] appavāraṇāya pācittiyaṃ, sesesu dukkaṭanti dve āpattiyo. Pañcasatikasattasatikesu kevalaṃ dhammo saṅgahaṃ āropito, natthi tattha āpattīti.

In the Saṅghabhedakkhandhaka, for followers of a schismatic there is a thullaccaya; for eating in a group, there is a pācittiya. These are two offenses. Regarding the passage beginning 'They will ask about proper conduct,' in the Vattakkhandhaka, there is only one dukkaṭa offense. It is incurred through disrespect in any of the duties. Likewise in the Pātimokkhaṭṭhapanakkhandhaka. In the Bhikkhunikkhandhaka, for omitting the pavāraṇā invitation, there is a pācittiya; in the remaining cases, there is a dukkaṭa. These are two offenses. In the Pañcasatika and Sattasatika Khandhakas, the Dhamma was merely compiled; there is no offense there.

Khandhakapucchāvāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Section of Questions on the Khandhakas is concluded.

Ekuttarikanayo

The Numerical Method

Ekakavāravaṇṇanā

Exposition of the Chapter on Singles

321. Āpattikarā [M 0.0157][V 0.0158] dhammā jānitabbātiādimhi ekuttarikanaye āpattikarā dhammā nāma cha āpattisamuṭṭhānāni. Etesañhi vasena puggalo āpattiṃ āpajjati, tasmā “āpattikarā”ti vuttā. Anāpattikarā nāma satta samathā. Āpatti jānitabbāti tasmiṃ tasmiṃ sikkhāpade ca vibhaṅge ca vuttā āpatti jānitabbā. Anāpattīti “anāpatti bhikkhu asādiyantassā”tiādinā nayena anāpatti jānitabbā. Lahukāti lahukena vinayakammena visujjhanato pañcavidhā āpatti. Garukāti garukena vinayakammena visujjhanato saṅghādisesā āpatti. Kenaci ākārena anāpattibhāvaṃ upanetuṃ asakkuṇeyyato pārājikāpatti ca. Sāvasesāti ṭhapetvā pārājikaṃ sesā. Anavasesāti pārājikāpatti. Dve āpattikkhandhā duṭṭhullā; avasesā aduṭṭhullā. Sappaṭikammadukaṃ sāvasesadukasadisaṃ. Desanāgāminidukaṃ lahukadukasaṅgahitaṃ.

321. In the Numerical Method, in the section beginning with 'Offense-causing things should be known,' the term 'offense-causing things' refers to the six origins of offenses. For it is by means of these that a person incurs an offense; hence, they are called 'offense-causing.' The term 'non-offense-causing things' refers to the seven settlements. 'An offense should be known'—the offense is to be known as stated in each training rule and its analysis. 'Non-offense'—non-offense should be known in the manner of 'There is no offense for a bhikkhu who does not consent,' and so forth. 'Light' refers to five kinds of offenses because they are purified by a light disciplinary procedure. 'Grave' refers to the Saṅghādisesa offense, because it is purified by a grave disciplinary procedure, and also the Pārājika offense, because it cannot be brought to a state of non-offense by any means. 'With remainder' refers to the remaining offenses, excluding the Pārājika. 'Without remainder' refers to the Pārājika offense. Two groups of offenses are gross; the rest are not gross. The pair 'requiring remedy' is similar to the pair 'with remainder.' The pair 'leading to confession' is included in the pair of light offenses.

Antarāyikāti sattapi āpattiyo sañcicca vītikkantā saggantarāyañceva mokkhantarāyañca karontīti antarāyikā. Ajānantena vītikkantā pana paṇṇattivajjāpatti neva saggantarāyaṃ na mokkhantarāyaṃ karotīti anantarāyikā. Antarāyikaṃ āpannassāpi desanāgāminiṃ desetvā vuṭṭhānagāminito vuṭṭhāya suddhipattassa sāmaṇerabhūmiyaṃ ṭhitassa ca avārito saggamokkhamaggoti. Sāvajjapaññattīti lokavajjā. Anavajjapaññattīti paṇṇattivajjā. Kiriyato samuṭṭhitā nāma yaṃ karonto āpajjati pārājikāpatti viya. Akiriyatoti [V 0.0159] yaṃ akaronto āpajjati, cīvaraanadhiṭṭhānāpatti viya. Kiriyākiriyatoti yaṃ karonto ca akaronto ca āpajjati, kuṭikārāpatti viya.

Obstructive offenses are the seven types of offenses that, when intentionally transgressed, create obstacles both to heaven and to liberation—thus they are called obstructive. However, an offense blameworthy by precept, when transgressed by one who is unknowing, creates neither an obstacle to heaven nor an obstacle to liberation—thus it is non-obstructive. Even for one who has fallen into an obstructive offense, after confessing what leads to confession and emerging from what leads to rehabilitation, upon attaining purity and for one standing at the stage of a novice, the path to heaven and liberation is not barred. Blameworthy precepts are those blameworthy in the world. Blameless precepts are those blameworthy by precept. Offenses originating from action are those for which one incurs an offense by doing, like a Pārājika offense. Offenses originating from inaction are those for which one incurs an offense by not doing, like the offense of not determining a robe. Offenses originating from both action and inaction are those for which one incurs an offense by both doing and not doing, like the offense related to building a hut.

Pubbāpattīti paṭhamaṃ āpannāpatti. Aparāpattīti pārivāsikādīhi pacchā āpannāpatti. Pubbāpattīnaṃ antarāpatti nāma mūlavisuddhiyā antarāpatti. Aparāpattīnaṃ antarāpatti nāma agghavisuddhiyā [P 7.1320] antarāpatti. Kurundiyaṃ pana “pubbāpatti nāma paṭhamaṃ āpannā. Aparāpatti nāma mānattārahakāle āpannā. Pubbāpattīnaṃ antarāpatti nāma parivāse āpannā. Aparāpattīnaṃ antarāpatti [M 0.0158] nāma mānattacāre āpannā”ti vuttaṃ. Idampi ekena pariyāyena yujjati.

A prior offense is the offense incurred first. A subsequent offense is the offense incurred later by those undergoing probation and so forth. The intermediate offense of the prior offenses is the intermediate offense for the purification of the root. The intermediate offense of the subsequent offenses is the intermediate offense for the purification of the culmination. However, in the Kurundī commentary, it is said: 'A prior offense is one incurred first. A subsequent offense is one incurred during the time one is liable for penance. The intermediate offense of the prior offenses is one incurred during probation. The intermediate offense of the subsequent offenses is one incurred during the observance of penance.' This too is applicable in one sense.

Desitā gaṇanūpagā nāma yā dhuranikkhepaṃ katvā puna na āpajjissāmīti desitā hoti. Agaṇanūpagā nāma yā dhuranikkhepaṃ akatvā saussāheneva cittena aparisuddhena desitā hoti. Ayañhi desitāpi desitagaṇanaṃ na upeti. Aṭṭhame vatthusmiṃ bhikkhuniyā pārājikameva hoti. Paññatti jānitabbātiādīsu navasu padesu paṭhamapārājikapucchāya vuttanayeneva vinicchayo veditabbo.

A confessed offense that is countable is one that is confessed after laying down the burden with the resolve, 'I will not incur it again.' An uncountable confessed offense is one that is confessed without laying down the burden, with a mind that is still eager and impure. Indeed, this offense, even though confessed, does not enter the count of confessed offenses. In the eighth case, for a bhikkhunī, it is a Pārājika offense. In the nine instances beginning with 'the precept should be known,' the decision should be understood according to the method stated in the question on the first Pārājika.

Thullavajjāti thulladose paññattā garukāpatti. Athullavajjāti lahukāpatti. Gihipaṭisaṃyuttāti sudhammattherassa āpatti, yā ca dhammikassa paṭissavassa asaccāpane āpatti, avasesā na gihipaṭisaṃyuttā. Pañcānantariyakammāpatti niyatā, sesā aniyatā. Ādikaroti sudinnattherādi ādikammiko. Anādikaroti makkaṭisamaṇādi anupaññattikārako. Adhiccāpattiko nāma yo kadāci karahaci āpattiṃ āpajjati. Abhiṇhāpattiko nāma yo niccaṃ āpajjati.

A 'gross fault' is a grave offense laid down on account of a gross fault. A 'non-gross fault' is a light offense. 'Connected with householders' refers to the offense of Sudhamma Thera and the offense of not making true a lawful promise; the remaining offenses are not connected with householders. The offense arising from the five deeds with immediate result is 'fixed'; the remaining are 'unfixed.' An 'originator' is an original perpetrator such as Sudinna Thera. A 'non-originator' is one who causes a subsequent precept to be laid down, such as the monkey-like ascetic. An 'occasional offender' is one who incurs an offense from time to time. A 'habitual offender' is one who constantly incurs an offense.

Codako nāma yo vatthunā vā āpattiyā vā paraṃ codeti. Yo pana evaṃ codito ayaṃ cuditako nāma. Pañcadasasu dhammesu appatiṭṭhahitvā abhūtena vatthunā codento adhammacodako nāma, tena tathā codito adhammacuditako nāma. Vipariyāyena dhammacodakacuditakā veditabbā. Micchattaniyatehi vā sammattaniyatehi vā dhammehi samannāgato niyato, viparīto aniyato.

An 'accuser' is one who accuses another based on a matter or an offense. The one thus accused is called the 'accused.' One who, without being established in the fifteen qualities, accuses with a false matter is called an 'unrighteous accuser'; one accused by him in such a way is called the 'unrighteously accused.' Conversely, the righteous accuser and the righteously accused should be understood. One endowed with qualities fixed in wrongness or fixed in rightness is 'fixed'; the opposite is 'unfixed.'

Sāvakā [V 0.0160] bhabbāpattikā nāma, buddhā ca paccekabuddhā ca abhabbāpattikā nāma. Ukkhepanīyakammakato ukkhittako nāma, avasesacatubbidhatajjanīyādikammakato anukkhittako nāma. Ayañhi uposathaṃ vā pavāraṇaṃ vā dhammaparibhogaṃ vā āmisaparibhogaṃ vā na kopeti. “Mettiyaṃ bhikkhuniṃ nāsetha, dūsako nāsetabbo, kaṇṭako samaṇuddeso nāsetabbo”ti evaṃ liṅgadaṇḍakamma-saṃvāsanāsanāhi nāsitabbo nāsitako nāma. Sesā sabbe anāsitakā. Yena saddhiṃ uposathādiko saṃvāso atthi, ayaṃ samānasaṃvāsako, itaro [M 0.0159] nānāsaṃvāsako[P 7.1321]. So kammanānāsaṃvāsako laddhinānāsaṃvāsakoti duvidho hoti. Ṭhapanaṃ jānitabbanti “ekaṃ bhikkhave adhammikaṃ pātimokkhaṭṭhapana”ntiādinā nayena vuttaṃ pātimokkhaṭṭhapanaṃ jānitabbanti attho.

Disciples are called 'liable to offense'; Buddhas and Paccekabuddhas are called 'not liable to offense.' One who has been subjected to the act of suspension is called 'suspended'; one who has been subjected to the remaining four types of disciplinary actions, such as censure, is called 'not suspended.' For this latter one does not disrupt the Uposatha, the Pavāraṇā, the communal partaking of the Dhamma, or the communal partaking of material goods. One who is to be expelled by means of expulsion by (revoking) the characteristic, expulsion by a punitive act, or expulsion from communion—as in, 'Mettiyā the bhikkhunī should be expelled, the corrupt one should be expelled, Kaṇṭaka the novice should be expelled'—is called 'expelled.' All others are called 'not expelled.' One with whom there is communion regarding Uposatha and so on is 'of the same communion'; the other is 'of a different communion.' The latter is of two kinds: of a different communion due to a formal act and of a different communion due to a view. 'The setting aside should be known' means that the setting aside of the Pātimokkha, as spoken of in the manner of 'Monks, one unrighteous setting aside of the Pātimokkha,' etc., should be known.

Ekakavāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition of the Section on Ones is concluded.

Dukavāravaṇṇanā

The Exposition of the Section on Twos

322. Dukesu sacittakā āpatti saññāvimokkhā, acittakā nosaññāvimokkhā. Laddhasamāpattikassa āpatti nāma bhūtārocanāpatti, aladdhasamāpattikassa āpatti nāma abhūtārocanāpatti. Saddhammapaṭisaññuttā nāma padasodhammādikā, asaddhammapaṭisaññuttā nāma duṭṭhullavācāpatti. Saparikkhārapaṭisaññuttā nāma nissaggiyavatthuno anissajjitvā paribhoge, pattacīvarānaṃ nidahane, kiliṭṭhacīvarānaṃ adhovane, malaggahitapattassa apacaneti evaṃ ayuttaparibhoge āpatti. Paraparikkhārapaṭisaññuttā nāma saṅghikamañcapīṭhādīnaṃ ajjhokāse santharaṇaanāpucchāgamanādīsu āpajjitabbā āpatti. Sapuggalapaṭisaññuttā nāma “mudupiṭṭhikassa lambissa ūrunā aṅgajātaṃ pīḷentassā”tiādinā nayena vuttāpatti. Parapuggalapaṭisaññuttā nāma methunadhammakāyasaṃsaggapahāradānādīsu vuttāpatti, “sikharaṇīsī”ti saccaṃ bhaṇanto garukaṃ āpajjati, “sampajānamusāvāde pācittiya”nti musā bhaṇanto lahukaṃ. Abhūtārocane musā bhaṇanto garukaṃ. Bhūtārocane saccaṃ bhaṇanto lahukaṃ.

322. In the dyads: an offense committed with consciousness is one from which there is release through absence of perception; an offense committed without consciousness is not one from which there is release through absence of perception. The offense of one who has attained meditative absorption is the offense of declaring a true superhuman state; the offense of one who has not attained meditative absorption is the offense of declaring an untrue superhuman state. An offense connected with the good Dhamma is one such as teaching the Dhamma word-by-word; an offense connected with what is not the good Dhamma is the offense of coarse speech. An offense connected with one's own requisites is an offense incurred through improper use, such as: using an item to be forfeited without having forfeited it, leaving out bowls and robes, not washing soiled robes, or not firing a bowl that has accumulated grime. An offense connected with another's requisites is an offense to be incurred for such things as spreading out the Saṅgha's beds, benches, etc., in an open space, or leaving without asking permission. An offense connected with oneself is an offense spoken of in the manner beginning with: 'for one with a soft back,' 'for one who is hanging,' 'for one pressing the sexual organ with the thigh.' An offense connected with another person is an offense spoken of in cases such as sexual intercourse, bodily contact, or giving a blow. One who speaks the truth, saying, 'She is a sikharaṇī,' incurs a grave offense. One who speaks falsely incurs a light offense, according to the rule 'in a case of intentional false speech, it is a pācittiya.' One who speaks falsely by declaring an untrue superhuman state incurs a grave offense. One who speaks the truth by declaring a true superhuman state incurs a light offense.

“Saṅghakammaṃ vaggaṃ karissāmī”ti antosīmāya ekamante nisīdanto bhūmigato āpajjati nāma. Sace pana aṅgulimattampi ākāse tiṭṭheyya, na āpajjeyya, tena vuttaṃ “no vehāsagato”ti. Vehāsakuṭiyā āhaccapādakaṃ mañcaṃ vā pīṭhaṃ vā abhinisīdanto vehāsagato [V 0.0161] āpajjati nāma. Sace pana taṃ bhūmiyaṃ paññāpetvā nipajjeyya na āpajjeyya, tena vuttaṃ – “no bhūmigato”ti. Gamiyo gamiyavattaṃ apūretvā gacchanto nikkhamanto āpajjati nāma, no pavisanto. Āgantuko āgantukavattaṃ apūretvā sachattupāhano pavisanto pavisanto āpajjati nāma, no nikkhamanto.

Thinking, 'I will make the Saṅgha act incomplete,' one who sits aside within the boundary, being on the ground, is said to incur an offense. But if he were to be even a finger's breadth in the air, he would not incur an offense; therefore it was said, 'not being in the air.' One who sits down upon a bed or a bench with insertable legs in a dwelling in the air, being in the air, is said to incur an offense. But if, having spread it on the ground, he were to lie down, he would not incur an offense; therefore it was said, 'not being on the ground.' A monk who is to travel, going without having fulfilled the duties of one who is to travel, is said to incur an offense when departing, not when entering. An incoming monk, entering with an umbrella and footwear without having fulfilled the duties of an incoming monk, is said to incur an offense when entering, not when departing.

Ādiyanto [M 0.0160] āpajjati nāma bhikkhunī atigambhīraṃ udakasuddhikaṃ [P 7.1322] ādiyamānā; dubbaṇṇakaraṇaṃ anādiyitvā cīvaraṃ paribhuñjanto pana anādiyanto āpajjati nāma. Mūgabbatādīni titthiyavattāni samādiyanto samādiyanto āpajjati nāma. Pārivāsikādayo pana tajjanīyādikammakatā vā attano vattaṃ asamādiyantā āpajjanti, te sandhāya vuttaṃ “atthāpatti na samādiyanto āpajjatī”ti. Aññātikāya bhikkhuniyā cīvaraṃ sibbanto vejjakammabhaṇḍāgārikakammacittakammādīni vā karonto karonto āpajjati nāma. Upajjhāyavattādīni akaronto akaronto āpajjati nāma. Aññātikāya bhikkhuniyā cīvaraṃ dadamāno dento āpajjati nāma. Saddhivihārikaantevāsikānaṃ cīvarādīni adento adento āpajjati nāma. Aññātikāya bhikkhuniyā cīvaraṃ gaṇhanto paṭiggaṇhanto āpajjati nāma. “Na bhikkhave ovādo na gahetabbo”ti vacanato ovādaṃ agaṇhanto na paṭiggaṇhanto āpajjati nāma.

A bhikkhunī who takes up purification with water that is excessively deep—more than two finger-breadths—is one who 'incurs an offense while taking up'. But a monk who uses a robe without having applied the marking-spot that makes it of a bad color is one who 'incurs an offense while not taking up'. A monk who undertakes the practices of sectarians, such as the vow of silence, is one who 'incurs an offense while undertaking'. But persons such as a monk on probation, or those on whom a formal act such as censure has been carried out, incur an offense if they do not properly undertake their own duty. Referring to them, it was said, 'There is an offense one incurs by not undertaking'. A monk who sews a robe for an unrelated bhikkhunī, or who does the work of a physician, the work of a storekeeper, the work of an artist, and so on, is one who 'incurs an offense while doing'. A person who does not perform the duties towards a preceptor, and so on, is one who 'incurs an offense while not doing'. A monk who gives a robe to an unrelated bhikkhunī is one who 'incurs an offense while giving'. A monk who does not give robes, and so on, to his co-residents and pupils is one who 'incurs an offense while not giving'. A monk who accepts a robe from an unrelated bhikkhunī is one who 'incurs an offense while accepting'. Because of the saying, 'Bhikkhus, exhortation is not to be not taken,' a monk who does not take exhortation is one who 'incurs an offense while not accepting'.

Nissaggiyavatthuṃ anissajjitvā paribhuñjanto paribhogena āpajjati nāma. Pañcāhikaṃ saṅghāṭicāraṃ atikkāmayamānā aparibhogena āpajjati nāma. Sahagāraseyyaṃ rattiṃ āpajjati nāma, no divā, dvāraṃ asaṃvaritvā paṭisallīyanto divā āpajjati, no rattiṃ. Ekarattachārattasattāhadasāhamāsātikkamesu vuttaāpattiṃ āpajjanto aruṇugge āpajjati nāma, pavāretvā bhuñjanto na aruṇugge āpajjati nāma.

A monk who uses an item to be forfeited without having forfeited it is one who 'incurs an offense through use'. A bhikkhunī who lets the five-day turn for the outer robe pass is one who 'incurs an offense through non-use'. Regarding sleeping together in a dwelling with a single roof, one incurs an offense at night, not during the day. A monk who goes into seclusion during the day without having closed the door incurs an offense during the day, not at night. In cases of exceeding one night, six nights, seven days, ten days, or a month, a monk who incurs the stated offense is one who 'incurs an offense at the rising of the dawn'. A monk who eats after having been formally satisfied is one who 'does not incur an offense at the rising of the dawn'.

Bhūtagāmañceva aṅgajātañca chindanto chindanto āpajjati nāma, kese vā nakhe vā na chindanto na chindanto āpajjati nāma. Āpattiṃ chādento chādento āpajjati nāma, “tiṇena vā paṇṇena vā paṭicchādetvā āgantabbaṃ, natveva naggena āgantabbaṃ, yo āgaccheyya āpatti dukkaṭassā”ti imaṃ pana āpattiṃ na chādento āpajjati nāma. Kusacīrādīni [V 0.0162] dhārento dhārento āpajjati nāma, “ayaṃ te bhikkhu patto yāva bhedanāya dhāretabbo”ti imaṃ āpattiṃ na dhārento āpajjati nāma.

A monk who cuts down plant life or the sexual organ is one who 'incurs an offense while cutting'. A monk who does not cut hair or nails is one who 'incurs an offense while not cutting'. A monk who conceals an offense is one who 'incurs an offense while concealing'. But regarding this offense spoken of as, 'One should come having covered oneself with grass or a leaf, but one should certainly not come naked; whoever should come thus incurs an offense of wrong-doing,' one who does not cover oneself is one who 'incurs an offense while not covering'. A monk who wears robes of kusa grass, and so on, is one who 'incurs an offense while wearing'. Regarding the rule, 'This bowl of yours, bhikkhu, is to be kept until it breaks,' one who does not keep it is one who 'incurs an offense while not keeping'.

“Attanā vā attānaṃ nānāsaṃvāsakaṃ karotī”ti ekasīmāyaṃ dvīsu saṅghesu nisinnesu ekasmiṃ pakkhe nisīditvā parapakkhassa laddhiṃ gaṇhanto yasmiṃ [M 0.0161] pakkhe nisinno tesaṃ attanāva attānaṃ nānāsaṃvāsakaṃ karoti nāma. Yesaṃ santike nisinno tesaṃ gaṇapūrako hutvā kammaṃ kopeti, itaresaṃ hatthapāsaṃ anāgatattā. Samānasaṃvāsakepi eseva [P 7.1323] nayo. Yesañhi so laddhiṃ roceti, tesaṃ samānasaṃvāsako hoti, itaresaṃ nānāsaṃvāsako. Satta āpattiyo satta āpattikkhandhāti āpajjitabbato āpattiyo, rāsaṭṭhena khandhāti evaṃ dveyeva nāmāni hontīti nāmavasena dukaṃ dassitaṃ. Kammena vā salākaggāhena vāti ettha uddeso ceva kammañca ekaṃ, vohāro ceva anussāvanā ca salākaggāho ca ekaṃ, vohārānussāvanasalākaggāhā pubbabhāgā, kammañceva uddeso ca pamāṇaṃ.

Regarding 'makes oneself of different communion by oneself': when two Saṅghas are seated in a single boundary, a monk who, having sat on one side, accepts the view of the other side, is said to make himself of different communion by himself from those on whose side he is seated. Being a quorum-filler for those near whom he is seated, he invalidates the formal act, because of not having come within the hand's reach of the others. This is the same method also in the case of one of the same communion. Indeed, for those whose view he approves, he is of the same communion; for the others, he is of different communion. 'Seven offenses, seven aggregates of offenses': because they are to be incurred, they are called 'offenses'; because of the meaning 'heap,' they are called 'aggregates.' Thus there are only two names; in this way the dyad has been shown by way of the name. Here, in the text 'By a formal act or by the taking of voting-tickets': the recitation of the Pātimokkha and the formal act are one cause; the speaking on the points causing schism, the announcement, and the taking of voting-tickets are one cause. The speaking, announcement, and taking of voting-tickets are the preliminary parts; the formal act and the recitation of the Pātimokkha are the standard.

Addhānahīno nāma ūnavīsativasso. Aṅgahīno nāma hatthacchinnādibhedo. Vatthuvipanno nāma paṇḍako tiracchānagato ubhatobyañjanako ca. Avasesā theyyasaṃvāsakādayo aṭṭha abhabbapuggalā karaṇadukkaṭakā nāma. Dukkaṭakiriyā dukkaṭakammā, imasmiṃyeva attabhāve katena attano kammena abhabbaṭṭhānaṃ pattāti attho. Aparipūro nāma aparipuṇṇapattacīvaro. No ca yācati nāma upasampadaṃ na yācati. Alajjissa ca bālassa cāti alajjī sacepi tepiṭako hoti, bālo ca sacepi saṭṭhivasso hoti, ubhopi nissāya na vatthabbaṃ. Bālassa ca lajjissa cāti ettha bālassa “tvaṃ nissayaṃ gaṇhā”ti āṇāyapi nissayo dātabbo, lajjissa pana yācantasseva. Sātisāranti sadosaṃ; yaṃ ajjhācaranto āpattiṃ āpajjati.

‘Deficient in age’ means less than twenty years old. ‘Deficient in limbs’ means of the kind whose hands have been cut off, etc. ‘Deficient in basis’ means a eunuch, an animal, or a hermaphrodite. The remaining eight incapable persons—such as one who co-resides by theft—are called 'those of wrong-doing'. Having wrong conduct, having wrong actions; the meaning is that by their own action done in this very existence, they have reached a state of incapability. ‘Incomplete’ means one with an incomplete bowl and robes. ‘One who does not request’ means one who does not request higher ordination. Regarding 'of the shameless and of the foolish': even if the shameless person is a master of the Tipiṭaka, and even if the foolish person is sixty years old, one should not live in dependence on either of them. Here, regarding 'of the foolish and of the modest': to the foolish person, dependence should be given even by command, saying, 'You, take dependence'; but to the modest person, only when they ask. 'With transgression' means 'with fault'; it is that which, by transgressing, one incurs an offense.

Kāyena paṭikkosanā nāma hatthavikārādīhi paṭikkosanā. Kāyena paṭijānātīti hatthavikārādīhi paṭijānāti. Upaghātikā nāma upaghātā. Sikkhūpaghātikā nāma sikkhūpaghāto. Bhogūpaghātikā nāma paribhogūpaghāto, tattha tisso sikkhā asikkhato sikkhūpaghātikāti veditabbā. Saṅghikaṃ vā puggalikaṃ vā dupparibhogaṃ bhuñjato bhogūpaghātikāti veditabbā. Dve venayikāti dve atthā vinayasiddhā. Paññattaṃ nāma sakale vinayapiṭake kappiyākappiyavasena paññattaṃ. Paññattānulomaṃ nāma catūsu mahāpadesesu daṭṭhabbaṃ. Setughātoti [V 0.0163] paccayaghāto; yena cittena akappiyaṃ kareyya, tassa cittassāpi anuppādananti attho. Mattakāritāti mattāya [M 0.0162] pamāṇena karaṇaṃ; pamāṇe ṭhānanti attho. Kāyena āpajjatīti kāyadvārikaṃ kāyena āpajjati; vacīdvārikaṃ vācāya. Kāyena vuṭṭhātīti tiṇavatthārakasamathe [P 7.1324] vināpi desanāya kāyeneva vuṭṭhāti; desetvā vuṭṭhahanto pana vācāya vuṭṭhāti. Abbhantaraparibhogo nāma ajjhoharaṇaparibhogo. Bāhiraparibhogo nāma sīsamakkhanādi.

'Refusal by body' means refusal by means of gestures of the hand, etc. 'One confesses by body' means one confesses by means of gestures of the hand, etc. 'Destructive' means acts of destruction. 'Destructive to the training' means destruction of the training. 'Destructive to requisites' means destruction of requisites. Therein, the destruction on the part of one who does not practice the three trainings should be known as 'destructive to the training'. The destruction on the part of one who makes wrong use of what belongs to the Sangha or to an individual should be known as 'destructive to requisites'. 'The two Vinaya matters' means two meanings established in the Vinaya. 'The prescribed' means what is laid down in the entire Vinayapiṭaka regarding what is allowable and unallowable. 'In conformity with the prescribed' should be seen in the four Great Authorities. 'Destroying the bridge' means destroying the cause; the meaning is the non-arising even of that mind with which one would do what is unallowable. 'Acting in moderation' means acting with measure; the meaning is standing within the proper measure. 'Commits by body' means one commits an offense of the body-door through the body, and an offense of the speech-door through speech. 'Emerges by body' means that in the 'covering with grass' settlement, one emerges by body alone, even without a confession; but one who emerges after confessing, emerges by speech. 'Internal use' means use by way of consuming. 'External use' means use such as anointing the head.

Anāgataṃ bhāraṃ vahatīti atherova samāno therehi vahitabbaṃ bījanagāhadhammajjhesanādibhāraṃ vahati; taṃ nittharituṃ vīriyaṃ ārabhati. Āgataṃ bhāraṃ na vahatīti thero therakiccaṃ na karoti, “anujānāmi bhikkhave therena bhikkhunā sāmaṃ vā dhammaṃ bhāsituṃ, paraṃ vā ajjhesituṃ, anujānāmi bhikkhave therādheyyaṃ pātimokkha”nti evamādi sabbaṃ parihāpetīti attho. Na kukkuccāyitabbaṃ kukkuccāyatīti na kukkuccāyitabbaṃ kukkuccāyitvā karoti. Kukkuccāyitabbaṃ na kukkuccāyatīti kukkuccāyitabbaṃ na kukkuccāyitvā karoti. Etesaṃ dvinnaṃ divā ca ratto ca āsavā vaḍḍhantīti attho. Anantaradukepi vuttapaṭipakkhavasena attho veditabbo. Sesaṃ tattha tattha vuttanayattā uttānamevāti.

'Carries a burden that has not come' means: though not an elder, one carries the burden that should be carried by elders, such as having a fan taken or being requested to teach the Dhamma, and one makes an effort to carry it through. 'Does not carry a burden that has come' means: being an elder, one does not do an elder's duty; the meaning is that one neglects all such duties as stated in: 'I allow, monks, an elder monk to teach the Dhamma himself or to request another; I allow, monks, the Pātimokkha dependent on the elder.' 'Is scrupulous about what one should not be scrupulous about' means one acts with scruples regarding something one should not be scrupulous about. 'Is not scrupulous about what one should be scrupulous about' means one acts without scruples regarding something one should be scrupulous about. The meaning is: for these two persons, the taints increase by day and by night. Also in the immediately following pair, the meaning should be understood by way of the opposite of what has been said. The remainder is self-evident, as the method has been stated in those respective places.

Dukavāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Section of Pairs is concluded.

Tikavāravaṇṇanā

The Commentary on the Section of Triples

323. Tikesu atthāpatti tiṭṭhante bhagavati āpajjatīti atthi āpatti, yaṃ tiṭṭhante bhagavati āpajjatīti attho. Eseva nayo sabbattha. Tattha lohituppādāpattiṃ tiṭṭhante āpajjati. “Etarahi kho panānanda, bhikkhū aññamaññaṃ āvusovādena samudācaranti, na vo mamaccayena evaṃ samudācaritabbaṃ, navakena, ānanda, bhikkhunā thero bhikkhū ‘bhante’ti vā ‘āyasmā’ti vā samudācaritabbo”ti vacanato theraṃ āvusovādena samudācaraṇapaccayā āpattiṃ parinibbute bhagavati āpajjati, no tiṭṭhante. Imā dve āpattiyo ṭhapetvā avasesā dharantepi bhagavati āpajjati, parinibbutepi.

323. In the Triples, the phrase 'an offense is incurred while the Blessed One is living' means: there is an offense that one incurs while the Blessed One is living. This same method applies everywhere. Among these, one incurs the offense of causing blood to flow while the Blessed One is living. Because it was said: 'But now, Ānanda, monks address one another with the term "āvuso". After my passing away, you should not address one another thus. Ānanda, a junior monk should address an elder monk as "bhante" or "āyasmā"', an offense is incurred after the Blessed One has attained Parinibbāna—not while He is living—due to addressing an elder with the term 'āvuso'. Setting aside these two offenses, the remaining offenses are incurred both while the Blessed One is living and after He has attained Parinibbāna.

Pavāretvā anatirittaṃ bhuñjanto āpattiṃ kāle āpajjati no vikāle. Vikālabhojanāpattiṃ [V 0.0164] pana vikāle āpajjati no kāle. Avasesā [M 0.0163] kāle ceva āpajjati vikāle ca. Sahagāraseyyaṃ rattiṃ āpajjati, dvāraṃ asaṃvaritvā paṭisallīyanaṃ divā. Sesā rattiñceva divā ca. “Dasavassomhi atirekadasavassomhī”ti bālo abyatto parisaṃ upaṭṭhāpento dasavasso āpajjati no ūnadasavasso[P 7.1325]. “Ahaṃ paṇḍito byatto”ti navo vā majjhimo vā parisaṃ upaṭṭhāpento ūnadasavasso āpajjati no dasavasso. Sesā dasavasso ceva āpajjati ūnadasavasso ca. “Pañcavassomhī”ti bālo abyatto anissāya vasanto pañcavasso āpajjati. “Ahaṃ paṇḍito byatto”ti navako anissāya vasanto ūnapañcavasso āpajjati. Sesaṃ pañcavasso ceva āpajjati ūnapañcavasso ca. Anupasampannaṃ padasodhammaṃ vācento, mātugāmassa dhammaṃ desento evarūpaṃ āpattiṃ kusalacitto āpajjati, pārājika-sukkavissaṭṭhi-kāyasaṃsagga-duṭṭhulla-attakāmapāricariya-duṭṭhadosa-saṅghabhedapahāradāna-talasattikādibhedaṃ akusalacitto āpajjati, asañcicca sahagāraseyyādiṃ abyākatacitto āpajjati, yaṃ arahāva āpajjati, sabbaṃ abyākatacittova āpajjati, methunadhammādibhedamāpattiṃ sukhavedanāsamaṅgī āpajjati, duṭṭhadosādibhedaṃ dukkhavedanāsamaṅgī, yaṃ sukhavedanāsamaṅgī āpajjati, taṃyeva majjhatto hutvā āpajjanto adukkhamasukhavedanāsamaṅgī āpajjati.

Having been invited and then eating what is not a leftover, one incurs an offense at the proper time, not at the wrong time. The offense of eating at the wrong time, however, is incurred at the wrong time, not at the proper time. The remaining offenses are incurred both at the proper time and at the wrong time. The offense of lying down in the same dwelling is incurred at night; the offense of being in seclusion without having closed the door is incurred during the day. The remaining offenses are incurred both at night and during the day. Thinking, 'I am of ten vassas' or 'I am of more than ten vassas', a foolish, unskilled monk attending to an assembly, being of ten vassas, incurs an offense; one of less than ten vassas does not. Thinking, 'I am wise and skilled', a junior or middle monk attending to an assembly, being of less than ten vassas, incurs an offense; one of ten vassas does not. The remaining offenses are incurred by both one of ten vassas and one of less than ten vassas. Thinking, 'I am of five vassas', a foolish, unskilled monk living without dependence, being of five vassas, incurs an offense. Thinking, 'I am wise and skilled', a junior monk living without dependence, being of less than five vassas, incurs an offense. The remaining offenses are incurred by both one of five vassas and one of less than five vassas. Teaching the Dhamma phrase by phrase to one not fully ordained, or teaching the Dhamma to a woman—one incurs such an offense with a wholesome mind. Offenses such as Pārājika, emission of semen, bodily contact, a grave transgression, self-gratification, malicious speech, causing a schism, giving a blow, or a blow with the palm, are incurred with an unwholesome mind. Unintentionally committing the offense of lying down in the same dwelling, etc., is incurred with an indeterminate mind. Whatever offense only an Arahant incurs, all of it is incurred with an indeterminate mind. Offenses such as sexual intercourse are incurred by one endowed with pleasant feeling; offenses such as malicious speech are incurred by one endowed with painful feeling. Whatever offense one endowed with pleasant feeling incurs, that very offense, when incurred by one who is neutral, is incurred by one endowed with neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling.

Tayo paṭikkhepāti buddhassa bhagavato tayo paṭikkhepā. Catūsu paccayesu mahicchatā asantuṭṭhitā kilesasallekhanapaṭipattiyā agopāyanā, ime hi tayo dhammā buddhena bhagavatā paṭikkhittā. Appicchatādayo pana tayo buddhena bhagavatā anuññātā, tena vuttaṃ “tayo anuññātā”ti.

The three rejections. These are the three rejections of the Buddha, the Blessed One: great desire regarding the four requisites, discontent, and lack of guarding in the practice of effacing the defilements. For these three things have been rejected by the Buddha, the Blessed One. But the three things beginning with having few wants have been allowed by the Buddha, the Blessed One. Therefore, it is said: 'Three are allowed.'

“Dasavassomhī”ti parisaṃ upaṭṭhāpento “pañcavassomhī”ti nissayaṃ agaṇhanto bālo āpajjati no paṇḍito, ūnadasavasso “byattomhī”ti bahussutattā parisaṃ upaṭṭhāpento ūnapañcavasso ca nissayaṃ agaṇhanto paṇḍito āpajjati no bālo; avasesaṃ paṇḍito ceva āpajjati bālo ca. Vassaṃ anupagacchanto kāḷe āpajjati no juṇhe; mahāpavāraṇāya appavārento juṇhe āpajjati no kāḷe; avasesaṃ kāḷe ceva āpajjati juṇhe ca. Vassūpagamanaṃ kāḷe [M 0.0164] kappati no juṇhe; mahāpavāraṇāya pavāraṇā juṇhe kappati no kāḷe; sesaṃ anuññātakaṃ kāḷe ceva kappati juṇhe ca.

A foolish monk attending to an assembly thinking, 'I am of ten vassas', and one not taking dependence thinking, 'I am of five vassas', incurs an offense; a wise one does not. A wise monk of less than ten vassas attending to an assembly due to being very learned and thinking, 'I am skilled', and one of less than five vassas not taking dependence, incurs an offense; a foolish one does not. For the remaining offenses, both a wise one and a foolish one incur them. One who does not enter the Rains-residence incurs an offense in the dark fortnight, not in the bright fortnight. One who does not perform the Pavāraṇā at the Great Pavāraṇā incurs an offense in the bright fortnight, not in the dark fortnight. For the remaining offenses, they are incurred in both the dark and bright fortnights. Entering the Rains-residence is allowable in the dark fortnight, not in the bright fortnight. The Pavāraṇā at the Great Pavāraṇā is allowable in the bright fortnight, not in the dark fortnight. The remaining permitted acts are allowable in both the dark and bright fortnights.

Kattikapuṇṇamāsiyā [V 0.0165] pacchime pāṭipadadivase vikappetvā ṭhapitaṃ vassikasāṭikaṃ nivāsento hemante āpajjati. Kurundiyaṃ pana “kattikapuṇṇamadivase apaccuddharitvā hemante āpajjatī”ti [P 7.1326] vuttaṃ, tampi suvuttaṃ. “Cātumāsaṃ adhiṭṭhātuṃ tato paraṃ vikappetu”nti hi vuttaṃ. Atirekamāse sese gimhāne pariyesanto atirekaḍḍhamāse sese katvā nivāsento ca gimhe āpajjati nāma. Satiyā vassikasāṭikāya naggo kāyaṃ ovassāpento vasse āpajjati nāma. Pārisuddhiuposathaṃ vā adhiṭṭhānuposathaṃ vā karonto saṅgho āpajjati. Suttuddesañca adhiṭṭhānuposathañca karonto gaṇo āpajjati. Ekako suttuddesaṃ pārisuddhiuposathañca karonto puggalo āpajjati. Pavāraṇāyapi eseva nayo.

On the first day of the waning moon after the Kattika full moon, a monk who wears a rains-cloth that has been set aside after having been alienated incurs an offense in the winter season. In the Kurundī commentary, however, it is said: 'By wearing a rains-cloth without having withdrawn the determination on the Kattika full moon day, one incurs an offense in the winter season,' and that too is well said. For it is said: 'One should determine it for four months, and after that, alienate it.' A monk who seeks a rains-cloth in the hot season when more than a month remains, and one who, having made it, wears it when more than half a month remains, indeed incurs an offense in the hot season. A monk who, while having a rains-cloth, being naked, lets his body be drenched by rain, indeed incurs an offense in the rainy season. The Saṅgha incurs an offense when performing either the Uposatha of purity or the Uposatha of determination. A group incurs an offense when performing both the recitation of the Sutta and the Uposatha of determination. A single individual incurs an offense when performing both the recitation of the Sutta and the Uposatha of purity. In the case of Pavāraṇā too, this is the same method.

Saṅghuposatho ca saṅghapavāraṇā ca saṅghasseva kappati. Gaṇuposatho ca gaṇapavāraṇā ca gaṇasseva kappati. Adhiṭṭhānuposatho ca adhiṭṭhānapavāraṇā ca puggalasseva kappati. “Pārājikaṃ āpannomhī”tiādīni bhaṇanto vatthuṃ chādeti na āpattiṃ, “methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevi”ntiādīni bhaṇanto āpattiṃ chādeti no vatthuṃ, yo neva vatthuṃ na āpattiṃ āroceti, ayaṃ vatthuñceva chādeti āpattiñca.

The Saṅgha Uposatha and the Saṅgha Pavāraṇā are allowable only for the Saṅgha. The Group Uposatha and the Group Pavāraṇā are allowable only for a group. The Uposatha of determination and the Pavāraṇā of determination are allowable only for an individual. One who says such things as, 'I have incurred a Pārājika offense,' conceals the basis but not the offense. One who says such things as, 'I have engaged in sexual intercourse,' conceals the offense but not the basis. Whoever declares neither the basis nor the offense, this person conceals both the basis and the offense.

Paṭicchādetīti paṭicchādi. Jantāgharameva paṭicchādi jantāgharapaṭicchādi. Itarāsupi eseva nayo. Dvāraṃ pidahitvā antojantāghare ṭhitena parikammaṃ kātuṃ vaṭṭati. Udake otiṇṇenāpi etadeva vaṭṭati. Ubhayattha khādituṃ bhuñjituṃ vā na vaṭṭati. Vatthapaṭicchādi sabbakappiyatāya paṭicchannena sabbaṃ kātuṃ vaṭṭati. Vahantīti yanti niyyanti; nindaṃ vā paṭikkosaṃ vā na labhanti. Candamaṇḍalaṃ abbhāmahikādhūmarajarāhuvimuttaṃ vivaṭaṃyeva virocati, na tesu aññatarena paṭicchannaṃ. Tathā sūriyamaṇḍalaṃ, dhammavinayopi vivaritvā vibhajitvā desiyamānova virocati no paṭicchanno.

Because it conceals, it is called a concealer (paṭicchādi). The sweat-house itself is a concealer; this is the sweat-house-concealer. This same method applies in the other cases as well. Having closed the door, it is allowable for one standing inside the sweat-house to perform the preparatory act. For one who has descended into water, this same thing is also allowable. In both cases, it is not allowable to chew or to eat. As for the cloth-concealer, because of its suitability for all purposes, it is allowable to do everything while concealed by it. 'They carry' (vahanti) means they proceed, they are released; they do not receive blame or reproach. The moon’s orb, freed from clouds, mist, smoke, dust, and Rāhu, shines only when it is uncovered; it does not shine when concealed by any of these. Likewise is the sun’s orb. So too, the Dhamma and Vinaya shine only when taught openly and analyzed; they do not shine when concealed.

Aññena [M 0.0165] bhesajjena karaṇīyena aññaṃ viññāpento gilāno āpajjati, na bhesajjena karaṇīyena bhesajjaṃ viññāpento agilāno āpajjati, avasesaṃ āpattiṃ gilāno ceva āpajjati agilāno ca.

A sick monk who, having a need that can be met by one medicine, requests another, incurs an offense. A not-sick monk who requests medicine for a non-medicinal purpose incurs an offense. For the remaining offense, both the sick and the not-sick incur it.

Anto āpajjati no bahīti anupakhajja seyyaṃ kappento anto āpajjati no bahi, bahi [V 0.0166] āpajjati no antoti saṅghikaṃ mañcādiṃ ajjhokāse santharitvā pakkamanto bahi āpajjati no anto, avasesaṃ pana anto ceva āpajjati bahi ca. Antosīmāyāti āgantuko āgantukavattaṃ adassetvā sachattupāhano vihāraṃ pavisanto upacārasīmaṃ okkantamattova āpajjati. Bahisīmāyāti gamiko [P 7.1327] dārubhaṇḍapaṭisāmanādigamikavattaṃ apūretvā pakkamanto upacārasīmaṃ atikkantamattova āpajjati. Avasesaṃ antosīmāya ceva āpajjati bahisīmāya ca. Sati vuḍḍhatare anajjhiṭṭho dhammaṃ bhāsanto saṅghamajjhe āpajjati nāma. Gaṇamajjhepi puggalasantikepi eseva nayo. Kāyena vuṭṭhātīti tiṇavatthārakasamathena vuṭṭhāti. Kāyaṃ acāletvā vācāya desentassa vācāya vuṭṭhāti. Vacīsampayuttaṃ kāyakiriyaṃ katvā desentassa kāyena vācāya vuṭṭhāti nāma. Saṅghamajjhe desanāgāminīpi vuṭṭhānagāminīpi vuṭṭhāti. Gaṇapuggalamajjhe pana desanāgāminīyeva vuṭṭhāti.

Regarding 'he incurs an offense inside, not outside': a monk who arranges a bed by encroaching incurs an offense inside, not outside. Regarding 'he incurs an offense outside, not inside': a monk who, having spread out a communal bed or the like in the open, departs without putting it away or informing someone, incurs an offense outside, not inside. As for the remaining case, however, he incurs an offense both inside and outside. Regarding 'within the boundary': a visiting monk who enters another monastery with his umbrella and footwear without performing the duties of a visitor incurs an offense the moment he enters the approach-boundary. Regarding 'outside the boundary': a monk who is leaving on a journey who departs without fulfilling the duties of a traveler, such as storing the wooden furniture, incurs an offense the moment he crosses the approach-boundary. For the remaining case, he incurs an offense both within the boundary and outside the boundary. When a more senior monk is present, one who speaks the Dhamma without being invited is said to incur an offense in the midst of the Saṅgha. This same method applies in the midst of a group and in the presence of an individual. Regarding 'he is rehabilitated by body': he is rehabilitated by the legal procedure of covering with grass. For one who confesses by voice without moving the body, he is rehabilitated by voice. For one who confesses after performing a bodily action connected with speech, he is said to be rehabilitated by body and by voice. In the midst of the Saṅgha, one is rehabilitated from both an offense expiated by confession and an offense requiring rehabilitation. But in the midst of a group or an individual, one is rehabilitated only from an offense expiated by confession.

Āgāḷhāya ceteyyāti āgāḷhāya daḷhabhāvāya ceteyya; tajjanīyakammādikatassa vattaṃ na pūrayato icchamāno saṅgho ukkhepanīyakammaṃ kareyyāti attho. Alajjī ca hoti bālo ca apakatatto cāti ettha bālo “ayaṃ dhammādhammaṃ na jānāti” apakatatto vā “āpattānāpattiṃ na jānātī”ti na ettāvatā kammaṃ kātabbaṃ; bālabhāvamūlakaṃ pana apakatattabhāvamūlakañca āpattiṃ āpannassa kammaṃ kātabbanti attho. Adhisīle sīlavipanno nāma dve āpattikkhandhe āpanno; ācāravipanno nāma pañca āpattikkhandhe āpanno; diṭṭhivipanno nāma antaggāhikāya diṭṭhiyā samannāgato. Tesaṃ āpattiṃ apassantānaṃ appaṭikarontānaṃ diṭṭhiñca anissajjantānaṃyeva kammaṃ kātabbaṃ.

Regarding 'One should urge with firmness' (Āgāḷhāya ceteyya): one should urge firmly, for the sake of steadfastness; the meaning is that if the Saṅgha wishes, it may carry out an act of suspension upon one who has had an act of censure or the like performed against him and does not fulfill his duties. Regarding 'He is shameless, a fool, and unproficient': here, a 'fool' is one who 'does not know Dhamma and not-Dhamma,' and an 'unproficient person' is one who 'does not know offense and non-offense.' A formal act should not be carried out for merely this much reason. Rather, the meaning is that a formal act should be carried out upon one who has fallen into an offense that is rooted in foolishness or rooted in being unproficient. One called 'corrupt in higher virtue' is one who has fallen into two classes of offenses. One called 'corrupt in conduct' is one who has fallen into five classes of offenses. One called 'corrupt in view' is one who is endowed with a wrong view that grasps at extremes. A formal act should be carried out only upon those who do not see their offense, do not make amends for it, and do not abandon their wrong view.

Kāyiko davo nāma pāsakādīhi jūtakīḷanādibhedo anācāro; vācasiko davo nāma mukhālambarakaraṇādibhedo anācāro; kāyikavācasiko nāma naccanagāyanādibhedo dvīhipi dvārehi anācāro[M 0.0166]. Kāyiko anācāro nāma kāyadvāre paññattasikkhāpadavītikkamo; vācasiko anācāro nāma vacīdvāre paññattasikkhāpadavītikkamo; kāyikavācasiko nāma dvāradvayepi paññattasikkhāpadavītikkamo. Kāyikena upaghātikenāti kāyadvāre paññattassa sikkhāpadassa asikkhanena, yo hi taṃ na sikkhati, so naṃ upaghāteti, tasmā tassa taṃ asikkhanaṃ “kāyikaṃ upaghātika”nti vuccati. Sesapadadvayepi eseva nayo. Kāyikena micchājīvenāti jaṅghapesanikādinā vā gaṇḍaphālanādinā vā vejjakammena[V 0.0167]; vācasikenāti sāsanauggahaṇaārocanādinā; tatiyapadaṃ ubhayasampayogavasena [P 7.1328] vuttaṃ.

Bodily sport is improper conduct of the type of playing dice games and so on. Verbal sport is improper conduct of the type of making mouth-drumming sounds and so on. Bodily and verbal sport is improper conduct through both doors—the body-door and the speech-door—of the type of dancing, singing, and so on. Bodily misconduct is the transgression of a training rule laid down for the body-door. Verbal misconduct is the transgression of a training rule laid down for the speech-door. Bodily and verbal misconduct is the transgression of a training rule laid down for both doors. Regarding 'destructive through the body': this refers to not training in the training rule laid down for the body-door. For whoever does not train in it, he is said to destroy it. Therefore, that lack of training is called 'destructive through the body.' This same method applies to the other two terms. Regarding 'wrong livelihood through the body': this is by acting as a messenger on foot, etc., or by medical work such as lancing a boil, etc. Regarding 'through speech': this is by taking and delivering verbal messages, etc. The third term is stated by way of the combination of both.

Alaṃ bhikkhu mā bhaṇḍananti alaṃ bhikkhu mā bhaṇḍanaṃ kari, mā kalahaṃ, mā vivādaṃ karīti attho. Na voharitabbanti na kiñci vattabbaṃ; vadatopi hi tādisassa vacanaṃ na sotabbaṃ maññanti. Na kismiñci paccekaṭṭhāneti kismiñci bījanaggāhādike ekasmimpi jeṭṭhakaṭṭhāne na ṭhapetabboti attho. Okāsakammaṃ kārentassāti “karotu āyasmā okāsaṃ, ahaṃ taṃ vattukāmo”ti evaṃ okāsaṃ kārentassa. Nālaṃ okāsakammaṃ kātunti “kiṃ tvaṃ karissasī”ti okāso na kātabbo. Savacanīyaṃ nādātabbanti vacanaṃ na ādātabbaṃ, vacanampi na sotabbaṃ; yattha gahetvā gantukāmo hoti, na tattha gantabbanti attho.

Regarding 'Enough, monk, do not quarrel' (Alaṃ bhikkhu mā bhaṇḍanaṃ): the meaning is, 'Enough, monk, do not engage in disputes, do not engage in quarrels, do not engage in conflict.' Regarding 'He should not be addressed': nothing at all should be said; for even if such a person speaks, they think his word should not be listened to. Regarding 'Not in any individual position': the meaning is that he should not be placed in even a single position of seniority, such as being the one to hold the fan. Regarding 'For one causing an opportunity to be made': this is for one who causes an opportunity to be made, saying, 'May the venerable one grant an opportunity; I wish to speak to you.' Regarding 'It is not right to make an opportunity for him': an opportunity should not be granted; one should say, 'What will you do?' Regarding 'His instruction should not be accepted': his word should not be accepted, nor should his word be listened to. The meaning is that one should not go where he wishes to go, taking one with him.

Tīhaṅgehi samannāgatassa bhikkhuno vinayoti yaṃ so jānāti, so tassa vinayo nāma hoti; so na pucchitabboti attho. Anuyogo na dātabboti “idaṃ kappatī”ti pucchantassa pucchāya okāso na dātabbo, “aññaṃ pucchā”ti vattabbo. Iti so neva pucchitabbo nāssa pucchā sotabbāti attho. Vinayo na sākacchātabboti vinayapañho na sākacchitabbo, kappiyākappiyakathā na saṃsandetabbā.

Regarding the Vinaya of a monk endowed with the three qualities: whatever Vinaya he knows, that is called his Vinaya. The meaning is that he should not be questioned about it. Regarding 'An inquiry should not be granted': when someone asks, 'Is this allowable?' an opportunity for the question should not be given; he should be told, 'Ask something else.' The meaning is that he should neither be questioned, nor should his question be listened to. Regarding 'The Vinaya should not be discussed with him': a question concerning the Vinaya should not be discussed with him, nor should talk about what is allowable and unallowable be held with him.

Idamappahāyāti etaṃ brahmacāripaṭiññātādikaṃ laddhiṃ avijahitvā. Suddhaṃ brahmacārinti khīṇāsavaṃ bhikkhuṃ. “Pātabyataṃ āpajjatī”ti pātabyabhāvaṃ paṭisevanaṃ āpajjati. “Idamappahāyā”ti vacanato pana taṃ brahmacāripaṭiññātaṃ pahāya khīṇāsavaṃ “musā mayā bhaṇitaṃ, khamatha me”ti [M 0.0167] khamāpetvā “natthi kāmesu doso”ti laddhiṃ vijahitvā gativisodhanaṃ kareyya. Akusalamūlānīti akusalāni ceva mūlāni ca, akusalānaṃ vā mūlāni akusalamūlāni. Kusalamūlesupi eseva nayo. Duṭṭhu caritāni virūpāni vā caritāni duccaritāni. Suṭṭhu caritāni sundarāni vā caritāni sucaritāni. Kāyena karaṇabhūtena kataṃ duccaritaṃ kāyaduccaritaṃ. Esa nayo sabbattha. Sesaṃ tattha tattha vuttanayattā uttānamevāti.

“Without abandoning this”—this means, without abandoning this view, which begins with the profession of the holy life. “A pure holy-life practitioner”—this means, a monk who has destroyed the taints. “He becomes liable to be partaken of”—he resorts to the state of being liable to be partaken of. However, from the statement “without abandoning this,” having abandoned that profession of the holy life, and having sought forgiveness from a monk who has destroyed the taints by confessing, “I have spoken falsely, forgive me,” he should purify his destination by giving up the view that “there is no fault in sensual pleasures.” Unwholesome roots—these are both unwholesome things and roots, or the roots of unwholesome things are unwholesome roots. The same method applies to wholesome roots. Misconduct—conduct that is badly done or perverse is misconduct. Good conduct—conduct that is well done or beautiful is good conduct. Misconduct done by the body, which has become the instrument, is bodily misconduct. This method applies everywhere. The rest is clear, as the method has been stated in the respective places.

Tikavāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The explanation of the Triad Section is finished.

Catukkavāravaṇṇanā

The Explanation of the Tetrad Section

324. Catukkesu [V 0.0168] sakavācāya āpajjati paravācāya vuṭṭhātīti vacīdvārikaṃ padasodhammādibhedaṃ āpattiṃ āpajjitvā tiṇavatthārakasamathaṭṭhānaṃ gato parassa kammavācāya vuṭṭhāti[P 7.1329]. Paravācāya āpajjati sakavācāya vuṭṭhātīti pāpikāya diṭṭhiyā appaṭinissagge parassa kammavācāya āpajjati, puggalassa santike desento sakavācāya vuṭṭhāti. Sakavācāya āpajjati sakavācāya vuṭṭhātīti vacīdvārikaṃ padasodhammādibhedaṃ āpattiṃ sakavācāya āpajjati, desetvā vuṭṭhahantopi sakavācāya vuṭṭhāti. Paravācāya āpajjati paravācāya vuṭṭhātīti yāvatatiyakaṃ saṅghādisesaṃ parassa kammavācāya āpajjati, vuṭṭhahantopi parassa parivāsakammavācādīhi vuṭṭhāti. Tato paresu kāyadvārikaṃ kāyena āpajjati, desento vācāya vuṭṭhāti. Vacīdvārikaṃ vācāya āpajjati, tiṇavatthārake kāyena vuṭṭhāti. Kāyadvārikaṃ kāyenaāpajjati, tameva tiṇavatthārake kāyena vuṭṭhāti. Vacīdvārikaṃ vācāya āpajjati, tameva desento vācāya vuṭṭhāti. Saṅghikamañcassa attano paccattharaṇena anattharato kāyasamphusane lomagaṇanāya āpajjitabbāpattiṃ sahagāraseyyāpattiñca pasutto āpajjati, pabujjhitvā pana āpannabhāvaṃ ñatvā desento paṭibuddho vuṭṭhāti. Jagganto āpajjitvā pana tiṇavatthārakasamathaṭṭhāne sayanto paṭibuddho āpajjati pasutto vuṭṭhāti nāma. Pacchimapadadvayampi vuttānusāreneva veditabbaṃ.

324. In the tetrads, the determination should be understood thus. 'One incurs by one's own speech and is absolved by another's speech': Having incurred a verbal-door offense of the kind beginning with padasodhamma, and having gone to the place for the settlement-as-if-by-covering-with-grass, one is absolved by another's formal act. 'One incurs by another's speech and is absolved by one's own speech': One incurs an offense by another's formal act in the case of not relinquishing a wicked view; by confessing in the presence of an individual, one is absolved by one's own speech. 'One incurs by one's own speech and is absolved by one's own speech': One incurs a verbal-door offense of the kind beginning with padasodhamma by one's own speech; one who is absolved by confessing is also absolved by one's own speech. 'One incurs by another's speech and is absolved by another's speech': One incurs a saṅghādisesa offense requiring a third admonition by another's formal act; one who is absolved is also absolved by another's formal acts beginning with probation. In the subsequent tetrads: One incurs a bodily-door offense by body; by confessing, one is absolved by speech. One incurs a verbal-door offense by speech; in the tiṇavatthāraka settlement, one is absolved by body. One incurs a bodily-door offense by body; from that very offense, in the tiṇavatthāraka settlement, one is absolved by body. One incurs a verbal-door offense by speech; from that very offense, by confessing, one is absolved by speech. Regarding a Saṅgha's bed, for one who does not spread their own covering, through bodily contact one incurs the offense determined by the counting of body-hairs, and also the offense of lying down in a shared dwelling, incurring them while asleep. However, having awakened and realized that an offense was incurred, by confessing, one is absolved while awake. Furthermore, having incurred an offense while awake, one who is sleeping in the place for the settlement-as-if-by-covering-with-grass is called 'one who incurs while awake and is absolved while asleep'. The last pair of phrases should also be understood in accordance with what has been said.

Acittakāpattiṃ [M 0.0168] acittako āpajjati nāma. Pacchā desento sacittako vuṭṭhāti. Sacittakāpattiṃ sacittako āpajjati nāma. Tiṇavatthārakaṭṭhāne sayanto acittako vuṭṭhāti. Sesapadadvayampi vuttānusāreneva veditabbaṃ. Yo sabhāgaṃ āpattiṃ deseti, ayaṃ desanāpaccayā dukkaṭaṃ āpajjanto pācittiyādīsu aññataraṃ deseti, tañca desento dukkaṭaṃ āpajjati. Taṃ pana dukkaṭaṃ āpajjanto pācittiyādito vuṭṭhāti. Pācittiyādito ca vuṭṭhahanto taṃ āpajjati. Iti ekassa puggalassa ekameva payogaṃ sandhāya “āpattiṃ āpajjanto desetī”ti idaṃ catukkaṃ vuttanti veditabbaṃ.

An offense without intention is called 'incurred without intention.' Later, when confessing, one is absolved with intention. An offense with intention is called 'incurred with intention.' While sleeping in the place for the settlement-as-if-by-covering-with-grass, one is absolved without intention. The remaining pair of phrases should also be understood in accordance with what has been stated. Whoever confesses a similar offense, this one, on account of the confession, while incurring a dukkaṭa offense, confesses one of the offenses beginning with a pācittiya. And by confessing that, one incurs a dukkaṭa offense. But by incurring that dukkaṭa offense, one is absolved from the pācittiya and so forth. And by being absolved from the pācittiya and so forth, one incurs that dukkaṭa offense. Thus, it should be understood that this tetrad, beginning with 'While incurring an offense, one confesses,' was spoken with reference to one person and one single act.

Kammacatukke pāpikāya diṭṭhiyā appaṭinissaggāpattiṃ kammena āpajjati, desento akammena vuṭṭhāti. Vissaṭṭhiādikaṃ akammena āpajjati, parivāsādinā kammena vuṭṭhāti. Samanubhāsanaṃ [V 0.0169] kammeneva āpajjati, kammena vuṭṭhāti. Sesaṃ akammena āpajjati, akammena vuṭṭhāti.

In the tetrad of formal acts, the meaning should be understood thus. One incurs the offense of not relinquishing a wicked view by a formal act; by confessing, one is absolved without a formal act. One incurs an offense beginning with emission without a formal act; one is absolved by a formal act such as probation. The offense incurred through formal admonition is incurred only by a formal act, and is absolved by a formal act. The remaining offense is incurred without a formal act and is absolved without a formal act.

Parikkhāracatukke paṭhamo [P 7.1330] sakaparikkhāro, dutiyo saṅghikova tatiyo cetiyasantako, catuttho gihiparikkhāro. Sace pana so pattacīvaranavakammabhesajjānaṃ atthāya āhaṭo hoti, avāpuraṇaṃ dātuṃ anto ṭhapāpetuñca vaṭṭati.

In the tetrad of requisites, the first is one's own requisites; the second belongs to the Saṅgha; the third belongs to a shrine; the fourth is the requisites of laypeople. However, if those have been brought for the purpose of bowls, robes, new work, or medicine, it is permissible to give the key and to have them placed inside.

Sammukhācatukke pāpikāya diṭṭhiyā appaṭinissaggāpattiṃ saṅghassa sammukhā āpajjati, vuṭṭhānakāle pana saṅghena kiccaṃ natthīti parammukhā vuṭṭhāti. Vissaṭṭhiādikaṃ parammukhā āpajjati, saṅghassa sammukhā vuṭṭhāti. Samanubhāsanaṃ saṅghassa sammukhā eva āpajjati, sammukhā vuṭṭhāti. Sesaṃ sampajānamusāvādādibhedaṃ parammukhāva āpajjati, parammukhāva vuṭṭhāti. Ajānantacatukkaṃ acittakacatukkasadisaṃ.

In the tetrad of presence, the meaning should be understood thus. One incurs the offense of not relinquishing a wicked view in the presence of the Saṅgha; however, at the time of absolution, since there is no duty for the Saṅgha, one is absolved in absence. An offense beginning with emission is incurred in absence; one is absolved in the presence of the Saṅgha. The offense incurred through formal admonition is incurred only in the presence of the Saṅgha, and is absolved in the presence of the Saṅgha. The remaining offense, of the kind beginning with intentional false speech, is incurred only in absence and is absolved only in absence. The tetrad of unknowing is similar to the tetrad of being without intention.

Liṅgapātubhāvenāti sayitasseva bhikkhussa vā bhikkhuniyā vā liṅgaparivatte jāte sahagāraseyyāpatti hoti idameva taṃ paṭicca vuttaṃ. Ubhinnampi pana asādhāraṇāpatti liṅgapātubhāvena vuṭṭhāti. Sahapaṭilābhacatukke yassa bhikkhuno liṅgaṃ parivattati, so saha liṅgapaṭilābhena paṭhamaṃ uppannavasena seṭṭhabhāvena ca purimaṃ purisaliṅgaṃ jahati, pacchime itthiliṅge patiṭṭhāti, purisakuttapurisākārādivasena pavattā kāyavacīviññattiyo paṭippassambhanti, bhikkhūti vā purisoti vā evaṃ pavattā paṇṇattiyo [M 0.0169] nirujjhanti, yāni bhikkhunīhi asādhāraṇāni chacattālīsa sikkhāpadāni tehi anāpattiyeva hoti. Dutiyacatukke pana yassā bhikkhuniyā liṅgaṃ parivattati, sā pacchāsamuppattiyā vā hīnabhāvena vā pacchimanti saṅkhyaṃ gataṃ itthiliṅgaṃ jahati, vuttappakārena purimanti saṅkhyaṃ gate purisaliṅge patiṭṭhāti. Vuttaviparītā viññattiyo paṭippassambhanti, bhikkhunīti vā itthīti vā evaṃ pavattā paṇṇattiyopi nirujjhanti, yāni bhikkhūhi asādhāraṇāni sataṃ tiṃsañca sikkhāpadāni, tehi anāpattiyeva hoti.

By the manifestation of the sexual characteristic: If, while a monk or a nun is asleep, a change of sexual characteristic occurs, the offense of sharing a sleeping place is incurred. This was stated in reference to this very thing. However, for both, a non-common offense is lifted by the manifestation of the sexual characteristic. In the fourfold classification of simultaneous acquisition, if a monk's sexual characteristic changes, he, simultaneously with the acquisition of the new sexual characteristic, due to its being first-arisen and its superiority, abandons the former male characteristic and is established in the later female characteristic. Bodily and verbal intimations that occurred by way of male conduct, male appearance, and so on, are pacified. Designations that occurred as 'monk' or 'man' cease. Regarding the forty-six training rules that are non-common to nuns, for him there is no offense. However, in the second fourfold classification, if a nun's sexual characteristic changes, she, due to its later arising or its inferiority, abandons the female characteristic which has gone to the designation of 'later,' and by the method described, is established in the male characteristic which has gone to the designation of 'former.' Intimations opposite to what was described are pacified. Designations that occurred as 'nun' or 'woman' also cease. Regarding the one hundred and thirty training rules that are non-common to monks, for her there is no offense.

Cattāro sāmukkaṃsāti cattāro mahāpadesā, te hi bhagavatā anuppanne vatthumhi sayaṃ ukkaṃsitvā ukkhipitvā ṭhapitattā “sāmukkaṃsā”ti vuccanti. Paribhogāti ajjhoharaṇīyaparibhogā[V 0.0170], udakaṃ pana akālikattā appaṭiggahitakaṃ vaṭṭati. Yāvakālikādīni appaṭiggahitakāni ajjhoharituṃ na vaṭṭanti. Cattāri mahāvikaṭāni kālodissattā yathāvutte kāle vaṭṭanti. Upāsako sīlavāti pañca vā dasa [P 7.1331] vā sīlāni gopayamāno.

The four sāmukkaṃsā are the four great authorities (mahāpadesā); indeed, because they were personally exalted, lifted up, and established by the Blessed One before a basis for them had arisen, they are called 'sāmukkaṃsā.' 'Consumables' are consumables that are to be eaten. However, water, because it is not a 'timed' item, is allowable even if not formally received. Items such as those for a limited time are not allowable to be consumed if they are not formally received. The four great potent medicines, because they are indicated for a specific time, are allowable at the prescribed time. 'A virtuous lay follower' is one who protects the five precepts or the ten precepts.

Āgantukādicatukke sachattupāhano sasīsaṃ pāruto vihāraṃ pavisanto tattha vicaranto ca āgantukova āpajjati, no āvāsiko. Āvāsikavattaṃ akaronto pana āvāsiko āpajjati, no āgantuko. Sesaṃ kāyavacīdvārikaṃ āpattiṃ ubhopi āpajjanti, asādhāraṇaṃ āpattiṃ neva āgantuko āpajjati, no āvāsiko. Gamiyacatukkepi gamiyavattaṃ apūretvā gacchanto gamiko āpajjati, no āvāsiko. Āvāsikavattaṃ akaronto pana āvāsiko āpajjati, no gamiko. Sesaṃ ubhopi āpajjanti, asādhāraṇaṃ ubhopi nāpajjanti. Vatthunānattatādicatukke catunnaṃ pārājikānaṃ aññamaññaṃ vatthunānattatāva hoti,naāpattinānattatā. Sabbāpi hi sā pārājikāpattiyeva. Saṅghādisesādīsupi eseva nayo. Bhikkhussa ca bhikkhuniyā ca aññamaññaṃ kāyasaṃsagge bhikkhussa saṅghādiseso bhikkhuniyā pārājikanti evaṃ āpattinānattatāva hoti, na vatthunānattatā, ubhinnampi hi kāyasaṃsaggova vatthu. Tathā “lasuṇakkhādane bhikkhuniyā pācittiyaṃ, bhikkhussa dukkaṭa”nti evamādināpettha nayena yojanā veditabbā. Catunnaṃ pārājikānaṃ terasahi saṅghādisesehi saddhiṃ vatthunānattatā ceva āpattinānattatā [M 0.0170] ca. Evaṃ saṅghādisesādīnaṃ aniyatādīhi. Ādito paṭṭhāya cattāri pārājikāni ekato āpajjantānaṃ bhikkhubhikkhunīnaṃ neva vatthunānattatā no āpattinānattatā. Visuṃ āpajjantesupi sesā sādhāraṇāpattiyo āpajjantesupi eseva nayo.

In the fourfold classification beginning with the visitor: one who enters a monastery with an umbrella and footwear, with one's head covered, and wanders about there, incurs an offense only as a visitor, not as a resident. However, a resident who does not perform the duties of a resident incurs an offense, not a visitor. Regarding the remaining offenses of the body-door and speech-door, both incur them. Regarding a non-common offense, neither the visitor nor the resident incurs it. Also in the fourfold classification of the traveler: a traveler who departs without fulfilling the duties of a traveler incurs an offense, not a resident. However, a resident who does not perform the duties of a resident incurs an offense, not a traveler. Regarding the remaining offenses, both incur them. Regarding a non-common offense, neither incurs it. In the fourfold classification beginning with difference of object: for the four pārājika offenses, among one another, there is only a difference of object, not a difference of offense. Indeed, all of them are only the pārājika offense. This same method applies also to the saṅghādisesa and other offenses. For a monk and a nun, in mutual physical contact, for the monk there is a saṅghādisesa offense, for the nun a pārājika offense. Thus, there is only a difference of offense, not a difference of object; indeed, for both, physical contact itself is the object. Similarly, with such as 'for eating garlic, for a nun there is a pācittiya, for a monk a dukkaṭa,' the application of the method should be understood here. For the four pārājika offenses together with the thirteen saṅghādisesa offenses, there is both a difference of object and a difference of offense. Thus it is for the saṅghādisesa and other offenses with the aniyata and other offenses. For monks and nuns who from the beginning incur the four pārājika offenses together, there is neither a difference of object nor a difference of offense. This same method applies also to those who incur them separately, and to those who incur the remaining common offenses.

Vatthusabhāgādicatukke bhikkhussa ca bhikkhuniyā ca kāyasaṃsagge vatthusabhāgatā, no āpattisabhāgatā, catūsu pārājikesu āpattisabhāgatā, no vatthusabhāgatā. Esa nayo saṅghādisesādīsu. Bhikkhussa ca bhikkhuniyā ca catūsu pārājikesu vatthusabhāgatā ceva āpattisabhāgatā ca. Esa nayo sabbāsu sādhāraṇāpattīsu. Asādhāraṇāpattiyaṃ neva vatthusabhāgatā no āpattisabhāgatā. Yo hi purimacatukke paṭhamo pañho, so idha dutiyo; yo ca tattha dutiyo, so idha paṭhamo. Tatiyacatutthesu nānākaraṇaṃ natthi.

In the fourfold classification beginning with sameness of object: for a monk and a nun, in physical contact, there is sameness of object, but not sameness of offense. In the four pārājika offenses, there is sameness of offense, but not sameness of object. This is the method in the saṅghādisesa and other offenses. For a monk and a nun, in the four pārājika offenses, there is both sameness of object and sameness of offense. This is the method in all common offenses. In a non-common offense, there is neither sameness of object nor sameness of offense. Indeed, whichever is the first question in the former fourfold classification, that is the second here; and whichever is the second there, that is the first here. In the third and fourth, there is no distinction.

Upajjhāyacatukke saddhivihārikassa upajjhāyena kattabbavattassa akaraṇe āpattiṃ upajjhāyo [V 0.0171] āpajjati, no saddhivihāriko upajjhāyassa kattabbavattaṃ akaronto saddhivihāriko āpajjati, no upajjhāyo; sesaṃ ubhopi [P 7.1332] āpajjanti, asādhāraṇaṃ ubhopi nāpajjanti. Ācariyacatukkepi eseva nayo.

In the fourfold classification of the preceptor: for the non-performance of the duty to be done by the preceptor towards the pupil, the preceptor incurs an offense, not the pupil. A pupil not performing the duty to be done towards the preceptor incurs an offense, not the preceptor. Regarding the remaining offenses, both incur them. Regarding a non-common offense, neither incurs it. In the fourfold classification of the teacher also, this is the same method.

Ādiyantacatukke pādaṃ vā atirekapādaṃ vā sahatthā ādiyanto garukaṃ āpajjati, ūnakapādaṃ gaṇhāhīti āṇattiyā aññaṃ payojento lahukaṃ āpajjati. Etena nayena sesapadattayaṃ veditabbaṃ.

In the fourfold classification of taking: one who takes a pāda or more than a pāda with one's own hand incurs a grave offense. One who employs another with the command, 'Take less than a pāda,' incurs a light offense. By this method, the remaining three items should be understood.

Abhivādanārahacatukke bhikkhunīnaṃ tāva bhattagge navamabhikkhunito paṭṭhāya upajjhāyāpi abhivādanārahā no paccuṭṭhānārahā. Avisesena ca vippakatabhojanassa bhikkhussa yo koci vuḍḍhataro. Saṭṭhivassassāpi pārivāsikassa samīpagato tadahupasampannopi paccuṭṭhānāraho no abhivādanāraho. Appaṭikkhittesu ṭhānesu vuḍḍho navakassa abhivādanāraho ceva paccuṭṭhānāraho ca. Navako pana vuḍḍhassa neva abhivādanāraho na paccuṭṭhānāraho. Āsanārahacatukkassa paṭhamapadaṃ purimacatukke dutiyapadena, dutiyapadañca paṭhamapadena atthato sadisaṃ.

In the tetrad concerning those worthy of salutation, for bhikkhunīs, beginning from the ninth bhikkhunī in the refectory, even the preceptress is worthy of salutation but not of the gesture of rising. Furthermore, without distinction, for a bhikkhu whose meal is unfinished, any senior is worthy of salutation but not of the gesture of rising. Even a bhikkhu of sixty vassas who is on probation is worthy of the gesture of rising, but not of salutation. In unprohibited places, a senior is worthy of both salutation and the gesture of rising from a junior. A junior, however, is worthy of neither salutation nor the gesture of rising from a senior. The first clause of the tetrad concerning those worthy of a seat is similar in meaning to the second clause in the preceding tetrad, and the second clause is similar to the first clause.

Kālacatukke [M 0.0171] pavāretvā bhuñjanto kāle āpajjati no vikāle, vikālabhojanāpattiṃ vikāle āpajjati no kāle, sesaṃ kāle ceva āpajjati vikāle ca, asādhāraṇaṃ neva kāle no vikāle. Paṭiggahitacatukke purebhattaṃ paṭiggahitāmisaṃ kāle kappati no vikāle. Pānakaṃ vikāle kappati, punadivasamhi no kāle. Sattāhakālikaṃ yāvajīvikaṃ kāle ceva kappati vikāle ca. Attano attano kālātītaṃ yāvakālikādittayaṃ akappiyamaṃsaṃ uggahitakamappaṭiggahitakañca neva kāle kappati no vikāle.

In the tetrad concerning time, one who eats after having indicated satisfaction incurs an offense at the proper time, not at the improper time. The offense of eating at the improper time is incurred at the improper time, not at the proper time. The remaining offenses are incurred both at the proper time and at the improper time. A non-common offense is incurred neither at the proper time nor at the improper time. In the tetrad concerning what is received, food received in the forenoon is allowable at the proper time, not at the improper time. Juices are allowable at the improper time, but not at the proper time on the following day. Seven-day and lifetime requisites are allowable both at the proper time and at the improper time. The three kinds of items beginning with temporary items that have exceeded their time limit, unallowable meat, what is taken up by oneself, and what is not formally received—these are allowable neither at the proper time nor at the improper time.

Paccantimacatukke samudde sīmaṃ bandhanto paccantimesu janapadesu āpajjati, no majjhimesu; pañcavaggena gaṇena upasampādento guṇaṅguṇūpāhanaṃ dhuvanahānaṃ cammattharaṇāni ca majjhimesu janapadesu āpajjati no paccantimesu. Imāni cattāri “idha na kappantī”ti vadantopi paccantimesu āpajjati, “idha kappantī”ti vadanto pana majjhimesu āpajjati. Sesāpattiṃ ubhayattha āpajjati, asādhāraṇaṃ na katthaci āpajjati. Dutiyacatukke pañcavaggena gaṇena upasampadādi catubbidhampi vatthu paccantimesu janapadesu kappati. “Idaṃ kappatī”ti [V 0.0172] dīpetumpi tattheva kappati no majjhimesu. “Idaṃ na kappatī”ti dīpetuṃ pana majjhimesu janapadesu kappati no paccantimesu[P 7.1333]. Sesaṃ “anujānāmi bhikkhave pañca loṇānī”tiādi anuññātakaṃ ubhayattha kappati. Yaṃ pana akappiyanti paṭikkhittaṃ, taṃ ubhayatthāpi na kappati.

In the tetrad concerning the border regions, one who establishes a boundary in the ocean incurs an offense in the border regions, not in the central regions. One who confers ordination with a chapter of five, and one who uses multi-layered footwear, bathes constantly, and uses leather ground-coverings, incurs an offense in the central regions, not in the border regions. Regarding these four things, even one who says, 'These are not allowable here,' incurs an offense in the border regions. But one who says, 'These are allowable here,' incurs an offense in the central regions. One incurs the remaining offenses in both places; one does not incur a non-common offense anywhere. In the second tetrad, the four kinds of matters, beginning with ordination by a chapter of five, are allowable in the border regions. To explain, 'This is allowable,' is also allowable only there, not in the central regions. But to explain, 'This is not allowable,' is allowable in the central regions, not in the border regions. The remaining allowed things, such as 'I allow, bhikkhus, five kinds of salt,' are allowable in both places. But what has been rejected as unallowable is not allowable in either place.

Antoādicatukke anupakhajja seyyādiṃ anto āpajjati no bahi, ajjhokāse saṅghikamañcādīni nikkhipitvā pakkamanto bahi āpajjati no anto, sesaṃ anto ceva bahi ca, asādhāraṇaṃ neva anto na bahi. Antosīmādicatukke āgantuko vattaṃ apūrento antosīmāya āpajjati, gamiyo bahisīmāya musāvādādiṃ antosīmāya ca bahisīmāya ca āpajjati,asādhāraṇaṃ na katthaci. Gāmacatukke antaragharapaṭisaṃyuttaṃ sekhiyapaññattiṃ gāme āpajjati no araññe. Bhikkhunī aruṇaṃ uṭṭhāpayamānā araññe āpajjati no gāme. Musāvādādiṃ gāme ceva āpajjati araññe ca, asādhāraṇaṃ na katthaci.

In the tetrad beginning with 'inside', regarding the offense of encroaching on a sleeping place and so forth, one incurs it inside, not outside. One who, having placed the Sangha’s beds and so forth in the open air, then departs, incurs an offense outside, not inside. The remaining offenses are incurred both inside and outside; a non-common offense is incurred neither inside nor outside. In the tetrad beginning with 'within the boundary', a visiting bhikkhu who does not fulfill his duties incurs an offense within the boundary. A departing bhikkhu who does not fulfill his duties incurs an offense outside the boundary. Offenses like false speech and so forth are incurred both within the boundary and outside the boundary. A non-common offense is not incurred anywhere. In the tetrad concerning the village, an offense against a training rule connected with going among houses is incurred in the village, not in the forest. A bhikkhunī who lets dawn break while on a journey incurs an offense in the forest, not in the village. False speech and so forth are incurred both in the village and in the forest. A non-common offense is not incurred anywhere.

Cattāro [M 0.0172] pubbakiccāti “sammajjanī padīpo ca udakaṃ āsanena cā”ti idaṃ catubbidhaṃ pubbakaraṇanti vuccatīti vuttaṃ. “Chandapārisuddhiutukkhānaṃ bhikkhugaṇanā ca ovādo”ti ime pana “cattāro pubbakiccā”ti veditabbā. Cattāro pattakallāti uposatho yāvatikā ca bhikkhū kammappattā te āgatā honti, sabhāgāpattiyo na vijjanti, vajjanīyā ca puggalā tasmiṃ na honti, pattakallanti vuccatīti. Cattāri anaññapācittiyānīti “etadeva paccayaṃ karitvā anaññaṃ pācittiya”nti evaṃ vuttāni anupakhajjaseyyākappanasikkhāpadaṃ “ehāvuso gāmaṃ vā nigamaṃ vā”ti sikkhāpadaṃ, sañcicca kukkuccaupadahanaṃ, upassutitiṭṭhananti imāni cattāri. Catasso bhikkhusammutiyoti “ekarattampi ce bhikkhu ticīvarena vippavaseyya aññatra bhikkhusammutiyā, aññaṃ navaṃ santhataṃ kārāpeyya aññatra bhikkhusammutiyā, tato ce uttari vippavaseyya aññatra bhikkhusammutiyā, duṭṭhullaṃ āpattiṃ anupasampannassa āroceyya aññatra bhikkhusammutiyā”ti evaṃ āgatā terasahi sammutīhi muttā sammutiyo. Gilānacatukke aññabhesajjena karaṇīyena lolatāya aññaṃ viññāpento gilāno āpajjati, abhesajjakaraṇīyena bhesajjaṃ viññāpento agilāno āpajjati, musāvādādiṃ ubhopi āpajjanti, asādhāraṇaṃ ubhopi nāpajjanti. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti.

The four preliminary duties are: It is said, 'Sweeping, a lamp, water, and a seat—this fourfold thing is called the preliminary task.' However, 'Bringing the declaration of purity and the announcement of the season, the counting of the bhikkhus, and the exhortation'—these are to be known as the 'four preliminary duties.' The four factors for a timely meeting are: It is the Uposatha day, the bhikkhus eligible for the formal act have arrived, there are no common offenses, and no excludable persons are present—this is called a 'timely meeting.'
