Tasmā ayamettha yojanā – yo bhikkhu pārājikādīnaṃ vatthuṃ na jānāti, catubbidhaṃ vipattiṃ na jānāti, sattavidhaṃ āpattiṃ na jānāti, “idaṃ sikkhāpadaṃ asukasmiṃ nāma nagare paññatta”nti evaṃ nidānaṃ na jānāti, “idaṃ purimavacanaṃ idaṃ pacchimavacana”nti pubbāparaṃ na jānāti, “idaṃ kataṃ idaṃ akata”nti katākataṃ na jānāti. Samena cāti teneva pubbāparaṃ ajānanassa samena aññāṇena, “katākataṃ na jānātī”ti vuttaṃ hoti; evaṃ tāva najānāti-padena saddhiṃ sambandho veditabbo. Yaṃ panetaṃ “ākāraakovido”ti vuttaṃ, tattha ākāraakovidoti kāraṇākāraṇe akovido. Iti yvāyaṃ [V 0.0212] vatthuādīnipi na jānāti, ākārassa ca akovido, sa ve tādisako bhikkhu apaṭikkhoti vuccati.

Regarding the text: 'He does not know the basis, the failure, the offense, the origin; being unskilled in the cause, he does not know what came before and after.' Herein, the words beginning with 'basis' are connected with the word 'he does not know.' The word 'unskilled' is connected with 'such a one indeed...' Therefore, this is the construction here: A monk who does not know the basis of the offenses beginning with Pārājika; who does not know the fourfold failure; who does not know the sevenfold offense; who does not know the origin, such as 'this training rule was laid down in such-and-such a city'; who does not know what came before and after, such as 'this is the earlier statement, this is the later statement'; who does not know what was done and what was not done, such as 'this was done, this was not done.' And by 'with the same,' it is said that 'he does not know what was done and what was not done' due to that same ignorance of not knowing what came before and after. Thus, the connection with the word 'he does not know' should be understood first. As for what is stated as 'unskilled in the cause,' herein 'unskilled in the cause' means one who is unskilled in what is a cause and what is not a cause. Thus, this monk who does not know the basis and so on, and is also unskilled in the cause—such a monk is indeed called 'one who is to be rejected.'

Kammañca adhikaraṇañcāti imesampi padānaṃ “na jānātī”ti padeneva sambandho. Ayaṃ panettha yojanā – tatheva iti yvāyaṃ kammañca na jānāti, adhikaraṇañca na jānāti, sattappakāre samathe cāpi akovido, rāgādīhi pana ratto duṭṭho ca mūḷho ca, bhayena bhayā gacchati, sammohena mohā gacchati, rattattā pana duṭṭhattā ca chandā dosā ca gacchati, paraṃ saññāpetuṃ asamatthatāya na ca saññattikusalo, kāraṇākāraṇadassane asamatthatāya nijjhattiyā ca akovido attano sadisāya parisāya laddhattā laddhapakkho, hiriyā paribāhirattā ahiriko, kāḷakehi kammehi samannāgatattā kaṇhakammo, dhammādariyapuggalādariyānaṃ abhāvato anādaro, sa ve tādisako bhikkhu apaṭikkhoti vuccati, na paṭikkhitabbo na oloketabbo, na sammannitvā issariyādhipaccajeṭṭhakaṭṭhāne ṭhapetabboti attho. Sukkapakkhagāthānampi yojanānayo vuttanayeneva veditabboti.

For the words 'And action and legal issue,' the connection is also with the word 'he does not know.' This is the construction here: In the same way, this monk who does not know action, does not know legal issue, and is also unskilled in the seven kinds of settlements; who, moreover, is impassioned, corrupted, and deluded by lust and so on; who goes astray out of fear, goes astray out of delusion; who, moreover, goes astray out of desire and ill will due to being impassioned and corrupted; who, being unable to make another understand, is not skilled in persuasion; who, being unable to demonstrate what is a cause and what is not a cause, is also unskilled in clarification; who, having obtained a following similar to himself, has gained a faction; who, being external to shame, is shameless; who, being endowed with dark deeds, is of dark deeds; who, due to the absence of regard for the Dhamma and for individuals, is disrespectful—such a monk is indeed called 'one who is to be rejected.' He is not to be accepted, not to be regarded; he is not to be appointed and established in a position of authority, lordship, or seniority. This is the meaning. The method of construction for the verses of the bright faction should also be understood in the way that has been stated.

Cūḷasaṅgāmavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Cūḷasaṅgāma is concluded.

Mahāsaṅgāmo

The Mahāsaṅgāma

Voharantena jānitabbādivaṇṇanā

Commentary on what is to be known by one engaged in a legal proceeding, and so forth.

368-374. Mahāsaṅgāme [P 7.1368][M 0.0210][V 0.0213] – vatthuto vā vatthuṃ saṅkamatīti “paṭhamapārājikavatthu mayā diṭṭhaṃ vā sutaṃ vā”ti vatvā puna pucchiyamāno nighaṃsiyamāno “na mayā paṭhamapārājikassa vatthu diṭṭhaṃ, na sutaṃ; dutiyapārājikassa vatthu diṭṭhaṃ vā sutaṃ vā”ti vadati. Eteneva nayena sesavatthusaṅkamanaṃ, vipattito vipattisaṅkamanaṃ āpattito āpattisaṅkamanañca veditabbaṃ. Yo pana “neva mayā diṭṭhaṃ, na suta”nti vatvā pacchā “mayāpetaṃ diṭṭhaṃ vā sutaṃ vā”ti vadati, “diṭṭhaṃ vā sutaṃ vā”ti vatvā pacchā “na diṭṭhaṃ vā na sutaṃ vā”ti vadati, ayaṃ avajānitvā paṭijānāti, paṭijānitvā avajānātīti veditabbo. Eseva aññenaññaṃ paṭicarati nāma.

368-374. In the Mahāsaṅgāma, the decision should be understood thus. 'He shifts from one basis to another basis' means this: having said, 'I have seen or heard the basis of the first pārājika offense,' when questioned again and pressed, he says, 'I have not seen the basis of the first pārājika, nor heard it; I have seen or heard the basis of the second pārājika.' In this same way, shifting to other bases, shifting from one failure to another failure, and shifting from one offense to another offense should be understood. Furthermore, one who, having said, 'I have neither seen nor heard,' later says, 'I too have seen or heard this,' or who, having said, 'I have seen or heard,' later says, 'I have not seen nor heard,' this one should be known as 'one who acknowledges after denying, and one who denies after acknowledging.' This very one is called 'one who conceals one thing with another.'

375. Vaṇṇāvaṇṇoti nīlādivaṇṇāvaṇṇavasena sukkavissaṭṭhisikkhāpadaṃ vuttaṃ. Vacanamanuppadānanti sañcarittaṃ vuttaṃ. Kāyasaṃsaggādittayaṃ sarūpeneva vuttaṃ. Iti imāni pañca methunadhammassa pubbabhāgo pubbapayogoti veditabbāni.

375. By 'of various colors,' the training rule on the emission of semen is stated by way of various colors such as blue, etc. By 'giving a message word by word,' the training rule on acting as a go-between is stated. The triad beginning with bodily contact is stated in its own form. Thus, these five should be known as the preliminary part and the preliminary effort for the pārājika offense of sexual intercourse.

376. Cattāri apalokanakammānīti adhammenavaggādīni. Sesesupi eseva nayo. Iti cattāri catukkāni soḷasa honti.

376. 'The four formal acts of proclamation' are those acts beginning with 'unrighteously, as a faction.' The same method applies to the remaining cases as well. Thus, the four tetrads become sixteen acts.

Agatiagantabbavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Improper Courses Not to Be Gone To

379. Bahujanaahitāya [V 0.0214] paṭipanno hotīti vinayadharena hi evaṃ chandādigatiyā adhikaraṇe vinicchite tasmiṃ vihāre saṅgho dvidhā bhijjati. Ovādūpajīviniyo bhikkhuniyopi dve bhāgā honti. Upāsakāpi upāsikāyopi dārakāpi dārikāyopi tesaṃ ārakkhadevatāpi tatheva dvidhā bhijjanti. Tato bhummadevatā ādiṃ katvā yāva akaniṭṭhabrahmāno dvidhāva honti. Tena vuttaṃ – “bahujanaahitāya paṭipanno hoti…pe… dukkhāya devamanussāna”nti.

379. Regarding 'He is practicing for the harm of many people': when a legal issue is decided by a Vinaya expert in such a manner, following the improper courses of desire and so forth, the Saṅgha in that monastery splits into two. The nuns who live by their instruction also become two groups. The male and female lay followers, the boys and girls, and even their guardian deities likewise split into two. Because of that, beginning with the earth-deities up to the Akaniṭṭha Brahmās, they also become divided into two. Therefore it was said: 'He is practicing for the harm of many people... for the suffering of devas and humans.'

382. Visamanissitoti [M 0.0211] visamāni kāyakammādīni nissito. Gahananissitoti micchādiṭṭhiantaggāhikadiṭṭhisaṅkhātaṃ gahanaṃ nissito. Balavanissitoti balavante abhiññāte bhikkhū nissito.

382. 'One who relies on the uneven' means: one who relies on uneven bodily actions and so forth. 'One who relies on the thicket' means: one who relies on the thicket called wrong view and extremist view. 'One who relies on the powerful' means: one who relies on powerful, renowned monks.

393. Tassa avajānantoti tassa vacanaṃ avajānanto. Upayogatthe vā sāmivacanaṃ, taṃ avajānantoti attho.

393. 'Disdaining him' means disdaining his words. Or, the genitive word is used in the sense of the accusative; the meaning is 'disdaining him.'

394. Yaṃ atthāyāti yadatthāya. Taṃ atthanti so attho. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti.

394. 'Yaṃ atthāya' means 'for which purpose.' 'Taṃ atthaṃ' means 'that purpose.' The rest is clear everywhere.

Mahāsaṅgāmavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Mahāsaṅgāma is concluded.

Kathinabhedaṃ

The Dissolution of the Kathina

Kathinaatthatādivaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Spreading of the Kathina and so forth

403. Kathine [P 7.1369][M 0.0212][V 0.0215] – aṭṭha mātikāti khandhake vuttā pakkamanantikādikā aṭṭha. Palibodhānisaṃsāpi pubbe vuttā eva.

403. Regarding the Kathina: the eight matrices are the eight, beginning with the pakkamanantikā, that were stated in the Khandhaka. The impediments and benefits have also been stated previously.

404. Payogassāti cīvaradhovanādino sattavidhassa pubbakaraṇassatthāya yo udakāharaṇādiko payogo kayirati, tassa payogassa. Katame dhammā anantarapaccayena paccayoti anāgatavasena anantarā hutvā katame dhammā paccayā hontīti attho. Samanantarapaccayenāti suṭṭhu anantarapaccayena, anantarapaccayameva āsannataraṃ katvā pucchati. Nissayapaccayenāti uppajjamānassa payogassa nissayaṃ ādhārabhāvaṃ upagatā viya hutvā katame dhammā paccayā hontīti attho. Upanissayapaccayenāti upetena nissayapaccayena; nissayapaccayameva upagatataraṃ katvā pucchati. Purejātapaccayenāti iminā paṭhamaṃ uppannassa paccayabhāvaṃ pucchati. Pacchājātapaccayenāti iminā pacchā uppajjanakassa paccayabhāvaṃ pucchati. Sahajātapaccayenāti iminā apubbaṃ acarimaṃ uppajjamānānaṃ paccayabhāvaṃ pucchati. Pubbakaraṇassāti dhovanādino pubbakaraṇassa. Paccuddhārassāti purāṇasaṅghāṭiādīnaṃ paccuddharaṇassa. Adhiṭṭhānassāti kathinacīvarādhiṭṭhānassa. Atthārassāti kathinatthārassa. Mātikānañca palibodhānañcāti aṭṭhannaṃ mātikānaṃ dvinnañca palibodhānaṃ. Vatthussāti saṅghāṭiādino kathinavatthussa; sesaṃ vuttanayameva.

404. As for payogassa, it means: for that effort, such as the fetching of water, which is undertaken for the sake of the sevenfold preliminary work, such as the washing of the robe-cloth. 'Which phenomena are conditions by way of the proximity condition?'—the meaning is: which phenomena, having become proximate in terms of the future, are conditions? 'By way of the immediate contiguity condition' means: thoroughly by the proximity condition; it asks by making the proximity condition even closer. 'By way of the dependence condition'—the meaning is: which phenomena are conditions by being, as it were, a foundation, having approached the state of being a support for the arising effort? 'By way of the decisive support condition' means: with an approached dependence condition; it asks by making the dependence condition even more approached. By 'by way of the prenascent condition,' it asks about the conditionality of what has arisen first. By 'by way of the postnascent condition,' it asks about the conditionality of what is to arise later. By 'by way of the conascent condition,' it asks about the conditionality of things arising neither before nor after. As for pubbakaraṇassa, it means: for the preliminary work of washing and so on. As for paccuddhārassa, it means: for the removal of the old outer robe and so on. As for adhiṭṭhānassa, it means: for the determination of the Kathina robe. As for atthārassa, it means: for the spreading of the Kathina. As for mātikānañca palibodhānañca, it means: of the eight matrices and of the two impediments. As for vatthussa, it means: of the Kathina material, such as the outer robe; the rest is in the same way as has been stated.

Evaṃ yañca labbhati yañca na labbhati, sabbaṃ pucchitvā idāni yaṃ yassa labbhati, tadeva dassento pubbakaraṇaṃ payogassātiādinā nayena vissajjanamāha. Tassattho – yaṃ vuttaṃ “payogassa [V 0.0216] katame dhammā”tiādi, tattha vuccate, pubbakaraṇaṃ payogassa anantarapaccayena paccayo, samanantaranissayaupanissayapaccayena paccayo. Payogassa hi sattavidhampi pubbakaraṇaṃ yasmā tena payogena nipphādetabbassa pubbakaraṇassatthāya so payogo kayirati, tasmā imehi catūhi paccayehi paccayo hoti. Purejātapaccaye panesa uddiṭṭhadhammesu ekadhammampi na [M 0.0213] labhati, aññadatthu pubbakaraṇassa sayaṃ purejātapaccayo hoti, payoge sati pubbakaraṇassa nipphajjanato[P 7.1370]. Tena vuttaṃ – “payogo pubbakaraṇassa purejātapaccayena paccayo”ti. Pacchājātapaccayaṃ pana labhati, tena vuttaṃ – “pubbakaraṇaṃ payogassa pacchājātapaccayena paccayo”ti. Pacchā uppajjanakassa hi pubbakaraṇassa atthāya so payogo kayirati. Sahajātapaccayaṃ pana mātikāpalibodhānisaṃsasaṅkhāte pannarasa dhamme ṭhapetvā añño payogādīsu ekopi dhammo na labhati, te eva hi pannarasa dhammā saha kathinatthārena ekato nipphajjantīti aññamaññaṃ sahajātapaccayā honti. Tena vuttaṃ – “pannarasa dhammā sahajātapaccayena paccayo”ti. Etenupāyena sabbapadavissajjanāni veditabbāni.

Thus, having asked about everything—both what is obtainable and what is not—the venerable Upāli now, showing just that which is obtainable for which, stated the answer by the method beginning, 'The preliminary work is for the effort...' and so on. The meaning of that is: regarding what was said, 'Which phenomena are for the effort?' and so on, the answer is stated there. The preliminary work is a condition for the effort by way of the proximity condition; it is a condition by way of the immediate contiguity, dependence, and decisive support conditions. Indeed, regarding the effort, since that effort is undertaken for the sake of the sevenfold preliminary work which is to be accomplished by that very effort, it is therefore a condition by way of these four conditions. But in the case of the prenascent condition, this effort does not obtain even one phenomenon among the phenomena pointed out. On the contrary, it is itself a prenascent condition for the preliminary work, because the preliminary work is accomplished only when the effort exists. Therefore it was said by the venerable elder Upāli: 'The effort is a condition for the preliminary work by way of the prenascent condition.' But it does obtain the postnascent condition. Therefore it was said: 'The preliminary work is a condition for the effort by way of the postnascent condition.' For that effort is undertaken for the sake of the preliminary work that is to arise later. As for the conascent condition, setting aside the fifteen phenomena reckoned as the matrices, impediments, and benefits, no other single phenomenon among the effort, etc., obtains it. Indeed, those very fifteen phenomena arise together at the same time with the spreading of the Kathina, and thus they are conditions for one another by way of conascence. Therefore it was said: 'The fifteen phenomena are a condition by way of the conascent condition.' By this method, all the explanations of the terms should be understood.

Pubbakaraṇanidānādivibhāgavaṇṇanā

The Commentary on the Division of Preliminary Actions, their Origins, and so forth.

405. Pubbakaraṇaṃ kiṃnidānantiādipucchāvissajjanaṃ uttānameva.

405. The answer to the question, 'What is the origin of the preliminary action?' and so forth, is self-evident.

406-7. “Payogo kiṃnidāno”tiādīsu pucchādvayavissajjanesu hetunidāno paccayanidānoti ettha cha cīvarāni hetu ceva paccayo cāti veditabbāni. Pubbapayogādīnañhi sabbesaṃ tāniyeva hetu, tāni paccayo. Na hi chabbidhe cīvare asati payogo atthi, na pubbakaraṇādīni, tasmā “payogo hetunidāno”tiādi vuttaṃ.

In the answers to the pair of questions, such as 'What is the origin of the application?' and so forth, in the text 'cause-originated' and 'condition-originated,' here the six robes should be understood as both cause and condition. For indeed, of all preliminary applications and the like, these very robes are the cause, and they are the condition. Because when the six kinds of robes are absent, there is no application, and there are no preliminary actions and the like. Therefore, it was said, 'The application is cause-originated,' and so forth.

408. Saṅgahavāre – vacībhedenāti “imāya saṅghāṭiyā, iminā uttarāsaṅgena, iminā antaravāsakena kathinaṃ attharāmī”ti etena vacībhedena. Katimūlādipucchāvissajjane – kiriyā majjheti paccuddhāro ceva adhiṭṭhānañca.

408. In the section on inclusion—'by this distinction of speech' means: by this distinction of speech, namely, 'With this double-layered robe, with this upper robe, with this inner robe, I spread the Kathina.' In the answer to the questions beginning with 'How many roots?'—'the action in the middle' means: the action in the middle is both the revocation and the determination.

411. Vatthuvipannaṃ hotīti akappiyadussaṃ hoti. Kālavipannaṃ nāma ajja dāyakehi dinnaṃ sve saṅgho kathinatthārakassa deti. Karaṇavipannaṃ nāma tadaheva chinditvā akataṃ.

411. 'It is defective in regard to the material' means: it is unsuitable cloth. 'Defective in regard to time' means: what is given by donors today, the Saṅgha gives tomorrow to the one who is to spread the Kathina. 'Defective in regard to making' means: having been cut on that very day, it is not made.

Kathinādijānitabbavibhāgavaṇṇanā

The Commentary on the Division of What Should Be Known Regarding the Kathina, and so forth.

412. Kathinaṃ [M 0.0214][V 0.0217] jānitabbantiādipucchāya vissajjane – tesaññeva dhammānanti yesu rūpādidhammesu sati kathinaṃ nāma hoti, tesaṃ samodhānaṃ missībhāvo. Nāmaṃ nāmakammantiādinā pana “kathina”nti idaṃ bahūsu dhammesu nāmamattaṃ, na paramatthato eko dhammo atthīti dasseti.

412. In the answer to the question beginning, 'How is the Kathina to be known?'—'of those very phenomena' means: the combination, the state of being mixed, of those very phenomena, such as form and so forth, in the presence of which the name 'Kathina' comes to be. But by the phrase beginning, 'Name, the act of naming,' it shows that this word 'Kathina' is merely a designation among many phenomena, and that in an ultimate sense, there is not one single phenomenon.

Catuvīsatiyā ākārehīti “na ullikhitamattenā”tiādīhi pubbe vuttakāraṇehi. Sattarasahi ākārehīti “ahatena atthataṃ hoti kathina”ntiādīhi pubbe vuttakāraṇehi. Nimittakammādīsu yaṃ vattabbaṃ sabbaṃ kathinakkhandhakavaṇṇanāyaṃ vuttaṃ.

'By twenty-four modes' means: by the twenty-four reasons previously stated, beginning with 'not merely by scratching.' 'By seventeen modes' means: by the seventeen reasons previously stated, beginning with 'the Kathina is spread with new cloth.' Whatever should be said regarding signs, actions, and so forth, all of that has been stated by me in the Commentary on the Kathina Section.

416. Ekuppādā ekanirodhāti uppajjamānāpi ekato uppajjanti, nirujjhamānāpi ekato nirujjhanti. Ekuppādā nānānirodhāti uppajjamānā ekato uppajjanti, nirujjhamānā nānā nirujjhanti. Kiṃ vuttaṃ hoti[P 7.1371]? Sabbepi atthārena saddhiṃ ekato uppajjanti, atthāre hi sati uddhāro nāma. Nirujjhamānā panettha purimā dve atthārena saddhiṃ ekato nirujjhanti, uddhārabhāvaṃ pāpuṇanti. Atthārassa hi nirodho etesañca uddhārabhāvo ekakkhaṇe hoti, itare nānā nirujjhanti. Tesu uddhārabhāvaṃ pattesupi atthāro tiṭṭhatiyeva. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti.

416. `Of single arising, of single cessation` means: when arising, they arise together; when ceasing, they cease together. `Of single arising, of various cessations` means: when arising, they arise together; when ceasing, they cease separately. What is meant by this? All removals arise together with the spreading, for indeed, when the spreading exists, there is what is called a removal. But when ceasing, among these, the first two cease together with the spreading; they reach the state of having been removed. Indeed, the cessation of the spreading and their state of having been removed occur in a single moment. The others cease separately. Even when those have reached the state of having been removed, the spreading still remains. The rest is self-evident everywhere.

Samantapāsādikāya vinayasaṃvaṇṇanāya

In the Samantapāsādikā, the Commentary on the Vinaya,

Kathinabhedavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Divisions of the Kathina is finished.

Paññattivaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Paññatti Section is finished.

Upālipañcakaṃ

The Upāli Pentad

Anissitavaggavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Chapter on the Unattached

417. Upālipañhesu [M 0.0215][V 0.0218] katihi nu kho bhanteti pucchāya ayaṃ sambandho. Thero kira rahogato sabbāni imāni pañcakāni āvajjetvā “bhagavantaṃ dāni pucchitvā imesaṃ nissāya vasanakādīnaṃ atthāya tantiṃ ṭhapessāmī”ti bhagavantaṃ upasaṅkamitvā “katihi nu kho bhante”tiādinā nayena pañhe pucchi. Tesaṃ vissajjane uposathaṃ na jānātīti navavidhaṃ uposathaṃ na jānāti. Uposathakammaṃ na jānātīti adhammenavaggādibhedaṃ catubbidhaṃ uposathakammaṃ na jānāti. Pātimokkhaṃ na jānātīti dve mātikā na jānāti. Pātimokkhuddesaṃ na jānātīti bhikkhūnaṃ pañcavidhaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ catubbidhanti navavidhaṃ pātimokkhuddesaṃ na jānāti.

417. In the Upāli Pentads, this is the connection to the question beginning, 'Venerable sir, by how many...?' It is said that the Elder, having gone to a secluded place and reflected upon all these pentads, thought, 'Having now asked the Blessed One, I will establish the textual lineage for the benefit of those who dwell in dependence on these, and so on.' Having approached the Blessed One, he asked the questions by the method beginning, 'Venerable sir, by how many...?' In the answer to those questions, 'does not know the Uposatha' means one does not know the ninefold Uposatha. 'Does not know the Uposatha act' means one does not know the fourfold Uposatha act, which is differentiated as that done unlawfully by an incomplete group, and so on. 'Does not know the Pātimokkha' means one does not know the two matrices. 'Does not know the recitation of the Pātimokkha' means one does not know the ninefold recitation of the Pātimokkha: the fivefold for bhikkhus and the fourfold for bhikkhunīs.

Pavāraṇaṃ na jānātīti navavidhaṃ pavāraṇaṃ na jānāti. Pavāraṇākammaṃ na jānātīti adhammenavaggādibhedaṃ catubbidhaṃ pavāraṇākammaṃ na jānāti.

'Does not know the Pavāraṇā' means one does not know the ninefold Pavāraṇā. 'Does not know the Pavāraṇā act' means one does not know the fourfold Pavāraṇā act, which is differentiated as that done unlawfully by an incomplete group, and so on.

Āpattānāpattiṃ na jānātīti tasmiṃ tasmiṃ sikkhāpade niddiṭṭhaṃ āpattiñca anāpattiñca na jānāti.

'Does not know offense and non-offense' means one does not know the offense and the non-offense that is pointed out in each and every training rule.

Āpanno kammakatoti āpattiṃ āpanno tappaccayāva saṅghena kammaṃ kataṃ hoti.

'One who has fallen [into an offense] and has had an act performed [against him]' means that having fallen into an offense, an act has been performed against him by the Sangha precisely on account of that.

Nappaṭippassambhanavaggavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Chapter on Not Revoking

420. Kammaṃ [V 0.0219] nappaṭippassambhetabbanti ayaṃ yasmā anulomavatte na vattati, tasmā nāssa kammaṃ paṭippassambhetabbaṃ; sarajjukova vissajjetabboti attho.

420. 'The act should not be revoked' means: because this monk does not conduct himself in accordance with the proper conduct, therefore the act against him should not be revoked. The meaning is that he should be released only while still with the rope.

421. Sace upāli saṅgho samaggakaraṇīyāni kammāni karotīti sace samaggehi karaṇīyāni uposathādīni kammāni karoti, uposathapavāraṇādīsu hi ṭhitāsu upatthambho na dātabbo. Sace hi saṅgho accayaṃ desāpetvā saṅghasāmaggiṃ karoti, tiṇavatthārakasamathaṃ vā katvā uposathapavāraṇaṃ karoti[P 7.1372], evarūpaṃ samaggakaraṇīyaṃ nāma kammaṃ hoti. Tatra ceti sace tādise kamme bhikkhuno nakkhamati, diṭṭhāvikammampi [M 0.0216] katvā tathārūpā sāmaggī upetabbā, evaṃ vilomaggāho na gaṇhitabbo. Yatra pana uddhammaṃ ubbinayaṃ satthu sāsanaṃ dīpenti, tattha diṭṭhāvikammaṃ na vaṭṭati, paṭibāhitvā pakkamitabbaṃ.

421. 'If, Upāli, the Sangha performs acts that should be done in concord' means: if it performs acts such as the Uposatha that should be done by those in concord, for when the Uposatha, Pavāraṇā, and so on are established, support [for schism] should not be given. For if the Sangha, having caused the transgression to be confessed, creates Sangha concord, or having performed the settlement by covering with grass, performs the Uposatha and Pavāraṇā, such an act is called 'one that should be done in concord.' And in that case, if in such an act it is not agreeable to a bhikkhu, even having performed the act of making one's view known, such concord should be approached; thus, a contrary stance should not be taken. But where they explain the Teacher's Dispensation as being contrary to the Dhamma and contrary to the Vinaya, there it is not proper to perform the act of making one's view known; having obstructed [the act], one should depart.

Ussitamantī cāti lobhadosamohamānussannaṃ vācaṃ bhāsitā kaṇhavāco anatthakadīpano. Nissitajappīti attano dhammatāya ussadayuttaṃ bhāsituṃ na sakkoti; atha kho “mayā saddhiṃ rājā evaṃ kathesi, asukamahāmatto evaṃ kathesi, asuko nāma mayhaṃ ācariyo vā upajjhāyo vā tepiṭako mayā saddhiṃ evaṃ kathesī”ti evaṃ aññaṃ nissāya jappati. Na ca bhāsānusandhikusaloti kathānusandhivacane ca vinicchayānusandhivacane ca akusalo hoti. Na yathādhamme yathāvinayeti na bhūtena vatthunā āpattiṃ sāretvā codetā hoti.

'And one who has an uplifted thought' means one who speaks words abounding in greed, hatred, delusion, and conceit; one of dark speech; one who explains what is not beneficial. 'One who mutters dependently' means that by his own nature, he is not able to speak words connected with arrogance; but rather, he mutters depending on another, saying, 'The king spoke with me thus; such-and-such a great minister spoke with me thus; such-and-such a great elder, a master of the Tipiṭaka, who is my teacher or preceptor, spoke with me thus.' 'And not skilled in the connection of speech' means he is unskilled in speech that connects a discussion and in speech that connects a legal decision. 'Not according to the Dhamma, according to the Vinaya' means he is not one who accuses by reminding one of an offense with a factual basis.

Ussādetā hotīti “amhākaṃ ācariyo mahātepiṭako paramadhammakathiko”tiādinā nayena ekaccaṃ ussādeti. Dutiyapade “āpattiṃ kiṃ so na jānātī”tiādinā ekaccaṃ apasādeti. Adhammaṃ gaṇhātīti aniyyānikapakkhaṃ gaṇhāti. Dhammaṃ paṭibāhatīti niyyānikapakkhaṃ paṭibāhati. Samphañca bahuṃ bhāsatīti bahuṃ niratthakakathaṃ katheti.

'Is one who raises up' means he raises up a certain person by the method beginning, 'Our teacher is a great master of the Tipiṭaka, a supreme Dhamma speaker.' In the second word, he puts down a certain person by the method beginning, 'What, does that person not know the offense?' 'Grasps the non-Dhamma' means he grasps the side that is not emancipating. 'Obstructs the Dhamma' means he obstructs the side that is emancipating. 'And speaks much pointless chatter' means he speaks much useless talk.

Pasayha pavattā hotīti anajjhiṭṭho bhāre anāropite kevalaṃ mānaṃ nissāya ajjhottharitvā anadhikāre kathetā hoti. Anokāsakammaṃ kāretvāti okāsakammaṃ akāretvā pavattā hoti. Na yathādiṭṭhiyā byākatā hotīti yassa attano diṭṭhi taṃ purakkhatvā na byākatā[V 0.0220]; laddhiṃ nikkhipitvā ayathābhuccaṃ adhammādīsu dhammādiladdhiko hutvā kathetā hotīti attho.

'Is one who proceeds forcefully' means that, not having been requested, the burden not having been placed upon him, relying only on conceit, having dominated, he is one who speaks in a matter that is not his authority. 'Having caused an act without permission to be done' means he proceeds without having caused the act of asking for permission to be done. 'Is not one who explains according to his view' means he does not explain by putting forward that which is his own view; having set aside his tenet, not in accordance with reality, he becomes one who holds the tenet that non-Dhamma, etc., is Dhamma, etc., and so he speaks. This is the meaning.

Vohāravaggavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Chapter on Legal Procedures

424. Āpattiyā payogaṃ na jānātīti “ayaṃ āpatti kāyappayogā, ayaṃ vacīpayogā”ti na jānāti. Āpattiyā vūpasamaṃ na jānātīti “ayaṃ āpatti desanāya vūpasamati, ayaṃ vuṭṭhānena, ayaṃ neva desanāya na vuṭṭhānenā”ti na jānāti. Āpattiyā na vinicchayakusalo [M 0.0217] hotīti “imasmiṃ vatthusmiṃ ayaṃ āpattī”ti na jānāti, dosānurūpaṃ āpattiṃ uddharitvā patiṭṭhāpetuṃ [P 7.1373] na sakkoti.

424. 'Does not know the effort for an offense' means he does not know: 'This offense has bodily effort; this has verbal effort.' 'Does not know the settlement of an offense' means he does not know: 'This offense is settled by confession; this by rehabilitation; this is settled neither by confession nor by rehabilitation.' 'Is not skilled in the determination of an offense' means he does not know: 'In this case, this is the offense,' and he is unable to point out and establish the offense that is appropriate to the fault.

Adhikaraṇasamuṭṭhānaṃ na jānātīti “idaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ aṭṭhārasa bhedakaravatthūni nissāya samuṭṭhāti, idaṃ catasso vipattiyo, idaṃ pañca vā satta vā āpattikkhandhe, idaṃ cattāri saṅghakiccāni nissāya samuṭṭhātī”ti na jānāti. Payogaṃ na jānātīti “idaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ dvādasamūlappayogaṃ, idaṃ cuddasamūlappayogaṃ, idaṃ chamūlapayogaṃ, idaṃ ekamūlapayoga”nti na jānāti. Adhikaraṇānañhi yathāsakaṃmūlameva payogā nāma honti, taṃ sabbampi na jānātīti attho. Vūpasamaṃ na jānātīti “idaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ dvīhi samathehi vūpasamati, idaṃ tīhi, idaṃ catūhi, idaṃ ekena samathena vūpasamatī”ti na jānāti. Na vinicchayakusalo hotīti adhikaraṇaṃ vinicchinitvā samathaṃ pāpetuṃ na jānāti.

He does not know the origination of a legal issue, that is, he does not know: 'This legal issue arises dependent on the eighteen grounds for causing schism; this on the four failures; this on the five or seven groups of offenses; this on the four Sangha acts.' He does not know the procedure, that is, he does not know: 'This legal issue has a twelve-rooted procedure; this has a fourteen-rooted procedure; this has a six-rooted procedure; this has a one-rooted procedure.' Indeed, for legal issues, their respective roots themselves are called 'procedures'; he does not know all of that—this is the meaning. He does not know the settlement, that is, he does not know: 'This legal issue is settled by two methods of settlement; this by three; this by four; this by one method of settlement.' He is not skilled in determination, that is, he does not know how to determine a legal issue and bring it to settlement.

Kammaṃ na jānātīti tajjanīyādi sattavidhaṃ kammaṃ na jānāti. Kammassa karaṇaṃ na jānātīti “idaṃ kammaṃ iminā nīhārena kātabba”nti na jānāti. Kammassa vatthuṃ na jānātīti “idaṃ tajjanīyassa vatthu, idaṃ niyassādīna”nti na jānāti. Vattanti sattasu kammesu heṭṭhā catunnaṃ kammānaṃ aṭṭhārasavidhaṃ tividhassa ca ukkhepanīyakammassa tecattālīsavidhaṃ vattaṃ na jānāti. Kammassa vūpasamaṃ na jānātīti “yo bhikkhu vatte vattitvā yācati, tassa kammaṃ paṭippassambhetabbaṃ, accayo desāpetabbo”ti na jānāti.

He does not know the Sangha act, that is, he does not know the seven kinds of acts beginning with the act of censure. He does not know the performance of the act, that is, he does not know: 'This act should be performed in this manner.' He does not know the ground for the act, that is, he does not know: 'This is the ground for the act of censure; this for the acts of subordination and so forth.' He does not know the duties, that is, among the seven acts, he does not know the eighteen kinds of duties for the lower four acts, and the forty-three kinds of duties for the three kinds of acts of suspension. He does not know the settlement of the act, that is, he does not know: 'For a bhikkhu who, having fulfilled the duties, requests it, the act should be revoked, and he should be made to confess his offense.'

Vatthuṃ na jānātīti sattannaṃ āpattikkhandhānaṃ vatthuṃ na jānāti. Nidānaṃ na jānātīti “idaṃ [V 0.0221] sikkhāpadaṃ imasmiṃ nagare paññattaṃ, idaṃ imasmi”nti na jānāti. Paññattiṃ na jānātīti paññattianupaññattianuppannapaññattivasena tividhaṃ paññattiṃ na jānāti. Padapaccābhaṭṭhaṃ na jānātīti sammukhā kātabbaṃ padaṃ na jānāti. “Buddho bhagavā”ti vattabbe “bhagavā buddho”ti heṭṭhupariyaṃ katvā padaṃ yojeti.

He does not know the ground, that is, he does not know the ground for the seven groups of offenses. He does not know the origin, that is, he does not know: 'This training rule was laid down in this town; this one in that town.' He does not know the formulation, that is, he does not know the threefold formulation by way of original formulation, supplementary formulation, and formulation for an unarisen matter. He does not know the reversed wording, that is, he does not know the correct sequence of words. When 'The Buddha, the Blessed One' should be said, he reverses the order and says, 'The Blessed One, the Buddha.'

Akusalo ca hoti vinayeti vinayapāḷiyañca aṭṭhakathāyañca akusalo hoti.

And he is unskilled in the Vinaya, that is, he is unskilled in both the Vinaya canon and its commentary.

Ñattiṃ na jānātīti saṅkhepato hi duvidhā ñatti – “esā ñattī”ti evaṃ niddiṭṭhā ca aniddiṭṭhā ca. Tattha yā evaṃ aniddiṭṭhā, sā “kammañatti” nāma hoti. Yā niddiṭṭhā, sā “kammapādañatti” nāma, taṃ sabbena sabbaṃ ñattiṃ na jānāti. Ñattiyā [M 0.0218] karaṇaṃ na jānātīti navasu ṭhānesu kammañattiyā karaṇaṃ na jānāti, dvīsu ṭhānesu [P 7.1374] kammapādañattiyā. Ñattiyā anussāvananti “imissā ñattiyā ekā anussāvanā, imissā tisso”ti na jānāti. Ñattiyā samathaṃ na jānātīti yvāyaṃ sativinayo, amūḷhavinayo, tassapāpiyasikā, tiṇavatthārakoti catubbidho samatho ñattiyā vinā na hoti, taṃ ñattiyā samathoti na jānāti. Ñattiyā vūpasamaṃ na jānātīti yaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ iminā catubbidhena ñattisamathena vūpasamati, tassa taṃ vūpasamaṃ “ayaṃ ñattiyā vūpasamo kato”ti na jānāti.

He does not know the motion: In brief, a motion is twofold: that which is indicated thus, 'This is the motion,' and that which is not so indicated. Therein, that which is not so indicated is called 'motion-as-act.' That which is indicated is called 'motion as the basis for an act.' He does not know that motion in its entirety. He does not know the performance of the motion: he does not know the performance of the motion-as-act in nine instances, nor that of the motion as the basis for an act in two instances. The announcement for the motion: he does not know, 'For this motion there is one announcement; for this one there are three.' He does not know the settlement by motion: he does not know that this fourfold settlement—verdict of mindfulness, verdict of innocence, verdict for past insanity, and covering over as with grass—does not occur without a motion, and he does not know this as 'settlement by motion.' He does not know the pacification by motion: when a legal issue is pacified by this fourfold settlement-by-motion, he does not know of that pacification, 'This pacification was done by a motion.'

Suttaṃ na jānātīti ubhatovibhaṅgaṃ na jānāti. Suttānulomaṃ na jānātīti cattāro mahāpadese na jānāti. Vinayaṃ na jānātīti khandhakaparivāraṃ na jānāti. Vinayānulomaṃ na jānātīti cattāro mahāpadeseyeva na jānāti. Na ca ṭhānāṭhānakusaloti kāraṇākāraṇakusalo na hoti.

He does not know the Sutta, that is, he does not know the Ubhatovibhaṅga. He does not know what is in conformity with the Sutta, that is, he does not know the four great references. He does not know the Vinaya, that is, he does not know the Khandhaka and the Parivāra. He does not know what is in conformity with the Vinaya, that is, he does not know the very same four great references. And he is not skilled in what is possible and impossible, that is, he is not skilled in cause and non-cause.

Dhammaṃ na jānātīti ṭhapetvā vinayapiṭakaṃ avasesaṃ piṭakadvayaṃ na jānāti. Dhammānulomaṃ na jānātīti suttantike cattāro mahāpadese na jānāti. Vinayaṃ na jānātīti khandhakaparivārameva na jānāti. Vinayānulomaṃ na jānātīti cattāro mahāpadese na jānāti. Ubhatovibhaṅgā panettha asaṅgahitā honti, tasmāyaṃ kurundiyaṃ vuttaṃ – “vinayanti sakalaṃ vinayapiṭakaṃ na jānātī”ti taṃ na gahetabbaṃ. Na ca pubbāparakusalo hotīti purekathāya ca pacchākathāya [V 0.0222] ca akusalo hoti. Sesaṃ sabbattha vuttapaṭipakkhavasena ñeyyattā pubbe pakāsitattā ca uttānamevāti.

He does not know the Dhamma, that is, setting aside the Vinaya Piṭaka, he does not know the remaining two Piṭakas. He does not know what is in conformity with the Dhamma, that is, he does not know the four great references of the Suttanta. He does not know the Vinaya, that is, he does not know just the Khandhaka and the Parivāra. He does not know what is in conformity with the Vinaya, that is, he does not know the four great references. Herein, the two Vibhaṅgas are not included; therefore, what is said in the Kurundī commentary—'By "he does not know the Vinaya" is meant he does not know the entire Vinaya Piṭaka'—should not be accepted. And he is not skilled in what is prior and posterior, that is, he is unskilled in preliminary talk and subsequent talk. The rest, in all cases, is clear, since it is to be understood by way of the opposite of what has been said and because it has been explained before.

Anissitavagganappaṭippassambhanavaggavohāravaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Anissita Chapter, the Nappaṭippassambhana Chapter, and the Vohāra Chapter is concluded.

Diṭṭhāvikammavaggavaṇṇanā

The Commentary on the Diṭṭhāvikamma Chapter

425. Diṭṭhāvikammavagge – diṭṭhāvikammāti diṭṭhīnaṃ āvikammāni; laddhippakāsanāni āpattidesanāsaṅkhātānaṃ vinayakammānametaṃ adhivacanaṃ. Anāpattiyā diṭṭhiṃ āvi karotīti anāpattimeva āpattīti desetīti attho[M 0.0219]. Adesanāgāminiyāti garukāpattiyā diṭṭhiṃ āvikaroti; saṅghādisesañca pārājikañca desetīti attho. Desitāyāti lahukāpattiyāpi desitāya diṭṭhiṃ āvikaroti; desitaṃ puna desetīti [P 7.1375] attho.

425. In the Diṭṭhāvikamma Chapter—the term 'diṭṭhāvikamma' means the acts of making views manifest, the acts of revealing a tenet; this is a designation for the Vinaya procedures called the confession of an offense. 'One makes a view manifest regarding a non-offense' means one confesses a non-offense itself as an offense. 'Regarding that which is not subject to confession' means one makes a view manifest regarding a grave offense; meaning one confesses a Saṅghādisesa offense and a Pārājika offense. 'Regarding a confessed [offense]' means one makes a view manifest even regarding a minor offense that has been confessed; meaning one confesses again what has already been confessed.

Catūhi pañcahi diṭṭhinti yathā catūhi pañcahi diṭṭhi āvikatā hoti, evaṃ āvikaroti; cattāro pañca janā ekato āpattiṃ desentīti attho. Manomānasenāti manasaṅkhātena mānasena diṭṭhiṃ āvikaroti; vacībhedaṃ akatvā citteneva āpattiṃ desetīti attho.

'A view by four or five' means: in such a way that a view is made manifest by four or five, so one makes it manifest; meaning four or five persons confess an offense together. 'By mind, by the mental' means: one makes a view manifest with the 'mānasa' which is called mind; meaning, without making an utterance, one confesses an offense with the mind alone.

Nānāsaṃvāsakassāti laddhinānāsaṃvāsakassa vā kammanānāsaṃvāsakassa vā santike diṭṭhiṃ āvikaroti; āpattiṃ desetīti attho. Nānāsīmāyāti samānasaṃvāsakassāpi nānāsīmāya ṭhitassa santike āvikaroti. Māḷakasīmāya hi ṭhitena sīmantarikāya ṭhitassa sīmantarikāya vā ṭhitena avippavāsasīmāya ṭhitassāpi āpattiṃ desetuṃ na vaṭṭati. Apakatattassāti ukkhittakassa vā, yassa vā uposathapavāraṇā ṭhapitā honti, tassa santike desetīti attho.

'To one of different communion' means: one makes a view manifest in the presence of one of different communion due to tenet or one of different communion due to a formal act; meaning one confesses an offense. 'In a different boundary' means: one makes it manifest in the presence of one who, although of the same communion, is standing in a different boundary. Indeed, for one standing in the Māḷaka-sīmā, it is not proper to confess an offense to one standing in the sīmantarikā; or for one standing in the sīmantarikā to one standing in the avippavāsa-sīmā. 'To one not in good standing' means: one confesses in the presence of a suspended monk, or of one for whom the Uposatha and Pavāraṇā have been set aside; this is the meaning.

430. Nālaṃ okāsakammaṃ kātunti na pariyattaṃ kātuṃ; na kātabbanti attho. Idhāpi apakatatto ukkhittako ca ṭhapitauposathapavāraṇo ca. Cāvanādhippāyoti sāsanato cāvetukāmo.

430. 'Not able to perform the okāsakamma' means: not competent to perform it; meaning it should not be done. Here also, 'one not in good standing' is a suspended monk and one for whom the Uposatha and Pavāraṇā have been set aside. 'With the intention of causing to fall' means: desiring to make one fall away from the Dispensation.

432. Mandattā [V 0.0223] momūhattāti mandabhāvena momūhabhāvena vissajjitampi jānituṃ asamattho, kevalaṃ attano momūhabhāvaṃ pakāsentoyeva pucchati ummattako viya. Pāpicchoti “evaṃ maṃ jano sambhāvessatī”ti pāpikāya icchāya pucchati. Paribhavāti paribhavaṃ āropetukāmo hutvā pucchati. Aññabyākaraṇesupi eseva nayo. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti. Attādānavagge ca dhutaṅgavagge ca yaṃ vattabbaṃ siyā, taṃ sabbaṃ heṭṭhā vuttameva.

432. 'Due to dullness, due to utter delusion' means: being unable to understand even what has been explained due to a state of dullness and a state of utter delusion, one asks a question merely while revealing one's own state of utter delusion, like a madman. 'One of evil wishes' means: one asks out of an evil wish, thinking, "In this way, people will praise me." 'Out of contempt' means: one asks, desiring to impute fault. In the declarations of final knowledge also, this is the same method. The remainder is self-evident everywhere. And in the Attādānavagga and the Dhutaṅgavagga, whatever should be said, all that has already been stated below.

Diṭṭhāvikammavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Diṭṭhāvikammavagga is concluded.

Musāvādavaggavaṇṇanā

The Commentary on the Section on False Speech

444. Musāvādavagge [M 0.0220] – pārājikaṃ gacchatīti pārājikagāmī; pārājikāpattibhāvaṃ pāpuṇātīti attho. Itaresupi eseva nayo. Tattha asantauttarimanussadhammārocanamusāvādo pārājikagāmī, amūlakena pārājikena anuddhaṃsanamusāvādo saṅghādisesagāmī, “yo te vihāre vasatī”tiādinā pariyāyena jānantassa vuttamusāvādo thullaccayagāmī, ajānantassa dukkaṭagāmī, “sampajānamusāvāde pācittiya”nti āgato pācittiyagāmīti veditabbo.

444. In the Section on False Speech, the determination should be understood thus. Because it 'reaches pārājika' (pārājikaṃ gacchati), it is 'leading to pārājika' (pārājikagāmī); the meaning is that one reaches the state of a pārājika offense. This same method applies to the other terms as well. Among these, the false speech of announcing a non-existent superhuman state is one that leads to pārājika. The false speech of accusing with a baseless pārājika offense is one that leads to saṅghādisesa. The false speech spoken by way of circumlocution to one who understands, such as, 'He who lives in your monastery…,' is one that leads to a thullaccaya offense. The false speech spoken to one who does not understand is one that leads to a dukkaṭa offense. The false speech that is mentioned in the rule, 'In intentional false speech, there is a pācittiya offense,' should be understood as one that leads to a pācittiya offense.

Adassanenāti vinayadharassa adassanena. Kappiyākappiyesu hi kukkucce uppanne [P 7.1376] vinayadharaṃ disvā kappiyākappiyabhāvaṃ paṭipucchitvā akappiyaṃ pahāya kappiyaṃ kareyya, taṃ apassanto pana akappiyampi kappiyanti karonto āpajjati. Evaṃ āpajjitabbaṃ āpattiṃ vinayadharassa dassanena nāpajjati, adassaneneva āpajjati, tena vuttaṃ “adassanenā”ti. Assavanenāti ekavihārepi vasanto pana vinayadharassa upaṭṭhānaṃ gantvā kappiyākappiyaṃ apucchitvā vā aññesañca vuccamānaṃ asuṇanto āpajjatiyeva, tena vuttaṃ “assavanenā”ti. Pasuttakatāti pasuttakatāya. Sahagāraseyyañhi pasuttakabhāvenapi āpajjati. Akappiye kappiyasaññitāya āpajjanto pana tathāsaññī āpajjati. Satisammosā ekarattātikkamādivasena āpajjitabbaṃ āpajjati. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti.

‘By not seeing’ (adassanena) means by not seeing a Vinaya-expert. For when scrupulousness arises regarding what is allowable and unallowable, one should, having seen a Vinaya-expert and having asked about the state of being allowable or unallowable, abandon the unallowable and do the allowable. But one who does not see him incurs an offense while doing even an unallowable act, thinking it is allowable. Thus, an offense that ought to be incurred is not incurred by seeing a Vinaya-expert; it is incurred only by not seeing one. Therefore, it was said, ‘by not seeing.’ ‘By not hearing’ (assavanena) means that even while living in the same monastery, one incurs an offense either by not asking about what is allowable and unallowable, or by not hearing it being spoken to others. Therefore, it was said, ‘by not hearing.’ ‘Due to being asleep’ (pasuttakatā) means due to the state of being asleep. For one incurs the offense of sharing a sleeping place even due to the state of being asleep. But one who incurs an offense due to perceiving the unallowable as allowable, incurs it while being one with such a perception. Due to a lapse of mindfulness, one incurs an offense that ought to be incurred by way of such things as exceeding one night. The rest is clear in all cases.

Musāvādavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Section on False Speech is concluded.

Bhikkhunovādavaggavaṇṇanā

The Commentary on the Bhikkhunovādavagga

450. Bhikkhunivagge [V 0.0224] – alābhāyāti catunnaṃ paccayānaṃ alābhatthāya; yathā paccaye na labhanti, tathā parisakkati vāyamatīti attho. Anatthāyāti anatthaṃ kalisāsanaṃ āropento parisakkati. Avāsāyāti avāsatthāya; yasmiṃ gāmakhette vasanti, tato nīharaṇatthāya. Sampayojetīti asaddhammapaṭisevanatthāya sampayojeti.

450. In the Bhikkhunīvagga: ‘For lack of gain’ (alābhāya) means for the purpose of the lack of the four requisites; the meaning is that one strives and makes an effort so that the nuns do not obtain the requisites. ‘For harm’ (anatthāya) means one strives while imposing a harmful and harsh command. ‘For non-residence’ (avāsāya) means for the purpose of non-residence; for the purpose of driving them out from whatever village or field in which the nuns reside. ‘Engages’ (sampayojeti) means one engages them for the purpose of practicing the unvirtuous act of sexual intercourse.

451. “Katihi [M 0.0221] nu kho bhante aṅgehi samannāgatāya bhikkhuniyā kammaṃ kātabba”nti sattannaṃ kammānaṃ aññataraṃ sandhāya pucchati.

451. ‘Venerable sir, with how many factors must a nun be endowed for a formal act to be performed upon her?’—he asks this with reference to one or another of the seven kinds of formal acts.

454. Na sākacchātabboti kappiyākappiyanāmarūpaparicchedasamathavipassanādibhedo kathāmaggo na kathetabbo. Yasmā pana khīṇāsavo bhikkhu na visaṃvādeti, tathārūpassa kathāmaggassa sāmī hutvā katheti, na itaro; tasmā paṭhamapañcake “na asekkhenā”ti paṭikkhipitvā dutiyapañcake “asekkhenā”tiādi vuttaṃ.

454. ‘One should not converse’ means a path of discussion that has distinctions such as allowable and unallowable matters, the delimitation of name-and-form, samatha and vipassanā, and so on, should not be spoken. However, since a monk whose cankers are destroyed does not mislead, he speaks, being a master of such a path of discussion; another person does not. Therefore, in the first pentad, having rejected it with ‘not by one beyond training,’ in the second pentad it was said, ‘by one beyond training,’ etc.

Na atthapaṭisambhidāpattoti aṭṭhakathāya paṭisambhidāpatto pabhedagatañāṇappatto na hoti. Na dhammapaṭisambhidāpattoti pāḷidhamme paṭisambhidāpatto na hoti. Na niruttipaṭisambhidāpattoti vohāraniruttiyaṃ paṭisambhidāpatto na hoti. Na paṭibhānapaṭisambhidāpattoti yāni tāni paṭibhānasaṅkhātāni atthapaṭisambhidādīni ñāṇāni, tesu paṭisambhidāpatto na hoti. Yathāvimuttaṃ [P 7.1377] cittaṃ na paccavekkhitāti catunnaṃ phalavimuttīnaṃ vasena yathāvimuttaṃ cittaṃ ekūnavīsatibhedāya paccavekkhaṇāya na paccavekkhitā hoti. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti.

‘Not having attained the discrimination of meaning’ means one has not attained discrimination in the commentary, has not attained the knowledge that has reached distinction. ‘Not having attained the discrimination of the Dhamma’ means one has not attained discrimination in the Pāli text. ‘Not having attained the discrimination of language’ means one has not attained discrimination in conventional language. ‘Not having attained the discrimination of ready wit’ means one has not attained discrimination in those knowledges, such as the discrimination of meaning, etc., which are called the knowledge of ready wit. ‘Is not one who has reviewed the mind as it is liberated’ means one is not one who has reviewed the mind, liberated by way of the four liberations of fruition, with the nineteen kinds of reviewing knowledge. The rest is clear in all cases.

Bhikkhunovādavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Bhikkhunovādavagga is concluded.

Ubbāhikavaggavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Ubbāhika Chapter

455. Ubbāhikavagge – na atthakusaloti na aṭṭhakathākusalo; atthuddhāre cheko na hoti. Na dhammakusaloti ācariyamukhato anuggahitattā pāḷiyaṃ na kusalo, na pāḷisūro. Na [V 0.0225] niruttikusaloti bhāsantaravohāre na kusalo. Na byañjanakusaloti sithiladhanitādivasena parimaṇḍalabyañjanāropane kusalo na hoti; na akkharaparicchede nipuṇoti attho. Na pubbāparakusaloti atthapubbāpare dhammapubbāpare niruttipubbāpare byañjanapubbāpare purekathāpacchākathāsu ca na kusalo hoti.

455. In the Ubbāhika Chapter – 'Not skilled in the meaning' means not skilled in the commentary; one is not adept at extracting the meaning. 'Not skilled in the Dhamma' means, because of not having learned directly from a teacher, one is not skilled in the Pāḷi, not bold in the Pāḷi. 'Not skilled in language' means not skilled in the conventions of other languages. 'Not skilled in expression' means not skilled in applying well-rounded expressions by means of light and heavy sounds, etc.; that is, not proficient in the division of syllables. 'Not skilled in sequence' means not skilled in the sequence of the commentary, the sequence of the Dhamma, the sequence of language, the sequence of expression, and in what should be said before and what should be said after.

Kodhanotiādīni yasmā kodhādīhi abhibhūto kāraṇākāraṇaṃ na jānāti, vinicchituṃ na sakkoti, tasmā vuttāni. Pasāretā hoti [M 0.0222] no sāretāti mohetā hoti, na satiuppādetā; codakacuditakānaṃ kathaṃ moheti pidahati na sāretīti attho. Sesamettha ubbāhikavagge uttānamevāti.

The words beginning with 'one who is angry' are stated because one who is overcome by anger and so on does not know cause and non-cause and is unable to decide; therefore, they were said. 'He is one who spreads out, not one who reminds' means he is one who causes confusion, not one who causes mindfulness to arise. He confuses and conceals the speech of the accuser and the accused; he does not cause it to be remembered—this is the meaning. The rest here in the Ubbāhika Chapter is self-evident.

Ubbāhikavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Ubbāhika Chapter is finished.

Adhikaraṇavūpasamavaggavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Chapter on the Settling of Legal Questions

457. Adhikaraṇavūpasamavagge – puggalagaru hotīti “ayaṃ me upajjhāyo, ayaṃ me ācariyo”tiādīni cintetvā tassa jayaṃ ākaṅkhamāno “adhammaṃ dhammo”ti dīpeti. Saṅghagaru hotīti dhammañca vinayañca amuñcitvā vinicchinanto saṅghagaruko nāma hoti. Cīvarādīni gahetvā vinicchinanto āmisagaruko nāma hoti, tāni aggahetvā yathādhammaṃ vinicchinanto saddhammagaruko nāma hoti.

457. In the Chapter on the Settling of Legal Questions – 'One who respects persons' means: having thought, 'This is my preceptor, this is my teacher,' and so on, and desiring victory for that person, one points out what is not-Dhamma as Dhamma. 'One who respects the Saṅgha' means: one who decides without abandoning the Dhamma and the Vinaya is called one who respects the Saṅgha. One who decides after taking robes and so on is called one who respects material gain; one who decides in accordance with the Dhamma without taking those things is called one who respects the good Dhamma.

458. Pañcahupāli ākārehīti pañcahi kāraṇehi saṅgho bhijjati – kammena, uddesena, voharanto, anussāvanena, salākaggāhenāti. Ettha kammenāti apalokanādīsu catūsu kammesu aññatarena kammena. Uddesenāti pañcasu pātimokkhuddesesu aññatarena uddesena. Voharantoti kathayanto; tāhi tāhi upapattīhi “adhammaṃ dhammo”tiādīni aṭṭhārasa bhedakaravatthūni dīpento[P 7.1378]. Anussāvanenāti “nanu tumhe jānātha mayhaṃ uccākulā pabbajitabhāvaṃ bahussutabhāvañca, mādiso nāma uddhammaṃ ubbinayaṃ satthu sāsanaṃ gāheyyāti cittampi uppādetuṃ tumhākaṃ yuttaṃ, kiṃ mayhaṃ avīci nīluppalavanamiva sītalo, kimahaṃ apāyato na bhāyāmī”tiādinā nayena kaṇṇamūle vacībhedaṃ katvā anussāvanena. Salākaggāhenāti evaṃ anussāvetvā [V 0.0226] tesaṃ cittaṃ upatthambhetvā anivattidhamme katvā “gaṇhatha imaṃ salāka”nti salākaggāhena.

458. 'Upāli, by five ways' means the Saṅgha is split by five causes: by an act, by a recitation, by a declaration, by a proclamation, and by the taking of voting-tickets. Here, 'by an act' means by any one of the four formal acts beginning with that of 'proposing a motion.' 'By a recitation' means by any one of the five recitations of the Pātimokkha. 'By a declaration' means by speaking; by pointing out with various suitable reasons the eighteen grounds for creating a schism, beginning with 'what is not-Dhamma is Dhamma.' 'By a proclamation' means by a proclamation made after creating a verbal split at their ear with words such as: 'Surely you know that I have gone forth from a high family and am very learned? Is it proper for you even to think that one such as I would take up what is not-Dhamma and not-Vinaya in the Teacher's Dispensation? Is the Avīci hell as cool as a blue lotus pond for me? Do I not fear the lower realms?' 'By the taking of voting-tickets' means, having thus proclaimed and having bolstered their minds and made them un-retreating, it is by the taking of voting-tickets, saying, 'Take this voting-ticket.'

Ettha ca kammameva uddeso vā pamāṇaṃ, vohārānussāvanasalākaggāhā pana pubbabhāgā. Aṭṭhārasavatthudīpanavasena hi voharante tattha rucijananatthaṃ [M 0.0223] anussāvetvā salākāya gāhitāyapi abhinnova hoti saṅgho. Yadā pana evaṃ cattāro vā atireke vā salākaṃ gāhetvā āveṇikaṃ kammaṃ vā uddesaṃ vā karoti, tadā saṅgho bhinno nāma hoti. Iti yaṃ saṅghabhedakakkhandhakavaṇṇanāyaṃ avocumhā “evaṃ aṭṭhārasasu vatthūsu yaṃkiñci ekampi vatthuṃ dīpetvā tena tena kāraṇena ‘imaṃ gaṇhatha, imaṃ rocethā’ti saññāpetvā salākaṃ gāhetvā visuṃ saṅghakamme kate saṅgho bhinno hoti. Parivāre pana ‘pañcahi, upāli, ākārehi saṅgho bhijjatī’tiādi vuttaṃ. Tassa iminā idha vuttena saṅghabhedalakkhaṇena atthato nānākaraṇaṃ natthi. Taṃ panassa nānākaraṇābhāvaṃ tattheva pakāsayissāmā”ti, svāyaṃ pakāsito hoti.

And here, the formal act itself or the recitation is the standard, while the declaration, proclamation, and taking of voting-tickets are the preliminary stages. For, even when one has made a declaration by way of explaining the eighteen grounds, has made a proclamation to generate interest in it, and the voting-tickets have been taken, the Saṅgha is still not split. But when, in this way, four or more, having had the voting-tickets taken, perform a separate formal act or recitation, then the Saṅgha is called 'split.' Thus, what we said in the commentary on the Saṅghabhedakakkhandhaka: 'In this way, having pointed out any single ground among the eighteen grounds, and for this or that reason having persuaded them, saying, "Accept this, approve of this," and having had the voting-tickets taken, when a separate Saṅgha act is performed, the Saṅgha is split. But in the Parivāra it is said, "Upāli, the Saṅgha is split by five ways," etc. There is no difference in meaning between that and this characteristic of a split Saṅgha stated here. And we will clarify that absence of difference right there.' That meaning has now been clarified.

Paññattetanti paññattaṃ etaṃ. Kva paññattaṃ? Vattakkhandhake. Tatra hi cuddasa khandhakavattāni paññattāni. Tenāha – “paññattetaṃ, upāli, mayā āgantukānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ āgantukavatta”ntiādi. Evampi kho upāli saṅgharāji hoti, no ca saṅghabhedoti ettāvatā hi saṅgharājimattameva hoti, na tāva saṅghabhedo; anupubbena pana ayaṃ saṅgharāji vaḍḍhamānā saṅghabhedāya saṃvattatīti attho. Yathārattanti rattiparimāṇānurūpaṃ; yathātheranti attho. Āvenibhāvaṃ karitvāti visuṃ vavatthānaṃ karitvā. Kammākammāni karontīti aparāparaṃ saṅghakammaṃ upādāya khuddakāni ceva mahantāni ca kammāni karonti. Sesametthāpi adhikaraṇavūpasamavagge uttānameva.

'It was laid down' means this was laid down. Where was it laid down? In the Vattakkhandhaka. For there, fourteen duties of the Khandhakas were laid down. Therefore, he said: 'Upāli, this was laid down by me: the duty for incoming monks,' etc. 'Even so, Upāli, there is a crack in the Saṅgha, but not a split in the Saṅgha' means that by this much there is only a crack in the Saṅgha; there is not yet a split in the Saṅgha. But gradually this crack in the Saṅgha, as it grows, leads to a split in the Saṅgha—this is the meaning. 'According to the nights' means according to the number of nights; that is, according to seniority. 'Having made a separation' means having made a separate arrangement. 'They perform various acts' means, undertaking one Saṅgha act after another, they perform both minor and major acts. The rest here in the Chapter on the Settling of Legal Questions is also self-evident.

Saṅghabhedakavaggadvayavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Two Sections on Schism in the Saṅgha

459. Saṅghabhedavaggadvaye [P 7.1379] – vinidhāya diṭṭhiṃ kammenāti tesu adhammādīsu adhammādayo eteti evaṃdiṭṭhikova hutvā taṃ diṭṭhiṃ vinidhāya te dhammādivasena dīpetvā visuṃ kammaṃ karoti. Iti yaṃ vinidhāya diṭṭhiṃ kammaṃ karoti, tena evaṃ katena vinidhāya diṭṭhiṃ kammena saddhiṃ pañcaṅgāni honti, “imehi kho upāli pañcahaṅgehī”ti ayamekasmiṃ pañcake atthayojanā[V 0.0227]. Etena nayena sabbapañcakāni veditabbāni. Etthāpi ca vohārādi aṅgattayaṃ pubbabhāgavaseneva vuttaṃ. Kammuddesavasena pana atekicchatā veditabbā. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānameva. Na hettha kiñci atthi yaṃ pubbe avuttanayaṃ.

459. In the two sections on schism in the Saṅgha: The phrase 'by an act, having concealed one's view' means this: Among those non-Dhamma things, etc., having become one with the view that 'these are non-Dhamma, etc.,' one conceals that view, explains those things in terms of Dhamma, etc., and performs a separate act. Thus, with that act performed by concealing the view, there are five factors. The phrase 'Indeed, Upāli, with these five factors' is the connection of meaning in one pentad. By this method, all pentads should be understood. Here too, the three factors, such as expression, are stated only in terms of the preliminary stage. However, in terms of the formal act and recitation, its incurability should be understood. The rest is clear throughout. There is nothing here whose method has not been previously stated.

Āvāsikavaggavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Section on Residents

461. Āvāsikavagge [M 0.0224] – yathābhataṃ nikkhittoti yathā āharitvā ṭhapito.

461. In the Section on Residents: ‘Yathābhataṃ nikkhitto’ means just as one is brought and placed in hell.

462. Vinayabyākaraṇāti vinayapañhe vissajjanā. Pariṇāmetīti niyāmeti dīpeti katheti. Sesamettha uttānameva.

462. ‘Vinayabyākaraṇā’ means answering questions on the Vinaya. ‘Pariṇāmeti’ means establishes, explains, or states. The rest here is clear.

Kathinatthāravaggavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Section on Spreading the Kathina

467. Kathinatthāravagge – otamasikoti andhakāragato; tañhi vandantassa mañcapādādīsupi nalāṭaṃ paṭihaññeyya. Asamannāharantoti kiccayapasutattā vandanaṃ asamannāharanto. Suttoti niddaṃ okkanto. Ekāvattoti ekato āvatto sapattapakkhe ṭhito verī visabhāgapuggalo vuccati; ayaṃ avandiyo. Ayañhi vandiyamāno pādenapi pahareyya. Aññavihitoti aññaṃ cintayamāno.

467. In the Section on Spreading the Kathina: ‘Otamasiko’ means one who has gone into darkness; indeed, for one venerating him, one's forehead might strike the legs of a couch, etc. ‘Asamannāharanto’ means one who does not pay attention to the veneration due to being engaged in some duty. ‘Sutto’ means one who has fallen asleep. ‘Ekāvatto’ refers to one who has turned to one side, standing on the side of the enemy, a hostile, discordant person; this person is not to be venerated. Indeed, if venerated, this person might even strike with his foot. ‘Aññavihito’ means one who is thinking of something else.

Khādantoti piṭṭhakhajjakādīni khādanto. Uccārañca passāvañca karonto anokāsagatattā avandiyo. Ukkhittakoti tividhenapi ukkhepanīyakammena ukkhittako avandiyo. Tajjanīyādikammakatā pana cattāro vanditabbā. Uposathapavāraṇāpi tehi saddhiṃ labbhanti. Ādito paṭṭhāya ca vuttesu avandiyesu naggañca ukkhittakañca vandantasseva āpatti. Itaresaṃ pana asāruppaṭṭhena ca antarā vuttakāraṇena ca vandanā paṭikkhittā. Ito paraṃ pacchāupasampannādayo dasapi āpattivatthubhāveneva avandiyā. Te vandantassa hi niyameneva āpatti. Iti imesu pañcasu pañcakesu terasa jane vandantassa anāpatti, dvādasannaṃ vandanāya āpatti.

‘Khādanto’ means one eating flour-based foods and the like. One who is defecating or urinating is not to be venerated because of being in an unsuitable place. ‘Ukkhittako’ means one who has been suspended by any of the three kinds of suspension acts; such a one is not to be venerated. However, the four who are subject to acts such as censure are to be venerated. Uposatha and Pavāraṇā are also permissible with them. Among those stated from the beginning who are not to be venerated, an offense is incurred only for venerating a naked person or a suspended person. For the others, however, veneration is prohibited because of unsuitability and for the reason stated in between. Furthermore, the ten persons such as one ordained later are not to be venerated simply because it is a basis for an offense. Indeed, for one who venerates them, there is definitely an offense. Thus, in these five pentads, for one who venerates thirteen persons, there is no offense; for venerating twelve, there is an offense.

468. Ācariyo [V 0.0228] vandiyoti pabbajjācariyo upasampadācariyo nissayācariyo uddesācariyo ovādācariyoti ayaṃ pañcavidhopi ācariyo vandiyo. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti.

468. ‘Ācariyo vandiyo’ means: the going-forth preceptor, the higher-ordination preceptor, the dependence preceptor, the recitation preceptor, and the admonition preceptor—this teacher of five kinds is to be venerated. The rest is clear throughout.

Kathinatthāravaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Section on Spreading the Kathina is concluded.

Niṭṭhitā ca upālipañcakavaṇṇanā.

And the Commentary on the Upālipañcaka is concluded.

Āpattisamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā

Exposition on the Origination of Offenses

470. Acittako [P 7.1380][M 0.0225] āpajjatītiādīsu sahaseyyādipaṇṇattivajjaṃ asañcicca āpajjanto acittako āpajjati, desento sacittako vuṭṭhāti. Yaṃkiñci sañcicca āpajjanto sacittako āpajjati, tiṇavatthārakena vuṭṭhahanto acittako vuṭṭhāti. Pubbe vuttameva tiṇavatthārakena vuṭṭhahanto acittako āpajjati, acittako vuṭṭhāti. Itaraṃ desento sacittako āpajjati, sacittako vuṭṭhāti. “Dhammadānaṃ karomī”ti padasodhammādīni karonto kusalacitto āpajjati, “buddhānaṃ anusāsaniṃ karomī”ti udaggacitto desento kusalacitto vuṭṭhāti. Domanassiko hutvā desento akusalacitto vuṭṭhāti, tiṇavatthārakena niddāgatova vuṭṭhahanto abyākatacitto vuṭṭhāti. Bhiṃsāpanādīni katvā “buddhānaṃ sāsanaṃ karomī”ti somanassiko desento akusalacitto āpajjati, kusalacitto vuṭṭhāti. Domanassikova desento akusalacitto vuṭṭhāti, vuttanayeneva tiṇavatthārakena vuṭṭhahanto abyākatacitto vuṭṭhāti. Niddokkantasamaye sahagāraseyyaṃ āpajjanto abyākatacitto āpajjati, vuttanayeneva panettha “kusalacitto vuṭṭhātī”tiādi veditabbaṃ.

470. In the phrases beginning with ‘Acittako āpajjati,’ the ruling should be understood thus: excluding the prescribed offenses beginning with lying down together, one who unintentionally commits an offense, does so without intention; when confessing it, one is absolved with intention. One who intentionally commits any offense whatsoever, does so with intention; when being absolved by the procedure of covering with grass, one is absolved without intention. Regarding the aforementioned offense, when being absolved by the procedure of covering with grass, one commits it without intention and is absolved without intention. Regarding the other type of offense, when confessing it, one commits it with intention and is absolved with intention. One who performs acts such as reciting the Dhamma word-by-word with the thought, ‘I am making a gift of the Dhamma,’ commits an offense with a wholesome mind. One who confesses with an uplifted mind, thinking, ‘I am carrying out the instruction of the Buddhas,’ is absolved with a wholesome mind. One who confesses while being afflicted with grief is absolved with an unwholesome mind. One who is absolved by the procedure of covering with grass while having fallen asleep is absolved with an indeterminate mind. Having performed acts such as frightening others, and then confessing with joy, thinking, ‘I am carrying out the teaching of the Buddhas,’ one commits an offense with an unwholesome mind but is absolved with a wholesome mind. If one confesses while being afflicted with grief, one is absolved with an unwholesome mind. In the way already stated, one who is absolved by the procedure of covering with grass is absolved with an indeterminate mind. At the time of falling asleep, one who commits the offense of lying down together does so with an indeterminate mind. Herein, the explanation beginning with ‘one is absolved with a wholesome mind’ should be understood in the way already stated.

Paṭhamaṃ pārājikaṃ katihi samuṭṭhānehītiādi pubbe vuttanayattā uttānameva.

The passage beginning, ‘Paṭhamaṃ pārājikaṃ katihi samuṭṭhānehi,’ and so on, is self-evident because the method has been stated previously.

473. Cattāro pārājikā katihi samuṭṭhānehītiādīsu ukkaṭṭhaparicchedato yaṃ yaṃ samuṭṭhānaṃ yassa yassa labbhati, taṃ sabbaṃ vuttameva hoti.

473. In the passages beginning, ‘Cattāro pārājikā katihi samuṭṭhānehi,’ and so on, by way of the ultimate analysis, whichever origination is obtained for whichever training rule, all that has indeed been stated.

Āpattisamuṭṭhānavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition on the Origination of Offenses is concluded.

Aparadutiyagāthāsaṅgaṇikaṃ

The Further Second Collection of Verses

(1) Kāyikādiāpattivaṇṇanā

(1) Exposition on Bodily Offenses and so on

474. “Kati [M 0.0226][V 0.0229] āpattiyo kāyikā”tiādigāthānaṃ vissajjane cha āpattiyo kāyikāti antarapeyyāle catutthena āpattisamuṭṭhānena cha āpattiyo āpajjati, “bhikkhu methunaṃ dhammaṃ paṭisevati, āpatti pārājikassā”tiādinā nayena vuttāpattiyo. Kāyadvāre samuṭṭhitattā hi etā kāyikāti vuccanti. Cha vācasikāti tasmiṃyeva antarapeyyāle pañcamena āpattisamuṭṭhānena cha āpattiyo āpajjati, “bhikkhu pāpiccho icchāpakato”tiādinā nayena vuttāpattiyo. Chādentassa tissoti vajjapaṭicchādikāya bhikkhuniyā pārājikaṃ, bhikkhussa saṅghādisesapaṭicchādane pācittiyaṃ, attano duṭṭhullāpattipaṭicchādane dukkaṭaṃ. Pañca saṃsaggapaccayāti [P 7.1381] bhikkhuniyā kāyasaṃsagge pārājikaṃ, bhikkhuno saṅghādiseso, kāyena kāyapaṭibaddhe thullaccayaṃ, nissaggiyena kāyapaṭibaddhe dukkaṭaṃ, aṅgulipatodake pācittiyanti imā kāyasaṃsaggapaccayā pañcāpattiyo.

474. In the answer to the verses beginning, ‘Kati āpattiyo kāyikā’: The phrase ‘cha āpattiyo kāyikā’ means that in the intermediate elided section, by the fourth origination of offenses, one commits six offenses—the offenses spoken of in the manner beginning with, ‘If a bhikkhu engages in sexual intercourse, it is a pārājika offense.’ Indeed, because they originate through the body-door, these are called ‘bodily.’ The phrase ‘cha vācasikā’ means that in that very same intermediate elided section, by the fifth origination of offenses, one commits six offenses—the offenses spoken of in the manner beginning with, ‘If a bhikkhu of evil wishes, overcome by desire...’ The phrase ‘chādentassa tisso’ means: for a bhikkhunī who conceals a fault, it is a pārājika offense; for a bhikkhu, in the concealing of a saṅghādisesa offense, it is a pācittiya offense; in concealing one's own grave offense, it is a dukkaṭa offense. The phrase ‘pañca saṃsaggapaccayā’ means: for a bhikkhunī, in bodily contact, it is a pārājika offense; for a bhikkhu, it is a saṅghādisesa offense; contact with the body on an object connected to the body is a thullaccaya offense; contact with an item to be forfeited on an object connected to the body is a dukkaṭa offense; in poking with a finger, it is a pācittiya offense. These are the five offenses conditioned by bodily contact.

Aruṇugge tissoti ekarattachārattasattāhadasāhamāsātikkamavasena nissaggiyaṃ pācittiyaṃ, bhikkhuniyā rattivippavāse saṅghādiseso, “paṭhamampi yāmaṃ chādeti, dutiyampi tatiyampi yāmaṃ chādeti, uddhaste aruṇe channā hoti āpatti, yo chādeti so dukkaṭaṃ desāpetabbo”ti imā aruṇugge tisso āpattiyo āpajjati. Dve yāvatatiyakāti ekādasa yāvatatiyakā nāma, paññattivasena pana dve honti bhikkhūnaṃ yāvatatiyakā bhikkhunīnaṃ yāvatatiyakāti. Ekettha aṭṭhavatthukāti bhikkhunīnaṃyeva ekā ettha imasmiṃ sāsane aṭṭhavatthukā nāma. Ekena sabbasaṅgahoti “yassa siyā āpatti, so āvikareyyā”ti iminā ekena nidānuddesena sabbasikkhāpadānañca sabbapātimokkhuddesānañca saṅgaho hoti.

(The phrase) 'at dawn, there are three' (means): a Nissaggiya Pācittiya offense by way of exceeding one night, six nights, seven days, ten days, or a month; for a bhikkhunī, a Saṅghādisesa offense for spending the night separated (from companions); and (the offense described as): 'She conceals it for the first watch, she conceals it for the second and third watch; when dawn has risen, the offense is concealed. Whoever conceals it should be made to confess a Dukkaṭa offense.' These are the three offenses one commits at dawn. (The phrase) 'two "up to the third time"' (means): there are eleven (offenses) called 'up to the third time', but by way of designation, there are two: those 'up to the third time' for bhikkhus and those 'up to the third time' for bhikkhunīs. (The phrase) 'herein, one with eight grounds' (means): only for bhikkhunīs is there one (offense) in this Dispensation called 'with eight grounds'. (The phrase) 'by one, there is inclusion of all' (means): by this one introductory passage, 'Should there be an offense for anyone, he should declare it,' there is an inclusion of all training rules and all Pātimokkha recitations.

Vinayassa dve mūlānīti kāyo ceva vācā ca. Garukā dve vuttāti pārājikasaṅghādisesā[V 0.0230]. Dve duṭṭhullacchādanāti vajjapaṭicchādikāya pārājikaṃ [M 0.0227] saṅghādisesaṃ paṭicchādakassa pācittiyanti imā dve duṭṭhullacchādanāpattiyo nāma.

(The phrase) 'the two roots of the Vinaya' (means): the body and speech. (The phrase) 'two grave (offenses) are spoken of' (means): Pārājika and Saṅghādisesa. (The phrase) 'two concealments of a grave offense' (means): for the (bhikkhunī) who conceals a fault, a Pārājika offense; and for the one who conceals a Saṅghādisesa offense, a Pācittiya offense. These are called the two offenses of concealing a grave offense.

Gāmantare catassoti “bhikkhu bhikkhuniyā saddhiṃ saṃvidahati, dukkaṭaṃ; aññassa gāmassa upacāraṃ okkamati, pācittiyaṃ; bhikkhuniyā gāmantaraṃ gacchantiyā parikkhitte gāme paṭhamapāde thullaccayaṃ, dutiyapāde saṅghādiseso; aparikkhittassa paṭhamapāde upacārokkamane thullaccayaṃ, dutiyapāde saṅghādiseso”ti imā gāmantare dukkaṭapācittiyathullaccayasaṅghaādisesavasena catasso āpattiyo. Catasso nadipārapaccayāti “bhikkhu bhikkhuniyā saddhiṃ saṃvidahati, dukkaṭaṃ; nāvaṃ abhiruhati, pācittiyaṃ; bhikkhuniyā nadipāraṃ gacchantiyā uttaraṇakāle paṭhamapāde thullaccayaṃ, dutiyapāde saṅghādiseso”ti imā catasso. Ekamaṃse thullaccayanti manussamaṃse. Navamaṃsesu dukkaṭanti sesaakappiyamaṃsesu.

(The phrase) 'in a village interval, four' (means): 'If a bhikkhu makes an arrangement with a bhikkhunī, it is a Dukkaṭa offense; if he enters the vicinity of another village, it is a Pācittiya offense; for a bhikkhunī going to another village: in an enclosed village, at the first step, it is a Thullaccaya offense, at the second step, a Saṅghādisesa offense; of an unenclosed (village), at the first step in entering the vicinity, it is a Thullaccaya offense, at the second step, a Saṅghādisesa offense.' Thus, these are the four offenses in a village interval, by way of Dukkaṭa, Pācittiya, Thullaccaya, and Saṅghādisesa. (The phrase) 'four conditioned by crossing a river' (means): 'If a bhikkhu makes an arrangement with a bhikkhunī, it is a Dukkaṭa offense; if he boards a boat, it is a Pācittiya offense; for a bhikkhunī going to the other side of a river, at the time of crossing, at the first step, it is a Thullaccaya offense, at the second step, a Saṅghādisesa offense.' These are the four. (The phrase) 'a Thullaccaya for one (kind of) flesh' (means): for human flesh. (The phrase) 'a Dukkaṭa for nine (kinds of) flesh' (means): for the remaining unsuitable kinds of flesh.

Dve vācasikā rattinti bhikkhunī rattandhakāre appadīpe purisena saddhiṃ hatthapāse ṭhitā sallapati[P 7.1382], pācittiyaṃ; hatthapāsaṃ vijahitvā ṭhitā sallapati, dukkaṭaṃ. Dve vācasikā divāti bhikkhunī divā paṭicchanne okāse purisena saddhiṃ hatthapāse ṭhitā sallapati, pācittiyaṃ; hatthapāsaṃ vijahitvā sallapati, dukkaṭaṃ. Dadamānassa tissoti maraṇādhippāyo manussassa visaṃ deti, so ce tena marati, pārājikaṃ; yakkhapetānaṃ deti, te ce maranti, thullaccayaṃ; tiracchānagatassa deti, so ce marati, pācittiyaṃ; aññātikāya bhikkhuniyā cīvaradāne pācittiyanti evaṃ dadamānassa tisso āpattiyo. Cattāro ca paṭiggaheti hatthaggāha-veṇiggāhesu saṅghādiseso, mukhena aṅgajātaggahaṇe pārājikaṃ, aññātikāya bhikkhuniyā cīvarapaṭiggahaṇe nissaggiyaṃ pācittiyaṃ, avassutāya avassutassa hatthato khādanīyaṃ bhojanīyaṃ paṭiggaṇhantiyā thullaccayaṃ; evaṃ paṭiggahe cattāro āpattikkhandhā honti.

(The phrase) 'two verbal (offenses) at night' (means): if a bhikkhunī, at night in darkness without a lamp, stands within arm's reach of a man and converses, it is a Pācittiya offense; if she stands having gone beyond arm's reach and converses, it is a Dukkaṭa offense. (The phrase) 'two verbal (offenses) during the day' (means): if a bhikkhunī, during the day in a concealed place, stands within arm's reach of a man and converses, it is a Pācittiya offense; if she converses having gone beyond arm's reach, it is a Dukkaṭa offense. (The phrase) 'for one who gives, three' (means): if, with the intention of causing death, one gives poison to a person, and if that person dies from it, it is a Pārājika offense; if one gives it to yakkhas or petas, and they die, it is a Thullaccaya offense; if one gives it to an animal, and it dies, it is a Pācittiya offense; in the giving of a robe to a non-relative bhikkhunī, it is a Pācittiya offense. Thus, for one who gives, there are three offenses. (The phrase) 'and four in receiving' (means): in grasping the hand or grasping a braid of hair, it is a Saṅghādisesa offense; in taking the sexual organ with the mouth, it is a Pārājika offense; in receiving a robe from a non-relative bhikkhunī, it is a Nissaggiya Pācittiya offense; for a lustful bhikkhunī receiving hard or soft food from the hand of a lustful man, it is a Thullaccaya offense. Thus, in receiving, there are four classes of offenses.

(2) Desanāgāminiyādivaṇṇanā

(2) The Explanation of Offenses Absolved by Confession, and so on

475. Pañca desanāgāminiyoti lahukā pañca. Cha sappaṭikammāti pārājikaṃ ṭhapetvā avasesā. Ekettha appaṭikammāti ekā pārājikāpatti.

475. (The phrase) 'five offenses absolved by confession' (means): five minor offenses. (The phrase) 'six are remediable' (means): the remaining offenses, setting aside the Pārājika. (The phrase) 'one here is irremediable' (means): the one Pārājika offense.

Vinayagarukā [V 0.0231] dve vuttāti pārājikañceva saṅghādisesañca. Kāyavācasikāni cāti sabbāneva sikkhāpadāni kāyavācasikāni, manodvāre paññattaṃ [M 0.0228] ekasikkhāpadampi natthi. Eko vikāle dhaññarasoti loṇasovīrakaṃ. Ayameva hi eko dhaññaraso vikāle vaṭṭati. Ekā ñatticatutthena sammutīti bhikkhunovādakasammuti. Ayameva hi ekā ñatticatutthakammena sammuti anuññātā.

(The phrase) 'two are grave in the Vinaya' (means): the Pārājika and the Saṅghādisesa. (The phrase) 'and they are bodily and verbal' (means): all training rules are bodily and verbal; there is not even one training rule laid down concerning the mind-door. (The phrase) 'one grain-juice at the wrong time' (means): salt-sour gruel. Indeed, only this one grain-juice is permissible at the wrong time. (The phrase) 'one authorization by a motion and three announcements' (means): the authorization of a bhikkhunī-admonisher. Indeed, only this one authorization is permitted by a formal act consisting of a motion and three announcements.

Pārājikā kāyikā dveti bhikkhūnaṃ methunapārājikaṃ bhikkhunīnañca kāyasaṃsaggapārājikaṃ. Dve saṃvāsabhūmiyoti attanā vā attānaṃ samānasaṃvāsakaṃ karoti, samaggo vā saṅgho ukkhittaṃ osāreti. Kurundiyaṃ pana “samānasaṃvāsakabhūmi ca nānāsaṃvāsakabhūmi cā”ti evaṃ dve saṃvāsabhūmiyo vuttā. Dvinnaṃ ratticchedoti pārivāsikassa ca mānattacārikassa ca paññattā. Dvaṅgulā duveti dve dvaṅgulapaññattiyo, “dvaṅgulapabbaparamaṃ ādātabba”nti ayamekā, “dvaṅgulaṃ vā dvemāsaṃ vā”ti ayamekā.

(The phrase) 'two are bodily Pārājika offenses' (means): for bhikkhus, the Pārājika for sexual intercourse, and for bhikkhunīs, the Pārājika for bodily contact. (The phrase) 'two grounds for communion' (means): one either makes oneself of the same communion, or a unified Saṅgha reinstates one who has been suspended. In the Kurundī commentary, however, two grounds for communion are stated thus: 'the ground of same communion and the ground of different communion.' (The phrase) 'the interruption of nights for two' (means): prescribed for one on probation and for one undergoing penance. (The phrase) 'two of two finger-breadths' (means): there are two regulations of two finger-breadths. 'To be taken at most a two-finger-joint length' is one; 'two finger-breadths or two months' is the other.

Dve attānaṃ vadhitvānāti bhikkhunī attānaṃ vadhitvā dve āpattiyo āpajjati; vadhati rodati, āpatti pācittiyassa; vadhati na rodati, āpatti dukkaṭassa. Dvīhi saṅgho bhijjatīti kammena ca salākaggāhena ca. Dvettha [P 7.1383] paṭhamāpattikāti ettha sakalepi vinaye dve paṭhamāpattikā ubhinnaṃ paññattivasena. Itarathā pana nava bhikkhūnaṃ nava bhikkhunīnanti aṭṭhārasa honti. Ñattiyā karaṇā duveti dve ñattikiccāni – kammañca kammapādakā ca. Navasu ṭhānesu kammaṃ hoti, dvīsu kammapādabhāvena tiṭṭhati.

(The phrase) 'two for having struck oneself' (means): a bhikkhunī, having struck herself, falls into two offenses; if she strikes and weeps, it is an offense of Pācittiya; if she strikes and does not weep, it is an offense of Dukkaṭa.
