(The phrase) 'the Saṅgha is split by two' (means): by a formal act and by the taking of voting tickets. (The phrase) 'two here are first offenses' (means): here, in the entire Vinaya, there are two first offenses by way of being laid down for both (bhikkhus and bhikkhunīs). Otherwise, however, there are nine for bhikkhus and nine for bhikkhunīs, thus making eighteen. (The phrase) 'two functions of a motion' (means): there are two duties of a motion—the formal act (kamma) and the basis of the formal act (kammapādakā). In nine instances, it is a formal act; in two, it stands as the basis of a formal act.

Pāṇātipāte tissoti “anodissa opātaṃ khaṇati, sace manusso marati, pārājikaṃ; yakkhapetānaṃ maraṇe thullaccayaṃ; tiracchānagatassa maraṇe pācittiya”nti imā tisso honti. Vācā pārājikā tayoti vajjapaṭicchādikāya ukkhittānuvattikāya aṭṭhavatthukāyāti. Kurundiyaṃ pana “āṇattiyā adinnādāne, manussamaraṇe, uttarimanussadhammaullapane cā”ti evaṃ tayo vuttā. Obhāsanā tayoti vaccamaggaṃ passāvamaggaṃ ādissa vaṇṇāvaṇṇabhāsane saṅghādiseso, vaccamaggaṃ passāvamaggaṃ ṭhapetvā adhakkhakaṃ ubbhajāṇumaṇḍalaṃ ādissa vaṇṇāvaṇṇabhaṇane thullaccayaṃ, ubbhakkhakaṃ adhojāṇumaṇḍalaṃ ādissa vaṇṇāvaṇṇabhaṇane dukkaṭaṃ. Sañcarittena vā tayoti paṭiggaṇhāti vīmaṃsati paccāharati[M 0.0229], āpatti saṅghādisesassa; [V 0.0232] paṭiggaṇhāti vīmaṃsati na paccāharati, āpatti thullaccayassa; paṭiggaṇhāti na vīmaṃsati na paccāharati, āpatti dukkaṭassāti ime sañcarittena kāraṇabhūtena tayo āpattikkhandhā honti.

Regarding the destruction of life, there are three offenses: 'If one digs a pit without specifying a target, and if a human dies, it is a pārājika; upon the death of yakkhas or petas, it is a thullaccaya; upon the death of an animal, it is a pācittiya'—these are the three. There are three verbal pārājikas: for a bhikkhunī who conceals an offense, for one who follows a suspended bhikkhu, and for one who is subject to the eight items. In the Kurundī commentary, however, three are stated thus: 'in taking what is not given through command, in causing the death of a human, and in speaking of a superhuman state.' There are three offenses of alluding: in speaking praise or blame with reference to the path of excrement or the path of urine, it is a saṅghādisesa; setting aside the path of excrement and the path of urine, in speaking praise or blame with reference to the area below the collarbones and above the kneecaps, it is a thullaccaya; in speaking praise or blame with reference to the area above the collarbones and below the kneecaps, it is a dukkaṭa. Or, there are three offenses through acting as a go-between: if one accepts, investigates, and reports back, it is an offense of saṅghādisesa; if one accepts and investigates but does not report back, it is an offense of thullaccaya; if one accepts but does not investigate and does not report back, it is an offense of dukkaṭa. Thus, these three classes of offenses occur through the act of being a go-between.

Tayo puggalā na upasampādetabbāti addhānahīno aṅgahīno vatthuvipanno ca tesaṃ nānākaraṇaṃ vuttameva. Apicettha yo pattacīvarena aparipūro, paripūro ca na yācati, imepi aṅgahīneneva saṅgahitā. Mātughātakādayo ca karaṇadukkaṭakā paṇḍakaubhatobyañjanakatiracchānagatasaṅkhātena vatthuvipanneneva saṅgahitāti veditabbā. Esa nayo kurundiyaṃ vutto. Tayo kammānaṃ saṅgahāti ñattikappanā, vippakatapaccattaṃ, atītakaraṇanti. Tattha “dadeyya kareyyā”tiādibhedā ñattikappanā; “deti karotī”tiādibhedaṃ vippakatapaccattaṃ; “dinnaṃ kata”ntiādibhedaṃ atītakaraṇaṃ nāmāti imehi tīhi kammāni saṅgayhanti. Aparehipi tīhi kammāni saṅgayhanti – vatthunā, ñattiyā, anussāvanāyāti. Vatthusampannañhi ñattisampannaṃ anussāvanasampannañca kammaṃ nāma hoti, tena vuttaṃ “tayo kammānaṃ saṅgahā”ti. Nāsitakā tayo nāma mettiyaṃ [P 7.1384] bhikkhuniṃ nāsetha, dūsako nāsetabbo, dasahaṅgehi samannāgato sāmaṇero nāsetabbo, kaṇṭakaṃ samaṇuddesaṃ nāsethāti evaṃ liṅgasaṃvāsadaṇḍakammanāsanāvasena tayo nāsitakā veditabbā. Tiṇṇannaṃ ekavācikāti “anujānāmi bhikkhave dve tayo ekānussāvane kātu”nti vacanato tiṇṇaṃ janānaṃ ekupajjhāyena nānācariyena ekānussāvanā vaṭṭati.

Three persons are not to be given the higher ordination: one deficient in age, one deficient in limbs, and one with a defective basis. The distinction among them has already been stated. Moreover, herein, one who is incomplete in bowl and robes, and one who, though complete, does not request ordination—these too are included under 'deficient in limbs.' Matricides and the like, and those who have committed a bad deed, are to be understood as included under 'defective basis' along with those classified as eunuchs, hermaphrodites, and animals. This method is stated in the Kurundī. Three are the ways of encompassing formal acts: the formulation of the motion, the present unfinished action, and the past action. Among these, the formulation of the motion is of the kind, 'He should give, he should do,' etc.; the present unfinished action is of the kind, 'He gives, he does,' etc.; and the past action is called, 'Given, done,' etc. By these three are formal acts encompassed. Formal acts are also encompassed by another three: by the basis, by the motion, and by the proclamation. For a formal act is called one that is complete in basis, complete in motion, and complete in proclamation; therefore it was said, 'Three are the ways of encompassing formal acts.' Three are those to be expelled: 'Expel the bhikkhunī Mettiyā,' 'The corrupting novice is to be expelled,' 'A novice endowed with ten factors is to be expelled,' and 'Expel the thorn-like novice under training.' Thus, three kinds of persons to be expelled are to be understood by way of expulsion of the characteristic, expulsion from co-residence, and expulsion by disciplinary act. A single proclamation for three: Because it is said, 'I allow, bhikkhus, for two or three to be ordained with a single proclamation,' it is permissible for three persons with one preceptor and different teachers to have a single proclamation.

Adinnādāne tissoti pāde vā atirekapāde vā pārājikaṃ, atirekamāsake thullaccayaṃ, māsake vā ūnamāsake vā dukkaṭaṃ. Catasso methunapaccayāti akkhayite pārājikaṃ, yebhuyyena khayite thullaccayaṃ, vivaṭakate mukhe dukkaṭaṃ, jatumaṭṭhake pācittiyaṃ. Chindantassa tissoti vanappatiṃ chindantassa pārājikaṃ, bhūtagāme pācittiyaṃ, aṅgajāte thullaccayaṃ. Pañca chaḍḍitapaccayāti anodissa visaṃ chaḍḍeti, sace tena manusso marati, pārājikaṃ; yakkhapetesu thullaccayaṃ; tiracchānagate pācittiyaṃ; vissaṭṭhichaḍḍane saṅghādiseso; sekhiyesu harite uccārapassāvachaḍḍane dukkaṭaṃ – imā chaḍḍitapaccayā pañcāpattiyo honti.

Regarding taking what is not given, there are three offenses: for a pāda or more than a pāda, it is a pārājika; for more than a māsaka, it is a thullaccaya; for a māsaka or less than a māsaka, it is a dukkaṭa. Four are the offenses conditioned by sexual intercourse: with a corpse not eaten by wild or domestic dogs, it is a pārājika; with a corpse mostly eaten, it is a thullaccaya; in an opened mouth, it is a dukkaṭa; for inserting a smooth stick of lac into the urinary passage, it is a pācittiya. Regarding one who cuts, there are three offenses: for one who cuts down a 'lord of the forest' tree, it is a pārājika; for destroying plant-life, it is a pācittiya; for cutting the generative organ, it is a thullaccaya. Five are the offenses conditioned by discarding: one discards poison without specifying a target; if a human being dies from it, it is a pārājika; in the case of yakkhas or petas, it is a thullaccaya; in the case of an animal, it is a pācittiya; for discarding semen, it is a saṅghādisesa; in the Sekhiyas, for discarding feces or urine on green growth, it is a dukkaṭa—these are the five offenses conditioned by discarding.

Pācittiyena [M 0.0230] dukkaṭā katāti bhikkhunovādakavaggasmiṃ dasasu sikkhāpadesu pācittiyena saddhiṃ [V 0.0233] dukkaṭā katā evāti attho. Caturettha navakā vuttāti paṭhamasikkhāpadamhiyeva adhammakamme dve, dhammakamme dveti evaṃ cattāro navakā vuttāti attho. Dvinnaṃ cīvarena cāti bhikkhūnaṃ santike upasampannāya cīvaraṃ dentassa pācittiyaṃ, bhikkhunīnaṃ santike upasampannāya dentassa dukkaṭanti evaṃ dvinnaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ cīvaraṃ dentassa cīvarena kāraṇabhūtena āpatti hotīti attho.

Regarding 'By a pācittiya, dukkaṭas are made': this means that in the ten training rules in the Bhikkhunī-instructing Chapter, dukkaṭas have been laid down together with a pācittiya. Regarding 'Herein four sets of nine are stated': this means that in the first training rule itself, two sets of nine in the case of an unlawful formal act and two in the case of a lawful formal act—thus four sets of nine are stated. Regarding 'And by a robe for two': for one who gives a robe to a bhikkhunī ordained in the presence of bhikkhus, it is a pācittiya; for one who gives a robe to a bhikkhunī ordained in the presence of bhikkhunīs, it is a dukkaṭa. Thus, for one who gives a robe to a bhikkhunī in these two cases, an offense is incurred with the robe as the cause—this is the meaning.

Aṭṭha pāṭidesanīyāti pāḷiyaṃ āgatā eva. Bhuñjantāmakadhaññena pācittiyena dukkaṭā katāti āmakadhaññaṃ viññāpetvā bhuñjantiyā pācittiyena saddhiṃ dukkaṭā katāyeva.

There are eight pāṭidesanīya offenses, which are those that have come down in the Pāḷi itself. Regarding 'By eating raw grain, a pācittiya with a dukkaṭa is made': this means that for a bhikkhunī who, having requested raw grain, eats it, a dukkaṭa is made together with a pācittiya.

Gacchantassa catassoti bhikkhuniyā vā mātugāmena vā saddhiṃ saṃvidhāya gacchantassa dukkaṭaṃ, gāmūpacārokkamane pācittiyaṃ, yā bhikkhunī ekā gāmantaraṃ gacchati, tassā gāmūpacāraṃ okkamantiyā paṭhamapāde thullaccayaṃ, dutiyapāde saṅghādisesoti gacchantassa imā catasso āpattiyo honti. Ṭhitassa cāpi tattakāti [P 7.1385] ṭhitassapi catasso evāti attho. Kathaṃ? Bhikkhunī andhakāre vā paṭicchanne vā okāse mittasanthavavasena purisassa hatthapāse tiṭṭhati, pācittiyaṃ; hatthapāsaṃ vijahitvā tiṭṭhati, dukkaṭaṃ; aruṇuggamanakāle dutiyikāya hatthapāsaṃ vijahantī tiṭṭhati, thullaccayaṃ; vijahitvā tiṭṭhati, saṅghādisesoti nisinnassa catasso āpattiyo. Nipannassāpi tattakāti sacepi hi sā nisīdati vā nipajjati vā, etāyeva catasso āpattiyo āpajjati.

For one who is going, there are four offenses: for a bhikkhu who goes by prearrangement with a bhikkhunī or a laywoman, it is a dukkaṭa; upon entering the village precincts, it is a pācittiya. For a bhikkhunī who goes alone to another village, upon entering the village precincts, with the first step it is a thullaccaya; with the second step, it is a saṅghādisesa. Thus, for one who is going, these are the four offenses. And for one who is standing, just as many: this means that for one who is standing also, there are four offenses. How? If a bhikkhunī, on account of friendly intimacy, stands within arm's reach of a man in the dark or in a concealed place, it is a pācittiya; if she stands having gone beyond arm's reach, it is a dukkaṭa. At dawn, if she stands while leaving the arm's reach of her female companion, it is a thullaccaya; if she stands after having left it, it is a saṅghādisesa. These are the four offenses for one who is sitting. For one who is lying down, there are also just as many: for if she sits or lies down, she incurs these same four offenses.

(3) Pācittiyavaṇṇanā

(3) The Commentary on the Pācittiya

476. Pañca pācittiyānīti pañca bhesajjāni paṭiggahetvā nānābhājanesu vā ekabhājane vā amissetvā ṭhapitāni honti, sattāhātikkame so bhikkhu pañca pācittiyāni sabbāni nānāvatthukāni ekakkhaṇe āpajjati, “imaṃ paṭhamaṃ āpanno, imaṃ pacchā”ti na vattabbo.

476. As for 'five Pācittiyas': Having received five medicines, they are kept without being mixed, either in various vessels or in one vessel. When seven days have passed, that bhikkhu incurs all five Pācittiya offenses, which have various bases, in a single moment. It should not be said, 'He incurred this one first, and this one later.'

Nava pācittiyānīti yo bhikkhu nava paṇītabhojanāni viññāpetvā tehi saddhiṃ ekato ekaṃ kabaḷaṃ omadditvā mukhe pakkhipitvā paragaḷaṃ atikkāmeti, ayaṃ nava pācittiyāni sabbāni [V 0.0234] nānāvatthukāni ekakkhaṇe āpajjati [M 0.0231] “imaṃ paṭhamaṃ āpanno, imaṃ pacchā”ti na vattabbo. Ekavācāya deseyyāti “ahaṃ, bhante, pañca bhesajjāni paṭiggahetvā sattāhaṃ atikkāmetvā pañca āpattiyo āpanno, tā tumhamūle paṭidesemī”ti evaṃ ekavācāya deseyya, desitāva honti, dvīhi tīhi vācāhi kiccaṃ nāma natthi. Dutiyavissajjanepi “ahaṃ, bhante, nava paṇītabhojanāni viññāpetvā bhuñjitvā nava āpattiyo āpanno, tā tumhamūle paṭidesemī”ti vattabbaṃ.

As for 'nine Pācittiyas': If a bhikkhu, having requested nine choice foods, presses them together into a single mouthful, places it in his mouth, and causes it to pass beyond the throat, this bhikkhu incurs all nine Pācittiya offenses, which have various bases, in a single moment. It should not be said, 'He incurred this one first, and this one later.' As for 'he should confess with a single statement': He should confess with a single statement thus: 'Venerable sir, having received five medicines and having let seven days pass, I have incurred five offenses. I confess them at your feet.' They are indeed confessed. There is no need for two or three statements. Also in the second answer, it should be said: 'Venerable sir, having requested and eaten nine choice foods, I have incurred nine offenses. I confess them at your feet.'

Vatthuṃ kittetvā deseyyāti “ahaṃ, bhante, pañca bhesajjāni paṭiggahetvā sattāhaṃ atikkāmesiṃ, yathāvatthukaṃ taṃ tumhamūle paṭidesemī”ti evaṃ vatthuṃ kittetvā deseyya, desitāva honti āpattiyo, āpattiyā nāmaggahaṇena kiccaṃ natthi. Dutiyavissajjanepi “ahaṃ, bhante, nava paṇītabhojanāni viññāpetvā bhutto, yathāvatthukaṃ taṃ tumhamūle paṭidesemī”ti vattabbaṃ.

As for 'he should confess by stating the matter': He should confess by stating the matter thus: 'Venerable sir, having received five medicines, I let seven days pass. I confess that to you at your feet according to the matter.' Thus, the offenses are indeed confessed; there is no need for mentioning the name of the offense. Also in the second answer, it should be said: 'Venerable sir, I have eaten after having requested nine choice foods. I confess that to you at your feet according to the matter.'

Yāvatatiyake tissoti ukkhittānuvattikāya pārājikaṃ bhedakānuvattakānaṃ kokālikādīnaṃ saṅghādisesaṃ, pāpikāya diṭṭhiyā appaṭinissagge caṇḍakāḷikāya ca bhikkhuniyā pācittiyanti imā yāvatatiyakā tisso āpattiyo. Cha vohārapaccayāti payuttavācāpaccayā cha [P 7.1386] āpattiyo āpajjatīti attho. Kathaṃ? Ājīvahetu ājīvakāraṇā pāpiccho icchāpakato asantaṃ abhūtaṃ uttarimanussadhammaṃ ullapati, āpatti pārājikassa. Ājīvahetu ājīvakāraṇā sañcarittaṃ samāpajjati, āpatti saṅghādisesassa. Ājīvahetu ājīvakāraṇā yo te vihāre vasati so arahāti vadati, āpatti thullaccayassa. Ājīvahetu ājīvakāraṇā bhikkhu paṇītabhojanāni attano atthāya viññāpetvā bhuñjati, āpatti pācittiyassa. Ājīvahetu ājīvakāraṇā bhikkhunī paṇītabhojanāni attano atthāya viññāpetvā bhuñjati, āpatti pāṭidesanīyassa. Ājīvahetu ājīvakāraṇā sūpaṃ vā odanaṃ vā agilāno attano atthāya viññāpetvā bhuñjati, āpatti dukkaṭassāti.

As for 'the three that are up to the third time': For the bhikkhunī who is a follower of a suspended bhikkhu, a Pārājika; for the followers of a schismatic, such as Kokālika and others, a Saṅghādisesa; and for the bhikkhunī Caṇḍakāḷī on account of not abandoning a wicked view, a Pācittiya. These are the three offenses that are 'up to the third time'. As for 'six due to the condition of conduct': The meaning is that one incurs six offenses due to the condition of uttered speech. How? For the sake of livelihood, for the reason of livelihood, being one with evil desires and overcome by desire, one boasts of a non-existent, untrue superhuman state; this is an offense of Pārājika. For the sake of livelihood, for the reason of livelihood, one engages in matchmaking; this is an offense of Saṅghādisesa. For the sake of livelihood, for the reason of livelihood, one says, 'He who lives in your monastery is an Arahant'; this is an offense of Thullaccaya. For the sake of livelihood, for the reason of livelihood, a bhikkhu requests choice foods for his own sake and eats them; this is an offense of Pācittiya. For the sake of livelihood, for the reason of livelihood, a bhikkhunī requests choice foods for her own sake and eats them; this is an offense of Pāṭidesanīya. For the sake of livelihood, for the reason of livelihood, being not ill, one requests soup or rice for one's own sake and eats it; this is an offense of Dukkaṭa.

Khādantassa tissoti manussamaṃse thullaccayaṃ, avasesesu akappiyamaṃsesu dukkaṭaṃ, bhikkhuniyā lasuṇe pācittiyaṃ. Pañca bhojanapaccayāti avassutā avassutassa purisassa hatthato bhojanaṃ gahetvā tattheva [M 0.0232] manussamaṃsaṃ lasuṇaṃ attano atthāya viññāpetvā gahitapaṇītabhojanāni avasesañca akappiyamaṃsaṃ pakkhipitvā vomissakaṃ omadditvā ajjhoharamānā [V 0.0235] saṅghādisesaṃ, thullaccayaṃ, pācittiyaṃ, pāṭidesanīyaṃ, dukkaṭanti imā pañca āpattiyo bhojanapaccayā āpajjati.

As for 'the three for one who is eating': On account of human flesh, a Thullaccaya is incurred; on account of the remaining unsuitable meats, a Dukkaṭa is incurred; for a bhikkhunī, on account of garlic, a Pācittiya is incurred. As for 'five due to the condition of food': When a lustful bhikkhunī, having taken food from the hand of a lustful man, right there adds and kneads together until mixed human flesh, garlic, choice foods that had been requested and received for her own sake, and the remaining unsuitable meat, and consumes it, she incurs these five offenses due to the condition of food: Saṅghādisesa, Thullaccaya, Pācittiya, Pāṭidesanīya, and Dukkaṭa.

Pañca ṭhānānīti “ukkhittānuvattikāya bhikkhuniyā yāvatatiyaṃ samanubhāsanāya appaṭinissajjantiyā ñattiyā dukkaṭaṃ, dvīhi kammavācāhi thullaccayaṃ, kammavācāpariyosāne āpatti pārājikassa, saṅghabhedāya parakkamanādīsu saṅghādiseso, pāpikāya diṭṭhiyā appaṭinissagge pācittiya”nti evaṃ sabbā yāvatatiyakā pañca ṭhānāni gacchanti. Pañcannañceva āpattīti āpatti nāma pañcannaṃ sahadhammikānaṃ hoti, tattha dvinnaṃ nippariyāyena āpattiyeva, sikkhāmānasāmaṇerisāmaṇerānaṃ pana akappiyattā na vaṭṭati. Iminā pariyāyena tesaṃ āpatti na desāpetabbā, daṇḍakammaṃ pana tesaṃ kātabbaṃ. Pañcannaṃ adhikaraṇena cāti adhikaraṇañca pañcannamevāti attho. Etesaṃyeva hi pañcannaṃ pattacīvarādīnaṃ atthāya vinicchayavohāro adhikaraṇanti vuccati, gihīnaṃ pana aḍḍakammaṃ nāma hoti.

As for 'five occasions': 'For a bhikkhunī who is a follower of a suspended one, who does not give it up after being admonished up to the third time with a formal procedure: by the motion, it is a Dukkaṭa; by the two proclamations, it is a Thullaccaya; at the end of the proclamation, it is an offense of Pārājika. For striving, etc., for schism in the Saṅgha, it is a Saṅghādisesa. For not abandoning a wicked view, it is a Pācittiya.' Thus, all 'up to the third time' offenses lead to five occasions. As for 'an offense for five': An offense is for the five co-religionists. Among them, for two (the bhikkhu and bhikkhunī), it is unequivocally an offense. But for female probationers, female novices, and male novices, because it is unsuitable, it is not allowable. By this reasoning, an offense for them should not be confessed, but a disciplinary action should be taken against them. As for 'and by a legal question for five': The meaning is that a legal question is also only for the five. Indeed, for these very five, the speech for making a decision for the sake of bowls, robes, etc., is called a 'legal question'. For householders, however, it is called a lawsuit.

Pañcannaṃ vinicchayo hotīti pañcannaṃ sahadhammikānaṃyeva vinicchayo nāma [P 7.1387] hoti. Pañcannaṃ vūpasamena cāti etesaṃyeva pañcannaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ vinicchitaṃ vūpasantaṃ nāma hotīti attho. Pañcannañceva anāpattīti etesaṃyeva pañcannaṃ anāpatti nāma hotīti attho. Tīhi ṭhānehi sobhatīti saṅghādīhi tīhi kāraṇehi sobhati. Katavītikkamo hi puggalo sappaṭikammaṃ āpattiṃ saṅghamajjhe gaṇamajjhe puggalasantike vā paṭikaritvā abbhuṇhasīlo pākatiko hoti, tasmā tīhi ṭhānehi sobhatīti vuccati.

As for 'there is a decision of five': A decision is only for the five co-religionists. As for 'and by the settlement for five': The meaning is that the legal question of these very five, having been decided, is called 'settled'. As for 'no-offense for five': The meaning is that 'no-offense' is for these very five. As for 'one shines in three ways': One shines for three reasons, such as the Saṅgha. Indeed, a person who has committed a transgression, having remedied a remediable offense in the midst of the Saṅgha, in the midst of a group, or in the presence of an individual, becomes one of fresh virtue, of a normal state. Therefore, it is said, 'one shines in three ways'.

Dve kāyikā rattinti bhikkhunī rattandhakāre purisassa hatthapāse ṭhānanisajjasayanāni kappayamānā pācittiyaṃ, hatthapāsaṃ vijahitvā ṭhānādīni kappayamānā dukkaṭanti dve kāyadvārasambhavā āpattiyo rattiṃ āpajjati. Dve kāyikā divāti eteneva upāyena divā paṭicchanne okāse dve āpattiyo āpajjati. Nijjhāyantassa ekā āpattīti “na ca, bhikkhave, sārattena mātugāmassa aṅgajātaṃ upanijjhāyitabbaṃ[M 0.0233]. Yo upanijjhāyeyya, āpatti dukkaṭassā”ti (pārā. 266) nijjhāyantassa ayamekā āpatti. Ekā piṇḍapātapaccayāti “na ca, bhikkhave, bhikkhādāyikāya mukhaṃ oloketabba”nti (cūḷava. 366) ettha dukkaṭāpatti, antamaso yāguṃ vā byañjanaṃ vā dentassa sāmaṇerassāpi hi mukhaṃ ullokayato [V 0.0236] dukkaṭameva. Kurundiyaṃ pana “ekā piṇḍapātapaccayāti bhikkhuniparipācitaṃ piṇḍapātaṃ bhuñjantassa pācittiya”nti vuttaṃ.

Two bodily offenses at night: A bhikkhunī who, in the darkness of night, prepares a place for standing, sitting, or sleeping within a man's arm's reach, incurs a pācittiya offense; if she prepares a place for standing and so on having gone beyond arm's reach, she incurs a dukkaṭa offense. Thus, at night, she incurs two offenses arising from the body-door. Two bodily offenses during the day: By this same method, two offenses are incurred during the day in a concealed place. One offense for gazing: 'Bhikkhus, one should not gaze intently at the private parts of a woman with lust. Whoever does so incurs a dukkaṭa offense.' Thus, for one who gazes, this is one offense. One offense on account of almsfood: 'Bhikkhus, one should not look at the face of a female alms-giver.' Herein, a dukkaṭa offense is incurred; at the very least, for looking up at the face of even a novice who is giving gruel or curry, a dukkaṭa offense is incurred. However, in the Kurundī, it is said: 'One offense on account of almsfood means a pācittiya offense is incurred for eating almsfood prepared by a bhikkhunī.'

Aṭṭhānisaṃse sampassanti kosambakakkhandhake vuttānisaṃse. Ukkhittakā tayo vuttāti āpattiyā adassane appaṭikamme pāpikāya ca diṭṭhiyā appaṭinissaggeti. Tecattālīsa sammāvattanāti tesaṃyeva ukkhittakānaṃ ettakesu vattesu vattanā.

Seeing the eight benefits: this refers to the benefits spoken of in the Kosambakakkhandhaka. Three suspended ones are spoken of: one suspended for not seeing an offense, one for not making amends for an offense, and one for not relinquishing a wrong view. Forty-three proper conducts: this refers to the conduct of those very suspended bhikkhus in these forty-three duties.

Pañcaṭhāne musāvādoti pārājikasaṅghādisesathullaccayapācittiyadukkaṭasaṅkhāte pañcaṭṭhāne musāvādo gacchati. Cuddasa paramanti vuccatīti dasāhaparamādinayena heṭṭhā vuttaṃ. Dvādasa pāṭidesanīyāti bhikkhūnaṃ cattāri bhikkhunīnaṃ aṭṭha. Catunnaṃ desanāya cāti catunnaṃ accayadesanāyāti attho. Katamā pana sāti? Devadattena payojitānaṃ abhimārānaṃ accayadesanā, anuruddhattherassa upaṭṭhāyikāya accayadesanā, vaḍḍhassa licchavino accayadesanā, vāsabhagāmiyattherassa ukkhepanīyakammaṃ katvā āgatānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ accayadesanāti ayaṃ catunnaṃ accayadesanā [P 7.1388] nāma.

False speech in five instances: False speech leads to the five instances designated as pārājika, saṅghādisesa, thullaccaya, pācittiya, and dukkaṭa. Fourteen are called 'at most': this was stated below by the method beginning with 'at most ten days'. Twelve to be confessed: four for bhikkhus and eight for bhikkhunīs. And by the confession of four: the meaning is the confession of transgression by four. And what is that? The confession of transgression by the assassins employed by Devadatta; the confession of transgression by the female attendant of the Elder Anuruddha; the confession of transgression by Vaḍḍha the Licchavi; and the confession of transgression by the bhikkhus who, having performed the act of suspension upon the Elder Vāsabhagāmiya, had returned. This is called the confession of transgression by four.

Aṭṭhaṅgiko musāvādoti “pubbevassa hoti musā bhaṇissa”nti ādiṃ katvā “vinidhāya sañña”nti pariyosānehi (pāci. 4-5; pari. 459) aṭṭhahi aṅgehi aṭṭhaṅgiko. Uposathaṅgānipi pāṇaṃ na hanetiādinā nayena vuttāneva. Aṭṭha dūteyyaṅgānīti “idha, bhikkhave, bhikkhu sotā ca hoti sāvetā cā”tiādinā (cūḷava. 347) nayena saṅghabhedake vuttāni. Aṭṭha titthiyavattāni mahākhandhake vuttāni.

Eightfold false speech: it is eightfold by the eight factors beginning with 'beforehand he has the intention: "I will speak falsely,"' and ending with 'misrepresenting perception.' The Uposatha factors also are just as stated by the method beginning with 'one should not kill a living being.' The eight factors of a messenger: these are spoken of in the Saṅghabhedakakkhandhaka by the method beginning with 'Here, Bhikkhus, a bhikkhu is a hearer and a reporter...' The eight practices of the sectarians are spoken of in the Mahākhandhaka.

Aṭṭhavācikā upasampadāti bhikkhunīnaṃ upasampadaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ. Aṭṭhannaṃ paccuṭṭhātabbanti bhattagge aṭṭhannaṃ bhikkhunīnaṃ itarāhi paccuṭṭhāya āsanaṃ dātabbaṃ[M 0.0234]. Bhikkhunovādako aṭṭhahīti aṭṭhahaṅgehi samannāgato bhikkhu bhikkhunovādako sammannitabbo.

The eight-statement ordination: this is said in reference to the ordination of bhikkhunīs. For eight one should rise up: in the refectory, other bhikkhunīs should rise up and give a seat to eight bhikkhunīs. An advisor to bhikkhunīs by eight: a bhikkhu endowed with eight factors should be appointed as an advisor to bhikkhunīs.

Ekassa chejjanti gāthāya navasu janesu yo salākaṃ gāhetvā saṅghaṃ bhindati, tasseva chejjaṃ hoti, devadatto viya pārājikaṃ āpajjati. Bhedakānuvattakānaṃ catunnaṃ thullaccayaṃ kokālikādīnaṃ [V 0.0237] viya, dhammavādīnaṃ catunnaṃ anāpatti. Imā pana āpattiyo ca anāpattiyo ca sabbesaṃ ekavatthukā saṅghabhedavatthukā eva.

In the verse beginning 'For one, there is cutting off': Among nine persons, whichever bhikkhu, having caused the voting-slips to be taken, splits the Saṅgha, for him alone there is a cutting off; like Devadatta, he incurs a pārājika offense. For the four followers of the schismatic, there is a thullaccaya offense, like for Kokālika and others; for the four who speak in accordance with the Dhamma, there is no offense. Furthermore, these offenses and non-offenses for all of them have a single basis; they have only the basis of splitting the Saṅgha.

Nava āghātavatthūnīti gāthāya navahīti navahi bhikkhūhi saṅgho bhijjati. Ñattiyā karaṇā navāti ñattiyā kātabbāni kammāni navāti attho. Sesaṃ uttānameva.

In the verse beginning 'Nine grounds for resentment': the word 'by nine' means the Saṅgha is split by nine bhikkhus. 'Nine acts by announcement': the meaning is that the formal acts to be done by announcement are nine. The rest is self-evident.

(4) Avandanīyapuggalādivaṇṇanā

(4) Exposition on Persons Unworthy of Veneration, etc.

477. Dasa puggalā nābhivādetabbāti senāsanakkhandhake vuttā dasa janā. Añjali sāmīcena cāti sāmīcikammena saddhiṃ añjali ca tesaṃ na kātabbo, neva pānīyāpucchanatālavaṇṭaggahaṇādi khandhakavattaṃ tesaṃ dassetabbaṃ, na añjali paggaṇhitabboti attho. Dasannaṃ dukkaṭanti tesaṃyeva dasannaṃ evaṃ karontassa dukkaṭaṃ hoti. Dasa cīvaradhāraṇāti dasa divasāni atirekacīvarassa dhāraṇā anuññātāti attho.

477. As for 'Ten persons are not to be venerated,' this refers to the ten persons mentioned in the Senāsanakkhandhaka. As for 'and an añjali with proper conduct,' it means that an añjali together with proper conduct should not be performed towards them. The duties of the Khandhaka, such as asking about drinking water, taking a palm-leaf fan, and so on, should not be shown to them. An añjali should not be raised; this is the meaning. As for 'For the ten, it is a dukkaṭa offense,' it means that for one who acts thus towards those same ten persons, there is a dukkaṭa offense. As for 'Ten [days for] wearing a robe,' it means that the wearing of an extra robe for ten days is permitted.

Pañcannaṃ vassaṃvuṭṭhānaṃ, dātabbaṃ idha cīvaranti pañcannaṃ sahadhammikānaṃ sammukhāva dātabbaṃ. Sattannaṃ santeti disāpakkantaummattakakhittacittavedanāṭṭānaṃ tiṇṇañca ukkhittakānanti imesaṃ sattannaṃ sante patirūpe gāhake parammukhāpi dātabbaṃ. Soḷasannaṃ na dātabbanti sesānaṃ cīvarakkhandhake vuttānaṃ paṇḍakādīnaṃ soḷasannaṃ na dātabbaṃ.

As for 'To the five who have completed the rains-residence, a robe should be given here,' it means a robe should be given in the presence of five co-religionists. As for 'For the seven, if there is [a proxy],' it means for these seven—a monk who has gone to another region, an insane monk, a monk with a deranged mind, a monk afflicted by pain, and the three kinds of suspended monks—if there is a suitable proxy, a robe should be given even in their absence. As for 'To the sixteen, it should not be given,' it means a robe should not be given to the remaining sixteen persons, such as the paṇḍaka and so on, who are mentioned in the Cīvarakkhandhaka.

Katisataṃ rattisataṃ, āpattiyo chādayitvānāti [P 7.1389] katisataṃ āpattiyo rattisataṃ chādayitvāna. Dasasataṃ rattisataṃ, āpattiyo chādayitvānāti dasasataṃ āpattiyo rattisataṃ chādayitvāna. Ayañhettha saṅkhepattho – yo divase sataṃ sataṃ saṅghādisesāpattiyo āpajjitvā dasa dasa divase paṭicchādeti, tena rattisataṃ āpattisahassaṃ paṭicchāditaṃ hoti, so sabbāva tā āpattiyo dasāhapaṭicchannāti [M 0.0235] parivāsaṃ yācitvā dasa rattiyo vasitvāna mucceyya pārivāsikoti.

As for 'For how many hundreds, for a hundred nights, having concealed offenses?', it means having concealed how many hundreds of offenses for a hundred nights. As for 'For ten hundreds, for a hundred nights, having concealed offenses?', it means having concealed ten hundred offenses for a hundred nights. Indeed, this is the meaning in brief here: A monk who, having committed one hundred Saṅghādisesa offenses each day, conceals them for ten days—by him, a thousand offenses have been concealed for a hundred nights. For all those offenses, having requested a consolidated probation on the grounds that they were 'concealed for ten days,' and having lived under it for ten nights, he would be released from that probation.

Dvādasa kammadosā vuttāti apalokanakammaṃ adhammenavaggaṃ, adhammenasamaggaṃ, dhammenavaggaṃ, tathā [V 0.0238] ñattikammañattidutiyakammañatticatutthakammānipīti evaṃ ekekasmiṃ kamme tayo tayo katvā dvādasa kammadosā vuttā.

As for 'Twelve faults of formal acts have been stated,' it means: an act by consent may be unlawful and by an incomplete chapter, unlawful and by a complete chapter, or lawful and by an incomplete chapter. Similarly, an act by motion, an act by motion and a second announcement, and an act by motion and a fourth announcement also have these three faults each. Thus, by taking three for each formal act, twelve faults of formal acts have been stated.

Catasso kammasampattiyoti apalokanakammaṃ dhammenasamaggaṃ, tathā sesānipīti evaṃ catasso kammasampattiyo vuttā.

As for 'Four accomplishments of formal acts,' it means: an act by consent that is lawful and by a complete chapter; similarly for the remaining acts as well. Thus, four accomplishments of formal acts have been stated.

Cha kammānīti adhammenavaggakammaṃ, adhammenasamaggakammaṃ, dhammapatirūpakenavaggakammaṃ, dhammapatirūpakenasamaggakammaṃ, dhammenavaggakammaṃ, dhammenasamaggakammanti evaṃ cha kammāni vuttāni. Ekettha dhammikā katāti ekaṃ dhammena samaggakammamevettha dhammikaṃ katanti attho. Dutiyagāthāvissajjanepi etadeva dhammikaṃ.

As for 'Six formal acts,' it means: an act that is unlawful and by an incomplete chapter; an act that is unlawful and by a complete chapter; an act that is a counterfeit of the Dhamma and by an incomplete chapter; an act that is a counterfeit of the Dhamma and by a complete chapter; an act that is lawful and by an incomplete chapter; and an act that is lawful and by a complete chapter. Thus, six formal acts have been stated. As for 'One among these is made lawful,' it means that among these, only the one act that is lawful and by a complete chapter is made lawful. Also in the answer to the second verse, this very one is made lawful.

Yaṃ desitāti yāni desitāni vuttāni pakāsitāni. Anantajinenātiādīsu pariyantaparicchedabhāvarahitattā anantaṃ vuccati nibbānaṃ, taṃ bhagavatā raññā sapattagaṇaṃ abhimadditvā rajjaṃ viya kilesagaṇaṃ abhimadditvā jitaṃ vijitaṃ adhigataṃ sampattaṃ, tasmā bhagavā “anantajino”ti vuccati. Sveva iṭṭhāniṭṭhesu nibbikāratāya tādi, vikkhambhanatadaṅgasamucchedapaṭipassaddhinissaraṇavivekasaṅkhātaṃ vivekapañcakaṃ addasāti vivekadassī; tena anantajinena tādinā vivekadassinā yāni āpattikkhandhāni desitāni vuttāni. Ekettha sammati vinā samathehīti ayamettha padasambandho, yāni satthārā satta āpattikkhandhāni desitāni, tattha ekāpi āpatti vinā samathehi na sammati, atha kho cha samathā cattāri adhikaraṇānīti sabbepime dhammā sammukhāvinayena sammanti, samāyogaṃ gacchanti. Ettha pana eko sammukhāvinayova vinā samathehi sammati, samathabhāvaṃ [P 7.1390] gacchati. Na hi tassa aññena samathena vinā anipphatti nāma atthi. Tena vuttaṃ – “ekettha sammati vinā samathehī”ti. Iminā tāva adhippāyena aṭṭhakathāsu attho vutto. Mayaṃ pana “vinā”ti nipātassa paṭisedhanamattamatthaṃ gahetvā “ekettha sammati vinā samathehī”ti [M 0.0236] etesu sattasu āpattikkhandhesu eko pārājikāpattikkhandho vinā samathehi sammatīti etamatthaṃ roceyyāma. Vuttampi cetaṃ “yā sā āpatti anavasesā, sā āpatti na katamena adhikaraṇena katamamhi ṭhāne na katamena samathena sammatī”ti.

As for 'What was taught,' it means those things that were taught, spoken, and made clear. In 'By the Infinite Conqueror,' etc.: Because it is without end or limit, Nibbāna is called 'infinite.' That Nibbāna was conquered, vanquished, attained, and reached by the Blessed One, having crushed the host of defilements, just as a king conquers a kingdom by crushing a host of enemies. Therefore, the Blessed One is called the 'Infinite Conqueror.' That same one, due to his imperturbability towards desirable and undesirable objects, is 'Such-like.' Because he saw the fivefold seclusion—namely, seclusion by suppression, by substitution of opposites, by cutting off, by tranquillization, and by escape—he is the 'Seer of Seclusion.' By that Infinite Conqueror, the Such-like One, the Seer of Seclusion, were the classes of offenses taught and spoken. As for 'Here one is settled without settlements,' this is the connection of the words here: Of the seven classes of offenses taught by the Teacher, not even one offense is settled without the settlements. Rather, all these things—the six settlements and the four legal issues—are settled by means of the face-to-face procedure; they come to a resolution. Here, however, the face-to-face procedure alone is settled without the other settlements; it reaches the state of a settlement. For indeed, there is no such thing as its non-completion without another settlement. Therefore it was said: 'Here one is settled without settlements.' With this intention, the meaning was stated in the ancient commentaries. We, however, taking the meaning of the particle 'vinā' as mere negation, would prefer this meaning for 'Here one is settled without settlements': Among these seven classes of offenses, one—the class of pārājika offenses—is settled without settlements. And this was also stated: 'That offense which is without remainder, that offense is not settled by any legal issue, in any situation, by any settlement.'

Chaūnadiyaḍḍhasatāti [V 0.0239] “idha, upāli, bhikkhu adhammaṃ dhammoti dīpeti, tasmiṃ adhammadiṭṭhi bhede adhammadiṭṭhi, tasmiṃ adhammadiṭṭhi bhede dhammadiṭṭhi, tasmiṃ adhammadiṭṭhi bhede vematiko, tasmiṃ dhammadiṭṭhi bhede adhammadiṭṭhi, tasmiṃ dhammadiṭṭhi bhede vematiko, tasmiṃ vematiko bhede adhammadiṭṭhi, tasmiṃ vematiko bhede dhammadiṭṭhi, tasmiṃ vematiko bhede vematiko”ti evaṃ yāni aṭṭhārasannaṃ bhedakaravatthūnaṃ vasena aṭṭhārasa aṭṭhakāni saṅghabhedakakkhandhake vuttāni, tesaṃ vasena chaūnadiyaḍḍhasataṃ āpāyikā veditabbā.

As for 'one hundred and fifty less six': 'Here, Upāli, a monk declares what is not-Dhamma as Dhamma. In that, having a wrong view, in a schism he has a wrong view; in that, having a wrong view, in a schism he has a right view; in that, having a wrong view, in a schism he is doubtful. In that, having a right view, in a schism he has a wrong view; in that, having a right view, in a schism he is doubtful. In that, being doubtful, in a schism he has a wrong view; in that, being doubtful, in a schism he has a right view; in that, being doubtful, in a schism he is doubtful.' Thus, by way of the eighteen grounds for causing a schism, eighteen octads have been stated in the chapter on schism in the Saṅgha. By way of these, one hundred and forty-four persons should be known as bound for the lower realms.

Aṭṭhārasa anāpāyikāti “idha, upāli, bhikkhu adhammaṃ dhammoti dīpeti, tasmiṃ dhammadiṭṭhi bhede dhammadiṭṭhi avinidhāya diṭṭhiṃ avinidhāya khantiṃ avinidhāya ruciṃ avinidhāya bhāvaṃ anussāveti, salākaṃ gāheti ‘ayaṃ dhammo, ayaṃ vinayo, idaṃ satthusāsanaṃ, imaṃ gaṇhatha, imaṃ rocethā’ti, ayampi kho, upāli, saṅghabhedako na āpāyiko na nerayiko na kappaṭṭho na atekiccho”ti evaṃ ekekasmiṃ vatthusmiṃ ekekaṃ katvā saṅghabhedakakkhandhakāvasāne vuttā aṭṭhārasa janā. Aṭṭhārasa aṭṭhakā chaūnadiyaḍḍhasatavissajjane vuttāyeva.

As for 'Eighteen not leading to downfall': 'Here, Upāli, a bhikkhu declares what is non-Dhamma as Dhamma. In that non-Dhamma, having the view that it is Dhamma, and in the schism, having the view that it is Dhamma, without abandoning his view, without abandoning his inclination, without abandoning his preference, without abandoning his intention, he repeatedly makes it known and causes voting-tickets to be taken, saying: “This is the Dhamma, this is the Vinaya, this is the Teacher’s teaching. Take this, approve of this.” This one too, Upāli, is a schism-maker, but he is not one destined for the plane of misery, not one destined for hell, not one who will remain for the aeon, and not one who is incurable.' Thus, taking each one in each case, are the eighteen persons spoken of at the end of the Chapter on Schism in the Sangha. The eighteen octads are indeed spoken of in the explanation of the one hundred and forty-four.

(5) Soḷasakammādivaṇṇanā

(5) Explanation of the Sixteen Kammas, and so forth.

478. Kati kammānītiādīnaṃ sabbagāthānaṃ vissajjanaṃ uttānamevāti.

478. The explanation of all the verses beginning with 'How many kammas?' and so forth is indeed clear.

Aparadutiyagāthāsaṅgaṇikavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the collection of the further second verses is concluded.

Sedamocanagāthā

The Sweat-Releasing Verses

(1) Avippavāsapañhāvaṇṇanā

(1) Commentary on the Question of Non-Separation

479. Sedamocanagāthāsu [P 7.1391][M 0.0237][V 0.0240] asaṃvāsoti uposathapavāraṇādinā saṃvāsena asaṃvāso. Sambhogo ekacco tahiṃ na labbhatīti akappiyasambhogo na labbhati, nahāpanabhojanādipaṭijagganaṃ pana mātarāyeva kātuṃ labbhati. Avippavāsena anāpattīti sahagāraseyyāya anāpatti. Pañhā mesā kusalehi cintitāti esā pañhā kusalehi paṇḍitehi cintitā. Assā vissajjanaṃ dārakamātuyā bhikkhuniyā veditabbaṃ, tassā hi puttaṃ sandhāyetaṃ vuttanti.

479. In the Sweat-Releasing Verses, 'non-communion' means non-communion by way of the communion of Uposatha, Pavāraṇā, and so forth. 'A certain sharing is not obtained there' means that improper sharing is not allowed; however, caring for by way of bathing, feeding, and so forth is allowed to be done by the mother herself. 'No offense through non-separation' means there is no offense in sleeping under the same roof. 'This question has been considered by the skilled' means this question has been considered by the skilled and wise. Its answer should be known through the bhikkhunī who is the child's mother, for this was said with reference to her son.

Avissajjitagāthā garubhaṇḍaṃ sandhāya vuttā, attho panassā garubhaṇḍavinicchaye vuttoyeva.

The unanswered verse was spoken with reference to heavy articles, and its meaning is indeed stated in the 'Decision on Heavy Articles'.

Dasa puggale na vadāmīti senāsanakkhandhake vutte dasa puggale na vadāmi. Ekādasa vivajjiyāti ye mahākhandhake ekādasa vivajjanīyapuggalā vuttā, tepi na vadāmi. Ayaṃ pañhā naggaṃ bhikkhuṃ sandhāya vuttā.

As for 'I do not speak of ten persons,' it means: I do not speak of the ten persons mentioned in the Chapter on Lodgings. As for 'Eleven are to be avoided,' it means: the eleven persons to be avoided who are mentioned in the Great Chapter, I do not speak of them either. This question was spoken with reference to a naked bhikkhu.

Kathaṃ nu sikkhāya asādhāraṇoti pañhā nahāpitapubbakaṃ bhikkhuṃ sandhāya vuttā. Ayañhi khurabhaṇḍaṃ pariharituṃ na labhati, aññe labhanti; tasmā sikkhāya asādhāraṇo.

The question, 'How is he uncommon in the training?' was spoken with reference to a bhikkhu who was formerly a barber. For this one is not allowed to possess a razor-case, while others are allowed; therefore, he is uncommon in the training.

Taṃ puggalaṃ katamaṃ vadanti buddhāti ayaṃ pañhā nimmitabuddhaṃ sandhāya vuttā.

The question, 'Which person do they call a Buddha?' was spoken with reference to a created Buddha.

Adhonābhiṃ [V 0.0241] vivajjiyāti adhonābhiṃ vivajjetvā. Ayaṃ pañhā yaṃ taṃ asīsakaṃ kabandhaṃ, yassa ure akkhīni ceva mukhañca hoti, taṃ sandhāya vuttā.

'Below the navel is to be avoided' means: having avoided below the navel. This question was spoken with reference to that which is a headless torso, on whose chest there are both eyes and a mouth.

Bhikkhu saññācikāya kuṭinti ayaṃ pañhā tiṇacchādanaṃ kuṭiṃ sandhāya vuttā. Dutiyapañhā sabbamattikāmayaṃ kuṭiṃ sandhāya vuttā.

The question, 'A bhikkhu, by requesting, a hut...' was spoken with reference to a grass-thatched hut. The second question was spoken with reference to a hut made entirely of clay.

Āpajjeyya garukaṃ chejjavatthunti ayaṃ pañhā vajjapaṭicchādikaṃ bhikkhuniṃ sandhāya vuttā. Dutiyapañhā paṇḍakādayo abhabbapuggale sandhāya vuttā. Ekādasapi hi te gihibhāveyeva pārājikaṃ pattā.

The question, 'Would one fall into a serious offense requiring expulsion?' was spoken with reference to a bhikkhunī who conceals an offense. The second question was spoken with reference to incapable persons such as paṇḍakas. For those eleven, even while in the state of a householder, have attained a state of defeat.

Vācāti [M 0.0238] vācāya anālapanto. Giraṃ no ca pare bhaṇeyyāti “iti ime sossantī”ti parapuggale sandhāya saddampi na nicchāreyya. Ayaṃ pañhā “santiṃ āpattiṃ nāvikareyya, sampajānamusāvādassa hotī”ti imaṃ musāvādaṃ sandhāya vuttā. Tassa hi bhikkhuno adhammikāya paṭiññāya tuṇhībhūtassa nisinnassa manodvāre āpatti nāma natthi. Yasmā pana āvikātabbaṃ na āvikaroti, tenassa [P 7.1392] vacīdvāre akiriyato ayaṃ āpatti samuṭṭhātīti veditabbā.

'Speech' means: not speaking with speech. 'And let others not speak a word' means: one should not even utter a sound with reference to other persons, thinking, 'Thus, they will hear.' This question was spoken with reference to this falsehood: 'Should one not make known an existing offense, it becomes for him a deliberate falsehood.' For that bhikkhu who is sitting silently due to an unlawful declaration, there is no offense, so to speak, at the mind-door. However, because he does not make known what should be made known, this offense arises for him at the speech-door through inaction, it should be understood.

Saṅghādisesā caturoti ayaṃ pañhā aruṇugge gāmantarapariyāpannaṃ nadipāraṃ okkantabhikkhuniṃ sandhāya vuttā, sā hi sakagāmato paccūsasamaye nikkhamitvā aruṇuggamanakāle vuttappakāraṃ nadipāraṃ okkantamattāva rattivippavāsagāmantaranadipāragaṇamhāohīyanalakkhaṇena ekappahāreneva caturo saṅghādisese āpajjati.

The question, 'Four Saṅghādisesa offenses...' was spoken with reference to a bhikkhunī who, at sunrise, had gone down to the other bank of a river that was included within another village. For she, having left her own village at dawn, at the time of sunrise, merely by having gone down to the other bank of the river of the aforesaid type, falls into four Saṅghādisesa offenses with a single stroke, by way of the characteristics of: spending the night away, being in another village, being on the other side of a river, and being left behind by the group.

Siyā āpattiyo nānāti ayaṃ pañhā ekatoupasampannā dve bhikkhuniyo sandhāya vuttā. Tāsu hi bhikkhūnaṃ santike ekatoupasampannāya hatthato gaṇhantassa pācittiyaṃ, bhikkhunīnaṃ santike ekatoupasampannāya hatthato gaṇhantassa dukkaṭaṃ.

The question, 'Might there be different offenses?' was spoken with reference to two bhikkhunīs ordained on one side only. For among them, for one who takes a robe from the hand of one ordained on one side in the presence of the bhikkhus, there is a Pācittiya offense; for one who takes a robe from the hand of one ordained on one side in the presence of the bhikkhunīs, there is a Dukkaṭa offense.

Caturo janā saṃvidhāyāti ācariyo ca tayo ca antevāsikā chamāsakaṃ bhaṇḍaṃ avahariṃsu, ācariyassa sāhatthikā tayo māsakā, āṇattiyāpi tayova tasmā thullaccayaṃ āpajjati[V 0.0242], itaresaṃ sāhatthiko ekeko, āṇattikā pañcāti tasmā pārājikaṃ āpajjiṃsu. Ayamettha saṅkhepo. Vitthāro pana adinnādānapārājike saṃvidāvahāravaṇṇanāyaṃ vutto.

'Four persons, having conspired...': A teacher and three resident pupils stole goods worth six māsakas. For the teacher, there were three māsakas by his own hand and also three by command; therefore, he falls into a Thullaccaya offense. For the others, there was one each by their own hand and five by command; therefore, they fell into a Pārājika offense. This is the summary here. The detailed explanation, however, is given in the 'Commentary on Theft by Conspiracy' in the Pārājika on Taking What Is Not Given.

(2) Pārājikādipañhāvaṇṇanā

(2) Explanation of Questions on the Pārājika, etc.

480. Chiddaṃ tasmiṃ ghare natthīti ayaṃ pañhā dussakuṭiādīni santhatapeyyālañca sandhāya vuttā.

480. The question, 'There is no hole in that house,' was spoken in reference to small huts made of cloth and the like, and to the santhatapeyyāla.

Telaṃ madhuṃ phāṇitanti gāthā liṅgaparivattaṃ sandhāya vuttā.

The verse, 'Oil, honey, molasses...' was spoken in reference to a person who has undergone a change of gender.

Nissaggiyenāti gāthā pariṇāmanaṃ sandhāya vuttā. Yo hi saṅghassa pariṇatalābhato ekaṃ cīvaraṃ attano, ekaṃ aññassāti dve cīvarāni “ekaṃ [M 0.0239] mayhaṃ, ekaṃ tassa dehī”ti ekapayaogena pariṇāmeti, so nissaggiyapācittiyañceva suddhikapācittiyañca ekato āpajjati.

The verse beginning, 'With what is to be relinquished...' was spoken in reference to the diversion of a gain. For if a bhikkhu diverts two robes from a gain that has accrued to the Sangha—one for himself and one for another—saying with a single utterance, 'Give one to me, and give one to him,' he simultaneously incurs both a nissaggiya pācittiya and a suddha pācittiya offense.

Kammañca taṃ kuppeyya vaggapaccayāti ayaṃ pañhā dvādasayojanapamāṇesu bārāṇasiādīsu nagaresu gāmasīmaṃ sandhāya vuttā.

The question, 'And would that act be nullified by the factor of an incomplete quorum?' was spoken in reference to a village boundary in cities such as Bārāṇasī, which are twelve yojanas in extent.

Padavītihāramattenāti gāthā sañcarittaṃ sandhāya vuttā, atthopi cassā sañcarittavaṇṇanāyameva vutto.

The verse beginning, 'Merely by stepping over...' was spoken in reference to acting as a go-between. And its meaning was also stated in the very explanation of the training rule on acting as a go-between.

Sabbāni tāni nissaggiyānīti ayaṃ pañhā aññātikāya bhikkhuniyā dhovāpanaṃ sandhāya vuttā. Sace hi tiṇṇampi cīvarānaṃ kākaūhadanaṃ vā kaddamamakkhitaṃ vā kaṇṇaṃ gahetvā bhikkhunī udakena dhovati, bhikkhussa kāyagatāneva nissaggiyāni honti.

The question, 'All those are to be relinquished,' was spoken in reference to having a robe washed by an unrelated bhikkhunī. For if a bhikkhunī, holding it by the edge, washes with water any of the three robes that has been soiled by crow droppings or smeared with mud, they become subject to relinquishment for the bhikkhu even while they are on his body.

Saraṇagamanampi na tassa atthīti saraṇagamanaupasampadāpi natthi. Ayaṃ pana [P 7.1393] pañhā mahāpajāpatiyā upasampadaṃ sandhāya vuttā.

'He has not even gone for refuge' means that the ordination by going for refuge also does not exist. This question, however, was spoken in reference to the ordination of Mahāpajāpatī.

Haneyya [V 0.0243] anariyaṃ mandoti tañhi itthiṃ vā purisaṃ vā anariyaṃ haneyya. Ayaṃ pañhā liṅgaparivattena itthibhūtaṃ pitaraṃ purisabhūtañca mātaraṃ sandhāya vuttā.

'A foolish one should kill a non-noble one' — this means one should kill that non-noble parent, whether a woman or a man. This question was spoken in reference to a father who has become a woman and a mother who has become a man through a change of gender.

Na tenānantaraṃ phuseti ayaṃ pañhā migasiṅgatāpasasīhakumārādīnaṃ viya tiracchānamātāpitaro sandhāya vuttā.

The question, 'He does not thereby attain the immediate fruit,' was spoken in reference to animal mothers and fathers, like those of Migasiṅga the ascetic, Sīhakumāra, and so forth.

Acodayitvāti gāthā dūtenupasampadaṃ sandhāya vuttā. Codayitvāti gāthā paṇḍakādīnaṃ upasampadaṃ sandhāya vuttā. Kurundiyaṃ pana “paṭhamagāthā aṭṭha asammukhākammāni, dutiyā anāpattikassa kammaṃ sandhāya vuttā”ti āgataṃ.

The verse beginning 'Without having admonished' was spoken in reference to ordination by messenger. The verse beginning 'Having admonished' was spoken in reference to the ordination of paṇḍakas and so forth. In the Kurundī commentary, however, it is stated: 'The first verse was spoken in reference to the eight acts performed not in the presence of the individual; the second, in reference to performing an act upon one who is without offense.'

Chindantassa āpattīti vanappatiṃ chindantassa pārājikaṃ, tiṇalatādiṃ chindantassa pācittiyaṃ, aṅgajātaṃ chindantassa thullaccayaṃ. Chindantassa anāpattīti kese ca nakhe ca chindantassa anāpatti. Chādentassa āpattīti attano āpattiṃ chādentassa aññesaṃ vā āpattiṃ. Chādentassa anāpattīti gehādīni chādentassa anāpatti.

'An offense for one who cuts': for one who cuts a large tree, there is a pārājika; for one who cuts grass, creepers, and so on, there is a pācittiya; for one who cuts the genital organ, there is a thullaccaya. 'No offense for one who cuts': for one who cuts hair and nails, there is no offense. 'An offense for one who conceals': there is an offense for one who conceals their own offense or the offense of others. 'No offense for one who conceals': there is no offense for one who covers dwellings and so on.

Saccaṃ [M 0.0240] bhaṇantoti gāthāya “sikharaṇīsi ubhatobyañjanāsī”ti saccaṃ bhaṇanto garukaṃ āpajjati, sampajānamusāvāde pana musā bhāsato lahukāpatti hoti, abhūtārocane musā bhaṇanto garukaṃ āpajjati, bhūtārocane saccaṃ bhāsato lahukāpatti hotīti.

Regarding the verse beginning 'Saccaṃ bhaṇanto': one who speaks the truth, saying, 'You are a sikharaṇī, you are an ubhatobyañjanā,' incurs a grave offense (saṅghādisesa). In the case of a deliberate lie, however, one who speaks falsely incurs a light offense. In the case of claiming a superior human state that one does not possess, one who speaks falsely incurs a grave offense (pārājika). In the case of claiming a superior human state that one does possess, one who speaks the truth incurs a light offense (pācittiya).

(3) Pācittiyādipañhāvaṇṇanā

(3) Explanation of Questions beginning with Pācittiya

481. Adhiṭṭhitanti gāthā nissaggiyacīvaraṃ anissajjitvā paribhuñjantaṃ sandhāya vuttā.

481. The verse beginning 'Adhiṭṭhitaṃ' was spoken with reference to a monk who uses a robe that requires forfeiture without having relinquished it.

Atthaṅgate sūriyeti gāthā romanthakaṃ sandhāya vuttā.

The verse beginning 'Atthaṅgate sūriye' was spoken with reference to a monk who ruminates.

Na rattacittoti gāthāya ayamattho – rattacitto methunadhammapārājikaṃ āpajjati. Theyyacitto adinnādānapārājikaṃ, paraṃ maraṇāya cetento manussaviggahapārājikaṃ, saṅghabhedako pana [V 0.0244] na rattacitto na ca pana theyyacitto na cāpi so paraṃ maraṇāya cetayi, salākaṃ panassa dentassa hoti chejjaṃ, pārājikaṃ hoti, salākaṃ paṭiggaṇhantassa bhedakānuvattakassa thullaccayaṃ.

Of the verse beginning 'Na rattacitto', this is the meaning: one with a lustful mind incurs the Pārājika offense for sexual intercourse. One with a thieving mind incurs the Pārājika offense for taking what is not given. One who intends another's death incurs the Pārājika offense for killing a human being. A schismatic, however, is not of lustful mind, nor of thieving mind, nor does he intend another's death. But for him who gives a voting ticket, it is a cutting off; it is a Pārājika offense. For the follower of the schismatic who receives the ticket, it is a Thullaccaya offense.

Gaccheyya aḍḍhayojananti ayaṃ pañhā suppatiṭṭhitanigrodhasadisaṃ ekakulassa rukkhamūlaṃ sandhāya vuttā.

This question, 'Gaccheyya aḍḍhayojanaṃ', was spoken with reference to the foot of a tree belonging to a single family, similar to a well-established banyan tree.

Kāyikānīti ayaṃ gāthā sambahulānaṃ itthīnaṃ kese vā aṅguliyo vā ekato gaṇhantaṃ sandhāya vuttā.

This verse beginning 'Kāyikāni' was spoken with reference to a monk who takes hold of the hair or the fingers of many women at once.

Vācasikānīti ayaṃ gāthā “sabbā tumhe sikharaṇiyo”tiādinā nayena duṭṭhullabhāṇiṃ sandhāya vuttā.

This verse beginning 'Vācasikāni' was spoken with reference to a monk who speaks coarse speech, in the manner of that beginning, 'All of you are sikharaṇīs.'

Tissitthiyo methunaṃ taṃ na seveti tisso itthiyo vuttā, tāsupi yaṃ taṃ methunaṃ nāma, taṃ na [P 7.1394] sevati. Tayo puriseti tayo purisepi upagantvā methunaṃ na sevati. Tayo anariyapaṇḍaketi ubhatobyañjanasaṅkhāte tayo anariye tayo ca paṇḍaketi imepi cha jane upagantvā methunaṃ na sevati. Na cācare methunaṃ byañjanasminti anulomapārājikavasenapi methunaṃ nācarati. Chejjaṃ siyā methunadhammapaccayāti siyā methunadhammapaccayā pārājikanti. Ayaṃ pañhā aṭṭhavatthukaṃ sandhāya [M 0.0241] vuttā, tassā hi methunadhammassa pubbabhāgaṃ kāyasaṃsaggaṃ āpajjituṃ vāyamantiyā methunadhammapaccayā chejjaṃ hoti.

Regarding 'Tissitthiyo methunaṃ taṃ na seveti': three kinds of women are spoken of, and one does not engage in that which is called sexual intercourse even with them. Regarding 'Tayo purise': one does not engage in sexual intercourse even by approaching three kinds of men. Regarding 'Tayo anariyapaṇḍake': one does not engage in sexual intercourse even by approaching these six persons, namely, three ignoble ones known as hermaphrodites and three eunuchs. Regarding 'Na cācare methunaṃ byañjanasmiṃ': one does not practice sexual intercourse even by way of a conforming Pārājika. Regarding 'Chejjaṃ siyā methunadhammapaccayā': it would be a cutting off, a Pārājika offense, on account of the act of sexual intercourse. This question was spoken with reference to the Aṭṭhavatthukā bhikkhunī. Indeed, for her, while striving to fall into bodily contact, which is the preliminary part of the act of sexual intercourse, there is a cutting off on account of the act of sexual intercourse.

Mātaraṃ cīvaranti ayaṃ gāthā piṭṭhisamaye vassikasāṭikatthaṃ satuppādakaraṇaṃ sandhāya vuttā. Vinicchayo panassā vassikasāṭikasikkhāpadavaṇṇanāyameva vutto.

This verse beginning 'Mātaraṃ cīvaraṃ' was spoken with reference to making a reminder for the sake of a rains-bathing cloth during the 'back-season' (i.e., outside the proper time). But the legal decision on this has been stated by me in the explanation of the training rule on the rains-bathing cloth itself.

Kuddho ārādhako hotīti gāthā titthiyavattaṃ sandhāya vuttā. Titthiyo hi vattaṃ pūrayamāno titthiyānaṃ vaṇṇe bhaññamāne kuddho ārādhako hoti, vatthuttayassa vaṇṇe bhaññamāne kuddho gārayho hotīti tatthevassā vitthāro vutto. Dutiyagāthāpi tameva sandhāya vuttā.

The verse beginning 'Kuddho ārādhako hoti' was spoken with reference to the practice of sectarians. Indeed, a sectarian, while fulfilling a practice, becomes angry yet conciliatory when the virtues of sectarians are spoken of; but he becomes angry and is blameworthy when the virtues of the Triple Gem are spoken of. The detailed explanation of this has been stated by me right there. The second verse was also spoken with reference to that very same thing.

Saṅghādisesantiādi [V 0.0245] gāthā yā bhikkhunī avassutāva avassutassa purisassa hatthato piṇḍapātaṃ gahetvā manussamaṃsalasuṇapaṇītabhojanasesaakappiyamaṃsehi saddhiṃ omadditvā ajjhoharati, taṃ sandhāya vuttā.

The verse beginning 'Saṅghādisesa' was spoken with reference to a bhikkhunī who, being impassioned, receives almsfood from the hand of an impassioned man and consumes it, having mixed it with human flesh, garlic, fine foods, and the remaining unallowable meats.

Eko upasampanno eko anupasampannoti gāthā ākāsagataṃ sandhāya vuttā. Sace hi dvīsu sāmaṇeresu eko iddhiyā kesaggamattampi pathaviṃ muñcitvā nisinno hoti, so anupasampanno nāma hoti. Saṅghenāpi ākāse nisīditvā bhūmigatassa kammaṃ na kātabbaṃ. Sace karoti, kuppati.

The verse beginning 'Eko upasampanno eko anupasampanno' was spoken with reference to one who is in the sky. For if, among two novices, one sits having released contact with the earth by even a hair's breadth through psychic power, he is called 'not fully ordained'. An act should not be performed by the Saṅgha while seated in the sky for one who is on the ground. If it does so, the act is invalid.

Akappakatanti gāthā acchinnacīvarakaṃ bhikkhuṃ sandhāya vuttā. Tasmiṃyeva cassā sikkhāpade vitthārena vinicchayopi vutto.

The verse beginning 'Akappakataṃ' was spoken with reference to a monk whose robe has been snatched away. And the detailed legal decision on this has been stated by me in that very training rule.

Na deti na paṭiggaṇhātīti nāpi uyyojikā deti, na uyyojitā tassā hatthato gaṇhāti. Paṭiggaho tena na vijjatīti teneva kāraṇena uyyojikāya hatthato uyyojitāya paṭiggaho na vijjati. Āpajjati garukanti evaṃ santepi avassutassa hatthato piṇḍapātaggahaṇe uyyojentī saṅghādisesāpattiṃ āpajjati. Tañca paribhogapaccayāti tañca pana āpattiṃ āpajjamānā tassā uyyojitāya paribhogapaccayā āpajjati[P 7.1395][M 0.0242]. Tassā hi bhojanapariyosāne uyyojikāya saṅghādiseso hotīti. Dutiyagāthā tassāyeva udakadantaponaggahaṇe uyyojanaṃ sandhāya vuttā.

Regarding 'Na deti na paṭiggaṇhāti': the instigating bhikkhunī does not give, nor does the instigated bhikkhunī receive from her hand. Regarding 'Paṭiggaho tena na vijjati': for that very reason, there is no receiving by the instigated one from the hand of the instigator. Regarding 'Āpajjati garukaṃ': even so, the instigating bhikkhunī incurs a Saṅghādisesa offense for instigating the receiving of almsfood from the hand of an impassioned man. Regarding 'Tañca paribhogapaccayā': moreover, in incurring that offense, she incurs it on account of the instigated one's consumption. Indeed, at the conclusion of her meal, the instigating bhikkhunī incurs a Saṅghādisesa. The second verse was spoken with reference to instigating that same bhikkhunī in the matter of receiving water and a tooth-stick.

Na bhikkhunī no ca phuseyya vajjanti sattarasakesu hi aññataraṃ āpattiṃ āpajjitvā anādariyena chādayamānāpi bhikkhunī chādanapaccayā vajjaṃ na phusati, aññaṃ navaṃ āpattiṃ nāpajjati, paṭicchannāya vā appaṭicchannāya vā āpattiyā pakkhamānattameva labhati. Ayaṃ pana bhikkhunīpi na hoti, sāvasesañca garukaṃ āpajjitvā chādetvā vajjaṃ na phusati. Pañhā mesā kusalehi cintitāti ayaṃ kira pañhā ukkhittakabhikkhuṃ sandhāya vuttā. Tena hi saddhiṃ vinayakammaṃ natthi, tasmā so saṅghādisesaṃ āpajjitvā chādento vajjaṃ na phusatīti.

Regarding 'Na bhikkhunī no ca phuseyya vajjaṃ': Indeed, among the seventeen offenses for bhikkhunīs, even a bhikkhunī who, having fallen into one of them, conceals it out of disrespect, does not incur a fault on account of the concealment, nor does she fall into a new offense; for that offense, whether concealed or unconcealed, she only receives the pakkhamānatta probation. But this one is not a bhikkhunī, and having fallen into a grave offense with a remainder and concealed it, does not incur a fault. Regarding 'Pañhā mesā kusalehi cintitā': this question, it is said, was spoken with reference to a suspended monk. Indeed, since there is no disciplinary act that can be done with him, therefore, if he falls into a Saṅghādisesa and conceals it, he does not incur a fault.

Sedamocanagāthāvaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The explanation of the Sedamocana verses is concluded.

Pañcavaggo

The Fifth Chapter

Kammavaggavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Chapter on Acts

482. Kammavagge [M 0.0243][V 0.0246] catunnaṃ kammānaṃ nānākaraṇaṃ samathakkhandhake vuttameva. Kiñcāpi vuttaṃ, atha kho ayaṃ kammavinicchayo nāma ādito paṭṭhāya vuccamāno pākaṭo hoti, tasmā ādito paṭṭhāyevettha vattabbaṃ vadissāma. Cattārīti kammānaṃ gaṇanaparicchedavacanametaṃ. Kammānīti paricchinnakammanidassanaṃ. Apalokanakammaṃ nāma sīmaṭṭhakasaṅghaṃ sodhetvā chandārahānaṃ chandaṃ āharitvā samaggassa saṅghassa anumatiyā tikkhattuṃ sāvetvā kattabbaṃ kammaṃ. Ñattikammaṃ nāma vuttanayeneva samaggassa saṅghassa anumatiyā ekāya ñattiyā kattabbaṃ kammaṃ. Ñattidutiyakammaṃ nāma vuttanayeneva samaggassa saṅghassa anumatiyā ekāya ñattiyā ekāya ca anussāvanāyāti evaṃ ñattidutiyāya anussāvanāya kattabbaṃ kammaṃ. Ñatticatutthakammaṃ nāma vuttanayeneva samaggassa saṅghassa anumatiyā ekāya ñattiyā tīhi ca anussāvanāhīti evaṃ ñatticatutthāhi tīhi anussāvanāhi kattabbaṃ kammaṃ.

482. In the Chapter on Acts, the distinction among the four acts has already been stated in the Samathakkhandhaka. Although it has been stated, this decision on acts, when explained from the beginning, becomes clear. Therefore, we shall speak from the very beginning about what should be said here. 'Four' is a term that defines the acts by number. 'Acts' indicates the acts that have been so defined. An act by announcement (apalokanakamma) is an act to be performed after purifying the Sangha within the boundary, bringing the consent of those worthy of consent, and announcing it three times with the agreement of a united Sangha. An act by motion (ñattikamma) is an act to be performed, by the method already stated, with the agreement of a united Sangha, with a single motion. An act with a motion as the second part (ñattidutiyakamma) is an act to be performed, by the method already stated, with the agreement of a united Sangha, with one motion and one proclamation; thus, it is an act performed with a proclamation that is second to the motion. An act with a motion as the fourth part (ñatticatutthakamma) is an act to be performed, by the method already stated, with the agreement of a united Sangha, with one motion and three proclamations; thus, it is an act performed with three proclamations that are fourth after the motion.

Tattha apalokanakammaṃ apaloketvāva kātabbaṃ, ñattikammādivasena na kātabbaṃ. Ñattikammampi ekaṃ ñattiṃ ṭhapetvāva kātabbaṃ, apalokanakammādivasena [P 7.1396] na kātabbaṃ. Ñattidutiyakammaṃ pana apaloketvā kātabbampi atthi, akātabbampi atthi.

Among these, an act by announcement should be performed only by announcing; it should not be performed by way of an act by motion and so on. An act by motion, too, should be performed only by laying down a single motion; it should not be performed by way of an act by announcement and so on. As for an act with a motion as the second part, however, there are instances when it should be performed by announcing, and instances when it should not.

Tattha sīmāsammuti, sīmāsamūhananaṃ, kathinadānaṃ, kathinuddhāro, kuṭivatthudesanā, vihāravatthudesanāti imāni cha kammāni garukāni apaloketvā kātuṃ na vaṭṭanti, ñattidutiyakammavācaṃ sāvetvāva kātabbāni. Avasesā terasa sammutiyo senāsanaggāhakamatakacīvaradānādisammutiyo [V 0.0247] cāti etāni lahukakammāni apaloketvāpi kātuṃ vaṭṭanti, ñattikamma-ñatticatutthakammavasena pana na kātabbameva. Ñatticatutthakammavasena kayiramānaṃ daḷhataraṃ hoti, tasmā kātabbanti ekacce vadanti. Evaṃ pana sati kammasaṅkaro hoti, tasmā na kātabbanti paṭikkhittameva. Sace pana akkharaparihīnaṃ vā padaparihīnaṃ vā duruttapadaṃ vā hoti[M 0.0244], tassa sodhanatthaṃ punappunaṃ vattuṃ vaṭṭati. Idaṃ akuppakammassa daḷhīkammaṃ hoti, kuppakamme kammaṃ hutvā tiṭṭhati.

Among these, these six weighty acts—agreement on a boundary, removal of a boundary, the giving of the kathina, the withdrawal of the kathina, the designation of a hut-site, and the designation of a monastery-site—are not allowable to be performed by announcing; they must be performed only after proclaiming the formal statement of an act with a motion as the second part. The remaining thirteen authorizations, such as the authorizations for appointing a distributor of lodgings, for giving the robe of a deceased monk, and so on—these, being light acts, are allowable to be performed even by announcing. However, they should certainly not be performed by way of an act by motion or an act with a motion as the fourth part. Some teachers say, 'When performed by way of an act with a motion as the fourth part, it becomes firmer; therefore, it should be performed.' However, if this is so, there is a confusion of acts; therefore, it should not be performed, as that view has indeed been rejected. But if there is a deficiency of syllables, a deficiency of words, or a poorly recited word, it is allowable to recite it again and again for the purpose of correction. This is a strengthening of an unshakeable act; in the case of a shakeable act, it becomes a valid act.

Ñatticatutthakammaṃ ñattiñca tisso ca kammavācāyo sāvetvāva kātabbaṃ, apalokanakammādivasena na kātabbaṃ. Pañcahākārehi vipajjantīti pañcahi kāraṇehi vipajjanti.

An act with a motion as the fourth part must be performed only after proclaiming the motion and the three formal statements; it should not be performed by way of an act by announcement and so on. 'They fail in five ways' means they fail because of five reasons.

483. Sammukhākaraṇīyaṃ kammaṃ asammukhā karoti, vatthuvipannaṃ adhammakammanti ettha atthi kammaṃ sammukhākaraṇīyaṃ; atthi asammukhākaraṇīyaṃ; tattha asammukhākaraṇīyaṃ nāma dūtenupasampadā, pattanikkujjanaṃ, pattukkujjanaṃ, ummattakassa bhikkhuno ummattakasammuti, sekkhānaṃ kulānaṃ sekkhasammuti, channassa bhikkhuno brahmadaṇḍo, devadattassa pakāsanīyakammaṃ, appasādanīyaṃ dassentassa bhikkhuno bhikkhunisaṅghena kātabbaṃ avandanīyakammanti aṭṭhavidhaṃ hoti, taṃ sabbaṃ tattha tattha vuttanayeneva veditabbaṃ. Idaṃ aṭṭhavidhampi kammaṃ asammukhā kataṃ sukataṃ hoti akuppaṃ.

483. Regarding the text, 'One performs in absence an act that should be performed in presence; it is an unlawful act, defective in its basis': here, there is an act that should be performed in presence, and there is an act that should be performed in absence. Among these, an act that should be performed in absence is of eight kinds: ordination by messenger, the overturning of the bowl, the setting upright of the bowl, the authorization of insanity for an insane monk, the authorization of trainee status for families of trainees, the Brahmā-punishment for the monk Channa, the act of proclamation against Devadatta, and the act of non-veneration to be performed by the order of nuns towards a monk who shows what is displeasing. All of this should be understood by the method stated in the respective places. These eight kinds of acts, when performed in absence, are well-performed and unshakeable.

Sesāni sabbakammāni sammukhā eva kātabbāni – saṅghasammukhatā, dhammasammukhatā, vinayasammukhatā, puggalasammukhatāti imaṃ catubbidhaṃ sammukhāvinayaṃ upanetvāva kātabbāni. Evaṃ katāni hi sukatāni honti. Evaṃ akatāni panetāni imaṃ sammukhāvinayasaṅkhātaṃ vatthuṃ vinā katattā vatthuvipannāni nāma honti. Tena vuttaṃ – “sammukhākaraṇīyaṃ [P 7.1397] kammaṃ asammukhā karoti, vatthuvipannaṃ adhammakamma”nti.

All remaining acts must be performed only in presence. They must be performed only after having brought them to this fourfold presence-procedure: presence of the Sangha, presence of the Dhamma, presence of the Vinaya, and presence of the individual. For when performed thus, they are well-performed. But when not performed thus, these acts, because they are performed without this basis called the presence-procedure, are called 'acts with a defective basis'. Therefore it was said: 'One performs in absence an act that should be performed in presence; it is an unlawful act, defective in its basis.'

Paṭipucchākaraṇīyādīsupi paṭipucchādikaraṇameva vatthu, taṃ vatthuṃ vinā katattā tesampi vatthuvipannatā veditabbā. Idaṃ panettha vacanatthamattaṃ. Paṭipucchā karaṇīyaṃ appaṭipucchā karotīti pucchitvā codetvā sāretvā kātabbaṃ apucchitvā acodetvā asāretvā karoti. Paṭiññāya karaṇīyaṃ [V 0.0248] appaṭiññāya karotīti paṭiññaṃ āropetvā yathādinnāya paṭiññāya kātabbaṃ appaṭiññāya karontassa vippalapantassa balakkārena karoti. Sativinayārahassāti dabbamallaputtattherasadisassa khīṇāsavassa. Amūḷhavinayārahassāti gaggabhikkhusadisassa ummattakassa. Tassapāpiyasikakammārahassāti upavāḷabhikkhusadisassa ussannapāpassa. Esa nayo sabbattha.

Also in the case of acts that should be performed with questioning and so on, the very performance of questioning and so on is the basis; because they are performed without that basis, their state of having a defective basis should also be understood. This here is merely the meaning of the words. 'What should be done with questioning, one does without questioning' means that what should be done after questioning, reproving, and reminding, one does without questioning, without reproving, and without reminding. 'What should be done with an admission, one does without an admission' means that what should be done after having him make an admission and in accordance with the admission given, one acts forcefully against one who does not admit and speaks evasively. 'One worthy of a procedure based on mindfulness' refers to one who has destroyed the taints, like the Elder Dabba Mallaputta. 'One worthy of a procedure for one who is not confused' refers to an insane one, like the monk Gagga. 'One worthy of the act of further condemnation' refers to one with much evil, like the monk Upavāḷa. This is the method in all cases.

Anuposathe [M 0.0245] uposathaṃ karotīti anuposathadivase uposathaṃ karoti. Uposathadivaso nāma ṭhapetvā kattikamāsaṃ avasesesu ekādasasu māsesu bhinnassa saṅghassa sāmaggidivaso ca yathāvuttacātuddasapannarasā ca. Etaṃ tippakārampi uposathadivasaṃ ṭhapetvā aññasmiṃ divase uposathaṃ karonto anuposathe uposathaṃ karoti nāma. Yatra hi pattacīvarādīnaṃ atthāya appamattakena kāraṇena vivadantā uposathaṃ vā pavāraṇaṃ vā ṭhapenti, tattha tasmiṃ adhikaraṇe vinicchite “samaggā jātāmhā”ti antarā sāmaggiuposathaṃ kātuṃ na labhanti, karontehi anuposathe uposatho kato nāma hoti.

‘On a non-Uposatha day, one performs the Uposatha’ means one performs the Uposatha on a day that is not an Uposatha day. An Uposatha day is defined as, setting aside the month of Kattika, in the remaining eleven months, the day of harmony for a divided Saṅgha, and also the aforementioned fourteenth and fifteenth days. One who performs the Uposatha on another day, setting aside these three kinds of Uposatha days, is said to perform the Uposatha on a non-Uposatha day. Indeed, in whatever monastery, when they dispute over minor matters concerning bowls, robes, and so forth, and postpone the Uposatha or Pavāraṇā, then, after that legal issue is settled, even if they say, ‘We have become harmonious,’ they are not permitted to perform a harmony-Uposatha in the interim. By those who do perform it, it is called an Uposatha performed on a non-Uposatha day.

Apavāraṇāya pavāretīti apavāraṇādivase pavāreti; pavāraṇādivaso nāma ekasmiṃ kattikamāse bhinnassa saṅghassa sāmaggidivaso ca paccukkaḍḍhitvā ṭhapitadivaso ca dve ca puṇṇamāsiyo. Evaṃ catubbidhampi pavāraṇādivasaṃ ṭhapetvā aññasmiṃ divase pavārento apavāraṇāya pavāreti nāma. Idhāpi appamattakassa vivādassa vūpasame sāmaggipavāraṇaṃ kātuṃ na labhanti, karontehi apavāraṇāya pavāraṇā katā hoti. Apica ūnavīsativassaṃ vā antimavatthuṃ ajjhāpannapubbaṃ vā ekādasasu vā abhabbapuggalesu aññataraṃ upasampādentassapi vatthuvipannaṃ adhammakammaṃ hoti. Evaṃ vatthuto kammāni vipajjanti.

‘On a non-Pavāraṇā day, one performs the Pavāraṇā’ means one performs the Pavāraṇā on a day that is not a Pavāraṇā day. A Pavāraṇā day is defined as, in the one month of Kattika, the day of harmony for a divided Saṅgha, the day that has been postponed, and the two full-moon days. Thus, one who performs the Pavāraṇā on another day, setting aside these four kinds of Pavāraṇā days, is said to perform the Pavāraṇā on a non-Pavāraṇā day. Here too, upon the calming of a minor dispute, they are not permitted to perform a harmony-Pavāraṇā; by those who do perform it, the Pavāraṇā is performed on a non-Pavāraṇā day. Furthermore, for a preceptor who ordains someone under twenty years of age, or someone who has previously fallen into a terminal offense, or one among the eleven disqualified individuals, it is an unlawful act with a faulty object. Thus, acts fail due to the object.

484. Ñattito vipattiyaṃ pana vatthuṃ na parāmasatīti yassa upasampadādikammaṃ [P 7.1398] karoti, taṃ na parāmasati, tassa nāmaṃ na gaṇhāti. “Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, ayaṃ dhammarakkhito āyasmato buddharakkhitassa upasampadāpekkho”ti vattabbe “suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, āyasmato buddharakkhitassa upasampadāpekkho”ti vadati; evaṃ vatthuṃ na parāmasati.

484. Now, in the case of failure due to the motion, ‘one does not refer to the object’ means that when performing an act such as ordination, one does not refer to that person, nor does one state his name. When it should be said, ‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. This Dhammarakkhita is the ordination candidate of the venerable Buddharakkhita,’ one says, ‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. The ordination candidate of the venerable Buddharakkhita.’ In this way, one does not refer to the object.

Saṅghaṃ na parāmasatīti saṅghassa nāmaṃ na gaṇhāti. “Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, ayaṃ dhammarakkhito”ti [V 0.0249] vattabbe “suṇātu me bhante, ayaṃ dhammarakkhito”ti vadati; evaṃ saṅghaṃ na parāmasati.

‘One does not refer to the Saṅgha’ means one does not state the name of the Saṅgha. When it should be said, ‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. This is Dhammarakkhita,’ one says, ‘Venerable sirs, may you hear me. This is Dhammarakkhita.’ In this way, one does not refer to the Saṅgha.

Puggalaṃ na parāmasatīti yo upasampadāpekkhassa upajjhāyo, taṃ na parāmasati, tassa nāmaṃ na gaṇhāti. “Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, ayaṃ dhammarakkhito āyasmato buddharakkhitassa upasampadāpekkho”ti vattabbe [M 0.0246] “suṇātu me, bhante saṅgho, ayaṃ dhammarakkhito upasampadāpekkho”ti vadati; evaṃ puggalaṃ na parāmasati.

‘One does not refer to the individual’ means that one does not refer to the preceptor of the ordination candidate, nor does one state his name. When it should be said, ‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. This Dhammarakkhita is the ordination candidate of the venerable Buddharakkhita,’ one says, ‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. This Dhammarakkhita is an ordination candidate.’ In this way, one does not refer to the individual.

Ñattiṃ na parāmasatīti sabbena sabbaṃ ñattiṃ na parāmasati. Ñattidutiyakamme ñattiṃ aṭṭhapetvā dvikkhattuṃ kammavācāya eva anussāvanakammaṃ karoti. Ñatticatutthakammepi ñattiṃ aṭṭhapetvā catukkhattuṃ kammavācāya eva anussāvanakammaṃ karoti; evaṃ ñattiṃ na parāmasati.

‘One does not refer to the motion’ means one does not refer to the motion at all. In a ñattidutiya act, without stating the motion, one performs the act of announcement with only the formal statement twice. Also in a ñatticatuttha act, without stating the motion, one performs the act of announcement with only the formal statement four times; in this way, one does not refer to the motion.

Pacchā vā ñattiṃ ṭhapetīti paṭhamaṃ kammavācāya anussāvanakammaṃ katvā “esā ñattī”ti vatvā “khamati saṅghassa tasmā tuṇhī evametaṃ dhārayāmī”ti vadati; evaṃ pacchā ñattiṃ ṭhapeti. Iti imehi pañcahākārehi ñattito kammāni vipajjanti.

‘Or, one states the motion afterward’ means that having first performed the act of announcement with the formal statement, and then having said, ‘This is the motion,’ one says, ‘It is approved by the Saṅgha, therefore it is silent. Thus do I bear this in mind.’ In this way, one states the motion afterward. Thus, by these five modes, acts fail due to the motion.

485. Anussāvanato vipattiyaṃ pana vatthuādīni vuttanayeneva veditabbāni. Evaṃ pana nesaṃ aparāmasanaṃ hoti – “suṇātu me bhante saṅgho”ti paṭhamānussāvane “dutiyampi etamatthaṃ vadāmi, tatiyampi etamatthaṃ vadāmi, suṇātu me bhante saṅgho”ti dutiyatatiyānussāvanāsu vā “ayaṃ dhammarakkhito āyasmato buddharakkhitassa upasampadāpekkho”ti vattabbe “suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, āyasmato buddharakkhitassā”ti vadanto vatthuṃ na parāmasati nāma. “Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, ayaṃ dhammarakkhito”ti vattabbe “suṇātu me bhante, ayaṃ dhammarakkhito”ti vadanto saṅghaṃ na parāmasati nāma. “Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, ayaṃ dhammarakkhito [P 7.1399] āyasmato buddharakkhitassā”ti vattabbe “suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, ayaṃ dhammarakkhito upasampadāpekkho”ti vadanto puggalaṃ na parāmasati nāma.

485. Now, in the case of failure due to the announcement, the object and so forth should be understood in the manner already stated. Their non-reference is as follows: in the first announcement, “Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me,” or in the second and third announcements, “A second time I speak on this matter, a third time I speak on this matter, venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me,” when it should be said, “This Dhammarakkhita is the ordination candidate of the venerable Buddharakkhita,” one who says, “Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me, of the venerable Buddharakkhita,” is said to not refer to the object. When it should be said, “Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me, this Dhammarakkhita,” one who says, “Venerable sirs, may you hear me, this Dhammarakkhita,” is said to not refer to the Saṅgha. When it should be said, “Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me, this Dhammarakkhita is the ordination candidate of the venerable Buddharakkhita,” one who says, “Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me, this Dhammarakkhita is an ordination candidate,” is said to not refer to the individual.

Sāvanaṃ hāpetīti sabbena sabbaṃ kammavācāya anussāvanaṃ na karoti, ñattidutiyakamme dvikkhattuṃ ñattimeva ṭhapeti, ñatticatutthakamme catukkhattuṃ ñattimeva ṭhapeti; evaṃ anussāvanaṃ hāpeti[V 0.0250]. Yopi ñattidutiyakamme ekaṃ ñattiṃ ṭhapetvā ekaṃ kammavācaṃ anussāvento akkharaṃ vā chaḍḍeti, padaṃ vā duruttaṃ karoti, ayampi anussāvanaṃ hāpetiyeva. Ñatticatutthakamme pana ekaṃ ñattiṃ ṭhapetvā sakimeva vā dvikkhattuṃ vā kammavācāya anussāvanaṃ karontopi akkharaṃ vā padaṃ vā [M 0.0247] chaḍḍentopi duruttaṃ karontopi anussāvanaṃ hāpetiyevāti veditabbo.

‘He omits the announcement’ means that he does not perform the announcement with the formal statement at all.
