In a ñattidutiya act, he states only the motion twice; in a ñatticatuttha act, he states only the motion four times; thus, he omits the announcement. Also, one who, in a ñattidutiya act, after stating the motion once, performs the announcement with the formal statement once but drops a syllable or mispronounces a word, also indeed omits the announcement. Now, in a ñatticatuttha act, it should be understood that one who, after stating the motion once, performs the announcement with the formal statement only once or twice; or one who drops a syllable or a word; or one who mispronounces, indeed omits the announcement.

Duruttaṃ karotīti ettha pana ayaṃ vinicchayo – yo hi aññasmiṃ akkhare vattabbe aññaṃ vadati, ayaṃ duruttaṃ karoti nāma. Tasmā kammavācaṃ karontena bhikkhunā yvāyaṃ –

Concerning ‘makes a faulty utterance,’ this is the determination: whoever, when a certain syllable should be spoken, speaks another, this is called making a faulty utterance. Therefore, by a monk performing the formal statement—

“Sithilaṃ dhanitañca dīgharassaṃ, garukaṃ lahukañca niggahitaṃ;

Sambandhaṃ vavatthitaṃ vimuttaṃ, dasadhā byañjanabuddhiyā pabhedo”ti.

“Loose and firm, long and short, heavy and light, and the restrained; connected, separated, and released—these are the tenfold distinctions in the understanding of consonants.”

Vutto, ayaṃ suṭṭhu upalakkhetabbo. Ettha hi “sithilaṃ” nāma pañcasu vaggesu paṭhamatatiyaṃ. “Dhanitaṃ” nāma tesveva dutiyacatutthaṃ. “Dīgha”nti dīghena kālena vattabbaṃ ākārādi. “Rassa”nti tato upaḍḍhakālena vattabbaṃ akārādi. “Garuka”nti dīghameva. Yaṃ vā āyasmato buddharakkhitattherassa yassa nakkhamatīti evaṃ saṃyogaparaṃ katvā vuccati. “Lahuka”nti rassameva. Yaṃ vā āyasmato buddharakkhitatherassa yassa na khamatīti evaṃ asaṃyogaparaṃ katvā vuccati. “Niggahita”nti yaṃ karaṇāni niggahetvā avissajjetvā avivaṭena mukhena sānunāsikaṃ katvā vattabbaṃ. “Sambandha”nti yaṃ parapadena sambandhitvā “tuṇhissā”ti vā “tuṇhassā”ti vā vuccati. “Vavatthita”nti yaṃ parapadena asambandhaṃ katvā vicchinditvā “tuṇhī assā”ti vā “tuṇha assā”ti vā vuccati. “Vimutta”nti yaṃ karaṇāni aniggahetvā vissajjetvā vivaṭena mukhena anunāsikaṃ akatvā [P 7.1400] vuccati.

This has been stated and should be thoroughly understood. Herein, ‘loose’ refers to the first and third letters of the five groups. ‘Firm’ refers to the second and fourth of those same groups. ‘Long’ means to be pronounced with a long duration, such as the letter ‘ā’. ‘Short’ means to be pronounced in half that duration, such as the letter ‘a’. ‘Heavy’ is the long letter itself. Or, it is that which is pronounced having made it followed by a conjunct consonant, as in “āyasmato buddharakkhitattherassa yassa nakkhamati”. ‘Light’ is the short letter itself. Or, it is that which is pronounced having made it not followed by a conjunct consonant, as in “āyasmato buddharakkhitatherassa yassa na khamati”. ‘Restrained’ is that which should be pronounced by restraining the organs of speech, without releasing them, with the mouth not open, making it nasalized. ‘Connected’ is that which is connected with the following word and is pronounced as “tuṇhissā” or “tuṇhassā”. ‘Separated’ is that which, having been made unconnected with the following word and being separated, is pronounced as “tuṇhī assā” or “tuṇha assā”. ‘Released’ is that which is pronounced without restraining the organs of speech, releasing them, with the mouth open, and not making it nasalized.

Tattha “suṇātu me”ti vattabbe ta-kārassa tha-kāraṃ katvā “suṇāthu me”ti vacanaṃ sithilassa dhanitakaraṇaṃ nāma. Tathā “pattakallaṃ, esā ñattī”ti vattabbe “patthakallaṃ, esā ñatthī”tiādivacanañca. “Bhante saṅgho”ti vattabbe bha-kāra gha-kārānaṃ ba-kāra ga-kāre katvā “bante saṅgo”ti vacanaṃ dhanitassa sithilakaraṇaṃ nāma. “Suṇātu me”ti vivaṭena mukhena vattabbe pana “suṇaṃtu me”ti vā “esā ñattī”ti vattabbe “esaṃ ñattī”ti vā avivaṭena [V 0.0251] mukhena anunāsikaṃ katvā vacanaṃ vimuttassa niggahitavacanaṃ nāma. “Pattakalla”nti avivaṭena mukhena anunāsikaṃ katvā vattabbe “pattakallā”ti vivaṭena mukhena anunāsikaṃ akatvā vacanaṃ niggahitassa vimuttavacanaṃ nāma.

Herein, when one should say “suṇātu me,” but instead, changing the ta-sound to a tha-sound, says “suṇāthu me,” that is called making the loose firm. Similarly, saying “patthakallaṃ, esā ñatthī” and so on, when one should say “pattakallaṃ, esā ñattī.” Saying “bante saṅgo” when one should say “bhante saṅgho,” changing the bha- and gha-sounds to ba- and ga-sounds, is called making the firm loose. Further, saying “suṇaṃtu me” instead of saying “suṇātu me” with an open mouth, or saying “esaṃ ñattī” instead of saying “esā ñattī,” pronouncing it with a mouth that is not open and making it nasalized, is called the restrained utterance of the released. Saying “pattakallā” with an open mouth and without making it nasalized when one should say “pattakallaṃ” with a mouth that is not open and making it nasalized, is called the released utterance of the restrained.

Iti [M 0.0248] sithile kattabbe dhanitaṃ, dhanite kattabbe sithilaṃ, vimutte kattabbe niggahitaṃ, niggahite kattabbe vimuttanti imāni cattāri byañjanāni antokammavācāya kammaṃ dūsenti. Evaṃ vadanto hi aññasmiṃ akkhare vattabbe aññaṃ vadati, duruttaṃ karotīti vuccati. Itaresu pana dīgharassādīsu chasu byañjanesu dīghaṭṭhāne dīghameva, rassaṭṭhāne ca rassamevāti evaṃ yathāṭhāne taṃ tadeva akkharaṃ bhāsantena anukkamāgataṃ paveṇiṃ avināsentena kammavācā kātabbā. Sace pana evaṃ akatvā dīghe vattabbe rassaṃ, rasse vā vattabbe dīghaṃ vadati; tathā garuke vattabbe lahukaṃ, lahuke vā vattabbe garukaṃ vadati; sambandhe vā pana vattabbe vavatthitaṃ, vavatthite vā vattabbe sambandhaṃ vadati; evaṃ vuttepi kammavācā na kuppati. Imāni hi cha byañjanāni kammaṃ na kopenti.

Thus, when a loose letter should be pronounced, a firm one is pronounced; when a firm letter should be pronounced, a loose one is pronounced; when a released letter should be pronounced, a restrained one is pronounced; when a restrained letter should be pronounced, a released one is pronounced—these four types of letters corrupt the formal act within the formal statement. For one who speaks thus, when one letter should be spoken, he speaks another, and it is said that he makes a faulty utterance. However, among the other six types of letters—long, short, and so on—in the place of a long sound, only the long should be spoken, and in the place of a short sound, only the short should be spoken. Thus, the formal statement should be performed by one who pronounces each respective letter correctly in its proper place, without disrupting the traditional sequence that has come down in succession. But if, not doing so, one speaks a short sound where a long should be spoken, or a long sound where a short should be spoken; or a light sound where a heavy should be spoken, or a heavy sound where a light should be spoken; or a separated sound where a connected sound should be spoken, or a connected sound where a separated sound should be spoken—even when spoken thus, the formal statement is not invalidated. For these six types of letters do not invalidate the formal act.

Yaṃ pana suttantikattherā “da-kāro ta-kāramāpajjati, ta-kāro da-kāramāpajjati, ca-kāro ja-kāramāpajjati, ja-kāro ca-kāramāpajjati, ya-kāro ka-kāramāpajjati, ka-kāro ya-kāramāpajjati; tasmā da-kārādīsu vattabbesu ta-kārādivacanaṃ na virujjhatī”ti vadanti, taṃ kammavācaṃ patvā na vaṭṭati. Tasmā vinayadharena neva da-kāro ta-kāro kātabbo…pe… na ka-kāro ya-kāro. Yathāpāḷiyā niruttiṃ sodhetvā dasavidhāya byañjananiruttiyā vuttadose pariharantena kammavācā kātabbā. Itarathā hi sāvanaṃ hāpeti nāma.

Moreover, what the Suttanta elders say, “The letter ‘da’ can become ‘ta,’ the letter ‘ta’ can become ‘da,’ the letter ‘ca’ can become ‘ja,’ the letter ‘ja’ can become ‘ca,’ the letter ‘ya’ can become ‘ka,’ and the letter ‘ka’ can become ‘ya.’ Therefore, when letters such as ‘da’ should be pronounced, the utterance of letters such as ‘ta’ is not incorrect,” is not permissible when it comes to a formal statement. Therefore, a Vinaya-holder should never make the letter ‘da’ into ‘ta’… and so on… nor the letter ‘ka’ into ‘ya.’ The formal statement should be performed having purified the pronunciation according to the Pāḷi, while avoiding the faults mentioned in the tenfold explanation of the pronunciation of letters. Otherwise, it is called diminishing the announcement.

Akāle vā sāvetīti sāvanāya akāle anokāse ñattiṃ aṭṭhapetvā [P 7.1401] paṭhamaṃyeva anussāvanakammaṃ katvā pacchā ñattiṃ ṭhapeti. Iti imehi pañcahākārehi anussāvanato kammāni vipajjanti.

Or he announces at an improper time—this means that at an improper time and on an inappropriate occasion for the announcement, without having established the motion, he performs the announcement procedure first and only afterward establishes the motion. Thus, by these five ways, formal acts fail due to the announcement.

486. Sīmato vipattiyaṃ pana atikhuddakasīmā nāma yā ekavīsati bhikkhū na gaṇhāti. Kurundiyaṃ pana “yattha ekavīsati bhikkhū nisīdituṃ na sakkontī”ti vuttaṃ. Tasmā yā evarūpā sīmā, ayaṃ sammatāpi asammatā, gāmakhettasadisāva hoti, tattha kataṃ kammaṃ [V 0.0252] kuppati. Esa nayo sesasīmāsupi. Ettha pana atimahatī nāma yā kesaggamattenāpi tiyojanaṃ atikkāmetvā sammatā hoti. Khaṇḍanimittā nāma aghaṭitanimittā vuccati. Puratthimāya disāya nimittaṃ kittetvā anukkameneva dakkhiṇāya pacchimāya uttarāya disāya kittetvā puna puratthimāya disāya pubbakittitaṃ nimittaṃ paṭikittetvāva ṭhapetuṃ vaṭṭati; evaṃ akhaṇḍanimittā hoti. Sace [M 0.0249] pana anukkamena āharitvā uttarāya disāya nimittaṃ kittetvā tattheva ṭhapeti, khaṇḍanimittā hoti. Aparāpi khaṇḍanimittā nāma yā animittupagaṃ tacasārarukkhaṃ vā khāṇukaṃ vā paṃsupuñjavālikāpuñjānaṃ vā aññataraṃ antarā ekaṃ nimittaṃ katvā sammatā hoti. Chāyānimittā nāma yā pabbatacchāyādīnaṃ yaṃkiñci chāyaṃ nimittaṃ katvā sammatā hoti. Animittā nāma yā sabbena sabbaṃ nimittāni akittetvā sammatā hoti.

486. Regarding failure on account of the boundary, that which is called an 'excessively small sīmā' is one that cannot accommodate twenty-one bhikkhus. In the Kurundī, however, it is said: 'Where twenty-one bhikkhus are unable to sit.' Therefore, any such sīmā, even if designated, is considered undesignated; it is just like a village-field, and an act performed there is invalid. This is the method for the other sīmās as well. Furthermore, among these, that which is called an 'excessively large sīmā' is one that is designated extending beyond three yojanas, even by a hair's breadth. A 'sīmā with broken markers' is said to be one with unconnected markers. Having proclaimed the marker in the eastern direction, and sequentially in the southern, western, and northern directions, it is proper to stop only after proclaiming again the previously proclaimed marker in the eastern direction; thus, it becomes a sīmā with unbroken markers. If, however, having proceeded sequentially, one proclaims the marker in the northern direction and stops right there, it becomes a sīmā with broken markers. Another 'sīmā with broken markers' is one that is designated by making a marker in between out of something unsuitable as a marker, such as a tree whose heartwood is its bark, or a stump, or one of a pile of earth or a pile of sand. A 'sīmā with a shadow marker' is one that is designated by making some kind of shadow, such as a mountain's shadow, into a marker. A 'markerless sīmā' is one that is designated without proclaiming the markers at all.

Bahisīme ṭhito sīmaṃ sammannati nāma nimittāni kittetvā nimittānaṃ bahi ṭhito sammannati. Nadiyā samudde jātassare sīmaṃ sammannatīti etesu nadiādīsu yaṃ sammannati, sā evaṃ sammatāpi “sabbā, bhikkhave, nadī asīmā, sabbo samuddo asīmo, sabbo jātassaro asīmo”ti (mahāva. 147) vacanato asammatāva hoti. Sīmāya sīmaṃ sambhindatīti attano sīmāya paresaṃ sīmaṃ sambhindati. Ajjhottharatīti attano sīmāya paresaṃ sīmaṃ ajjhottharati. Tattha yathā sambhedo ca ajjhottharaṇañca hoti, taṃ sabbaṃ uposathakkhandhake vuttameva. Iti imā ekādasapi sīmā asīmā gāmakhettasadisā eva, tāsu nisīditvā kataṃ kammaṃ kuppati. Tena vuttaṃ “imehi ekādasahi ākārehi sīmato kammāni vipajjantī”ti.

'Designating a sīmā while standing outside the sīmā' means: having proclaimed the markers, one stands outside the markers and designates. As for 'designating a sīmā in a river, in the ocean, or in a natural lake': whatever sīmā one designates in these places such as rivers, that sīmā, even though so designated, is indeed undesignated on account of the saying: 'Bhikkhus, every river is not a formally bounded sīmā, the entire ocean is not a sīmā, every natural lake is not a sīmā.' 'Intermingling a sīmā with a sīmā' means one intermingles the sīmā of others with one's own sīmā. 'Overlapping' means one overlaps the sīmā of others with one's own sīmā. Therein, how intermingling and overlapping occur, all that has been spoken of in the Uposathakkhandhaka. Thus, these eleven sīmās are not formally bounded sīmās; they are just like village-fields. An act performed while sitting in them is invalid. Therefore, it was said: 'By these eleven modes, acts fail on account of the sīmā.'

487-488. Parisato kammavipattiyaṃ pana kiñci anuttānaṃ nāma natthi. Yampi tattha kammappattachandārahalakkhaṇaṃ vattabbaṃ siyā, tampi parato “cattāro bhikkhū [P 7.1402] pakatattā kammappattā”tiādinā nayena vuttameva. Tattha pakatattā kammappattāti catuvaggakaraṇe kamme cattāro pakatattā anukkhittā anissāritā parisuddhasīlā cattāro bhikkhū kammappattā kammassa arahā anucchavikā sāmino. Na tehi vinā taṃ kammaṃ kayirati, na tesaṃ chando vā pārisuddhi vā eti. Avasesā pana sacepi sahassamattā honti, sace samānasaṃvāsakā, sabbe chandārahāva honti. Chandapārisuddhiṃ datvā āgacchantu vā mā vā, kammaṃ pana tiṭṭhati. Yassa [V 0.0253] pana saṅgho parivāsādikammaṃ karoti, so neva kammappatto, nāpi chandāraho. Apica yasmā taṃ puggalaṃ vatthuṃ katvā saṅgho kammaṃ karoti, tasmā “kammāraho”ti vuccati. Sesakammesupi eseva nayo.

487-488. However, in the failure of an act on account of the assembly, there is nothing called an obscure term. Whatever characteristic of being qualified for the act and worthy of consent should be stated there, that too has already been stated later by the method beginning with: 'Four bhikkhus, being of normal status, are qualified for the act.' Here, 'of normal status and qualified for the act' means that for an act to be done by a chapter of four, four bhikkhus of normal status—not suspended, not expelled, and of pure morality—are qualified for the act, worthy of the act, suitable for it, and masters of the act. Without them, that act cannot be performed; their consent or purity is not brought from elsewhere. As for the remaining bhikkhus, even if they are a thousand in number, if they are in communion, they are all worthy of giving consent. Having given their consent and purity, whether they come or not, the act stands. But the one on whom the Saṅgha performs an act such as parivāsa is neither qualified for the act nor worthy of giving consent. Moreover, because the Saṅgha performs the act making that person the object, he is therefore called 'worthy of the act.' This same method applies to the remaining acts as well.

489. Puna [M 0.0250] cattāri kammānītiādiko nayo paṇḍakādīnaṃ avatthubhāvadassanatthaṃ vutto. Sesamettha uttānameva.

489. Again, the method beginning with 'four acts' was spoken to show the state of not being a proper object for unqualified persons such as paṇḍakas. The remainder here is self-evident.

Apalokanakammakathā

Discourse on the Act of Announcement

495-496. Idāni tesaṃ kammānaṃ pabhedadassanatthaṃ “apalokanakammaṃ kati ṭhānāni gacchatī”tiādimāha. Tattha “apalokanakammaṃ pañca ṭhānāni gacchati – osāraṇaṃ, nissāraṇaṃ, bhaṇḍukammaṃ, brahmadaṇḍaṃ, kammalakkhaṇaññeva pañcama”nti ettha “osāraṇaṃ nissāraṇa”nti padasiliṭṭhatāyetaṃ vuttaṃ. Paṭhamaṃ pana nissāraṇā hoti, pacchā osāraṇā. Tattha yā kaṇṭakasāmaṇerassa daṇḍakammanāsanā, sā “nissāraṇā”ti veditabbā. Tasmā etarahi sacepi sāmaṇero buddhassa vā dhammassa vā saṅghassa vā avaṇṇaṃ bhaṇati, “akappiyaṃ kappiya”nti dīpeti, micchādiṭṭhiko hoti antaggāhikāya diṭṭhiyā samannāgato, so yāvatatiyaṃ nivāretvā taṃ laddhiṃ nissajjāpetabbo. No ce vissajjeti, saṅghaṃ sannipātetvā “vissajjehī”ti vattabbo. No ce vissajjeti, byattena bhikkhunā apalokanakammaṃ katvā nissāretabbo. Evañca pana kammaṃ kātabbaṃ –

495-496. Now, to show the classification of those acts, it is said, beginning with: 'To how many instances does the act of announcement apply?' Herein, in the passage 'the act of announcement applies to five instances—reinstatement, expulsion, the act of making bald, the Brahma-penalty, and the fifth is simply the characteristic of the act,' the phrase 'reinstatement, expulsion' is stated for smoothness of phrasing. In fact, first there is expulsion, and afterwards there is reinstatement. Herein, the punishment-act of ruin for the thorn-like novice is to be understood as 'expulsion.' Therefore, at present, if a novice speaks in dispraise of the Buddha, the Dhamma, or the Sangha, if he explains the unallowable as allowable, if he is of wrong view, possessed of a view that grasps at extremes, he should be prevented up to the third time and made to renounce that doctrine. If he does not renounce it, the Sangha should be assembled and he should be told, 'Renounce it!' If he does not renounce it, he should be expelled by a competent bhikkhu after performing an act of announcement. And the act should be performed thus:

“Saṅghaṃ, bhante, pucchāmi – ‘ayaṃ itthannāmo sāmaṇero buddhassa dhammassa saṅghassa avaṇṇavādī micchādiṭṭhiko, yaṃ aññe sāmaṇerā labhanti, dirattatirattaṃ bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ sahaseyyaṃ, tassa alābhāya nissāraṇā ruccati saṅghassā’ti. Dutiyampi… tatiyampi, bhante, saṅghaṃ pucchāmi – ‘ayaṃ itthannāmo sāmaṇero buddhassa…pe… ruccati saṅghassā’ti cara pire vinassā”ti.

‘Venerable sirs, I ask the Sangha: This novice named so-and-so speaks in dispraise of the Buddha, the Dhamma, and the Sangha, and is of wrong view. Is the expulsion of this novice, for the purpose of his not obtaining the co-residence with bhikkhus for two or three nights that other novices obtain, agreeable to the Sangha? For the second time… For the third time, venerable sirs, I ask the Sangha: This novice named so-and-so speaks in dispraise of the Buddha… is it agreeable to the Sangha? Go, outsider! Perish!’

So aparena samayena “ahaṃ, bhante, bālatāya aññāṇatāya alakkhikatāya [P 7.1403] evaṃ akāsiṃ, svāhaṃ saṅghaṃ khamāpemī”ti khamāpento yāvatatiyaṃ yācāpetvā apalokanakammeneva osāretabbo[V 0.0254]. Evaṃ pana osāretabbo, saṅghamajjhe byattena bhikkhunā saṅghassa anumatiyā sāvetabbaṃ –

If, at a later time, while asking for forgiveness, he says, 'Venerable sirs, due to my foolishness, ignorance, and unfortunate nature, I acted thus. I ask the Sangha for forgiveness,' he should be made to request this up to the third time and then be reinstated by an act of announcement itself. And he should be reinstated thus: it should be announced in the midst of the Sangha by a competent bhikkhu with the Sangha's consent—

“Saṅghaṃ, bhante, pucchāmi – ayaṃ itthannāmo sāmaṇero buddhassa dhammassa saṅghassa avaṇṇavādī micchādiṭṭhiko, yaṃ aññe sāmaṇerā labhanti, bhikkhūhi saddhiṃ dirattatirattaṃ sahaseyyaṃ, tassa [M 0.0251] alābhāya nissārito, svāyaṃ idāni sorato nivātavutti lajjidhammaṃ okkanto hirottappe patiṭṭhito katadaṇḍakammo accayaṃ deseti, imassa sāmaṇerassa yathā pure kāyasambhogasāmaggidānaṃ ruccati saṅghassā”ti.

‘Venerable sirs, I ask the Sangha: This novice named so-and-so, who speaks in dispraise of the Buddha, the Dhamma, and the Sangha, and is of wrong view, was expelled for the purpose of his not obtaining the co-residence with bhikkhus for two or three nights that other novices obtain. Now he is docile, of humble conduct, has undertaken the state of the conscientious, is established in moral shame and moral dread, has undergone the punishment-act, and confesses his transgression. Is the giving of harmony in bodily association to this novice, as it was before, agreeable to the Sangha?’

Evaṃ tikkhattuṃ vattabbaṃ. Evaṃ apalokanakammaṃ osāraṇañca nissāraṇañca gacchati. Bhaṇḍukammaṃ mahākhandhakavaṇṇanāyaṃ vuttameva. Brahmadaṇḍo pañcasatikakkhandhake vuttoyeva. Na kevalaṃ panesa channasseva paññatto, yo aññopi bhikkhu mukharo hoti, bhikkhū duruttavacanehi ghaṭṭento khuṃsento vambhento viharati, tassapi dātabbo. Evañca pana dātabbo, saṅghamajjhe byattena bhikkhunā saṅghassa anumatiyā sāvetabbaṃ –

This should be stated three times. Thus the act of announcement applies to both reinstatement and expulsion. The act of making bald has already been stated by me in the commentary on the Mahākhandhaka. The Brahma-penalty has already been stated in the Pañcasatikakkhandhaka. Moreover, this was not laid down for Channa alone; it should also be given to any other bhikkhu who is foul-mouthed and lives harassing, reviling, and disparaging the bhikkhus with ill-spoken words. And it should be given thus: it should be announced in the midst of the Sangha by a competent bhikkhu with the Sangha's consent—

“Bhante, itthannāmo bhikkhu mukharo, bhikkhū duruttavacanehi ghaṭṭento viharati. So bhikkhu yaṃ iccheyya, taṃ vadeyya. Bhikkhūhi itthannāmo bhikkhu neva vattabbo, na ovaditabbo, na anusāsitabbo. Saṅghaṃ, bhante, pucchāmi – ‘itthannāmassa bhikkhuno brahmadaṇḍassa dānaṃ, ruccati saṅghassā’ti. Dutiyampi pucchāmi, tatiyampi pucchāmi – ‘itthannāmassa, bhante, bhikkhuno brahmadaṇḍassa dānaṃ, ruccati saṅghassā’”ti.

‘Venerable sirs, the bhikkhu named so-and-so is foul-mouthed; he lives harassing the bhikkhus with ill-spoken words. Whatever that bhikkhu may wish to say, let him say it. The bhikkhus should neither speak to the bhikkhu named so-and-so, nor advise him, nor instruct him. Venerable sirs, I ask the Sangha: Is the giving of the Brahma-penalty to the bhikkhu named so-and-so agreeable to the Sangha? For the second time I ask... For the third time I ask, venerable sirs: Is the giving of the Brahma-penalty to the bhikkhu named so-and-so agreeable to the Sangha?’

Tassa aparena samayena sammā vattitvā khamāpentassa brahmadaṇḍo paṭippassambhetabbo. Evañca pana paṭippassambhetabbo, byattena bhikkhunā saṅghamajjhe sāvetabbaṃ –

For him who, at a later time, having conducted himself well, asks for forgiveness, the Brahma-penalty should be revoked. And it should be revoked thus: it should be announced in the midst of the Sangha by a competent bhikkhu—

“Bhante, bhikkhusaṅgho asukassa bhikkhuno brahmadaṇḍaṃ adāsi, so bhikkhu sorato nivātavutti lajjidhammaṃ okkanto hirottappe patiṭṭhito, paṭisaṅkhā āyatiṃ saṃvare tiṭṭhati, saṅghaṃ, bhante, pucchāmi, tassa bhikkhuno brahmadaṇḍassa paṭippassaddhi, ruccati saṅghassā”ti.

‘Venerable sirs, the Sangha of bhikkhus gave the Brahma-penalty to such-and-such a bhikkhu. That bhikkhu is now docile, of humble conduct, has undertaken the state of the conscientious, is established in moral shame and moral dread, and, having reflected, stands in future restraint. Venerable sirs, I ask the Sangha: Is the revocation of the Brahma-penalty for that bhikkhu agreeable to the Sangha?’

Evaṃ [V 0.0255] yāvatatiyaṃ vatvā apalokanakammeneva [P 7.1404] brahmadaṇḍo paṭippassambhetabboti.

Thus, having spoken up to the third time, the Brahma-penalty should be revoked by an act of announcement itself.

Kammalakkhaṇaññeva pañcamanti yaṃ taṃ bhagavatā bhikkhunikkhandhake “tena kho pana samayena chabbaggiyā bhikkhū bhikkhuniyo kaddamodakena osiñcanti, ‘appeva nāma amhesu sārajjeyyu’nti, kāyaṃ vivaritvā bhikkhunīnaṃ dassenti[M 0.0252], ūruṃ vivaritvā bhikkhunīnaṃ dassenti, aṅgajātaṃ vivaritvā bhikkhunīnaṃ dassenti, bhikkhuniyo obhāsenti, bhikkhunīhi saddhiṃ sampayojenti, ‘appeva nāma amhesu sārajjeyyu’nti. Imesu vatthūsu tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ dukkaṭaṃ paññapetvā ‘anujānāmi bhikkhave tassa bhikkhuno daṇḍakammaṃ kātu’nti. Atha kho bhikkhunīnaṃ etadahosi – ‘kiṃ nu kho daṇḍakammaṃ kātabba’nti. Bhagavato etamatthaṃ ārocesuṃ – ‘avandiyo so bhikkhave bhikkhu bhikkhunisaṅghena kātabbo’”ti evaṃ avandiyakammaṃ anuññātaṃ, taṃ kammalakkhaṇaññeva pañcamaṃ imassa apalokanakammassa ṭhānaṃ hoti. Tassa hi kammaññeva lakkhaṇaṃ, na osāraṇādīni; tasmā “kammalakkhaṇa”nti vuccati. Tassa karaṇaṃ tattheva vuttaṃ. Apica naṃ paṭippassaddhiyā saddhiṃ vitthārato dassetuṃ idhāpi vadāma, bhikkhunupassaye sannipatitassa bhikkhunisaṅghassa anumatiyā byattāya bhikkhuniyā sāvetabbaṃ –

“The fifth is simply the characteristic of an act.” This is what the Blessed One declared in the Bhikkhunī Khandhaka: “At that time, the group-of-six bhikkhus sprinkled bhikkhunīs with muddy water, thinking, ‘Perhaps they might become attached to us.’ They exposed their bodies to the bhikkhunīs, exposed their thighs to the bhikkhunīs, exposed their genitals to the bhikkhunīs, spoke abusively to the bhikkhunīs, and associated with bhikkhunīs, thinking, ‘Perhaps they might become attached to us.’ In these cases, having laid down a dukkaṭa offense for those bhikkhus, he said, ‘Monks, I allow a penalty-act to be done to that bhikkhu.’ Then this occurred to the bhikkhunīs: ‘What penalty-act should be done?’ They reported this matter to the Blessed One—‘Monks, that bhikkhu should be made unworthy of veneration by the community of bhikkhunīs.’” Thus, the act of making unworthy of veneration was allowed. That, which has the characteristic of an act itself, is the fifth basis for this act of announcement. For its characteristic is the act itself, not reinstatement and so on; therefore, it is called “characteristic of an act.” The procedure for it has been stated right there. Moreover, to explain it in detail together with its revocation, we will speak here also. In the bhikkhunī residence, it should be announced by a competent bhikkhunī with the consent of the assembled Bhikkhunī Saṅgha—

“Ayye asuko nāma ayyo bhikkhunīnaṃ apāsādikaṃ dasseti, etassa ayyassa avandiyakaraṇaṃ ruccatīti bhikkhunisaṅghaṃ pucchāmi, dutiyampi… tatiyampi bhikkhunisaṅghaṃ pucchāmī”ti.

“Venerable ladies, the bhikkhu named so-and-so displays what is displeasing to the bhikkhunīs. Does the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha approve of making this bhikkhu unworthy of veneration? I ask the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha. For the second time… For the third time I ask the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha.”

Evaṃ tikkhattuṃ sāvetvā apalokanakammena avandiyakammaṃ kātabbaṃ.

Having announced it three times thus, the act of making unworthy of veneration should be done by an act of announcement.

Tato paṭṭhāya so bhikkhu bhikkhunīhi na vanditabbo. Sace avandiyamāno hirottappaṃ paccupaṭṭhapetvā sammā vattati, tena bhikkhuniyo khamāpetabbā. Khamāpentena bhikkhunupassayaṃ agantvā vihāreyeva saṅghaṃ vā gaṇaṃ vā ekaṃ bhikkhuṃ vā upasaṅkamitvā ukkuṭikaṃ nisīditvā añjaliṃ paggahetvā “ahaṃ bhante paṭisaṅkhā āyatiṃ saṃvare tiṭṭhāmi, na puna apāsādikaṃ dassessāmi, bhikkhunisaṅgho mayhaṃ khamatū”ti khamāpetabbaṃ. Tena saṅghena [P 7.1405] vā gaṇena vā ekaṃ bhikkhuṃ pesetvā ekabhikkhunā vā sayameva gantvā bhikkhuniyo vattabbā – “ayaṃ bhikkhu paṭisaṅkhā [V 0.0256] āyatiṃ saṃvare ṭhito, iminā accayaṃ desetvā bhikkhunisaṅgho khamāpito, bhikkhunisaṅgho imaṃ vandiyaṃ karotū”ti. So vandiyo kātabbo. Evañca pana kātabbo, bhikkhunupassaye sannipatitassa bhikkhunisaṅghassa anumatiyā byattāya bhikkhuniyā sāvetabbaṃ –

From then on, that bhikkhu is not to be venerated by the bhikkhunīs. If, while not being venerated, he establishes moral shame and moral dread and conducts himself properly, he should ask for forgiveness from the bhikkhunīs. When asking for forgiveness, without going to the bhikkhunī residence, he should approach the Saṅgha, or a group, or a single bhikkhu in the monastery itself, sit squatting, raise his joined palms, and ask for forgiveness, saying: “Venerable sirs, having reflected, I stand in future restraint. I will not again show what is displeasing. May the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha forgive me.” That Saṅgha or group should send a bhikkhu, or that single bhikkhu should go himself, and the bhikkhunīs should be told: “This bhikkhu, having reflected, stands in future restraint. Having confessed his transgression, he has asked for the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha's forgiveness. May the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha make him worthy of veneration.” He should be made worthy of veneration. And he should be made so thus: in the bhikkhunī residence, it should be announced by a competent bhikkhunī with the consent of the assembled Bhikkhunī Saṅgha—

“Ayaṃ ayye asuko nāma ayyo bhikkhunīnaṃ apāsādikaṃ dassetīti bhikkhunisaṅghena avandiyo kato, so lajjidhammaṃ okkamitvā paṭisaṅkhā āyatiṃ saṃvare ṭhito accayaṃ desetvā bhikkhunisaṅghaṃ [M 0.0253] khamāpesi, tassa ayyassa vandiyakaraṇaṃ ruccatīti bhikkhunisaṅghaṃ pucchāmī”ti –

“Venerable ladies, this venerable one named so-and-so was made unworthy of veneration by the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha for displaying what is displeasing to the bhikkhunīs. He, having entered into the state of one who has shame, stands in future restraint after reflection, has confessed his transgression, and has asked for the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha's forgiveness. Does the making of this venerable one worthy of veneration meet with the approval of the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha? I ask the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha.”

Tikkhattuṃ vattabbaṃ evaṃ apalokanakammeneva vandiyo kātabbo.

It should be said three times. Thus, he is to be made worthy of veneration by an act of declaration alone.

Ayaṃ panettha pāḷimuttakopi kammalakkhaṇavinicchayo. Idañhi kammalakkhaṇaṃ nāma bhikkhunisaṅghamūlakaṃ paññattaṃ, bhikkhusaṅghassāpi panetaṃ labbhatiyeva. Yañhi bhikkhusaṅgho salākaggayāgaggabhattaggauposathaggesu apalokanakammaṃ karoti, etampi kammalakkhaṇameva. Acchinnacīvarajiṇṇacīvaranaṭṭhacīvarānañhi saṅghaṃ sannipātetvā byattena bhikkhunā yāvatatiyaṃ sāvetvā apalokanakammaṃ katvā cīvaraṃ dātuṃ vaṭṭati. Appamattakavissajjakena pana cīvaraṃ karontassa senāsanakkhandhakavaṇṇanāyaṃ vuttappabhedāni sūciādīni anapaloketvāpi dātabbāni. Tesaṃ dāne soyeva issaro, tato atirekaṃ dentena apaloketvā dātabbaṃ. Tato hi atirekadāne saṅgho sāmī. Gilānabhesajjampi tattha vuttappakāraṃ sayameva dātabbaṃ. Atirekaṃ icchantassa apaloketvā dātabbaṃ. Yopi ca dubbalo vā chinniriyāpatho vā pacchinnabhikkhācārapatho vā mahāgilāno, tassa mahāvāsesu tatruppādato devasikaṃ nāḷi vā upaḍḍhanāḷi vā ekadivasaṃyeva vā pañca vā dasa vā taṇḍulanāḷiyo dentena apalokanakammaṃ katvāva dātabbā. Pesalassa bhikkhuno tatruppādato iṇapalibodhampi bahussutassa saṅghabhāranitthārakassa bhikkhuno anuṭṭhāpanīyasenāsanampi saṅghakiccaṃ karontānaṃ kappiyakārakādīnaṃ bhattavetanampi apalokanakammena [P 7.1406] dātuṃ vaṭṭati.

Here, furthermore, is the legal decision on the characteristic of an act, even that which is independent of the Pāḷi. Indeed, this 'characteristic of an act' was laid down with the Bhikkhunī Saṅgha as its basis; however, it is certainly obtainable for the Bhikkhu Saṅgha as well. For whatever act of declaration the Bhikkhu Saṅgha performs in places for distributing tickets, gruel, meals, or in Uposatha halls, this too is a 'characteristic of an act'. For bhikkhus whose robes have been stolen, are worn out, or are lost, it is allowable to give a robe after assembling the Saṅgha, having a competent bhikkhu announce it up to the third time, and performing an act of declaration. However, a distributor of minor requisites may give to a bhikkhu who is making a robe, even without an act of declaration, needles and so on of the types mentioned in the commentary on the Senāsana Khandhaka. In the giving of these, he himself is the authority. When giving more than that, it should be given after making a declaration. For in giving more than that, the Saṅgha is the owner. Medicine for the sick, of the type described there, may also be given by himself. For one desiring more, it should be given after making a declaration. And for whichever bhikkhu is weak, or whose postures are interrupted, or whose alms-round path is cut off, or is gravely ill, to him, from the income of the large monasteries, one giving a nāḷi or half a nāḷi of rice daily, or on a single day five or ten nāḷis of rice, should give it only after performing an act of declaration. For a virtuous bhikkhu, from the income there, even a debt-impediment; for a learned bhikkhu who carries out the burdens of the Saṅgha, even a lodging from which he should not be made to rise; and for stewards and so on who perform the Saṅgha's duties, even a food-allowance—it is allowable to give by an act of declaration.

Catupaccayavasena dinnatatruppādato saṅghikaṃ āvāsaṃ jaggāpetuṃ vaṭṭati. “Ayaṃ bhikkhu issaravatāya vicāretī”ti kathāpacchindanatthaṃ pana salākaggādīsu vā antarasannipāte vā saṅghaṃ pucchitvāva [V 0.0257] jaggāpetabbo. Cīvarapiṇḍapātatthāya odissadinnatatruppādatopi apaloketvā āvāso jaggāpetabbo. Anapaloketvāpi vaṭṭati. “Sūro vatāyaṃ bhikkhu cīvarapiṇḍapātatthāya dinnato āvāsaṃ jaggāpetī”ti evaṃ uppannakathāpacchedanatthaṃ pana apalokanakammameva katvā jaggāpetabbo.

It is allowable to have a monastery belonging to the Saṅgha maintained from property that arose there, given by means of the four requisites. However, for the purpose of cutting off talk such as, 'This bhikkhu manages things with the authority of an owner,' it should be maintained only after having asked the Saṅgha, either at the place for distributing tickets and so on, or in an intermediate assembly. From property that arose there, given specifically for the purpose of robes and alms-food, the monastery should also be maintained after making an announcement. It is also allowable without making an announcement. However, for the purpose of cutting off such talk that has arisen, 'This bhikkhu is indeed bold; he has the monastery maintained from what was given for the purpose of robes and alms-food,' it should be maintained only after having performed the act of announcement.

Cetiye [M 0.0254] chattaṃ vā vedikaṃ vā bodhigharaṃ vā āsanagharaṃ vā akataṃ vā karontena jiṇṇaṃ vā paṭisaṅkharontena sudhākammaṃ vā karontena manusse samādapetvā kātuṃ vaṭṭati. Sace kārako natthi, cetiyassa upanikkhepato kāretabbaṃ. Upanikkhepepi asati apalokanakammaṃ katvā tatruppādato kāretabbaṃ, saṅghikenapi. Saṅghikena hi apaloketvā cetiyakiccaṃ kātuṃ vaṭṭati. Cetiyassa santakena apaloketvāpi saṅghikakiccaṃ kātuṃ na vaṭṭati. Tāvakālikaṃ pana gahetvā pākatikaṃ kātuṃ vaṭṭati.

At a cetiya, whether one is making what has not yet been made—such as a canopy, a railing, a Bodhi-tree shrine-house, or an image shrine-house—or repairing what is old, or doing plasterwork, it is allowable to do so after having caused laypeople to undertake it. If there is no worker, it should be caused to be done from the funds deposited for the cetiya. If there are no deposited funds either, it should be caused to be done from the property that arose in that monastery after performing an act of announcement, even with property belonging to the Saṅgha. Indeed, it is allowable to do the work of the cetiya with Saṅgha property after making an announcement. It is not allowable to do the work of the Saṅgha with the property of the cetiya, even after making an announcement. However, it is allowable to take it for temporary use and then restore it to its original condition.

Cetiye sudhākammādīni karontehi pana bhikkhācārato vā saṅghato vā yāpanamattaṃ alabhantehi cetiyasantakato yāpanamattaṃ gahetvā paribhuñjantehi vattaṃ kātuṃ vaṭṭati, “vattaṃ karomā”ti macchamaṃsādīhi saṅghabhattaṃ kātuṃ na vaṭṭati. Ye vihāre ropitā phalarukkhā saṅghena pariggahitā honti, jagganakammaṃ labhanti, yesaṃ phalāni ghaṇṭiṃ paharitvā bhājetvā paribhuñjanti, tesu apalokanakammaṃ na kātabbaṃ. Ye pana apariggahitā, tesu apalokanakammaṃ kātabbaṃ. Taṃ pana salākaggayāgaggabhattaggaantarasannipātesupi kātuṃ vaṭṭati, uposathagge pana vaṭṭatiyeva. Tattha hi anāgatānampi chandapārisuddhi āhariyati, tasmā taṃ suvisodhitaṃ hoti.

But for bhikkhus doing plasterwork and so on at a cetiya, who do not receive even enough for sustenance from the alms-round or from the Saṅgha, it is allowable for them to perform their duty while consuming just enough for sustenance taken from the cetiya's property. It is not allowable to prepare a Saṅgha meal with fish, meat, and so on, saying, 'We are performing our duty.' For those fruit trees planted in the monastery that are claimed by the Saṅgha, receive maintenance, and whose fruits are consumed after striking a bell and distributing them, an act of announcement should not be performed. But for those that are not claimed, an act of announcement should be performed. And that act is allowable to be performed at the place for taking tickets, the place for taking gruel, the place for taking food, or in intermediate assemblies. But in the Uposatha hall, it is certainly allowable. For there, the consent and purity of even those who have not arrived is brought; therefore, that act is well-purified.

Evañca pana kātabbaṃ, byattena bhikkhunā bhikkhusaṅghassa anumatiyā sāvetabbaṃ –

And it should be done thus: by a competent bhikkhu, an announcement should be made with the consent of the Saṅgha of bhikkhus:

“Bhante, yaṃ imasmiṃ vihāre antosīmāya saṅghasantakaṃ mūlatacapattaaṅkurapupphaphalakhādanīyādi atthi, taṃ sabbaṃ āgatāgatānaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ yathāsukhaṃ paribhuñjituṃ ruccatīti [P 7.1407] saṅghaṃ pucchāmī”ti tikkhattuṃ pucchitabbaṃ.

“Venerable sirs, whatever property of the Saṅgha exists within the boundary of this monastery—edibles such as roots, bark, leaves, sprouts, flowers, and fruits—I ask the Saṅgha: does it please the Saṅgha that all of it be consumed by all arriving bhikkhus according to their pleasure?” This should be asked three times.

Catūhi pañcahi bhikkhūhi kataṃ sukatameva. Yasmiṃ vihāre dve tayo janā vasanti, tehi [V 0.0258] nisīditvā katampi saṅghena katasadisameva. Yasmiṃ pana vihāre eko bhikkhu hoti, tena bhikkhunā uposathadivase pubbakaraṇapubbakiccaṃ katvā nisinnena katampi katikavattaṃ saṅghena katasadisameva hoti.

An act performed by four or five bhikkhus is indeed well performed. In a monastery where two or three persons reside, an act performed by them sitting down is just like one performed by the Saṅgha. But in a monastery where there is one bhikkhu, an agreement made by that bhikkhu sitting down on the Uposatha day, after having performed the preliminary duties and preliminary functions, is just like one made by the Saṅgha.

Karontena [M 0.0255] pana phalavārena kātumpi cattāro māse cha māse ekasaṃvaccharanti evaṃ paricchinditvāpi aparicchinditvāpi kātuṃ vaṭṭati. Paricchinne yathāparicchedaṃ paribhuñjitvā puna kātabbaṃ. Aparicchinne yāva rukkhā dharanti tāva vaṭṭatiyeva. Yepi tesaṃ rukkhānaṃ bījehi aññe rukkhā ropitā honti, tesampi sā eva katikā.

But when making the agreement, it is allowable to do so by turn of fruit, or by delimiting a period of four months, six months, or one year, or without delimiting a period. In the case of a delimited agreement, after consuming according to the delimitation, it should be made again. In the case of an undelimited agreement, it is allowable for as long as the trees last. And for other trees that are planted from the seeds of those trees, that same agreement applies.

Sace pana aññasmiṃ vihāre ropitā honti, tesaṃ yattha ropitā, tasmiṃyeva vihāre saṅgho sāmī. Yepi aññato bījāni āharitvā purimavihāre pacchā ropitā, tesu aññā katikā kātabbā. Katikāya katāya puggalikaṭṭhāne tiṭṭhanti, yathāsukhaṃ phalādīni paribhuñjituṃ vaṭṭati. Sace panettha taṃ taṃ okāsaṃ parikkhipitvā pariveṇāni katvā jagganti, tesaṃ bhikkhūnaṃ puggalikaṭṭhāne tiṭṭhanti. Aññe paribhuñjituṃ na labhanti, tehi pana saṅghassa dasabhāgaṃ datvā paribhuñjitabbāni. Yopi majjhevihāre rukkhaṃ sākhāhi parivāretvā rakkhati, tassāpi eseva nayo.

But if they are planted in another monastery, the Saṅgha is the owner in that very monastery where they are planted. And for those trees that are later planted in the former monastery with seeds brought from another place, another agreement should be made. When the agreement has been made, they stand in the place of personal property, and it is allowable to consume the fruits and so on according to pleasure. If, here in this monastery, they tend to them after having enclosed those various places and made cells, they stand in the place of personal property of those bhikkhus. Other bhikkhus do not get to consume them; but they should be consumed by them after having given a tenth part to the Saṅgha. For a bhikkhu who protects a tree in the middle of the monastery by surrounding it with branches, this is the same method.

Porāṇavihāraṃ gatassa sambhāvanīyabhikkhuno “thero āgato”ti phalāphalaṃ āharanti, sace tattha mūle sabbapariyattidharo bahussutabhikkhu vihāsi, “addhā ettha dīghā katikā katā bhavissatī”ti nikkukkuccena paribhuñjitabbaṃ. Vihāre phalāphalaṃ piṇḍapātikānampi vaṭṭati, dhutaṅgaṃ na kopeti. Sāmaṇerā attano ācariyupajjhāyānaṃ bahūni phalāni denti, aññe bhikkhū alabhantā khiyyanti, khiyyanamattameva cetaṃ hoti.

To a respected bhikkhu who has gone to an ancient monastery, they bring fruits great and small, thinking, “The elder has come.” If, at the origin of that place, a very learned bhikkhu, a master of the entire scriptural teaching, has dwelt, one should consume them without scruple, thinking, “Surely, a long-standing agreement must have been made here.” Fruits great and small in the monastery are allowable even for those who undertake the ascetic practice of alms-going; it does not damage the ascetic practice. Novices give many fruits to their own teachers and preceptors. Other bhikkhus, not receiving any, are resentful, but this is merely resentment.

Sace pana dubbhikkhaṃ hoti, ekaṃ panasarukkhaṃ nissāya saṭṭhipi janā jīvanti, tādise kāle sabbesaṃ saṅgahakaraṇatthāya bhājetvā khāditabbaṃ, ayaṃ sāmīci. Yāva pana katikavattaṃ na paṭippassambhati, tāva tehi khāyitaṃ sukhāyitameva. Kadā pana katikavattaṃ paṭippassambhati? Yadā samaggo saṅgho sannipatitvā “ito paṭṭhāya bhājetvā khādantū”ti sāveti. Ekabhikkhuke pana [P 7.1408] vihāre ekena sāvitepi purimakatikā paṭippassambhatiyeva. Sace [V 0.0259] paṭippassaddhāya katikāya sāmaṇerā neva rukkhato pātenti, na bhūmito gahetvā bhikkhūnaṃ denti, patitaphalāni pādehi paharantā vicaranti, tesaṃ dasabhāgato paṭṭhāya yāva upaḍḍhaphalabhāgena phātikammaṃ kātabbaṃ. Addhā phātikammalobhena āharitvā [M 0.0256] dassanti. Puna subhikkhe jāte kappiyakārakesu āgantvā sākhāparivārādīni katvā rukkhe rakkhantesu sāmaṇerānaṃ phātikammaṃ na dātabbaṃ, bhājetvā paribhuñjitabbaṃ.

But if there is a famine, and sixty monks can survive by relying on a single jackfruit tree, in such a time, for the purpose of supporting all of them, it should be divided and eaten. This is a proper practice. Furthermore, as long as the formal agreement has not ceased, what is eaten by them is indeed well eaten. When, then, does the formal agreement cease? When the harmonious Saṅgha assembles and announces, “From now on, let them divide and eat.” Furthermore, in a monastery with a single monk, even when announced by that one monk, the previous agreement indeed ceases. If, after the agreement has ceased, the novices neither make the fruit fall from the tree nor pick it up from the ground and give it to the monks, but walk about kicking the fallen fruit with their feet, an allowance for them, starting from a tenth portion up to a half portion of the fruit, should be made. Surely, out of greed for the allowance, they will bring and give it. Again, when there is abundance, and stewards come and guard the trees after making enclosures with branches and so on, the allowance should not be given to the novices; it should be divided and consumed.

“Vihāre phalāphalaṃ atthī”ti sāmantagāmehi manussā gilānānaṃ vā gabbhinīnaṃ vā atthāya āgantvā “ekaṃ nāḷikeraṃ detha, ambaṃ detha, labujaṃ dethā”ti yācanti, dātabbaṃ na dātabbanti? Dātabbaṃ. Adīyamāne hi te domanassikā honti, dentena pana saṅghaṃ sannipātetvā yāvatatiyaṃ sāvetvā apalokanakammaṃ katvāva dātabbaṃ, katikavattaṃ vā katvā ṭhapetabbaṃ, evañca pana kātabbaṃ, byattena bhikkhunā saṅghassa anumatiyā sāvetabbaṃ –

Hearing, “There are fruits in the monastery,” people from the surrounding villages come for the sake of the sick or pregnant women and ask, “Give us one coconut, give us a mango, give us a labuja fruit.” Should it be given or not? It should be given. For if it is not given, they become displeased. However, one who is giving should do so only after having assembled the Saṅgha, announced it up to the third time, and performed the act of seeking approval. Or, a formal agreement should be made and established. And this is how it should be done: a competent monk should make an announcement with the Saṅgha's approval:

“Sāmantagāmehi manussā āgantvā gilānādīnaṃ atthāya phalāphalaṃ yācanti, dve nāḷikerāni, dve tālaphalāni, dve panasāni, pañca ambāni, pañca kadaliphalāni gaṇhantānaṃ anivāraṇaṃ, asukarukkhato ca asukarukkhato ca phalaṃ gaṇhantānaṃ anivāraṇaṃ ruccati bhikkhusaṅghassā”ti tikkhattuṃ vattabbaṃ.

“People from the surrounding villages come and ask for fruits for the sake of the sick and others. Is the non-prevention of those taking two coconuts, two palm fruits, two jackfruits, five mangoes, and five bananas, and the non-prevention of those taking fruit from such-and-such a tree and from such-and-such a tree, agreeable to the Saṅgha of monks?” This should be said three times.

Tato paṭṭhāya gilānādīnaṃ nāmaṃ gahetvā yācantā “gaṇhathā”ti na vattabbā, vattaṃ pana ācikkhitabbaṃ – “nāḷikerādīni iminā nāma paricchedena gaṇhantānaṃ asukarukkhato ca asukarukkhato ca phalaṃ gaṇhantānaṃ anivāraṇaṃ kata”nti. Anuvicaritvā pana “ayaṃ madhuraphalo ambo, ito gaṇhathā”tipi na vattabbā. Phalabhājanakāle pana āgatānaṃ sammatena upaḍḍhabhāgo dātabbo, asammatena apaloketvā dātabbaṃ.

From then on, to those who ask, taking the name of the sick and others, one should not say, “Take them.” Instead, the rule should be explained: “Non-prevention has been established for those taking coconuts and other fruits by this specific limit, and for those taking fruit from such-and-such a tree and from such-and-such a tree.” Furthermore, one should not, after going around with them, say, “This is a sweet-fruited mango tree; take from here.” Furthermore, at the time of distributing the fruit, a half-share should be given to those who have come by an authorized monk. By an unauthorized monk, it should be given after seeking approval.

Khīṇaparibbayo vā maggagamiyasatthavāho vā añño vā issaro āgantvā yācati, apaloketvāva dātabbaṃ. Balakkārena gahetvā khādanto na vāretabbo. Kuddho hi so rukkhepi chindeyya, aññampi anatthaṃ kareyya. Puggalikapariveṇaṃ āgantvā gilānassa gāmena yācanto “amhehi chāyādīnaṃ atthāya ropitaṃ, sace atthi, tumhe jānāthā”ti vattabbo. Yadi [P 7.1409] pana phalabharitāva [V 0.0260] rukkhā honti, kaṇṭake bandhitvā phalavārena khādanti, apaccāsīsantena hutvā dātabbaṃ. Balakkārena gaṇhanto na vāretabbo, pubbe vuttamevettha kāraṇaṃ.

If a traveler whose provisions are exhausted, or a caravan leader on a journey, or another powerful person comes and asks, it should be given only after seeking approval. One who takes by force and eats should not be prevented. For, being angry, he might even cut down the trees or cause other harm. To one who comes to a private monastic compound and asks in the name of a sick person, it should be said: 'It was planted by us for the sake of shade and so on. If there is any fruit, it is for you to know.' But if the trees are laden with fruit, and they tie thorns and eat by turn of the fruit, it should be given without expectation. One who takes by force should not be prevented; the reason here is as stated before.

Saṅghassa [M 0.0257] phalārāmo hoti, paṭijagganaṃ na labhati, sace taṃ koci vattasīsena jaggati, saṅghasseva hoti. Athāpi kassaci paṭibalassa bhikkhuno “imaṃ sappurisa jaggitvā dehī”ti saṅgho bhāraṃ karoti, so ce vattasīsena jaggati, evampi saṅghasseva hoti. Phātikammaṃ paccāsīsantassa pana tatiyabhāgena vā upaḍḍhabhāgena vā phātikammaṃ kātabbaṃ. “Bhāriyaṃ kamma”nti vatvā ettakena anicchanto pana sabbaṃ taveva santakaṃ katvā “mūlabhāgaṃ dasabhāgamattaṃ datvā jaggāhī”tipi vattabbo. Garubhaṇḍattā pana mūlacchejjavasena na dātabbaṃ. So mūlabhāgaṃ datvā khādanto akatāvāsaṃ vā katvā katāvāsaṃ vā jaggitvā nissitakānaṃ ārāmaṃ niyyādeti, tehipi mūlabhāgo dātabbova. Yadā pana bhikkhū sayaṃ jaggituṃ pahonti, atha tesaṃ jaggituñca na dātabbaṃ, jaggitakāle ca na vāretabbā, jagganakāleyeva vāretabbā. “Bahuṃ tumhehi khāyitaṃ, idāni mā jaggittha, bhikkhusaṅghoyeva jaggissatī”ti vattabbaṃ.

There is a fruit orchard belonging to the Saṅgha, but it does not receive tending. If someone tends it as a matter of duty, it still belongs to the Saṅgha. Moreover, if the Saṅgha entrusts a capable monk with the responsibility, saying, 'Good sir, tend this and give the fruit,' and he tends it as a matter of duty, even then it still belongs to the Saṅgha. But for one who expects an allowance, an allowance of a third part or a half part should be made. If, saying, 'The work is burdensome,' he is unwilling for that much, he may also be told: 'Having made the entire property your own, give a base share of about a tenth part and tend it.' However, because it is valuable property, it should not be given away outright. He, having given the base share, consumes the fruit, and having either built a dwelling where there was none or tended an existing dwelling, he hands over the orchard to his dependents; they too must give the base share. But when the monks are able to tend it themselves, then the tending should not be given to them, and during the time it has been tended they should not be prevented; they should be prevented only at the time of assigning the tending. They should be told: 'You have consumed much; now do not tend it, the Saṅgha of monks itself will tend it.'

Sace pana neva vattasīsena jagganto atthi, na phātikammena, na saṅgho jaggituṃ pahoti, eko anāpucchitvāva jaggitvā phātikammaṃ vaḍḍhetvā paccāsīsati, apalokanakammena phātikammaṃ vaḍḍhetvā dātabbaṃ. Iti imaṃ sabbampi kammalakkhaṇameva hoti. Apalokanakammaṃ imāni pañca ṭhānāni gacchati.

But if there is no one to tend it as a matter of duty, nor for an allowance, and the Saṅgha is not able to tend it, and someone, without asking, tends it, increases the yield, and expects an allowance, an increased allowance should be given through an act of seeking approval. Thus, all this indeed has the characteristic of a formal act. The act of seeking approval applies to these five instances.

Ñattikammaṭṭhānabhede pana “suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, itthannāmo itthannāmassa āyasmato upasampadāpekkho, anusiṭṭho so mayā. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, itthannāmo āgaccheyyāti, āgacchāhīti vattabbo”ti evaṃ upasampadāpekkhassa osāraṇā osāraṇā nāma.

Now, in the section on the basis for a formal act with a motion: 'Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. This person named so-and-so is a candidate for higher ordination under the venerable so-and-so. He has been instructed by me. If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, let so-and-so come.' He should be told, 'Come.' Thus, the summoning of a candidate for higher ordination is called the Summoning Motion.

“Suṇantu me āyasmantā, ayaṃ itthannāmo bhikkhu dhammakathiko imassa neva suttaṃ āgacchati, no suttavibhaṅgo, so atthaṃ asallakkhetvā byañjanacchāyāya atthaṃ paṭibāhati. Yadāyasmantānaṃ pattakallaṃ, itthannāmaṃ bhikkhuṃ vuṭṭhāpetvā avasesā imaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ vūpasameyyāmā”ti evaṃ ubbāhikāvinicchaye dhammakathikassa bhikkhuno nissāraṇā nissāraṇā nāma.

‘Venerable sirs, listen to me. This monk, named so-and-so, is a Dhamma speaker, yet neither the Sutta nor the Suttavibhaṅga comes to him. Without discerning the meaning, he rejects the meaning by the shadow of the letter. If it is the right time for the venerable ones, having removed this monk named so-and-so, may we, the remaining ones, settle this legal issue.’ Thus, in the legal procedure of ubbāhikā, the removal of a Dhamma-speaking monk is called the Removal Motion.

“Suṇātu me bhante [P 7.1410] saṅgho, ajjuposatho pannaraso. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho uposathaṃ kareyyā”ti evaṃ uposathakammavasena ṭhapitā ñatti uposatho nāma.

‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. Today is the fifteenth Uposatha day. If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, may the Saṅgha perform the Uposatha.’ Thus, the motion established by way of the Uposatha formal act is called the Uposatha Motion.

“Suṇātu [M 0.0258] me bhante saṅgho, ajja pavāraṇā pannarasī. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho pavāreyyā”ti evaṃ pavāraṇākammavasena ṭhapitā ñatti pavāraṇā nāma.

‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. Today is the fifteenth Pavāraṇā day. If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, may the Saṅgha perform the Pavāraṇā.’ Thus, the motion established by way of the Pavāraṇā formal act is called the Pavāraṇā Motion.

“Suṇātu [V 0.0261] me bhante saṅgho, itthannāmo itthannāmassa āyasmato upasampadāpekkho. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, ahaṃ itthannāmaṃ anusāseyya”nti. “Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, itthannāmo itthannāmaṃ anusāseyyā”ti. “Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, ahaṃ itthannāmaṃ antarāyike dhamme puccheyya”nti. “Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, itthannāmo itthannāmaṃ antarāyike dhamme puccheyyā”ti. “Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, ahaṃ itthannāmaṃ vinayaṃ puccheyya”nti. “Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, itthannāmo itthannāmaṃ vinayaṃ puccheyyā”ti. “Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, ahaṃ itthannāmena vinayaṃ puṭṭho vissajjeyya”nti. “Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, itthannāmo itthannāmena vinayaṃ puṭṭho vissajjeyyā”ti evaṃ attānaṃ vā paraṃ vā sammannituṃ ṭhapitā ñatti sammuti nāma.

‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. This person named so-and-so is a candidate for higher ordination of the venerable one named so-and-so. If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, I should instruct this person named so-and-so.’ ‘If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, may so-and-so instruct so-and-so.’ ‘If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, I should question this person named so-and-so about the obstructive conditions.’ ‘If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, may so-and-so question so-and-so about the obstructive conditions.’ ‘If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, I should question this person named so-and-so about the Vinaya.’ ‘If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, may so-and-so question so-and-so about the Vinaya.’ ‘If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, I, having been questioned about the Vinaya by so-and-so, should answer.’ ‘If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, may so-and-so, having been questioned about the Vinaya by so-and-so, answer.’ Thus, the motion established to appoint oneself or another is called the Appointment Motion.

“Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, idaṃ cīvaraṃ itthannāmassa bhikkhuno nissaggiyaṃ saṅghassa nissaṭṭhaṃ. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho imaṃ cīvaraṃ itthannāmassa bhikkhuno dadeyyā”ti. “Yadāyasmantānaṃ pattakallaṃ, āyasmantā imaṃ cīvaraṃ itthannāmassa bhikkhuno dadeyyu”nti evaṃ nissaṭṭhacīvarapattādīnaṃ dānaṃ dānaṃ nāma.

‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. This robe of the monk named so-and-so is to be forfeited; it has been relinquished to the Saṅgha. If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, may the Saṅgha give this robe to the monk named so-and-so.’ ‘If it is the right time for the venerable ones, may the venerable ones give this robe to the monk named so-and-so.’ Thus, the giving of relinquished items such as robes and bowls is called the Giving Motion.

“Suṇātu me bhante saṅgho, ayaṃ itthannāmo bhikkhu āpattiṃ sarati, vivarati, uttāniṃ karoti, deseti. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, ahaṃ itthannāmassa bhikkhuno āpattiṃ paṭiggaṇheyyanti. Yadāyasmantānaṃ pattakallaṃ, ahaṃ itthannāmassa bhikkhuno āpattiṃ paṭiggaṇheyya”nti. Tena vattabbo “passasī”ti. “Āma passāmī”ti. Āyatiṃ saṃvareyyāsīti evaṃ āpattipaṭiggaho paṭiggaho nāma.

‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. This monk named so-and-so remembers an offense, reveals it, makes it plain, and confesses it. If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, I should accept the offense of the monk named so-and-so.’ ‘If it is the right time for the venerable ones, I should accept the offense of the monk named so-and-so.’ By that one it should be said: ‘Do you see it?’ ‘Yes, I see it.’ ‘In the future, you should restrain yourself.’ Thus, the acceptance of an offense is called acceptance.

“Suṇantu me, āyasmantā āvāsikā. Yadāyasmantānaṃ pattakallaṃ, idāni uposathaṃ kareyyāma, pātimokkhaṃ uddiseyyāma, āgame kāḷe pavāreyyāmā”ti. Te ce, bhikkhave, bhikkhū [V 0.0262] bhaṇḍanakārakā [P 7.1411] kalahakārakā vivādakārakā bhassakārakā saṅghe adhikaraṇakārakā taṃ kāḷaṃ anuvaseyyuṃ, āvāsikena bhikkhunā byattena paṭibalena āvāsikā bhikkhū ñāpetabbā – “suṇantu me, āyasmantā āvāsikā. Yadāyasmantānaṃ [M 0.0259] pattakallaṃ, idāni uposathaṃ kareyyāma, pātimokkhaṃ uddiseyyāma, āgame juṇhe pavāreyyāmā”ti evaṃ katā pavāraṇāpaccukkaḍḍhanā paccukkaḍḍhanā nāma.

‘Venerable resident sirs, listen to me. If it is the right time for the venerable ones, we should now perform the Uposatha and recite the Pātimokkha; we should perform the Pavāraṇā in the coming dark fortnight.’ If, monks, those monks who are makers of factions, makers of strife, makers of disputes, makers of much talk, and makers of legal issues in the Saṅgha should continue to reside during that dark fortnight, a competent and capable resident monk should inform the resident monks: ‘Venerable resident sirs, listen to me. If it is the right time for the venerable ones, we should now perform the Uposatha and recite the Pātimokkha; we should perform the Pavāraṇā in the coming bright fortnight.’ Thus, the postponement of the Pavāraṇā, having been done in this way, is called postponement.

Sabbeheva ekajjhaṃ sannipatitabbaṃ, sannipatitvā byattena bhikkhunā paṭibalena saṅgho ñāpetabbo – “suṇātu me, bhante, saṅgho amhākaṃ bhaṇḍanajātānaṃ kalahajātānaṃ vivādāpannānaṃ viharataṃ bahuṃ assāmaṇakaṃ ajjhāciṇṇaṃ bhāsitaparikkantaṃ. Sace mayaṃ imāhi āpattīhi aññamaññaṃ kāressāma, siyāpi taṃ adhikaraṇaṃ kakkhaḷattāya vāḷattāya bhedāya saṃvatteyya. Yadi saṅghassa pattakallaṃ, saṅgho imaṃ adhikaraṇaṃ tiṇavatthārakena vūpasameyya ṭhapetvā thullavajjaṃ, ṭhapetvā gihipaṭisayutta”nti evaṃ tiṇavatthārakasamathena katvā sabbapaṭhamā sabbasaṅgāhikañatti kammalakkhaṇaṃ nāma.

All should assemble together in one place. Having assembled, the Saṅgha should be informed by a competent and capable monk: ‘Venerable sirs, may the Saṅgha hear me. While we have been living, having become quarrelsome, strife-ridden, and having fallen into disputes, much that is not the work of recluses has been transgressed and practiced by us, spoken and acted upon. If we were to have one another dealt with for these offenses, that legal issue might possibly lead to harshness, fierceness, and schism. If it is the right time for the Saṅgha, the Saṅgha should settle this legal issue by the method of covering with grass, setting aside grave offenses and offenses connected with householders.’ Thus, the very first, all-inclusive motion, having been made with the Tiṇavatthāraka settlement, is called a characteristic of a formal act.

Tathā tato parā ekekasmiṃ pakkhe ekekaṃ katvā dve ñattiyo iti yathāvuttappabhedaṃ osāraṇaṃ nissāraṇaṃ…pe… kammalakkhaṇaññeva navamanti ñattikammaṃ imāni nava ṭhānāni gacchati.

Likewise, after that, there are two motions made by making one in each faction. Thus, with the divisions as have been stated: reinstatement, removal… and so on… with the ninth being the characteristic of a formal act itself. Thus, a formal act with a single motion applies to these nine instances.

Ñattidutiyakammaṭṭhānabhede pana vaḍḍhassa licchavino pattanikkujjanavasena khandhake vuttā nissāraṇā. Tasseva pattukkujjanavasena vuttā osāraṇā ca veditabbā.

Now, in the classification of the instances of a formal act with a motion as the second part, the removal of Vaḍḍha the Licchavi by way of overturning the bowl, as spoken of in the Khandhaka, should be understood. And the reinstatement of that very person, spoken of by way of righting the bowl, should also be understood.

Sīmāsammuti ticīvarena avippavāsasammuti, santhatasammuti, bhattuddesaka-senāsanaggāhāpaka-bhaṇḍāgārika-cīvarapaṭiggāhaka-cīvarabhājaka-yāgubhājakaphalabhājaka-khajjabhājaka-appamattakavissajjaka-sāṭiyaggāhāpaka-pattaggāhāpaka-ārāmikapesakasāmaṇerapesakasammutīti etāsaṃ sammutīnaṃ vasena sammuti veditabbā. Kathinacīvaradānamatakacīvaradānavasena dānaṃ veditabbaṃ.

Appointment should be understood by way of these appointments: appointment of a boundary; appointment of not being separated from the three robes; appointment of a spreading-cloth; appointment of a meal-assigner, a lodging-assigner, a storeroom-keeper, a robe-receiver, a robe-distributor, a gruel-distributor, a fruit-distributor, a hard-food-distributor, a distributor of minor items, a receiver of bathing cloths, a receiver of bowls, one who sends monastery attendants, and one who sends novices. Giving should be understood by way of giving the Kathina robe and giving robes for the deceased.

Kathinuddhāravasena uddhāro veditabbo. Kuṭivatthuvihāravatthudesanāvasena desanā veditabbā. Yā [V 0.0263] pana tiṇavatthārakasamathe sabbasaṅgāhikañattiñca ekekasmiṃ pakkhe ekekaṃ ñattiñcāti tisso ñattiyo ṭhapetvā puna ekasmiṃ pakkhe ekā, ekasmiṃ pakkhe ekāti dve ñattidutiyakammavācā vuttā, tāsaṃ vasena kammalakkhaṇaṃ veditabbaṃ[P 7.1412]. Iti ñattidutiyakammaṃ imāni satta ṭhānāni gacchati.

Withdrawal should be understood by means of the withdrawal of the kathina. Indication should be understood by means of indicating the site for a hut and the site for a monastery. Furthermore, in the Tiṇavatthāraka settlement, having set aside the three motions—namely, the all-inclusive motion and one motion for each faction—the characteristic of a formal act should be understood by means of the two formal acts with a motion as the second part that were stated, that is, one in one faction and one in the other faction. Thus, a formal act with a motion as the second part applies to these seven instances.

Ñatticatutthakammaṭṭhānabhede [M 0.0260] pana tajjanīyakammādīnaṃ sattannaṃ kammānaṃ vasena nissāraṇā, tesaṃyeva ca kammānaṃ paṭippassambhanavasena osāraṇā veditabbā. Bhikkhunovādakasammutivasena sammuti veditabbā. Parivāsadānamānattadānavasena dānaṃ veditabbaṃ. Mūlāyapaṭikassanakammavasena niggaho veditabbo. “Ukkhittānuvattikā aṭṭha, yāvatatiyakā ariṭṭho caṇḍakāḷī ca ime te yāvatatiyakā”ti imāsaṃ ekādasannaṃ samanubhāsanānaṃ vasena samanubhāsanā veditabbā. Upasampadākammaabbhānakammavasena pana kammalakkhaṇaṃ veditabbaṃ. Iti ñatticatutthakammaṃ imāni satta ṭhānāni gacchati.

Furthermore, in the classification of the instances of a formal act with a motion as the fourth part, removal should be understood by means of the seven formal acts beginning with the act of rebuke, and reinstatement should be understood by means of the pacification of those same formal acts. Appointment should be understood by means of the appointment as an instructor of bhikkhunīs. Giving should be understood by means of the giving of probation and the giving of mānatta. Censure should be understood by means of the formal act of drawing back to the root. Formal accusation should be understood by means of these eleven formal accusations: ‘The eight followers of a suspended bhikkhu, the “up to the third time” cases, Ariṭṭha and Caṇḍakāḷī—these are the “up to the third time” cases.’ Furthermore, the characteristic of a formal act should be understood by means of the formal act of higher ordination and the formal act of rehabilitation. Thus, a formal act with a motion as the fourth part applies to these seven instances.

497. Iti kammāni ca kammavipattiñca vipattivirahitānaṃ kammānaṃ ṭhānapabhedagamanañca dassetvā idāni tesaṃ kammānaṃ kārakassa saṅghassa paricchedaṃ dassento puna “catuvaggakaraṇe kamme”tiādimāha. Tassattho parisato kammavipattivaṇṇanāyaṃ vuttanayeneva veditabboti.

497. Thus, having shown the formal acts, the failure of formal acts, and the proceeding to different instances for formal acts free from failure, now, wishing to show the definition of the Saṅgha that performs those formal acts, the venerable Upāli again spoke the passage beginning, ‘In a formal act to be performed by a chapter of four…’. The meaning of this should be understood only by the method stated in the explanation of the failure of a formal act on account of the assembly.

Kammavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Chapter on Formal Acts is concluded.

Atthavasavaggādivaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Chapter on the Benefits of the Training Rules and so on

498. Idāni yāni tāni tesaṃ kammānaṃ vatthubhūtāni sikkhāpadāni, tesaṃ paññattiyaṃ ānisaṃsaṃ dassetuṃ “dve atthavase paṭiccā”tiādi āraddhaṃ. Tattha diṭṭhadhammikānaṃ verānaṃ saṃvarāyāti pāṇātipātādīnaṃ pañcannaṃ diṭṭhadhammikaverānaṃ saṃvaratthāya pidahanatthāya. Samparāyikānaṃ verānaṃ paṭighātāyāti vipākadukkhasaṅkhātānaṃ samparāyikaverānaṃ paṭighātatthāya, samucchedanatthāya anuppajjanatthāya. Diṭṭhadhammikānaṃ vajjānaṃ saṃvarāyāti tesaṃyeva pañcannaṃ verānaṃ saṃvaratthāya. Samparāyikānaṃ vajjānanti tesaṃyeva vipākadukkhānaṃ. Vipākadukkhāneva hi idha vajjanīyabhāvato [V 0.0264] vajjānīti vuttāni. Diṭṭhadhammikānaṃ bhayānanti garahā upavādo tajjanīyādīni kammāni uposathapavāraṇānaṃ ṭhapanaṃ akittipakāsanīyakammanti etāni diṭṭhadhammikabhayāni nāma, etesaṃ saṃvaratthāya. Samparāyikabhayāni pana vipākadukkhāniyeva[P 7.1413], tesaṃ paṭighātatthāya. Diṭṭhadhammikānaṃ akusalānanti pañcaveradasaakusalakammapathappabhedānaṃ [M 0.0261] akusalānaṃ saṃvaratthāya. Vipākadukkhāneva pana akkhamaṭṭhena samparāyikaakusalānīti vuccanti, tesaṃ paṭighātatthāya. Gihīnaṃ anukampāyāti agārikānaṃ saddhārakkhaṇavasena anukampanatthāya. Pāpicchānaṃ pakkhupacchedāyāti pāpicchapuggalānaṃ gaṇabandhabhedanatthāya gaṇabhojanasikkhāpadaṃ paññattaṃ. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānameva. Yañhettha vattabbaṃ siyā, taṃ sabbaṃ paṭhamapārājikavaṇṇanāyameva vuttanti.

498. Now, in order to show the benefit in the laying down of those training rules which are the basis for those formal acts, the venerable Upāli Thera undertook the passage beginning, ‘Dependent on two benefits…’. Therein, ‘for the restraint of enmities in the present life’ means for the purpose of restraining, for the purpose of shutting out, the five enmities in the present life, beginning with the destruction of living beings. ‘For the warding off of enmities in the future life’ means for the purpose of warding off, for the purpose of cutting off without remainder, for the purpose of the non-arising of enmities in the future life, which are known as the suffering of results. ‘For the restraint of faults in the present life’ means for the purpose of restraining those same five enmities. As for ‘faults in the future life,’ this refers to that same suffering of results. Indeed, here the suffering of results is called ‘faults’ because it is to be avoided. As for ‘dangers in the present life,’ blame in one's presence, reproach in one's absence, formal acts beginning with the act of rebuke, the suspension of the Uposatha and Pavāraṇā, and the formal act of making one's ill-repute known—these are called dangers in the present life; it is for the purpose of restraining these. Dangers in the future life, however, are just the suffering of results; it is for the purpose of warding these off. As for ‘unwholesome states in the present life,’ it is for the purpose of restraining unwholesome states classified as the five enmities and the ten unwholesome courses of action. The suffering of results, however, is called ‘unwholesome states in the future life’ in the sense of being unbearable; it is for the purpose of warding these off. ‘Out of compassion for householders’ means for the purpose of compassion towards house-dwellers by means of protecting their faith. ‘For the breaking up of the faction of those with evil wishes’ means for the purpose of destroying the faction-forming of persons with evil wishes, the training rule on group meals was laid down. The remainder is clear in every case. For whatever should be said here has all been stated by me in the commentary on the first Pārājika.

Sikkhāpadesu atthavasena vaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the training rules by way of benefits is concluded.

499. Pātimokkhādīsu pātimokkhuddesoti bhikkhūnaṃ pañcavidho bhikkhunīnaṃ catubbidho. Parivāsadānādīsu osāraṇīyaṃ paññattanti aṭṭhārasasu vā tecattālīsāya vā vattesu vattamānassa osāraṇīyaṃ paññattaṃ. Yena kammena osārīyati, taṃ kammaṃ paññattanti attho. Nissāraṇīyaṃ paññattanti bhaṇḍanakārakādayo yena kammena nissārīyanti, taṃ kammaṃ paññattanti attho.

499. In the section on the Pātimokkha and so on, ‘the recitation of the Pātimokkha’ is fivefold for bhikkhus and fourfold for bhikkhunīs. In the section on the giving of probation and so on, ‘the act of reinstatement is laid down’ means that the act of reinstatement is laid down for one who is observing either the eighteen or the forty-three duties. The meaning is: the formal act by which one is reinstated is laid down. ‘The act of expulsion is laid down’ means: the formal act by which quarrel-makers and so on are expelled is laid down.

500. Apaññattetiādīsu apaññatte paññattanti sattāpattikkhandhā kakusandhañca sammāsambuddhaṃ koṇāgamanañca kassapañca sammāsambuddhaṃ ṭhapetvā antarā kenaci apaññatte sikkhāpade paññattaṃ nāma. Makkaṭivatthuādivinītakathā sikkhāpade paññatte anupaññattaṃ nāma. Sesaṃ sabbattha uttānamevāti.

500. In the section on ‘not laid down’ and so on, ‘laid down where not laid down’ is the name for the laying down of the training rules comprising the seven groups of offenses, which, setting aside the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One Kakusandha, the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One Koṇāgamana, and the Perfectly Self-Enlightened One Kassapa, had not been laid down by anyone in the interim. The resolved case of the monkey-story and so on is the name for an ‘additional laying down,’ when a training rule has already been laid down. The remainder is clear in every case.

Ānisaṃsavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Chapter of Benefits is concluded.

501. Idāni sabbasikkhāpadānaṃ ekekena ākārena navadhā saṅgahaṃ dassetuṃ “nava saṅgahā”tiādimāha. Tattha vatthusaṅgahoti vatthunā saṅgaho. Evaṃ sesesupi padattho veditabbo. Ayaṃ panettha atthayojanā – yasmā hi ekasikkhāpadampi avatthusmiṃ paññattaṃ natthi, tasmā sabbāni vatthunā saṅgahitānīti evaṃ tāva vatthusaṅgaho veditabbo.

501. Now, in order to show the ninefold inclusion of all the training rules by way of each single aspect, the venerable Upāli Thera spoke the passage beginning, ‘Nine inclusions…’. Therein, ‘inclusion by basis’ means inclusion by basis. In this way, the meaning of the remaining terms should also be understood. This is the application of the meaning here: because not even a single training rule is laid down without a basis, therefore all are included by basis. Thus, first, the inclusion by basis should be understood.

Yasmā [M 0.0262][V 0.0265] pana dve āpattikkhandhā sīlavipattiyā saṅgahitā, pañcāpattikkhandhā ācāravipattiyā, cha sikkhāpadāni [P 7.1414] ājīvavipattiyā saṅgahitāni, tasmā sabbānipi vipattiyā saṅgahitānīti evaṃ vipattisaṅgaho veditabbo.

Furthermore, because two groups of offenses are included by failure in morality, five groups of offenses are included by failure in conduct, and six training rules are included by failure in livelihood, therefore all are included by failure. Thus, the inclusion by failure should be understood.

Yasmā pana sattahāpattīhi muttaṃ ekasikkhāpadampi natthi, tasmā sabbāni āpattiyā saṅgahitānīti evaṃ āpattisaṅgaho veditabbo.

Furthermore, because there is not even a single training rule that is free from the seven offenses, therefore all are included by offense. Thus, the inclusion by offense should be understood.

Sabbāni ca sattasu nagaresu paññattānīti nidānena saṅgahitānīti evaṃ nidānasaṅgaho veditabbo.

Furthermore, all the training rules were laid down in the seven cities; thus, they are included by origin. In this way, the inclusion by origin should be understood.

Yasmā pana ekasikkhāpadampi ajjhācārikapuggale asati paññattaṃ natthi, tasmā sabbāni puggalena saṅgahitānīti evaṃ puggalasaṅgaho veditabbo.

Since not even a single training rule is laid down in the absence of a transgressing person, therefore all are included by the person. In this way, the inclusion by person should be understood.

Sabbāni pana pañcahi ceva sattahi ca āpattikkhandhehi saṅgahitāni, sabbāni na vinā chahi samuṭṭhānehi samuṭṭhantīti samuṭṭhānena saṅgahitāni. Sabbāni ca catūsu adhikaraṇesu āpattādhikaraṇena saṅgahitāni. Sabbāni sattahi samathehi samathaṃ gacchantīti samathehi saṅgahitāni. Evamettha khandhasamuṭṭhānaadhikaraṇasamathasaṅgahāpi veditabbā. Sesaṃ pubbe vuttanayamevāti.

Furthermore, all are included by the five and seven groups of offenses. All do not arise without the six origins; thus, they are included by origin. Furthermore, all are included among the four legal issues by the legal issue concerning offenses. All are settled by the seven means of settlement; thus, they are included by the means of settlement. In this way, here, the inclusions by group, origin, legal issue, and settlement should also be understood. The remainder is just as the method previously stated.

Samantapāsādikāya vinayasaṃvaṇṇanāya

In the Samantapāsādikā, the commentary on the Vinaya,

Navasaṅgahitavaggavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Chapter of the Ninefold Inclusion is concluded.

Niṭṭhitā ca parivārassa anuttānatthapadavaṇṇanāti.

And the commentary on the terms of unclear meaning in the Parivāra is concluded.

Nigamanakathā

The Concluding Discourse

Ettāvatā [M 0.0263] ca –

And thus far:

Ubhato vibhaṅgakhandhaka-parivāravibhattidesanaṃ nātho;

Vinayapiṭakaṃ vinento, veneyyaṃ yaṃ jino āha.

The Lord, the Conqueror, while training those fit to be trained, spoke the Vinaya Piṭaka, the teaching divided into the two Vibhaṅgas, the Khandhakas, and the Parivāra.

Samadhikasattavīsati-sahassamattena [V 0.0266] tassa ganthena;

Saṃvaṇṇanā samattā, samantapāsādikā nāma.

The commentary on that Vinaya Piṭaka, named Samantapāsādikā, is completed, with a text measuring more than twenty-seven thousand units.

Tatridaṃ samantapāsādikāya samantapāsādikattasmiṃ –

In that regard, this concerns the Samantapāsādikā's quality of being 'All-Around Pleasing':

Ācariyaparamparato, nidānavatthuppabhedadīpanato;

Parasamayavivajjanato, sakasamayavisuddhito ceva.

Through the succession of teachers, through the elucidation of the divisions of the origins and subject matters, through the avoidance of other doctrines, and through the purity of its own doctrine.

Byañjanaparisodhanato, padatthato pāḷiyojanakkamato;

Sikkhāpadanicchayato, vibhaṅganayabhedadassanato.

Through the purification of the phrasing, through the meaning of the words, through the sequential method of construing the Pāḷi, through the determination of the training rules, and through showing the different methods of analysis as in the Vibhaṅga.

Sampassataṃ na dissati, kiñci apāsādikaṃ yato ettha;

Viññūnamayaṃ tasmā, samantapāsādikātveva.

Since nothing unpleasing is seen herein by the wise who clearly perceive, therefore this commentary is indeed 'All-Around Pleasing'.

Saṃvaṇṇanā pavattā, vinayassa vineyyadamanakusalena;

Vuttassa lokanāthena, lokamanukampamānenāti.

This commentary on the Vinaya, spoken by the Lord of the World who is skilled in taming those fit to be trained and has compassion for the world, has come to be.

Mahāaṭṭhakathañceva[P 7.1415], mahāpaccarimevaca;

Kurundiñcāti tissopi, sīhaḷaṭṭhakathā imā.

The Mahā-aṭṭhakathā, the Mahāpaccarī, and the Kurundī—these three are the Sinhalese Commentaries.

Buddhamittoti nāmena, vissutassa yasassino;

Vinayaññussa dhīrassa, sutvā therassa santike.

Having heard from the Elder—the wise one, knower of the Vinaya, renowned and glorious, known by the name of Buddhamitta.

Mahāmeghavanuyyāne, bhūmibhāge patiṭṭhito;

Mahāvihāro yo satthu, mahābodhivibhūsito.

That Mahāvihāra which is established on a plot of ground in the Mahāmeghavana park, adorned with the great Bodhi tree of the Teacher.

Yaṃ tassa dakkhiṇe bhāge, padhānagharamuttamaṃ;

Sucicārittasīlena, bhikkhusaṅghena sevitaṃ.

Which excellent meditation hall is in its southern part, frequented by the community of monks of pure conduct and virtue.

Uḷārakulasambhūto[V 0.0267], saṅghupaṭṭhāyako sadā;

Anākulāya saddhāya, pasanno ratanattaye.

Born into a noble family, always a supporter of the Saṅgha, devoted to the Triple Gem with untroubled faith.

Mahānigamasāmīti[M 0.0264], vissuto tattha kārayi;

Cārupākārasañcitaṃ, yaṃ pāsādaṃ manoramaṃ.

He, famous as the Lord of Mahānigama, had a delightful mansion built there, enclosed by a beautiful wall.

Sītacchāyatarūpetaṃ, sampannasalilāsayaṃ;

Vasatā tatra pāsāde, mahānigamasāmino.

A mansion endowed with trees of cool shade and furnished with a reservoir of water. While I was dwelling in that mansion of the Lord of Mahānigama,

Sucisīlasamācāraṃ, theraṃ buddhasirivhayaṃ;

Yā uddisitvā āraddhā, iddhā vinayavaṇṇanā.

This prosperous commentary on the Vinaya was undertaken, having been dedicated to the Elder named Buddhasiri, who is of pure virtue and conduct.

Pālayantassa sakalaṃ, laṅkādīpaṃ nirabbudaṃ;

Rañño sirinivāsassa, siripālayasassino.

Of the renowned King Siripāla, the abode of glory, who was protecting the entire island of Laṅkā, free from calamity.

Samavīsatime kheme, jayasaṃvacchare ayaṃ;

Āraddhā ekavīsamhi, sampatte pariniṭṭhitā.

This commentary was begun in the twentieth, the peaceful and victorious year of his reign, and was completed when the twenty-first year arrived.

Upaddavā kule loke, nirupaddavato ayaṃ;

Ekasaṃvacchareneva, yathā niṭṭhaṃ upāgatā.

Just as this commentary, in a world full of calamities, reached its completion without trouble in just one year,

Evaṃ sabbassa lokassa, niṭṭhaṃ dhammūpasaṃhitā;

Sīghaṃ gacchantu ārambhā, sabbepi nirupaddavā.

So too, may all undertakings of the entire world that are connected with the Dhamma, being without trouble, quickly reach completion.

Ciraṭṭhitatthaṃ dhammassa, karontena mayā imaṃ;

Saddhammabahumānena, yañca puññaṃ samācitaṃ.

And whatever merit has been accumulated by me, who made this commentary for the sake of the long endurance of the Dhamma and with great respect for the True Dhamma,

Sabbassa [V 0.0268] ānubhāvena, tassa sabbepi pāṇino;

Bhavantu dhammarājassa, saddhammarasasevino.

Through the power of all that merit, may all beings become partakers of the taste of the True Dhamma of the King of Dhamma.

Ciraṃ tiṭṭhatu saddhammo, kāle vassaṃ ciraṃ pajaṃ;

Tappetu devo dhammena, rājā rakkhatu medininti.

May the True Dhamma long endure. May the deva send rain in due season and long satisfy the people. May the king righteously protect the earth.

Paramavisuddhasaddhābuddhivīriyapaṭimaṇḍitena [P 7.1416] sīlācārajjavamaddavādiguṇasamudayasamuditena sakasamayasamayantaragahanajjhogāhaṇasamatthena paññāveyyattiyasamannāgatena tipiṭakapariyattippabhede sāṭṭhakathe satthusāsane appaṭihataññāṇappabhāvena mahāveyyākaraṇena karaṇasampattijanitasukhaviniggatamadhurodāravacanalāvaṇṇayuttena yuttamuttavādinā vādivarena mahākavinā pabhinnapaasambhidāparivāre chaḷabhiññādipabhedaguṇapaṭimaṇḍite uttarimanussadhamme [M 0.0265] suppatiṭṭhitabuddhīnaṃ theravaṃsappadīpānaṃ therānaṃ mahāvihāravāsīnaṃ vaṃsālaṅkārabhūtena vipulavisuddhabuddhinā buddhaghosoti garūhi gahitanāmadheyyena therena katā ayaṃ samantapāsādikā nāma vinayasaṃvaṇṇanā –

This commentary on the Vinaya, named the Samantapāsādikā, was composed by the elder whose name 'Buddhaghosa' was given by his venerable teachers; who was adorned with exceedingly pure faith, wisdom, and energy; who was an assemblage of virtues such as morality, good conduct, uprightness, and gentleness; who was capable of penetrating the thickets of his own and other doctrines; who was endowed with sharpness of wisdom; who possessed the power of unimpeded knowledge in the Teacher's Dispensation, which consists of the Tipiṭaka scriptures with its commentaries; who was a great grammarian; who was endowed with the charm of sweet and noble words emerging with ease from the perfection of the organs of speech; who was a speaker of what is suitable and pleasing; who was foremost among speakers and a great poet; who possessed vast and pure wisdom; and who became an ornament to the lineage of the elders residing in the Mahāvihāra—those who were lamps unto the lineage of the Theras, whose wisdom was well-established in the superior human state, which is adorned with qualities such as the six super-knowledges and accompanied by the analytical knowledges.

Tāva tiṭṭhatu lokasmiṃ, lokanittharaṇesinaṃ;

Dassentī kulaputtānaṃ, nayaṃ sīlavisuddhiyā.

For so long may it remain in the world, showing the method for the purity of virtue to the sons of good family who seek to escape from the world.

Yāva buddhoti nāmampi, suddhacittassa tādino;

Lokamhi lokajeṭṭhassa, pavattati mahesinoti.

As long as the name 'Buddha' of the pure-minded Such One, the foremost of the world, the great sage, prevails in the world.

Samantapāsādikā nāma

Named the Samantapāsādikā.

Vinaya-aṭṭhakathā niṭṭhitā.

The Vinaya Commentary is finished.
